# Progress

## Phase 0 — Architecture and scaffold (complete, 2026-09-26)

**Delivered**
- Architecture package in `docs/` (sections #1–#31; see the section map at the top of ARCHITECTURE.md).
- Canonical Zod schemas for 22 record kinds in 5 layers (`src/core/schema/`), including nested IDs, the reference registry (`references.ts`) and the dataset container.
- Typed contracts for every module (`**/contracts.ts`): dataset/ChangeSet, references, indexes, validation, commands/store/editor sessions, migrations, text, graph/projections/route search, persistence, io, layout, render scene, chem, search, worker RPC.
- Fixtures: `data/fixtures/schema/v1/minimal-valid.orn.json`; legacy `v6-demo-export.json`, `v4-sample.json` and the oracle `v4-sample.normalized-by-v6.json` generated by executing the legacy app's own data code.
- Toolchain config: Vite (production CSP), Vitest, ESLint (security + core purity rules), dependency-cruiser boundaries, file-size and hardcode guard scripts, schema smoke test.
- `CLAUDE.md` with rules, amendment procedure and per-phase read map.

**Verification performed**
- `tsc` 6.0.3 type-check of all schemas and contracts against zod 3.23.8 + immer 10 (the schemas use only the API subset shared by Zod 3 and 4). Re-check against Zod 4 after `npm install`.
- Runtime smoke (tsx): the minimal fixture parses and round-trips unchanged; unknown keys, wrong prefixes, prefix/kind mismatch, product→reagent refs, bidi characters, blank names, `javascript:`/credential URLs, oversized SMILES, NaN/Infinity and missing arrays are rejected; Unicode names and large molfiles accepted.
- `check:size` and `check:hardcode` pass.

**Decisions** — ADR-001…026 (docs/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md).

**Findings from the legacy code worth remembering**
- v6 hubs carry representative SMILES (Arenes = anthracene); v4 reagent strings put temperatures such as "443 K" into reagent labels; v6 auto-marks the first product as major; condition flags were regex-inferred. All handled in MIGRATION.md §4.

**Open items** — see `openItems` in PROJECT_STATE.json.

### Step 1.2 — Maximal and invalid fixtures; full schema suite (2026-09-26)

**Delivered**
- `data/fixtures/schema/v1/maximal-valid.orn.json`: every record kind, every optional field, every array non-empty, every discriminated-union branch (Temperature/Pressure/Duration kinds, SubstanceRef/SpeciesOrClassRef kinds, all four ElectronSite kinds). Referentially consistent: nested IDs are globally unique, and local/nested references resolve. Records sorted by id; keys in schema order. Built around a Grignard synthesis (ketone → 3° alcohol → alkene route, reverse_of dehydration/hydration, SET and polar mechanism variants). Every record is `review_required` with the reason "Schema fixture … not curated teaching content".
- `data/fixtures/schema/v1/invalid/`: 32 L1 files (unknown keys at record and nested level, missing field, wrong type, malformed ID, wrong prefix, `{kind,id}` mismatch, product→reagent, operational ID as a reference, kind literal, enum, blank/bidi/control/newline text, text/array/structure caps, `javascript:` and credential URLs, `1e999` → Infinity, non-integer, out of range, zero coefficient, zone-offset timestamp, stored `feeds_into`, bad sha256, wrong format, newer schemaVersion, missing/unknown collection, bad datasetId). 9 L2 files (REF-001…REF-008, with REF-006 split into record and nested ID). `manifest.json` records the expectations.
- Test helpers (`src/test-support/`): `schema-walk.ts` (Zod 4 introspection: static node walk, value walk, coverage expectations/observations), `schema-shapes.ts` (shared value schemas, text vs machine-string classification), `schema-mutations.ts` (schema-derived mutations with expected accept/reject).
- Tests (`src/core/schema/`):
  - `maximal-fixture.test.ts`: parses unchanged; 335 schema-derived coverage expectations met; unique IDs; sorted collections.
  - `schema-mutations.test.ts`: every record kind in the maximal fixture is mutated at every node, plus the document envelope. Covers caps at the limit and one past it (strings, arrays, numbers), ID patterns and prefixes, enums (every value), literals, discriminators, required keys, type errors, NaN/±Infinity, control/bidi/newline rules per text type, injected UI keys, and cross-kind rejection. Adding a plain string field without classifying it fails this suite.
  - `ids.test.ts`, `primitives.test.ts`: prefix registry, ID body grammar, text caps and character classes, timestamps, numbers, SafeUrl, StructureRep, JsonValue, Names.
  - `schema-layering.test.ts`: typed ID fields respect layers (INV-05/29), no curriculum fields in layer 1–2 (INV-13/30), selectivity only on outcomes (INV-08), derived relations not storable (INV-11), relationship specs, RecordRef/SubstanceRef/SpeciesOrClassRef kinds (INV-02, ADV-023), presentation records carry provenance only (INV-14).
  - `invalid-fixtures.test.ts`: manifest ↔ files; each L1 file fails only at its declared path; each L2 file passes L1 unchanged and names an existing record and a registry path.
- Invariants/ADV rows tested at L1: INV-01 (fixture), 02, 05, 08, 10, 11, 13, 14 (schema shape), 24, 29, 30; ADV-023, 029, 033 (schema part), 034, 036 (schema keeps unparsable SMILES), 037 (NaN/Infinity; the below-absolute-zero part is RXN-014, step 1.8).

**Decisions**
- Fixture completeness is derived from the schema, not from a hand-kept list. Reusable value schemas (ConditionParameters, refs, StructureRep, …) are covered once across all locations. Record-level fields are covered per kind.
- The fixture generator was a throwaway script. Fixtures are maintained as data from now on, and the coverage test lists exactly what a schema change needs added.
- `scripts/check-hardcode.mjs` now collects IDs only from `id` keys, where they are defined. Before, it collected every ID-shaped string, which flagged enum values such as `non_polar` and `non_aromatic` in `src/core/schema` as dataset IDs. Verified that a real fixture ID in production code still fails the check.

**Verification** — `npm run check` green: 7 test files, 236 tests; check:hardcode 140 ids / 99 names.

**Open items**
- RESOLVED in step 1.2a — `SafeUrlSchema` control/bidi gap and `embeddedAssets[].base64` character set (see Amendments).
- DECIDED (step 1.2a) — REF-002 vs REF-003: a reference to a higher layer reports only REF-003; step 1.7 checks the layer first and emits exactly one diagnostic (VALIDATION_RULES.md §3).
- `check:hardcode` name matching will flag schema enum literals when demo names coincide (e.g. a concept named "Elimination" vs the `elimination` step kind). ### Step 1.2a — URL and base64 tightening (2026-09-26)
See Amendments. 5 invalid fixtures added (2 URL, 3 base64) to `invalid/manifest.json`; new tests for URL control/bidi characters and `Base64Schema` (including a linear-time check at the 28 MB cap). The mutation generator skips cap padding for base64 (padding would break the alphabet; the cap is tested directly). `npm run check` green (see report).

### Step 1.3 — `core/text`: normaliser and mini-markup parser (2026-09-26)

**Delivered** (`src/core/text/`, contracts unchanged)
- `normalizer.ts`: `fold` (NFKC → lowercase → Latin diacritics removed → Greek names → apostrophes removed → dash unification → whitespace collapse) and `tokens` (folded words, adjacent pairs joined, whole phrase joined; edge punctuation stripped; deduplicated, deterministic, ≤ 3n tokens). `fold` is total, linear and idempotent.
- `richtext.ts`: `parseRichText` (paragraphs, bullets, `*em*`, `**strong**`, `_{sub}`, `^{sup}`, `[[id]]`/`[[id|label]]` links) and `richTextLinks` (distinct links, first-appearance order, for REF-008). Total: malformed markup is kept as literal text; no character is dropped.
- `index.ts` barrel. Tests: `normalizer.test.ts` (28), `richtext.test.ts` (37) incl. malformed-literal table, HTML/Markdown inertness (ADV-033), bounded-work cases and a seeded 300-run totality check.
- Invariants/ADV touched: INV-24 (fold never stored, parsing never alters data), ADV-022 (duplicate-name folding basis), ADV-033 (markup inert), REF-008 (link extraction).

**Decisions**
- Dash rule (refines SEARCH.md §2 item 4, which is otherwise self-contradictory for `Br⁻ → br-`): a dash directly after a letter/digit and not before one is a charge sign and is kept as `-`; every other dash variant becomes a space. Side effect: `10⁻³` folds to `10 3`. Docs updated in the same commit.
- Only U+0300–U+036F are stripped as diacritics so Devanagari and other scripts survive.
- Emphasis delimiters must touch non-space text (`2 * 3 * 4` stays literal). Sub/sup/link bodies are single-line; sub/sup non-empty; link IDs must be valid record IDs, an empty label after `|` is malformed, and a missing label defaults to the target ID.
- Bounds: inline nesting depth ≤ 4 (deeper content literal); closer searches share a work budget of 1,000 + 50 × length, after which openers are literal. Adversarial inputs (16k unclosed openers) parse in well under a second.

**Open items**
- Renderers (step 5.x) resolve a link label that equals its target ID to the record's display name; the parser stays chemistry-free.

### Step 1.4 — `core/references`: path walker and reference engine (2026-09-26)

**Delivered** (`src/core/references/`, contracts and registry unchanged)
- `paths.ts`: registry path parser (`a[].b`), bounded walker (`matchPath`, own properties only, wrongly typed parts yield no match) and immutable `applyEdits` (sets first, removals last-to-first so recorded indices stay valid, duplicates applied once, unchanged input returns the same object).
- `engine.ts`: `createReferenceEngine(specs, nestedSpecs)` implementing `ReferenceEngine`: `extract` (concrete path, target ID/kind, `containerRecordId` for nested targets via sibling/root `via`), `nestedIds`, `remap` (own ID + nested IDs + every reference, including `{kind,id}` objects), `detach` (only `detach` specs; removes the spec's `container` or the value itself). No field or kind is named in code. A `container` that is not a leading part of its path, or a malformed path, throws at construction (programmer error).
- Tests (42): `paths.test.ts`, `engine.test.ts` (custom registry covering every target type, container removal, index shifting, malformed data, immutability), `registry-coverage.test.ts` over the maximal fixture: every ID-shaped value is covered (with a non-vacuity check), every spec matches something in the fixture, every extracted reference resolves with the right kind/local/nested container, nested IDs unique with record IDs (INV-01), full remap equals a plain string replacement and stays schema-valid, detaching any single target keeps the record schema-valid, restrict/cascade references are never detached.
- Invariants touched: INV-01 (nested ID uniqueness input), INV-24 (engine returns new values, never mutates), INV-05 layering data is left to L2 (step 1.7).

**Decisions**
- `detach` removes exactly the registry-declared location. Where that leaves a record needing a rule (a mechanism step species with only its `ref`, MEC-002) the L3 rule reports it; the engine does not invent a replacement.
- `provenance.legacy.legacyId` and `provenance.legacy.unmapped` hold foreign identifiers, so the coverage test excludes them explicitly (named list in the test). They are never resolved or remapped.
- `targetKind` is set for `id` and `ref` targets only (as the contract says); for nested targets the expected kind is `spec.target.kind`.

**Open items**
- `remap` does not rewrite `[[id]]` links inside rich text (not registry references; REF-008 warns on dangling ones). Import-as-copy (step 3.x) must decide whether to rewrite them via `richTextLinks`.
- `embeddedAssets[].assetId` is a document-level reference outside the record registry; handled by the import/export code, not here.

### Step 1.5 — `core/dataset`: document ↔ state, serialisation, ID factory, ChangeSets (2026-09-26)

**Delivered** (`src/core/dataset/`)
- `ids.ts`: `createIdFactory(clock, rng)` → `{ now(), newId(prefix) }` (structurally a `CommandContext`). `<prefix>_<lowercase ULID>`: 10 time + 16 random Crockford-base32 characters. Monotonic per factory (same millisecond increments the random part; a clock stepping backwards keeps the last time), so IDs sort in creation order. Overflow, a malformed prefix, an out-of-range clock or an RNG value outside [0, 1) throw `RangeError` (programmer errors).
- `document.ts`: `documentToState` (deep-frozen state; duplicate IDs across all kinds returned as a value, never merged; embedded asset bytes returned beside the state) and `stateToDocument` (records sorted by ID in code-unit order, every kind present, `embeddedAssets` only when supplied and non-empty).
- `serialize.ts`: `serializeDocument` / `deserializeDocument`. Key order is schema order because the document is re-parsed through the strict schema; 2-space indent, LF, trailing newline. Invalid input yields `{ ok: false, issues }` with paths; nothing is repaired.
- `changeset.ts`: `assembleChangeSet` classifies created/updated/deleted from Immer patches in O(patches) (create-then-delete collapses to nothing, delete-then-recreate is `updated`, lists sorted by kind order then ID). Patches too coarse to name a record (whole table or whole state) and `cause: 'replace'` fall back to `diffStates`, which skips unchanged tables and records by identity.
- Contracts: `Clock`, `RandomSource`, `IdFactory` added to `dataset/contracts.ts` (additive types only; `ChangeSet` unchanged, no schema change, no version bump).
- Tests (51): `ids.test.ts` (12), `document.test.ts` (23, both fixtures), `changeset.test.ts` (16).
- Invariants touched: INV-01 (duplicate reporting), INV-03 (ULID IDs, injected RNG/clock), INV-19 / P-02 in memory (both fixtures serialise byte-identically to the committed files, through state, from shuffled keys and reversed insertion order), INV-23 (embedded assets not dropped), INV-10 (unknown keys refused), INV-27 (patch/inverse round trip, undo swaps created/deleted), ADV-020 (ID uniqueness input for regenerated nested IDs).

**Decisions**
- Embedded asset bytes are not canonical state, so `documentToState` returns them next to the state and `stateToDocument` takes them in its options. Callers (io, step 3.x) own them.
- Free-form JSON (`provenance.legacy.unmapped`) keeps its own key order; it is user data and the schema cannot mark it. Round trips stay byte-identical because JSON preserves order.
- `serializeDocument` validates with the full strict schema, so exporting an invalid state is refused with issues rather than written.
- `ChangeSet` `created/updated/deleted` are net effects; intermediate states inside one command are not visible.

**Open items**
- Immer patches require `enablePatches()`. Nothing in `src/core` calls it yet; the store (step 1.9) must, once at module load. `changeset.test.ts` calls it itself.
- `deserializeDocument` is strict and single-shot. The tolerant envelope, quarantine and prototype-key renaming (PERSISTENCE.md §7) belong to the importer (step 3.x).

### Step 1.6 — `core/indexes` part 1: reverse references, folded names, hierarchy ancestry (2026-09-26)

**Delivered** (`src/core/indexes/`)
- `engine.ts`: `coreIndexEngine` (`build`, `apply`) implementing the new `CoreIndexEngine`/`CoreIndexes` contract: `get`, `referrersOf` (record and nested IDs, sorted by owner ID then path, carrying the registry delete policy), `byFoldedName` (per kind, sorted by ID), `ancestorsOf` / `descendantsOf` / `classAncestors`. Every record contributes only from its own content, so `apply` retracts and re-adds just the created/updated/deleted records (O(changed × references)) and equals a rebuild (INV-18).
- `hierarchy.ts`: parent-field table (class, concept, named_reaction `parentIds`; topic `parentId`), cycle-safe iterative breadth-first closure. Descendants come from the reverse-reference index, so no separate children map exists to keep in sync.
- `names.ts`: structural folded-name extraction (`name`, `names.*` except `qualifier`, `aliases`) through `core/text` `fold`.
- `src/test-support/state-fixture.ts`: fixture states and a `commit` helper yielding (state, ChangeSet) as the store will.
- Tests (38): `engine.test.ts` (12: reverse references against an independent oracle over the maximal fixture, ordering, `get`, folded names), `hierarchy.test.ts` (14: table checked against the registry, chain/diamond/cycle/self-parent/dangling, all four hierarchies in the maximal fixture, 5,000-deep chain), `incremental.test.ts` (12: scenarios incl. delete/recreate, undo, replace and revision gap, stale handle, snapshot stability, and four seeded 100-edit random walks compared with a rebuild after every step).
- Invariants touched: INV-11 (nothing stored), INV-18 (apply ≡ build), INV-01 (nested IDs are reverse-index keys), ADV-022 input (duplicate folded names are all returned).

**Decisions**
- In-place update with stale-handle guard: `apply` mutates the shared index data and returns the handle for the new revision; the previous handle then throws on use (a loud programmer error rather than an O(dataset) copy per commit). Arrays already returned stay valid snapshots (buckets are replaced, never mutated). A `replace` ChangeSet or a revision gap rebuilds from `next` instead, leaving the previous handle usable.
- `byFoldedName` covers every name of a record (canonical, display, IUPAC, common, aliases); the caller supplies the folded query. Rules that need canonical-only matching (SPC-001, step 1.8) filter on the record.
- Ancestor walks skip parents that have no record (dangling parents are L2 findings); cycles are walked once and never list the start.
- Hierarchy walks are computed on demand, not cached: work is bounded by the reachable records. Caching is a Phase 2 concern if profiling asks for it.

**Open items**
- Part 2 (step 2.1) adds the remaining `ReadonlyIndexes` methods (participations, productions, by reagent/…, topics of a record, relationships, caveats, anchors, display) and a combined engine; the property test P-07 over random commands is step 1.11.

### Step 1.7 — `core/validation`: L1 wrapper, L2 rules, finding IDs, finding store (2026-09-26)

**Delivered** (`src/core/validation/`, plus `src/core/schema/rich-text.ts`)
- `findings.ts`: `findingId` (`fnd_` + 64 bits from two FNV-1a passes over `ruleId ␀ kind:id ␀ path`; message, data and related never enter the hash), `makeFinding`, `plainText` (control, bidi and separator characters become U+FFFD; messages cut at 500 characters, paths at 200).
- `l1.ts`: `parseRecord(kind, value)` (the `Validator.parseRecord` contract). One `SCHEMA` finding per failing path (several problems at one path are joined), unknown keys named but never their values, at most 50 findings per record plus a summary finding at path `*` with `data.omitted`. Never throws, never modifies input; an unknown kind or a non-object is a finding. The subject ID is the caller's ID sanitised and cut to 80 characters, or empty; it is never invented.
- `runner.ts`: `runRules(rules, record, ctx)` routes by kind, stamps subject/level/severity, sorts by rule then path, and throws if one rule reports one (rule, path) twice (their IDs would collide). `assertUniqueRuleIds`.
- `rules/reference-analysis.ts` + `rules/references.ts`: REF-001…REF-008 as eight `ValidationRule` objects, all views of one registry-driven pass (memoised per index handle and record). Precedence per VALIDATION_RULES.md §3: layer (REF-003) → allowed kind / prefix (REF-002) → existence (REF-001) → self-reference (REF-007); one diagnostic per bad reference. Local references (REF-004) resolve within the record, nested references (REF-005) inside the container record named by the sibling/root field; a missing or wrong-kind container is reported only on the field that names it. REF-006 reports a nested ID defined twice in one record or also by another record, on every defining record. REF-008 is one finding per rich-text field listing all its missing `[[links]]`. Each rule's `kinds` is derived from the registries.
- `rules/registry.ts`: `RULES` (L2 only for now; step 1.8 appends L3), unique-ID check at load.
- `l2.ts`: `duplicateRecordFindings` turns `documentToState`'s duplicate-record report into REF-006 findings (a record ID listed twice cannot exist in `CanonicalState`).
- `store.ts`: `createFindingStore()`, the `FindingStore` contract. Replaces per (subject, level); frozen, deterministically sorted snapshots whose identity changes only when content changes; counts by severity; subscribers are notified once per effective change and all run even if one throws (first error rethrown). A finding of the wrong level or about an unlisted subject is a programmer error (`TypeError`), state unchanged.
- `src/core/schema/rich-text.ts`: `RICH_TEXT_PATHS`, every rich-text field per kind (companion of the reference registry).
- `src/core/indexes`: new `definersOf(nestedId)` (see Amendments).
- Tests (103 new; 558 in total, 24 files): `findings.test.ts` (10), `l1.test.ts` (44: all 28 L1 fixture records, valid records unchanged, ADV-029/034/037 at record level, caps, hostile input), `rules/references.test.ts` (27: every L2 invalid fixture raises exactly its declared rule on its declared record and field and nothing else; both valid fixtures have zero findings; precedence; deleted-record dependents; duplicates; rich text; nested), `runner.test.ts` (5), `store.test.ts` (12), `schema/rich-text.test.ts` (2), `indexes/engine.test.ts` (+3 definers; `incremental.test.ts` compares definers after every step).
- Invariants touched: INV-01 (REF-006), INV-02 (prefix vs kind, REF-002), INV-04, INV-05, INV-07, INV-11 (findings apart from records), INV-24 (parse and rules never modify data), INV-18 (definers apply ≡ build). ADV-029, ADV-034, ADV-037 (non-finite part) at record level.

**Decisions**
- `validateTouched` / `validateAll` (dependents via the reverse index, scope-aware re-runs) are step 1.8, as the plan says; this step delivers the rule runner they will call. Rule `scope` values are set now (`record`, `neighbourhood`, `global`) so 1.8 can skip cheap re-runs for dependents.
- A missing container for a nested reference (a participant `profileId` whose `ref` is not a reagent) is REF-005 here. RXN-011 in step 1.8 describes the same situation; 1.8 must decide whether it stays a separate rule or defers to REF-005 so one problem does not give two errors.
- Findings are level-tagged, and the store replaces one level at a time, so the commit pipeline (1.9) calls `replaceForSubjects` once per level 1–3 with the touched subjects plus dependents.

**Open items**
- Step 1.8 must expose the set of subjects a `validateTouched` call covered (touched plus dependents) so the caller can clear findings of records that are now clean; the store cannot infer them from findings alone.
- `RuleContext.indexes` and the `Validator` methods now take `CoreIndexes` (Amendments); L3 rules that need Phase 2 indexes must derive from record content or wait for step 2.1.

### Step 1.8 — L3 rule catalogue, `validateTouched`, `validateAll` (2026-09-26)

**Delivered** (`src/core/validation/`)
- L3 rules as data-driven `ValidationRule` objects, one file per family under `rules/`: `reactions.ts` (RXN-001…012), `reactions-more.ts` (RXN-013, 016…024), `conditions.ts` (RXN-014, 015 for reaction stages/variants, reagent profiles and caveat triggers), `substances.ts` (SPC-001…007, CLS-001/002, RGT-001/002, HIER-001…003), `mechanisms.ts` (MEC-001…004, CAV-001/002), `relationships.ts` (REL-001…006), `routes.ts` (RTE-001…004), `curriculum.ts` (CUR-001…006, PRS-001…003, AST-001, RVW-001/002). `rules/registry.ts` lists them (`L2_RULES`, `L3_RULES`, `RULES`); `rules/external.ts` names the catalogue IDs that core cannot evaluate. `rules/helpers.ts` and `rules/reaction-utils.ts` hold shared pieces; no rule names a record, ID or specific reaction.
- `validator.ts`: `createValidator(rules)` / `validator` implementing `Validator` (`parseRecord`, `validateTouched`, `validateAll`, `scopeOf`) and `commitFindings(store, scope, findings)`. Per record: L1 (`parseRecord`) → L2 → L3, each level running only if the previous found no ERROR. `scopeOf` = touched records + their referrers + their referents (registry references) + the peers their rules declare, for the new and the previous version of each touched record. `validateAll` is sorted, deterministic and throws an `AbortError` when its signal is aborted (never returns a partial result).
- `src/core/indexes`: `linkersOf(id)` (records whose rich text links to an ID; `indexes/links.ts`).
- `src/test-support/rule-harness.ts`: `edit`, `add`, `drop`, `ruleFindings`, `validateEverything`.
- Tests (114 new; 672 in total, 31 files): one file per rule family (`reactions` 14, `reactions-more` 20, `substances` 20, `structure` 18, `curriculum` 13), `validator.test.ts` (20: catalogue coverage against VALIDATION_RULES.md, level gating, INV-12 on both fixtures, INV-14/ADV-028, scope, abort, store round trip), `incremental.test.ts` (7: 10 seeded walks of about 100 random edits; after every edit the store maintained through `scopeOf` + `validateTouched` equals a fresh `validateAll`), `indexes` (+2 linkers).
- Invariants/ADV touched: INV-04, 05, 06 (RXN-001/002/003 published = ERROR), 07, 08, 09 (HIER-001/003, REL-005, RTE-001), 12, 14 (ADV-028), 15 (REL-001…004), 24; ADV-005, 008, 009, 010, 016, 022, 023, 025, 026, 037 (RXN-014).

**Decisions** (interpretations of ambiguous catalogue rows; also in VALIDATION_RULES.md §5)
- Level gating: an L2 ERROR on a record suppresses its L3 rules (matches the note in the REF-007 fixture); an L1 failure suppresses L2/L3. WARNING-level L2 findings (REF-008) do not.
- RXN-011 owns "participant `profileId` whose ref is not a reagent"; REF-005 reports only unresolved profiles of an existing reagent. Step 1.7's L2 pass is amended accordingly (it reported the former as REF-005): a nested reference whose sibling field names no container of the right kind is left to the L3 rule that describes it. This supersedes the 1.7 decision.
- RXN-008 and RXN-009 compare variant sets only (an empty list means every variant); RXN-010 also requires the same isomer group. RXN-020 does not flag variants when an outcome applies to all variants. RXN-021 treats uses in different stages or variant scopes as different. RXN-016 lists which of the three classifications is missing. RXN-017 compares reactants (role `reactant`) with products by kind and ID.
- Duplicate rules that name a winner (CUR-006, PRS-001, PRS-002) keep the lowest ID clean and flag the others; SPC-001, CLS-002, REL-003 and CUR-004 flag every member. SPC-001/CLS-002 compare canonical names only and are silenced when the folded qualifiers differ.
- SPC-006 (transitive ancestors), CLS-001 (descendants) and RTE-002/004 (exact or by class) use the class hierarchy index. Cycle and chain rules (HIER-001/003, REL-005) and route peers walk at most 10,000 nodes; on overflow they fall back to revalidating every record of the kind or say so in an INFO finding (REL-005), never silently.
- MEC-003 recognises atom maps only in SMILES/CXSMILES (`[…:n]`); a molfile is treated as possibly mapped (only the chem worker reads it; CHEM-008).
- CAV-002 roles: affected_reaction → reaction/named reaction/transformation; substrate/product → species/class; reagent → reagent/class; mechanism → mechanism; context → any.
- AST-001 counts a scheme's `preferAssetId` as use. AST-002 (blob missing) and AST-003 (hash mismatch) need storage and bytes: they belong to the persistence/import layers (phase 3) and use the same `Finding` shape; listed in `rules/external.ts`. CHEM-001…009 are L4 (phase 4).
- SPC-007 (property key `other` without label) was in the catalogue but not in the step summary; implemented (the catalogue-coverage test found it).

**Open items**
- `validateTouched` reruns all rules for every subject in `scopeOf`. For hub records (a class with many descendants, a species used by hundreds of reactions) this can exceed the 16 ms commit budget; Phase 2 benchmarks decide whether to use rule `scope` to skip record-scope rules for dependents.
- RXN-016 is INFO for the maximal fixture's published reactions (no reaction type); the demo dataset (step 1.12) should carry classifications or accept these hints.
- The random-walk equivalence tests take about 20 s of the suite; trim seeds if `npm test` time becomes a problem.
- The commit pipeline (step 1.9) must call `scopeOf` and `validateTouched` with the previous state, then `commitFindings`.

### Step 1.9 — `core/commands`: store, dispatch pipeline, delete impact, undo/redo, replaceState, builders (2026-09-26)

**Delivered** (`src/core/commands/`, `src/test-support/command-kit.ts`)
- `store.ts`: `createCanonicalStore(options)` implementing `CanonicalStore`, returning a `StoreHandle` (adds `findings`, `getIndexes()`, `peekHistory()` for selection hints; the contract file is unchanged). One commit step shared by dispatch, undo, redo: ChangeSet → `indexes.apply` → `scopeOf` + `validateTouched` → any ERROR rejects with state, indexes and findings unchanged (INV-12) → commit (revision + 1, `commitFindings`, history, subscribers). Read-only is an injected predicate. Subscribers are notified in commit order even under re-entrant dispatch; a throwing subscriber does not stop the others (`onListenerError`, default: rethrow from a microtask).
- `plan.ts` / `batch.ts`: each command is planned against the state left by the previous ones (so re-point-then-delete works), then produced with Immer; patches concatenated, inverse patches reversed, so a batch is one exact history entry. Errors are values; a command that changes nothing is `EMPTY_COMMAND`, never a silent no-op.
- `impact.ts`: generic delete-impact closure over the reverse index (nested IDs of deleted records count; cascade is transitive and bounded by record count).
- `history.ts`: linear history, 500 entries / 50 MB (JSON length estimate), newest entry always kept.
- `replace.ts` + `replaceState`: shape check, `validateAll`, index rebuild, findings replaced for old and new subjects, history cleared; the ChangeSet carries table-replace patches (and inverse) so patch followers can reproduce it.
- `builders/`: `duplicateRecord` (all record and nested IDs regenerated via the injected context; provenance restarts; verified review not carried over), `mergeRecords` (optional caller-merged target, `replaceReferences` + restrict delete), `reorderItems` (permutation of IDs, optionally `within` an item).
- Tests (52 new; 724 in 36 files): impact (10), store (17), undo/history (10), builders (12), ADV-044 random walk ×3 seeds (200 commands, undo all / redo all exact at each step, INV-18 and findings ≡ `validateAll` checked along the way).
- Invariants/ADV touched: INV-01, 03, 11, 12, 18, 24, 27; ADV-017, 018, 019, 020, 021, 044, 045 (replaceState part; snapshot restore is phase 3).

**Decisions**
- `record.delete` mode `restrict` refuses whenever the impact is not empty (blockers, cascades or detaches), as `DELETE_RESTRICTED` with the impact; `apply_cascade` refuses only when blocked. Without this a restrict delete with detach-only referrers would fail later with a less clear REF-001.
- The store does not stamp `provenance.updatedAt` or `meta.updatedAt`: records are stored exactly as commanded (INV-24 spirit). Editor-session commit (6.1) sets timestamps via `CommandContext.now()`.
- `IMMUTABLE_FIELD` is returned when an update's `kind` disagrees with its ID prefix (INV-02/03).
- `historyLabels()` lists the next action first.
- Undo/redo are validated like commits (ARCHITECTURE §23); a failure would be `VALIDATION_FAILED`/`INTERNAL` with the store unchanged.

**Open items**
- A rejected commit rebuilds the indexes (O(records)) because `apply` consumes the old handle. Phase 2/9 may add a cheap revert; measure in step 2.7.
- `scopeOf` is computed twice per commit (once directly, once inside `validateTouched`); fold together if the 16 ms budget is at risk.
- Records handed to commands are frozen by Immer.
- Editor sessions (`EditorSession`, `DraftCommitOutcome`) are step 6.1; domain builders such as AttachOutcome are phase 6.

### Step 1.10 — `core/migrations` runner, no steps yet (2026-09-26)

**Delivered** (`src/core/migrations/`)
- `runner.ts`: `createMigrationRunner(migrations, target)` and the app runner `migrationRunner` (target `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION`). Steps are indexed by `from`; construction throws (programmer errors) on a step that is not `from → from + 1`, a duplicate `from`, or a step beyond the target. `run` applies steps in order on the tolerant envelope, never mutates its input, returns the same object and no reports when the document is already current, and returns error values for `INVALID_VERSION`, `NEWER_VERSION`, `MISSING_STEP`, `STEP_FAILED` (a throwing step) and `STEP_CONTRACT` (a step whose output `schemaVersion` or report versions disagree with its declaration). No partial result is ever returned. The loop is bounded by the target version.
- `steps/index.ts`: `MIGRATIONS = []` (schema version 1 has no predecessors). `index.ts` re-exports the public surface.
- Tests (23 new; 747 in 37 files): both v1 fixtures pass through unchanged, newer/invalid versions, ordered chain and mid-chain start, gaps, throwing and contract-violating steps, determinism on a frozen input, construction errors.
- Invariants touched: INV-23 (no silent loss: a failing chain returns no document), MIGRATION.md §1 (newer versions refused, never guessed). Undo history clearing after migration belongs to `replaceState` (step 1.9) and the caller in phase 3.

**Decisions**
- The runner works on the tolerant envelope only; strict parsing, L1–L3 and `replaceAll` after migration are the caller's job (phase 3), as MIGRATION.md §2 describes.
- The report `from/to` and the output `schemaVersion` of each step are verified by the runner, so a buggy step cannot silently skip or repeat a version.

**Open items**
- Per-step tests (frozen `v<N>` fixtures → committed `v<N+1>` output, P-03 chain test) start with the first schema bump; the runner's chain test covers the mechanism until then.

### Step 1.11 — `test-support` generator, fast-check arbitraries, property tests P-02, P-04…P-08 (2026-09-26)

**Delivered** (`src/test-support/`, `tests/property/`)
- `generator.ts` + `generator-shapes.ts`: seeded (mulberry32) `generateState` / `generateDataset` / `generateTier` for `{ species, classes, reagents, reactions, relationshipsPerReaction, topics, shape, seed }`; six shapes (`chain`, `wide`, `dense`, `cyclic`, `duplicate_endpoint`, `hub_explosion`) as pure reaction-index → endpoint functions; `tierParams(tier, shape, seed)` for tiers `tiny` (tests), `initial`, `design`, `stress` (PERFORMANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md §1: entities split 70/10/20 species/classes/reagents; each reaction gets one topic mapping; relationships fill the rest of the link budget). Records are minimal but complete (species, classes with parents, reagents, reactions with participants and one exclusive outcome, topics, mappings, `alternative_to`/`competing_with` relationships between distinct reactions, no duplicates). Output is a document sorted by ID, deterministic per seed.
- `arbitraries.ts`: model-based command generation. A sequence is abstract `Intent`s (op + three integers, 10 ops: rename, edit note, create species, create reaction, duplicate, merge species, reorder participants, cascade delete, restrict delete, retitle); `resolveIntent(state, intent, ctx)` picks concrete targets from the current state by index modulo, so commands are schema-valid by construction and shrink to short sequences of small numbers. `smallParamsArb`, `frozenStateOf`, `pickRecord`, `renamed` helpers.
- `tests/property/`: `harness.ts` (run count `ORN_PROPERTY_RUNS`, default 25; per-test timeout scaled with it; `runIntents`; independent dangling-reference oracle), `export-roundtrip.test.ts` (P-02: both fixtures, generated datasets of every shape, and states after random edits; also state and document equality), `commands.test.ts` (P-04 cascade deletes leave no dangling reference, checked with an oracle independent of the validator; P-05 rename changes no ID or reference and reverse references stay identical; P-06 duplicates give globally unique record and nested IDs; P-07 live indexes equal a rebuild after random sequences; P-08 undo^n/redo^n restore exact states and bytes), `generator-tiers.test.ts` (Gate A: zero L1–L3 ERRORs at every tier: initial for all six shapes, design for dense and hub, stress for chain and duplicate-endpoint).
- Tests (47 new; 794 in 41 files): generator 28, property 9, tier validity 10. Non-vacuity guards assert that P-04/P-07/P-08 actually commit and delete. All also pass at `ORN_PROPERTY_RUNS=200`.
- Invariants/ADV touched: INV-04, INV-12, INV-18, INV-19, INV-27; ADV-019, ADV-020, ADV-044 (property form).

**Decisions**
- Command intents are data, not closures over state, so shrinking works and a failing sequence can be replayed from the printed seed and intents.
- The generator does not build every record kind (no mechanisms, routes, exams, assets, presentation): it targets graph and index scale (PERFORMANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md §10). The maximal fixture covers kind breadth; the demo dataset (1.12) covers content.
- Tier validity at design and stress is tested for two shapes each to keep `npm test` near 25 s; all shapes are covered at initial and tiny. Full tier × shape sweeps belong to the benchmarks (step 2.7).
- Random walks contain rejected commands (a cascade that would create an L3 ERROR is refused, INV-12); the properties assert that a rejection changes nothing.

**Open items**
- P-01 (fake-indexeddb) is phase 3; P-03 needs the first migration step; P-09/P-10 are phase 2.
- `ORN_PROPERTY_RUNS` should be raised in the gate reviews (Gate A: 200).

### Vitest 4.1.11 upgrade and coverage re-baseline (2026-09-27)

- `vitest` and `@vitest/coverage-v8` `^3.2.x` → `^4.1.11` (locked 4.1.11), closing GHSA-82fw-gwwq-j7x9 (fixed in 4.1.11). Own commit at the Phase 1 boundary (ADR-020). `engines.node` unchanged (`>=22`; 4.1.11 supports `^20 || ^22 || >=24`). `npm audit`: 0 vulnerabilities.
- `npm run check` green (47 files, 876 tests). Under 4.x parallel load the 5 s default timeout was exceeded by `walk.test.ts` (ADV-044 random walk) and `indexes/incremental.test.ts` (apply ≡ build walk); both now have an explicit 30 000 ms per-test timeout with a comment. Global default and all assertions unchanged.
- Coverage (`npm run test:coverage`, `src/core`, 61 files), old (3.2.7) → new (4.1.11): lines 99.46 % (5211/5239) → 99.21 % (1896/1911); branches 95.07 % (1816/1910) → 92.68 % (1356/1463); functions 99.15 % (351/354) → 98.90 % (541/547); statements not previously recorded → 98.02 % (2334/2381).
- Cause: Vitest 4's `@vitest/coverage-v8` maps V8 coverage through `ast-v8-to-istanbul` (imported by `dist/provider.js`; dependency `^1.0.0`, installed 1.0.7), an "AST-aware" conversion that builds statements, branches and functions from the parsed source instead of the raw V8 ranges that 3.x remapped. Denominators shrink (fewer synthetic lines and branches, e.g. in the large declarative schema files: 1634 → 225 lines) while the numerators of real code stay. No changelog or migration note ships in `node_modules`, so this rests on the provider source and that package's README; no option restoring the old counting was used. Baselines before and after are not comparable.
- Enforcement: `vitest.config.ts` now sets `coverage.thresholds['src/core/**'] = { lines: 90, branches: 85 }`, so `npm run test:coverage` fails below the targets (probed: raising branches to 99 exits 1). Coverage stays out of `npm run check`.
- Files below 90 % lines or 85 % branches: `commands/history.ts` (100 % / 75.00 %), `commands/impact.ts` (100 / 83.33), `commands/plan.ts` (96.00 / 81.16), `commands/replace.ts` (89.47 / 63.16), `dataset/serialize.ts` (100 / 83.33), `validation/runner.ts` (100 / 83.33), `validation/rules/helpers.ts` (88.89 / 83.33), `validation/rules/routes.ts` (100 / 83.33). The folder aggregates all clear the targets; the thresholds are on the `src/core` aggregate, not per file.
- Per folder (lines / branches / functions): commands (11) 97.28 % / 87.11 % / 97.96 %; dataset (4) 100 % / 95.18 % / 100 %; indexes (4) 100 % / 98.88 % / 100 %; migrations (1) 100 % / 100 % / 100 %; references (2) 100 % / 96.75 % / 100 %; schema (17) 100 % / 100 % / 100 %; text (2) 100 % / 96.26 % / 100 %; validation (20) 99.36 % / 92.29 % / 98.63 %.

### Step 2.1 — Remaining indexes (2026-09-27)

**Delivered**

Exported from `src/core/indexes`. No contract, schema or fixture change (the `ReadonlyIndexes` interface was already declared); no amendment.
- Tests: `src/core/indexes/full.test.ts` (17): every answer for every ID vs a scan oracle (`src/test-support/index-snapshot.ts`) on the demo and the maximal fixture, non-vacuity probe, descendants on request, ADV-051 participations part, ADV-052 productions part (25 reactions, one product), ADV-057 topics part, apply ≡ build scenarios and 4 seeded random walks (36 edits each, rebuild compared after every step, oracle every third), stale / replace / gap / foreign-handle behaviour. **P-07 extended** in `tests/property/commands.test.ts`: the full indexes follow the store through ChangeSets (commands and undo) and equal a rebuild (INV-18).

**Decisions**
- Cross-record answers are joined at query time (family → reagents → reactions; descendants), so each record's contribution depends on that record only and retraction stays exact.
