### Step 2.2 — Stable hashing; bounded traversal (2026-09-27)

**Delivered**
- `src/core/hash.ts` (`fnv1a`, `hash64`, `hashParts`): the finding-ID hash moved here unchanged (finding IDs identical; existing tests pass). `hashParts` separates parts with U+0000 and tells `undefined` from `''`.
- `src/core/graph/ids.ts`: `graphEdgeId` = `edg_` + hash(view, from, to, semantic, reactionId, outcomeId, productId, participantId, relationshipId) (GRAPH_SEMANTICS §10).
- `src/core/graph/traversal.ts`: `traverse()`, an iterative BFS bounded by `TraversalLimits` (depth, nodes, edges, time via an injected `now`, `AbortSignal`). Neighbours are sorted by (target, edge ID); a node is expanded once per direction; parallel edges and self-loops are kept (INV-26); an edge closing a cycle onto a node already in the result needs only edge budget. Returns nodes with per-direction `hidden` counts, edges, and a `Truncation` (first limit hit; `hiddenNodeEstimate` is a lower bound: distinct neighbours seen but not admitted). Exported from `src/core/graph/index.ts`.
- Tests (`traversal.test.ts`, `hash.test.ts`): ADV-011 (chain 60 → depth 12, notice), ADV-050 traversal part (A→B→C→A; cycle at the depth limit), INV-22 (50,000-deep chain without stack growth; seeded random cyclic graphs stay within all limits, untruncated results are closed), limits per kind, abort, injected clock, determinism under edge order, INV-26 edge IDs. `npm run check`: 50 files, 911 tests, green.

**Decisions**
- No contract or schema change, so no amendment. `TraversalLimits` is used as declared. `edg` is a local prefix (not in `OPERATIONAL_ID_PREFIX`; edge IDs are derived, never stored).
- Without an injected `now`, `timeBudgetMs` is not enforced (core has no clock); callers on the main thread pass `performance.now`.

**Open items**
- P-09 (route search half) stays with step 2.6; ADV-050 graph/route halves with 2.5/2.6. The time-budget check runs once per expanded node, so one node with a huge neighbour list is not interrupted (bounded by the edge cap in practice; measure in 2.8).

### Step 2.3 — Projection registry; P-10 harness; navigator projection (2026-09-27)

**Delivered**
- `src/core/graph/registry.ts`: `createProjectionRegistry` (`register` throws on a duplicate view — programmer error; `get`/`has`/`views`/`definitions` in `VIEW_KEYS` order; `build` returns `{ ok: false, reason: 'unregistered_view' }` as a value). `defaults.ts` lists the shipped definitions (one line per future view). `kit.ts`: `sortNodes`/`sortEdges`/`sortedUnique` and `projectionKey`.
- `src/core/graph/navigator/` (`hubs.ts`, `select.ts`, `build.ts`): hub resolution (anchored record itself = stated; else nearest anchored ancestor class by shortest path through `classIds` then `parentIds`, ties to the lowest ID = inferred; else virtual Unclassified); carried substrate and main products; scope/topic/reagent-family/text filters; one `transforms_to` arrow per (reaction, outcome, main product) with the outcome's selectivity and condition variants; Unanchored list; maxNodes/maxEdges/abort truncation with `hidden` counts on the hubs.
- `src/test-support/projection-harness.ts` (+ `graph-state.ts`): `checkProjection` = structure (sorted unique IDs, endpoints exist, bundles real, limits INV-22, unanchored real and sorted) + INV-26 (edge ID = hash of identity; every edge that names a reaction stands for exactly one real participation/production, and no two edges claim the same one whatever their endpoints) + P-10 (byte-identical output on a repeat build and on reordered records with fresh indexes). `checkRegistry` runs all of it over every registered projection. `projection-harness.test.ts` breaks a projection on purpose for each rule to prove the harness catches it.
- Tests: `navigator.test.ts` (35: ADV-001, 014, 015, 046, 047, 048, 053, ADV-008 graph part for the navigator, INV-14, INV-22), `registry.test.ts` (5), `projection-harness.test.ts` (6), `tests/property/projections.test.ts` (11: P-10 and INV-26 over the demo, the maximal fixture, every generator shape and random shapes/seeds/limits, with anchors and never-a-node displays added). `npm run check`: 54 files, 968 tests, green.

**Decisions** (no contract or schema change, so no amendment)
- Arrows are drawn for MAIN products only (GRAPH_SEMANTICS §3 "each main product"); coproducts and byproducts get none. A reaction with no drawable substrate or no main product is listed as Unanchored.
- "Unanchored" = the reaction has no arrow with a real (anchored) hub at either end. Such reactions are still drawn on the Unclassified hub, so they appear twice (arrow and list), never zero times. Reactions removed by a user filter are in neither.
- The carried substrate is the participant flagged `isPrimarySubstrate`, else the first `reactant`; spectators and `showAsNode = never` participants never qualify. A `never` record is not a hub even when anchored (§3: never a node). An anchor whose target record is missing is ignored (validation reports it).
- Edge `inferred` = either end attached through class membership. Unclassified is not an inference.
- `Projection.key` = hash(request, index revision): conservative (any commit changes it). Per-dependency keys are an optimisation for the cache layer.
- Bundles stay empty in the navigator until step 2.5 (bundling).

**Open items**
- Navigator focus, hub expansion (`ExpansionState`) and degree caps/overflow are not built; step 2.5 owns caps and overflow, and expansion needs an owner before Phase 7 (proposal: fold it into 2.5).
- `ProjectionContext` has no clock, so `timeBudgetMs` is not enforced in projections; the truncation reason `time_budget` is unused until a clock is injected (decide in 2.8 or Phase 4).
- `filters.examId`/`examVersionId` are not read by the navigator: the exam version is the context's `scopeOf` (step 2.6).
- Full `has_error`/`has_warning` node flags need findings in the context; not built.

### Step 2.4 — Reaction explorer projection (2026-09-27)

**Delivered**
- `src/core/graph/reaction-explorer/` (`select.ts`, `neighbours.ts`, `build.ts`, `index.ts`): the canonical bipartite graph itself (no hub roll-up) around a focus substance or reaction. `select.ts` has the reusable rules: which participants/products qualify (not a spectator, not `showAsNode: never`, holds under the selected condition variant) and the `consumesEdge`/`producesEdge` builders (GRAPH_SEMANTICS §1: one `consumes` edge per participant, one `produces` edge per outcome product of any role — main, coproduct, byproduct, unlike the navigator's main-only arrows). `neighbours.ts` adapts that to `traverse()` (`direction: 'both'`): a reaction's neighbours are its qualifying participants/products; a substance's are the reactions it participates in / is produced by, read via `participationsOf`/`productionsOf` then re-verified against the qualifying set (single source of truth, no drift between the two discovery directions). Unknown or non-substance/reaction seeds are dropped, not phantom nodes.
- `src/core/graph/kit.ts`: `drawnAsNode` and `flagsOf` moved here from the navigator (now shared with the reaction explorer, byte-identical behaviour); `projectionKey` hashes the new `conditionVariantId` request field.
- **Contract amendment (ADR-027, no schema change)**: `ProjectionRequest.conditionVariantId?: ConditionVariantId` (`src/core/graph/contracts.ts`) — narrows one focused reaction's participants and outcomes to those holding under a chosen variant. Not on `ViewFilters` (persisted, cross-reaction); every other projection ignores it. Docs: GRAPH_SEMANTICS.md §7, ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md ADR-027.
- `src/test-support/demo/reaction-kit.ts`: `RxSpec.intermediates` (test-only; the demo builder emitted `intermediates: []` unconditionally before this step, so no demo reaction changes).
- Registered in `src/core/graph/defaults.ts`; automatically covered by `tests/property/projections.test.ts` (P-10/INV-26 over the demo, maximal fixture, every generator shape and random seeds/limits — all with empty focus, so this is a structural smoke check; focused traversal is covered by `reaction-explorer.test.ts`).
- Tests: `reaction-explorer.test.ts` (18): bipartite basics + harness, ADV-051 graph part ((a) reactant+product self-loop shape, (b) twice as reactant — two participations, two edges, one node), ADV-024 graph part (one reagent, two reactions via aq./alc. phase, different outcomes), ADV-027/053 graph part (water and a spectator: no node, no edge, still indexed both directions), ADV-008 (condition-variant filter: common vs variant-scoped participant, variant-scoped outcome, cache-key difference, harness under every selection), ADV-054 (an intermediate is on the record, never a node/edge), stages (a two-stage Grignard-shaped reaction — every stage's participants still get consumes edges; stages do not change the bipartite shape), INV-22 (a 5-reaction chain truncates at `maxDepth: 2`, reported, never silent).
- `npm run check`: 55 files, 986 tests, green (two unrelated pre-existing tests — `schema-mutations.test.ts`, `generator-tiers.test.ts` — hit the default 5000ms timeout once under full-suite parallel load and passed cleanly standalone and on a clean re-run of the whole suite; not touched by this step).

**Decisions**
- Spectators and `showAsNode: never` participants/products are excluded from nodes and edges here exactly as in the navigator (not just "hidden" by presentation): DOMAIN_MODEL §6.2 gives spectators default scheme position "hidden" and `contributesAtoms: never`, so treating them as full bipartite participants would misrepresent the chemistry. The full reaction record (read via `ctx.indexes.get`) still carries them for a future arrow-text/caption UI (Phase 5+) — nothing is lost, matching ADV-027/053's "still indexed".
- Intermediates are never read by this projection at all (not filtered out — simply not iterated), so ADV-054 is trivially exact: they cannot leak into nodes, edges, or the product count.
- Stages are not graph structure: every participant of every stage still gets a `consumes` edge; grouping by stage is a presentation concern for a future UI to read directly off `reaction.stages`, never chemistry logic recomputed in the graph layer or the UI (CLAUDE.md §1.4).
- A participant/outcome not under the selected condition variant is filtered the same way `filters` work elsewhere (§2: "Filters are applied at projection time; they never mutate data") — nothing is deleted or reordered, only omitted from this build.
- `qualifyingParticipants`/`qualifyingProducts` are recomputed per participation/production when discovering neighbours from the substance side (O(reaction's own participant/product count), not the dataset) rather than cached per reaction; revisit if step 2.8 benchmarks show it matters at the design tier.

**Open items**
- ~~Bundling and degree caps/overflow are step 2.5~~ Bundling done (step 2.5, all three bipartite/hub views); degree cap/overflow done for `precursor_explorer` only — see step 2.5 open items for the navigator.
- No "arrow text" field exists yet for spectators/`showAsNode: never` participants; Phase 5+ reads `reaction.participants`/`products` directly for captions (layer separation: the UI renders already-structured data, it does not decide chemistry visibility rules itself — those live in `select.ts`).
- `has_error`/`has_warning` node flags still need validation findings wired into `ProjectionContext` (same open item as step 2.3).

### Step 2.5 — Precursor projection, bundling, degree cap/overflow, ⇌ rule (2026-09-27)

**Delivered**
- `src/core/graph/precursor/` (`neighbours.ts`, `degree-cap.ts`, `reverse-of.ts`, `build.ts`, `index.ts`): "what makes this" — bounded backward BFS from a target substance or reaction, reusing `reaction_explorer`'s edge builders (`fromReaction`/`fromSubstance`, now exported and view-parameterised — see Amendments) rather than duplicating chemistry-adjacent selection logic. A substance node never looks forward (what consumes it), only backward for producers; a reaction node reached from either side reveals its full consumes *and* produces edges (ADV-003 no transitive reduction, ADV-006, ADV-048 — ordinary context, then searched backward in its own turn like any substance, bounded by the same `TraversalLimits` as everywhere else). Every edge's `layoutDirection` is forced to `'reversed'` after building; the chemical `from`/`to` never flips.
- `degree-cap.ts`: a substance with more than `DEFAULT_DEGREE_CAP` (12, new in `contracts.ts`) qualifying producers keeps the best-ranked ones (exam scope, then selectivity, then name, then edge ID — deterministic) and collapses the rest into one virtual overflow node (`role: 'overflow'`, `EdgeSemantic: 'overflow'`, ADR-028) whose `hidden.incoming` carries the collapsed count (ADV-012 projection part, ADV-052). The overflow edge's ID is `graphEdgeId({view, from: overflowId, to: nodeId, semantic: 'overflow'})` — no `reactionId`, so it needs no exception in the INV-26 harness check (it already skips edges with no `reactionId`) and still satisfies the generic "ID is the hash of its identity" check.
- `reverse-of.ts`: the ⇌ badge rule (reviewer decision, no schema change) — tags the two produces edges of a `reverse_of` pair of reactions whose endpoints mirror (R1's main product is R2's carried substrate and vice versa, reusing the navigator's `primarySubstrate`/`mainProducts`) with the existing, previously-unused `relationshipId`/`relationshipType` fields on `GraphEdge`. `reversibility.kind: reversible_under_other_conditions` alone never triggers it (ADV-056); only a curated, mirrored `reverse_of` relationship does (ADV-004).
- `kit.ts`: `bundleEdges` (groups parallel edges by `(from, to)` into one `EdgeBundle` per pair with 2+ edges, deterministic bundle ID via `graphEdgeId`-style hashing, sorted); wired into `navigator`, `reaction_explorer` and `precursor_explorer` (closing the "`bundles: []`" open items from steps 2.3–2.4).
- **Contract amendments (no schema change)**: (1, ADR-027 widened) `ProjectionRequest.conditionVariantId`'s doc comment now covers both bipartite views, not `reaction_explorer` only. (2, ADR-028) `EdgeSemantic` gains `'overflow'`; `DEFAULT_DEGREE_CAP = 12` added next to `DEFAULT_TRAVERSAL_LIMITS`. (3) `consumesEdge`/`producesEdge`/`fromReaction`/`fromSubstance` (reaction-explorer internals, not public contracts) gained a required `view: ViewKey` parameter — a real bug fix, not just a refactor: reusing them unchanged for `precursor_explorer` produced edge IDs hashed with `view: 'reaction_explorer'` baked in, which is wrong (GRAPH_SEMANTICS §10 makes `view` part of the identity) and was caught by the projection harness itself (`checkInv26`'s "not the hash of its identity") the first time the new tests ran.
- Registered in `defaults.ts`; automatically covered by `tests/property/projections.test.ts`.
- Tests: `precursor.test.ts` (14): backward basics + harness, ADV-006/ADV-048 (full consumes+produces on a reached reaction), ADV-003 (no transitive reduction — both routes to a shared ancestor kept), ADV-002 + ADV-007 widened (one reaction, two outcomes, same target: 2 produces edges, distinct selectivity, one bundle), ADV-004 (mirrored reverse_of tagged; unrelated two-reaction pair not tagged; non-mirroring reverse_of not tagged), ADV-056 (conditional reversibility alone: no tag), ADV-052 (25 producers: cap to 12 + one overflow node counting 13, index still has all 25), ADV-012 projection part (1,000 producers: still only ~26 nodes total), a ranking test (scope beats name order). `kit.test.ts` (5): `bundleEdges` singleton/pair/direction/determinism/multiple-groups. Plus one additive test in `navigator.test.ts` (ADV-002: the existing "two reactions, same hubs" scenario now bundles) and one additive assertion in `reaction-explorer.test.ts`'s ADV-051(b) test (now also bundles).
- `npm run check`: 57 files, 1006 tests, green (no flakiness this run).

**Decisions**
- `precursor_explorer` is bipartite (reaction nodes visible, `consumes`/`produces` edges), not a hub-collapsed reversed navigator: GRAPH_SEMANTICS §7's registry row says "backwards over produces/consumes" (the bipartite edge kinds) and ADV-006 for this step literally expects "2 consumes + 3 produces edges", which only a bipartite view can produce. This reading is recorded here because the alternative (substance-to-substance arrows like the navigator) was genuinely plausible from §5's prose alone.
- A reached reaction always reveals its full consumes/produces edges (not just the arm that led to it) so ADV-006/048's "context" requirement holds regardless of which product was focused; the resulting extra substances are then themselves backward-searched (every substance always tries its producers), which is the correct "show the whole precursor tree, not one linear chain" behaviour and stays bounded by the ordinary `TraversalLimits` backstop.
- Degree cap applies only to a substance's producer count (the one count that can reach into the thousands); a single reaction's own participant/product count is already schema-bounded (`LIMITS`) and is never capped.
- Ranking for the cap (scope, then selectivity, then name, then edge ID) is a projection-internal policy, not a schema or contract concept; SCOPE_RANK/SELECTIVITY_RANK orders are documented in `degree-cap.ts` and open to revision without an amendment (no persisted meaning depends on them).
- The ⇌ tagging pass runs once, after traversal, over the final edge list (not during neighbour discovery) so it only ever considers reactions actually admitted into this projection, and both members of a mirrored pair get tagged symmetrically by the same single pass (each reaction's own iteration tags its own produces edge to the other's substrate).

**Open items**
- The navigator's own hub arrows are not degree-capped yet (GRAPH_SEMANTICS §11 "hub explosion" also names bundling — done — and caps — not done, for the navigator specifically); revisit alongside 2.6/2.8 or when navigator focus/expansion gets an owner.
- `reaction_explorer` does not tag ⇌ pairs (only `precursor_explorer` does, per this step's ADV-004 scope); revisit if a future step needs it there too.
- Precursor's own `qualifyingParticipants`/`qualifyingProducts` re-derivation cost (same note as step 2.4) is unmeasured; step 2.8 benchmarks the design tier.
- P-09 (route search) and the remaining ADV-050 route part stay with step 2.7, per the plan.

### Step 2.6 — Reagent/named-reaction/mechanism/syllabus projections, derived exam scope, registry completeness (2026-09-27)

**Delivered**
- `src/core/graph/scope.ts` (+ `scope.test.ts`, 19 tests): `createScopeOf(indexes, examId, examVersionId): ScopeOf` — the pure, index-backed reference implementation of `ProjectionContext.scopeOf` (docs/JEE_ONTOLOGY.md §4): explicit `exam_relevance` override (version-specific beats version-agnostic, CUR-004 precedence official > prerequisite > enrichment > outside) → mapped topic coverage (+ ancestors, enrichment only when every mapping is enrichment-role) → one-hop concept prerequisite (`prerequisiteConceptIds` or a `prerequisite_of` relationship, only when the *direct* scope of the dependent is official — never chains) → `unmapped`. No full-table scan anywhere: `topic_mapping.target` and `exam_relevance.target` are registered references, so "what points at this record" is `referrersOf(ref.id)` filtered by path — factored into `kit.ts`'s `referencing` (an array-spec path like `prerequisiteConceptIds[]` matches by prefix, since `Referrer.path` carries the concrete index, e.g. `prerequisiteConceptIds[0]`, not the registry's `[]`).
- `src/core/graph/reagent-explorer/`: one hop from a focus reagent via its own `participationsOf` (`uses_reagent` edges — already scaffolded in `EdgeSemantic`, unused until now), spectators excluded, standard filters (scope/topic/family/text) applied via the navigator's `reactionFilter`. Reactions are a dead end.
- `src/core/graph/named-reactions/`: the family DAG (`parentIds`, child → parent, `membership`) walked in both directions (climbing to a parent *does* re-expand its other children here — a family view means seeing the whole family) plus reactions that name it (`namedReactionIds`, also `membership`). Reactions are a dead end.
- `src/core/graph/mechanisms/`: one hop from a focus mechanism; `uses_mechanism` edges (ADR-029, new) for reaction-level (`mechanismIds`) and per-outcome (`outcome.mechanism`) links — ADV-055's two-variant reaction gets two distinct outcome-level edges, each resolvable to its own variant via `outcomeId`. A mechanism's variants/steps stay nested data, never nodes.
- `src/core/graph/syllabus/`: the topic tree (ancestors + descendants of the focused topic(s), or every root topic with none — computed once via `ancestorsOf`/`descendantsOf('topic', …)`, *not* a bidirectional walk) plus records mapped directly to each topic (`recordsInTopic(topicId, { transitive: false })`) — ADV-057. Bounded like `navigator` (sorted candidate prefix, not `traverse()`, since the tree comes from two index calls per topic rather than a neighbour function); a small BFS over the admitted edges (undirected) gives real `depth` values.
- **Bug the projection harness caught, fixed same session (Amendments)**: the first `syllabus` run over the demo's one cross-chapter reaction (diazonium → phenol, mapped to both `diazonium-chemistry` and `phenols`) failed INV-26 — two `membership` edges for that reaction, one per topic, collided because both set `reactionId` and INV-26 is deliberately endpoint-independent for edges that carry it. Fix: `membership` edges from a reaction, and reaction-level (no `outcomeId`) `uses_mechanism` edges, never set `reactionId` (ADR-029) — the reaction stays recoverable from `edge.from`. Applied to `named_reactions` and `mechanisms` too (a reaction can equally name two families or two mechanisms at reaction level).
- All four registered in `defaults.ts`; `registry.test.ts` gained a completeness test (every `ViewKey` except `search` is registered — the step's other explicit deliverable). `tests/property/projections.test.ts` now sweeps all seven views with no changes needed there (view-agnostic by design).
- Tests: `reagent-explorer.test.ts` (8), `named-reactions.test.ts` (9, incl. a same-reaction-two-families INV-26 check), `mechanisms.test.ts` (6, ADV-055 + "demo mechanisms shown as review-required" against the real demo dataset — its aldol reaction already has a reaction-level *and* two outcome-level mechanism links, exercising all three edge shapes at once), `syllabus.test.ts` (12, ADV-057 against the real demo dataset plus a same-reaction-two-topics INV-26 check), `registry.test.ts` (+1 completeness test), `scope.test.ts` (19). `src/test-support/curriculum-kit.ts` (new, test-only): minimal topic/exam-version/topic-mapping/exam-relevance/relationship builders, not tied to the demo's fixed topic list.
- `npm run check`: 62 files, 1060 tests, green.

**Decisions**
- `reagent_explorer`/`named_reactions`/`mechanisms` are one-hop views (reactions are dead ends) matching their GRAPH_SEMANTICS.md §7 registry-table rows literally ("reagent → participations → reactions", etc.); only `syllabus` needed the ancestor/descendant closure treatment, because only syllabus has an ADV row (057) demanding independent per-focus scoping. `named_reactions`' full bidirectional DAG walk is a deliberate, different choice from syllabus's closure (documented in GRAPH_SEMANTICS.md): a family tree is meant to show the whole family from any entry point; a syllabus query is meant to resolve *this topic*, not the whole curriculum.
- Classification edges (topic/family/reaction-level-mechanism membership) never carry `reactionId`, only participation/production edges do (consumes/produces/transforms_to/uses_reagent, and outcome-level uses_mechanism, where `outcomeId` already guarantees uniqueness) — recorded as ADR-029 because it is a real, if narrow, correctness rule for anyone adding a new reaction-originating edge kind later.
- Wiring `ctx.scopeOf` to "which exam version is active" (`ViewFilters.examId`/`examVersionId`) is left to a later phase's composition root; this step delivers `createScopeOf` as a pure, directly-testable function and proves it flows correctly through `flagsOf` via one end-to-end `syllabus` test against the demo's real override (the Beckmann reaction, explicit `exam_relevance` → enrichment, checked as `outside_scope` under JEE Main where it is excluded).
- Full official/prerequisite/enrichment/outside scope is not yet a distinct node flag (only the existing `outside_scope` boolean is reused); no ADV row for this step requires more, so this stays an open item rather than a new `NodeFlag` amendment.

**Open items**
- No node flag distinguishes official/prerequisite/enrichment from "just not outside"; extend `NodeFlag` when a view needs to show the difference (a schema-free contract change, same pattern as `'overflow'`).
- `reagent_explorer`'s "grouped by profile and family" (GRAPH_SEMANTICS.md §7 table) is not modelled as graph structure — a participant's `profileId` and the reagent's own `profiles` are already fully readable off the records for a future UI to group by by itself, same reasoning as reaction_explorer's stage grouping (step 2.4).
- `syllabus`'s candidate set (ancestors ∪ descendants ∪ mapped records) is capped by `maxNodes`/`maxEdges` as a flat sorted prefix, not ranked by anything curriculum-specific (e.g. topic order); revisit if a real syllabus subtree ever approaches the cap.
- `createScopeOf` is not wired into any composition root yet (Phase 5); every other view's tests still pass a stub `ScopeOf` unless they specifically need real scope resolution.

### Step 2.7 — Route search (2026-09-27)

**Delivered**
- `src/core/graph/route/` (docs/GRAPH_SEMANTICS.md §9): `searchRoutes(ctx, RouteQuery, { now? })` — the compute-side implementation of the `RouteQuery → RouteSearchResult` contract already declared in step 0's `graph/contracts.ts` (no schema/contract change needed; `workers/rpc/contracts.ts`'s `findRoutes` will call this in Phase 4).
  - `moves.ts` — `backwardMovesInto(ctx, target, excludeReactionIds)`: every backward step that could have produced `target` as a *main* product (byproducts/coproducts are never a continuable target). For each qualifying reaction, every non-spectator, drawn `reactant`-role participant is a candidate "carried" substance (not only the one flagged `isPrimarySubstrate` — GRAPH_SEMANTICS §9 says "reactants", plural, and a multi-reactant reaction like the Grignard step genuinely has two valid entry points); the reaction's other qualifying participants become `sideInputs`. A class-typed reactant yields the class itself (exact) *and* one inferred move per transitive member species (`indexes.speciesInClass`), each carrying `CLASS_LEVEL_MATCH_WARNING` — the class-membership inference GRAPH_SEMANTICS §9 calls out, never presented as a stated fact (§4). `sharedVariants` intersects a participant's own condition-variant restriction with the outcome's (DOMAIN_MODEL.md §6.4 empty = common); a `null` result is a dead combination the search skips.
  - `rank.ts` — `rankRoutes`: dedup by a hash of the step sequence, then the §9 order (fewer steps → major/exclusive over minor → stated over inferred → published over draft → reaction-ID sequence), trimmed to `maxRoutes`. `FoundRoute.score` is an informational numeric summary only; the returned array order is authoritative.
  - `search.ts` — the search itself: a **backward**, per-path-cycle-safe walk from the target (`carriedOut → carriedIn`, reversed to chronological order for the result). This computes the same thing GRAPH_SEMANTICS §9's forward description does ("moves follow produces edges whose reactants include the current substance") but from the target end, which is what makes `startFrom` optional cheap: with no `startFrom`, *every* substance reached is itself an acceptable completed route ("any source is acceptable"); with `startFrom`, a route completes only on reaching one of those substances, exactly or by class membership in either direction (a class in `startFrom` containing the reached species, or a reached class containing a listed species) — the completing step gets `CLASS_LEVEL_MATCH_WARNING` when that hop was the inferred one. Bounded by `maxSteps` clamped to `limits.maxDepth` (a real clamp is reported as `truncation: { reason: 'max_depth' }` only if it actually cut off unexplored moves — never just because the parameters differ), an explored-state counter against `limits.maxNodes`/`maxEdges`, `limits.timeBudgetMs` via an injected clock (kept out of the serialisable `RouteQuery`, same pattern as `traverse`'s `now`), and `limits.signal`. A side input is rejected — the move is skipped, not the whole route — when it equals a substance already on the current path (including the target): otherwise a reversible pair like Fischer esterification ⇌ hydrolysis lets a route "assume available" the very thing it is trying to make (found by the demo test on ethanol → ethanoic acid, which surfaced a 4th, spurious route through `fischer-esterification` + `ethyl-ethanoate-hydrolysis` before the fix). `only_listed_inputs` is a separate, post-hoc check over the assembled route: every side input in every step must be in `startFrom` or already produced by an earlier step (the route's own first substance is always available).
  - `variantsOfParticipant` (`reaction-explorer/select.ts`) exported (was module-private) — `sharedVariants` needed it and it was already exactly right; no other change to that module.
  - `DEFAULT_ROUTE_MAX_STEPS = 5`, `DEFAULT_ROUTE_MAX_ROUTES = 10` added to `graph/contracts.ts` next to `DEFAULT_TRAVERSAL_LIMITS` (same precedent, additive, no interface change). `searchRoutes` re-exported from `graph/index.ts`.
- Tests: `moves.test.ts` (11: exact/side-input basics, main-vs-byproduct target, `excludeReactionIds`, `showAsNode: never` exclusion, class-membership inference incl. the exact-class move, `sharedVariants`' four combinations). `search.test.ts` (15): **ADV-049** (diamond A→B, A→C, B→D, C→D — both A→D routes, no transitive reduction), **ADV-050** (A→B→C→A — terminates on one real route, plus a tight-node-budget variant that truncates without silence), **P-09** (a 200-run fast-check property over random small directed graphs — possibly cyclic, possibly with self-loops — asserting the explored-state budget is never exceeded, `maxRoutes` is never exceeded, and no route revisits a substance), ranking (selectivity, step count vs. selectivity, stated-vs-inferred with the warning, deterministic reaction-ID tie-break), both `assumption` values, `excludeReactionIds`, `maxRoutes` trimming, the no-`startFrom` "any source" rule, and an unknown target (empty result, not an error). `search.demo.test.ts` (4): the three step-2.7 demo requirements against the real dataset — ethanol → propan-2-ol (the curated PCC route plus the dichromate alternative), aniline → phenol (diazotisation → hydrolysis, exactly one route), ethanol → ethanoic acid (all three of: the direct oxidation, PCC-then-oxidise, dichromate-then-oxidise) — plus a target with no producers.
- `npm run check`: 65 files, 1090 tests, green.

**Decisions**
- Any qualifying `reactant`-role participant is a valid backward "carried" substance, not only the one flagged `isPrimarySubstrate` (that flag is a *navigator* display concept — GRAPH_SEMANTICS §3 — reused nowhere else); GRAPH_SEMANTICS §9 says "reactants" without singling one out, and the curated demo route through the Grignard step (two reactants: methylmagnesium bromide and ethanal) only comes out right this way.
- Only a *main*-role product is a valid backward target; a route search that could arrive at a byproduct or coproduct (water, N₂, HCl) would suggest nonsensical "syntheses". This mirrors `navigator`'s and `reaction-explorer`'s own main-product-only arrow rule (GRAPH_SEMANTICS §3), read across to route search rather than invented fresh.
- A side input must not already be on the current path (§ above, Fischer/hydrolysis case) — a correctness rule with no ADV row of its own, found by running the demo test rather than specified in advance; recorded here rather than as a schema/contract amendment because it changes no interface, only `search.ts`'s internal admissibility check.
- `FoundRoute.score` is populated (`steps.length*1000 + worstSelectivityRank*100 + inferredCount*10 + draftCount`) but documented as informational: the ranking rule's final tie-break (reaction-ID sequence) is lexicographic on strings, which a single number cannot encode losslessly against the earlier criteria, so the authoritative order is the array `rankRoutes` returns, not a derived sort of `score`.
- No worker wiring yet (Phase 4): `searchRoutes` is a pure, synchronous core function; `workers/rpc/contracts.ts`'s `findRoutes(params: RouteQuery, ctx): Promise<RouteSearchResult>` will wrap it with cancellation/progress plumbing when the compute worker is built.

**Open items**
- `RouteQuery.startFrom` class-membership completion enumerates `speciesInClass(…, { transitive: true })` for every `startFrom` entry that is a class; for a very broad class this is a real fan-out bounded only by the node/edge budget (same trade-off `precursor_explorer` already accepts for its own widening, ADV-002/007) — revisit only if a real syllabus-scale class ever makes this slow.
- Route search never reads `ViewFilters` (scope/topic/reagent-family/text) — `RouteQuery` has no such field and GRAPH_SEMANTICS §9 does not ask for one; if a future UI wants "routes using only official-scope reactions", that is a new, deliberate field, not something to retrofit silently.

### Step 2.8 — Benchmarks, full generator sweep, Phase 1 perf open items measured, baseline.json, Gate B packet (2026-09-27)

**Delivered**
- `tests/perf/*.bench.ts` (docs/PERFORMANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md §9, `vitest bench`, run by `npm run bench`, not part of `npm run check`): `commit.bench.ts` (dispatch, undo/redo, a rejected commit, `scopeOf` once vs. twice, `validateTouched` on a hub — both the design-tier hub and the literal ADV-012 hub), `projection.bench.ts` (navigator/reaction_explorer/precursor_explorer builds), `route-search.bench.ts`, `validate.bench.ts` (`validateAll`, whole state) — design tier, `dense` and `hub_explosion` shapes (the two shapes PERFORMANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md §1/§10 ties to ADV-013 and ADV-012).
- `src/test-support/perf-kit.ts`: `tierState`, `buildIndexes`, `mostReferencedSpecies`/`leastReferencedSpecies` (find "the hub" of any generated shape by counting `participationsOf`, never by a generated ID — no reaction-specific hack), and `oneHubState()` — a literal ADV-012 scenario (one hub species, exactly 1,000 reactions, via `generateDataset` with `shape: 'hub_explosion'` and `species: 60` so `floor(60/50) = 1` hub), reusing the existing generator rather than a bespoke fixture.
- `tests/perf/baseline.json`: today's measured numbers (mean ms) against each §2 budget row, with pass/fail and the two open items' quantified cost. Regenerable with `npm run bench`.
- **Measured the three Phase 1 performance open items** (docs/PROGRESS.md step 1.12 / Gate A review §8.3):
  - **`validateTouched` on hubs**: 0.54–4.99 ms on the design-tier hub (well inside the 16 ms commit budget) but **24.34 ms on the ADV-012 literal hub** (1,000 reactions) — a real, quantified budget miss, dominated by rule execution over the touched record's full referrer fan-out, not by `scopeOf` itself.
  - **Index rebuild after a rejected commit**: 163–180 ms at the design tier (no §2 budget row exists for this specific case; compared informally against Commit's 16 ms, it is ~10× worse).
  - **`scopeOf` computed twice per commit** (`core/commands/store.ts` calls it, then `validateTouched` calls it again internally): confirmed exactly 2.0–2.1× the once-cost at every scale measured, but that doubling is only ≈3–5% of a hub-heavy `validateTouched` call — real, but not the dominant cost.
- **Found, along the way, that `navigator`'s projection build also misses the 16 ms budget at the design tier** (21.81–27.2 ms; not one of the three listed open items, but the same "measure the design tier" mandate surfaced it) — root cause: it scans every reaction in the state once per build (GRAPH_SEMANTICS.md §3: a whole-dataset hub overview, not a local seeded traversal), so its cost tracks total reaction count, not the focused hub's degree. Confirmed by re-running it on the literal ADV-012 case (1,000 total reactions): 3.65 ms, comfortably inside budget. `reaction_explorer`/`precursor_explorer` (genuinely local, `TraversalLimits`-bounded) stay under budget at every scale tested (1.22–8.67 ms at the design tier).
- `tests/property/generator-tiers.test.ts`: full 4-tier × 6-shape sweep (24 combinations, was 6 + 4 sampled = 10), closing step 1.11's sampling gap. Measured cost ≈12 s total, added to `npm run check` without a noticeable difference (see below).
- `tests/adversarial/gate-b-performance.test.ts`: single, warmed-up, generously-margined (5×, absorbing both single-measurement noise and `test:coverage`'s v8 instrumentation overhead — probed directly, 2× was not enough under coverage) timing assertions naming **ADV-013** (dense 5k-reaction graph: `reaction_explorer`/`precursor_explorer` builds under budget — `navigator` is deliberately not asserted here; see above) and **ADV-012**'s performance part (the literal 1,000-reaction-hub `navigator` build, under budget at that scale).
- Found and closed a real test-coverage gap while measuring `src/core/graph/route`'s branch coverage for the Gate B packet: `search.ts`'s two class-membership `startFrom`-completion branches (a class in `startFrom` containing the reached species, or vice versa) were untested since step 2.7. Two tests added to `search.test.ts`.
- `docs/reviews/gate-b-packet.md`: Gate B criteria, the full Gate B ADV/INV/P title-level scan (25/25 rows titled, re-run via `tests/adversarial/gate-matrix.ts`'s existing `gateRows`/`titledIds`), per-folder coverage detail for `src/core/graph`, the benchmark table above, the full generator sweep, decisions carried from steps 2.1–2.7, and open items — in the Gate A packet's format. Not part of `npm run check`'s gate (reference material, like the Gate A packet).
- `npm run check`: 66 files, 1,110 tests, green. `npm run test:coverage`: 97.11% statements, 91.12% branches, 98.55% functions, 98.8% lines. Property suite at `ORN_PROPERTY_RUNS=200`: 5 files, 48 tests, pass.

**Decisions**
- The step's own framing is "measure", not "fix": neither `navigator`'s full-scan cost nor `validateTouched`'s hub-fan-out cost nor the doubled `scopeOf` call was changed. All three are architecture-level characteristics (confirmed by tracing the code, not guessed), not implementation bugs, and each has a real but non-trivial fix (an incrementally-maintained navigator-arrows index; splitting scope computation out of `validateTouched`'s contract) that deserves its own reviewed step rather than a rushed change bundled into a benchmarking step. Recorded as open items and in the Gate B packet for the reviewer to prioritise.
- `tests/perf/*.bench.ts` files are **not** matched by `vitest.config.ts`'s `test.include` (`*.test.{ts,tsx}`), only by `benchmark.include` — so they never run under `npm run check`, matching TEST_STRATEGY.md §1 ("e2e and benchmarks run at every gate and before tagging a phase", not every commit). `tests/adversarial/gate-b-performance.test.ts` is the one performance-adjacent file that *does* run under `npm run check`, deliberately kept to single-measurement, wide-margin assertions rather than statistical benchmarking, so it stays fast and non-flaky there.
- `baseline.json`'s numbers are one run on a developer workstation, not a dedicated CI runner class (PERFORMANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md §9 assumes "the same runner class" for its regression rule) — recorded as-is, with that caveat in the file itself, rather than presented as more precise than it is.

**Open items**
- The two measured budget misses (`navigator` at the design tier; `validateTouched` on an extreme hub) and the doubled `scopeOf` call are unresolved — see `docs/reviews/gate-b-packet.md` §0 and §5 for the reviewer's decision.
- No CI workflow exists to enforce PERFORMANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md §9's ">20% regression against baseline fails" rule automatically; `baseline.json` is recorded evidence today, not yet diffed by any script on a schedule.
- `src/core/graph/route`'s branch coverage (73.7% in isolation) has a few remaining defensive/near-unreachable guards not chased to 100% (gate-b-packet.md §4) — named there with a reason each, same precedent as Gate A's own `commands/replace.ts` / `validation/rules/helpers.ts`.

### Gate B review recorded (2026-09-27)

Gate B reviewed by the human reviewer (Opus), 2026-09-27: **NOT PASSED** — the "benchmarks meet budgets
at the design tier" criterion is not satisfied. The self-review's proposed documented exception (§0 item 1
of `docs/reviews/gate-b-packet.md`) was accepted for `validateTouched` on an extreme hub but **not** for
`navigator`'s projection build: a full-dataset scan on every build is an architecture-level cost that grows
with total reaction count regardless of the focused hub, and the reviewer asked for it to be fixed (an
incrementally-maintained index) rather than carried as a permanent exception. Fixes assigned to steps 2.9
(benchmark percentiles, since the packet's verdict was computed on single-run means against a p95 budget;
the incremental navigator index itself) and 2.10 (the remaining open items: rejected-commit index rebuild,
the doubled `scopeOf` call, the route-search injected-clock coverage gap). `phase-2` is not tagged. Per the
amended CLAUDE.md §6 / SONNET_HANDOFF.md §20 procedure, tagging happens only in a separate session after a
**passed** verdict is recorded here.

### Step 2.9 — Benchmark percentiles, `bench:compare`, incremental navigator roll-up index (2026-09-27)

**Delivered**
- **Commit 1** (docs/process only; see its own message for detail): untagged `phase-2` (local only, no
  remote); recorded Gate B's not-passed verdict in `PROJECT_STATE.json`/`PROGRESS.md`; added steps 2.9/2.10
  to `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §2; the gate-tagging procedure (CLAUDE.md §6, SONNET_HANDOFF.md §20/§21); one
  read map (CLAUDE.md §8 now points at `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §4); ADV-005's Phase/Gate split;
  ADV-013 marked fail (navigator) in the packet; closed the already-resolved propanone/ethanal open item.
- **Commit 2**: `tests/perf/budgets.mjs` (per-benchmark metadata, keyed by Vitest's full test name);
  `scripts/bench-report.mjs` (runs `tests/perf/*.bench.ts` via `vitest/node`'s benchmark API — no CLI
  fail-on-regression mode exists — and joins each task's mean/p75/p99/sample count with its budget);
  `npm run bench:baseline` (`scripts/generate-baseline.mjs`, regenerates `baseline.json` with a verdict
  computed on p99, documented as a conservative stand-in for the §2 budgets' p95); `npm run bench:compare`
  (`scripts/bench-compare.mjs`, fails on a >20% p99 regression against the committed baseline; gates only,
  not in `npm run check`). `perf-kit.ts`: `designTierHubState()`, a design-tier-scale (5,000-reaction)
  scenario where one species is referenced by 1,000 of them ("like water"); `commit.bench.ts` benchmarks
  commit + `validateTouched` on it. Recomputing on p99 instead of step 2.8's single-run means changed
  several verdicts from pass to fail (commit dispatch/undo-redo and reaction_explorer/precursor_explorer
  projection builds on `hub_explosion`/`dense` at the design tier) — a materially worse picture than the
  original packet described, independently supporting the reviewer's verdict.
- **Commit 3**: `src/core/indexes/navigator-rollup.ts` — the navigator's hub roll-up (`NavigatorRollup`,
  `HubResolver`, `hubTargets`, `primarySubstrateOf`/`mainProductsOf`), moved down from
  `core/graph/navigator/{hubs,select}.ts` because `core/graph` already imports `core/indexes` and the
  reverse would be a cycle (`no-circular`). `ReadonlyIndexes` gains `navigatorRollup(): NavigatorRollupIndex`
  (`hubs`, `unanchoredReactionIds`, `candidatesFrom`/`candidatesTo` — additive, ADR-030). `full.ts`'s
  `IndexEngine.apply`: a touched reaction retracts and recomputes its own candidates; a species'/reagent's
  `classIds` change invalidates its memoised hub resolution and recomputes only the reactions referencing
  it (`participations`/`productions`, already available); a deleted species/reagent gets the same
  treatment (a reaction whose participant/product ref now dangles must not keep its last cached hub — see
  Amendments below); an anchor/class/`entity_display` change rebuilds the whole roll-up (cheap relative to
  a full `IndexEngine` rebuild — one pass over reactions only, no other table touched). The roll-up
  outlives many commits, so it is *rebound* (not recreated) to each commit's fresh `CoreIndexes`/derived
  tables on every `apply()` — its `basis.get`/`displayOf` closures would otherwise go stale.
  `src/core/graph/navigator/build.ts` reads `ctx.indexes.navigatorRollup()` instead of scanning
  `state.records.reaction`; `hubs.ts`/`select.ts` become thin re-exports/wrappers so every existing caller
  (`precursor/reverse-of.ts`, tests) keeps its old signature.
  `src/test-support/navigator-reference.ts`: the pre-2.9 full-scan algorithm, kept as the differential
  test's oracle. `tests/property/navigator-differential.test.ts` (20 tests): index-backed build equals the
  reference byte-for-byte on the demo, on all 6 generator shapes at tiny/initial/design tiers, and after
  random command sequences through the store (with the *live* incrementally-applied `indexEngine`, not a
  fresh rebuild — the store itself still only carries the part-1 `coreIndexEngine`, step 2.1's open item).
  P-07 (`tests/property/commands.test.ts`) and the apply-≡-build scenarios (`src/core/indexes/full.test.ts`)
  extended to the new index for free: `derivedSnapshot`/`index-snapshot.ts` gained
  `navigatorRollupSnapshot` (hubs, unanchored reactions, every hub's candidates both ways), so every
  existing apply-≡-build comparison now covers it without a second test file. P-10/INV-26
  (`tests/property/projections.test.ts`) needed no changes and stayed green — the navigator's public
  `Projection` shape did not change.
- `npm run check`: 67 files, 1130 tests (was 66/1110), green. `npm run bench:baseline`: `tests/perf/baseline.json`
  regenerated (see the numbers below).

**A real bug this step's own extended tests caught (not shipped)**: the first version of the incremental
update recomputed a class-membership change's referencing reactions, but did nothing when a
species/reagent was *deleted* while still dangling-referenced by some reaction's participant/product (a
raw-mutation scenario the ADV-051/052-style random-walk tests in `full.test.ts` exercise, bypassing
validation on purpose). The reaction's own record never changed, so nothing marked it touched, and its
last-cached hub resolution (from before the deletion) stayed in the roll-up forever. Fixed: `changes.deleted`
species/reagent refs now also invalidate their memoised hub resolution and recompute every reaction that
still references them (ownership of the `participations`/`productions` entries is the *referencing
reaction's*, not the deleted record's, so `derived` still names them correctly after `removeRecord`
retracts the deleted record's own contributions). Caught by `full.test.ts`'s seeded random-walk apply-≡-
build comparison — exactly the kind of divergence step 2.9's extended P-07 coverage exists to catch.

**A second bug the new differential test caught**: the pre-2.9 reference algorithm scanned reactions in ID
order (then each reaction's outcomes/products in array order); the index-backed build gathers candidates
hub-by-hub. Both orders are internally deterministic (P-10 holds for either alone), but they are *different
orders* — invisible when nothing truncates, but `maxEdges`/`maxNodes` admits candidates in whatever order
they arrive and stops, so a tight limit could keep a different subset depending on which algorithm produced
it. Neither order was ever a documented contract. Fixed by making it one: both `build.ts` and
`navigator-reference.ts` now sort candidates by `(reactionId, outcomeId, productId)` before the admission
pass (documented in GRAPH_SEMANTICS.md §10).

**Navigator build p99 at the design tier, before/after** (`tests/perf/baseline.json`; developer workstation,
single-run noise applies — see the file's own caveats, and its low-sample-count note: several entries here
have only 10–70 samples, so p99 is close to the observed max, not a stable percentile):

| Scenario | Before (step 2.8/2.9 commit 2, full scan, same percentile method) | After (this commit, incremental index) |
|---|---|---|
| design tier, `dense`, unanchored generated state | p99 102.68 ms, mean 79.02 ms | p99 17–33 ms, mean 18–22 ms (un-anchored: everything resolves to the single Unclassified hub, so cost still tracks total reactions — see below) |
| design tier, `hub_explosion`, unanchored generated state | p99 102.38 ms, mean 78.12 ms | p99 11–25 ms, mean 9–13 ms |
| design tier, `dense`/`hub_explosion`, **anchored** (`withNavigatorAnchors`, ~100 hubs) | *(not measured before; the generator creates no anchors)* | `dense`: p99 27–150 ms, mean 18–56 ms (noisy; budget still missed most runs); `hub_explosion`: p99 10–29 ms, mean 7–23 ms (budget met most runs) |
| ADV-012 literal case (1 hub, 1,000 of 1,000 reactions) | p99 19.07 ms, mean 10.42 ms | p99 5–12 ms, mean 3.0–3.5 ms — **pass** |

The generator itself creates no `navigator_anchor` records (docs/PERFORMANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md §10 is
graph/index scale only, not curated content), so the un-anchored benchmark state is the *worst case for
this fix specifically*: every reaction resolves to the one virtual Unclassified hub, so the roll-up's
"proportional to hubs and output" saving collapses to "proportional to total reactions" because the output
*is* every reaction. `withNavigatorAnchors()` (new, `perf-kit.ts`) anchors every second generated class
(~100 hubs at the design tier — more than a real curriculum's few dozen, so not tuned to flatter the
number) to make the benchmark represent a curated dataset (the demo has 15 anchored hubs) instead of the
degenerate case. Even so, `dense`'s p99 still exceeds the 16 ms budget on most runs (mean is close, 18–22
ms un-anchored / 18–56 ms anchored) — a real, disclosed remaining gap, not claimed as fixed. `hub_explosion`
now meets the budget on mean and on p99 most runs. Both are a 3–10× improvement over the full-scan
baseline, and the removed cost (repeating hub resolution and candidate extraction on every build call
regardless of whether the state changed) is architectural, not a benchmark artefact — it is exactly what
ADR-030 targets. Reviewer/step 2.10 should decide whether `dense`'s remaining gap needs further work
(a candidate ceiling per hub, or applying `ViewFilters` before gathering candidates rather than after) or
is an accepted, scoped exception given the scenario's own honesty about noise and worst-case shape.

**Decisions**
- Per ADR-030: `core/indexes` cannot import `core/graph` (would cycle with `core/graph` already importing
  `core/indexes`), so hub resolution moved down rather than being duplicated. `core/graph/navigator/hubs.ts`
  and the `primarySubstrate`/`mainProducts` half of `select.ts` are now re-exports/thin wrappers, not new
  logic — behaviour is unchanged, confirmed by the differential test and by `navigator.test.ts`'s unchanged
  35 tests all staying green.
- The roll-up stores two tables (`byFromHub`, `byToHub`) holding the same candidate objects by reference,
  not one keyed by an actual `(from, to)` pair string — querying "everything out of hub X" or "everything
  into hub X" independently is what `build.ts` and a future per-hub UI both need; nothing currently reads
  `candidatesTo` directly (build.ts derives incoming counts from the edges it already has `.to` on), but it
  is exercised and checked for correctness by every apply-≡-build/P-07 comparison via
  `navigatorRollupSnapshot`, the same level of testing the rest of `ReadonlyIndexes`'s query methods get.
- Anchor/class/`entity_display` changes rebuild the whole navigator roll-up rather than trying to trace
  which reactions could be indirectly affected (a reparented class, or a newly/un-anchored target, can
  shift resolution for reactions with no direct reference to the changed record — there is no bounded way
  to find "affected" reactions short of re-deriving resolution for everything). This is *only* this index,
  not the full `IndexEngine` rebuild a rejected commit pays for (docs/PROGRESS.md step 2.8's other open
  item) — one pass over `state.records.reaction`, nothing else. Not separately benchmarked this step (these
  record kinds are rare authoring edits, not a hot path); worth measuring in 2.10 if the reviewer asks.
- Truncation order (`(reactionId, outcomeId, productId)`) was chosen over the alternative of trying to
  reproduce the pre-2.9 algorithm's incidental reaction-ID-then-array-order exactly: the latter is not
  reproducible from the index at all (the roll-up does not preserve per-reaction outcome/product array
  position, only the fields needed to draw an edge), and was never a documented contract to begin with.

**Open items**
- `dense`-shape navigator build at the design tier still exceeds the 16 ms p99 budget when anchored
  realistically (§ above) — architectural (output size, not redundant computation), not yet resolved;
  candidate approaches (a per-hub output ceiling, applying `ViewFilters` before gathering candidates) are
  not attempted this step. Carried to 2.10 / the next reviewer decision.
- `store.getIndexes()` still returns only the part-1 `coreIndexEngine` (docs/PROGRESS.md step 2.1's open
  item, unchanged by this step): nothing in the live app can call `ctx.indexes.navigatorRollup()` yet
  through the store directly. `tests/property/navigator-differential.test.ts`'s random-walk test works
  around this exactly as P-07 does, by subscribing and applying the full `indexEngine` independently.
- Remaining Gate B items (rejected-commit index rebuild, the doubled `scopeOf` call, the route-search
  injected-clock coverage gap, the full packet re-measurement) are step 2.10's, not attempted here.

### Step 2.10 — Incremental rollback, field-aware scope investigation, route-search clock test, re-measure (2026-09-27)

**Commit 1 — roll back indexes incrementally on rejected commits.** `core/indexes/engine.ts`'s
`coreIndexEngine.apply` now accepts a one-step-backward revision (`previous.revision - 1`) as incrementally
appliable, alongside the existing one-step-forward case — the retract-then-add mechanics don't care about
direction, only about a well-formed `ChangeSet`. `core/commands/store.ts`'s rejection branch builds the
inverse `ChangeSet` (before/after and patches/inversePatches swapped, `revision` set back to the pre-attempt
value) and applies it to the just-computed `nextIndexes`, instead of `coreIndexEngine.build(state, revision)`
from scratch. No interface change. `store.test.ts`: `expectConsistent` now also checks `linkersOf` and
`classAncestors`; three new tests cover a rejected L3 error (HIER-001, a two-class cycle — a self-parent
trips REF-007 at L2 first and suppresses L3, so this needed two records) and a rejected L2 error (REF-001,
a dangling relationship target) on different record kinds, plus several rejections in a row.
`tests/property/commands.test.ts`'s P-07 now checks the store's own core indexes against a fresh rebuild
after *every* intent, not once at the end — `intentsArb`'s model does generate invalid commands (confirmed
by a `p07Rejections > 0` non-vacuity assertion), so this exercises the rollback path inside the same random
walk rather than needing a separate one. PERFORMANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md §2's "Commit" row reworded to "Commit,
accepted or rejected" (no new row; recorded as an amendment below, though it changes no contract).
Measured: 163–580 ms (full rebuild, steps 2.8/2.9) → 12.7–23.3 ms (incremental rollback) at the design tier,
a 10–45× improvement; mean is now under the 16 ms budget on both shapes, p99 is close but noisy (see the
before/after table below).

**Commit 2 — field-aware `validateTouched` scope: report and decision.**

*How `scopeOf`/`validateTouched` use `watches`/`peers` today* (`src/core/validation/validator.ts`):
`scopeOf` adds, for every touched record (in both its new and previous version): (a) **every** referrer
from `indexes.referrersOf`, unconditionally; (b) every referent the record's own references point at; (c)
for rules that declare `.peers()`, whose `kinds` or `watches` include the record's kind, whatever
`.peers(record, ctx)` returns. `validateTouched` then runs **every** L2/L3 rule applicable to each scope
member's kind (`findingsOf`), regardless of *why* that member is in scope. `watches` is a record-*kind*
list ("besides `kinds`, which other kinds' changes should make me consider `.peers()`"); `.peers()` is
rule-specific but still returns whole records, not fields. **Neither carries any field-level information at
all** — there is no way, from what a rule declares today, to ask "did the field this rule actually reads
change on the touched record?" The closest existing lever is `ValidationRule.scope` (`'record' |
'neighbourhood' | 'global'`): a `'record'`-scoped rule's verdict on a *referrer* R can never change from R
merely being a referrer of some other touched record — R's own content didn't change. In principle this
lets some rule executions be skipped for context-only scope members. In practice it does **not** save
anything safely: `commitFindings`/`FindingStore.replaceForSubjects` does a wholesale replace of a subject's
findings at a level — if `validateTouched` simply omits a `'record'`-scoped rule's finding for R (rather
than recomputing it), the store would wrongly clear a still-valid finding, since nothing carries the old
value forward without a `FindingStore` reference in `validateTouched`'s signature (which would itself be a
contract change). And since `'record'`-scoped rules by definition read only the record itself (cheap,
local checks — kind/prefix match, self-reference, local-ID resolution), *recomputing* them is not the
expensive part of a hub-record commit; the expense is the sheer count of referrers run through the whole
rule pipeline (`runRules`, finding-ID hashing, sorting) regardless of which specific rules fire.
**Conclusion: no safe, correct narrowing of `validateTouched`'s scope is possible with today's metadata
(kind-level `watches`, record-level `peers`, three-valued `scope`) without adding field-level dependency
information to `ValidationRule` — a contract change.** Per this step's instructions, the contract is left
unchanged. Instead: PERFORMANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md §2 gains a new row, "Commit on a record with ≥ 500
referrers: < 50 ms p95" (not a new "Commit" row, a *different* operation with its own, looser budget — a
hub-record commit is a qualitatively different case, not a regression of the plain one). A regression
benchmark already existed for this shape (`commit.bench.ts`'s `validateTouched` benches on both the
ADV-012 literal hub and the design-tier "like water" hub, step 2.9); `tests/perf/budgets.mjs` now maps both
to the new row instead of the plain 16 ms Commit budget.
*Doubled `scopeOf` call*: unaffected by this step (no scoping change was made to remove it) — recorded as
a **Phase 9 candidate** (PROJECT_STATE.json open items), not attempted here, per this step's own framing.

*Measured* (`tests/perf/baseline.json`; both scenarios' referrer count is ~1,000, ≥ 500):

| Scenario | validateTouched p99 | Verdict vs. the new 50 ms row |
|---|---|---|
| ADV-012 literal hub (60 species, 1,000 of 1,000 reactions) | 32–107 ms across runs (20–92 ms mean) | fails most runs; passed when benchmarked in isolation (§ note on contention below) |
| Design-tier "like water" hub (250 species, 1,000 of 5,000 reactions) | 120–129 ms (91–95 ms mean) | fails consistently |

Both exceed even the new, looser 50 ms budget most of the time — this is a genuinely expensive case, not
just a mis-set budget; the new row makes that explicit rather than measuring it against the wrong (16 ms,
plain-commit) budget as before. A real difference worth flagging: benchmarked *alone*
(`vitest bench --run tests/perf/commit.bench.ts`), the ADV-012 case measured p99 20–32 ms (well under 50 ms);
benchmarked as part of the *full* `npm run bench:baseline` suite, the same scenario measured p99 92–107 ms.
This is workstation contention (other benchmarks' GC/CPU pressure carrying over), not a real difference in
the scenario — consistent with `baseline.json`'s own noise caveat, but large enough here to be worth calling
out explicitly rather than picking whichever number looks better. The design-tier scenario is consistently
expensive either way; a plausible (not confirmed) explanation is `relationshipsPerReaction: 1` in that
generator config versus `0` in `oneHubState()` — each of the 1,000 referring reactions then also carries a
REL-xxx check inside `findingsOf`. Not chased further this step (out of scope for a scope-metadata
investigation); a candidate for whoever picks up the doubled-`scopeOf`/Phase 9 work.

**Commit 3 — route-search clock test; re-measure; Gate B packet.** `src/core/graph/route/search.test.ts`:
"INV-22: time budget uses the injected clock" (2 new tests) — a 10-step chain (kept within
`DEFAULT_TRAVERSAL_LIMITS.maxDepth` so the depth clamp cannot also fire and confound the reported
truncation reason), an injected `now` that advances 2 ms per call against a 5 ms budget stops the search
and reports `truncation.reason === 'time_budget'`; a companion test with no injected clock confirms the
budget is simply not enforced (no crash, no false truncation, the whole chain explored) — the same pattern
`traverse()`'s own clock test uses (`traversal.test.ts`), now covered for the second bounded primitive with
the same shape. Closes the one genuinely-untested branch the step 2.8 packet's coverage detail named.

Re-ran all benches (`npm run bench:baseline`) and `npm run bench:compare` against the freshly-regenerated
file. `bench:compare` flagged 3 entries as regressing p99 > 20% (undo/redo on `dense`, `scopeOf` twice on
`hub_explosion`, `validateTouched` on the design-tier hub) — re-running it again flagged a *different* set
of entries against the *same* committed baseline, confirming these are workstation noise (exactly the
caveat `baseline.json` documents), not a real regression from anything in this step. Rewrote
`docs/reviews/gate-b-packet.md` in full: every §5 verdict now computed on p99 (not step 2.8's single-run
means); §0 answered with what 2.9–2.10 did about each of the step 2.8 reviewer's five items; ADV-013
re-judged specifically on the navigator (fails on `dense`, passes on `hub_explosion` this run); §8's stale
open items (the rejected-commit rebuild, the missing clock test) removed as closed. Gate B's status stays
**self-reviewed; human review pending** (`docs/PROJECT_STATE.json`) — not marked passed, not tagged.

### Step 2.12 — Field-aware validation scope (2026-09-28)

**Commit 1 — `phase-2.12: field-aware validation scope (ADR-031)`.** Reviewer decision: implement
field-aware scoping in `validateTouched` (PERFORMANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md §4, "re-runs only affected rules for
touched records and their reverse-index dependents") through an additive `ValidationRule` amendment; a rule that
does not declare it keeps today's behaviour. Full amendment under "Amendments" below, and ADR-031.

- **Contract** (`src/core/validation/contracts.ts`): `referencedReads?: ReferencedReads` - what a rule's `check`
  reads of the records its subject refers to. The shape sits beside `watches`/`peers`: those say which *other*
  records a change reaches, at kind/record level; this says which *fields* of the records a rule refers to it
  reads, the missing half. Keys are a referenced kind or `'*'`; values are top-level field names; `[]` is "only
  whether it exists", an absent kind is "not read".
- **Scoping** (`src/core/validation/scoping.ts`, used by `validator.ts`): `changeOf` diffs the touched record's
  new and previous versions by top-level field, by reference (exact under structural sharing; a created or
  deleted record, or a call without `previous`, is `'all'`); `referrerFilter` answers once per referrer *kind*
  per touched record, so a 1,000-referrer hub costs one rule scan, not 1,000. A referrer is kept if any L2/L3
  rule of its kind reads a changed field. Referent expansion and `peers`/`watches` are untouched (RXN-018 and
  AST-001 read their own referrers and depend on the referent expansion). The referrers lookup now runs once
  per touched record instead of once per version (same ID).
- **Declared: 69 of 74 rules; 60 declare `{}` (read nothing of any referenced record)**: every `scope: 'record'`
  rule; the eight REF rules (REF-001 `'*': []` existence; REF-005 the container fields, derived from
  `REFERENCE_SPECS`; the rest `{}`); RXN-013 (`reaction: scope, instanceOfId`), RXN-018 (`{}`: it reads its own
  referrers, which the referent expansion brings in), REL-003/005 (`{}`, peers), REL-006 (`reaction:
  reversibility`), SPC-001/005 (`{}`, peers), CLS-001 (`species: classIds`), CLS-002, HIER-002 (`topic:
  topicKind`), CUR-003 (`exam_version: examId, label`), CUR-004/006 (`{}`, peers), CUR-005 (`reference:
  refKind`), PRS-001/002, AST-001, RVW-002 (`'*': review`). **Left undeclared: SPC-006, HIER-001, HIER-003,
  RTE-002, RTE-004** - they read derived hierarchy, composition or route walks, where a field list would be
  right only by reasoning about `peers`, which is not "clearly correct". Effect: 15 kinds are fully declared
  (`reaction`, `relationship`, `mechanism`, `caveat`, `exam`, `exam_version`, `exam_relevance`, `topic_mapping`,
  `reaction_scheme`, `reference`, `transformation`, `view_preset`, `asset`, `entity_display`,
  `navigator_anchor`), so a referrer of those kinds is skipped when nothing it reads changed; `species`,
  `class`, `concept`, `named_reaction`, `topic`, `reagent` and `route` each still have one undeclared rule and
  keep re-running on any change to what they refer to (their peers already re-run most of them anyway).
- **Tests**: `src/core/validation/scoping.test.ts` (23): `changeOf`/`readsChange`/`referrerFilter`; a hub of 120
  referrers with rule invocations counted - renaming it, adding an alias, editing its notes, changing its classes
  or making it an ion re-runs **no** referrer rule, and findings still equal `validateAll`; changing
  `review.lifecycle` (RVW-002) and deleting then re-creating it (REF-001) re-run **every** referrer; one
  undeclared rule of the referrer kind restores the old behaviour; no `previous` re-runs everyone; one read-field
  test each for RXN-013, REL-006, CLS-001, HIER-002, CUR-003/005, REF-005 (the read field re-runs the referrer, an
  unread one does not). `src/core/validation/reads-audit.test.ts` (4): every declared rule is run over the
  maximal fixture (also with every exam version official), the demo and every generator shape, with the records
  it fetches through its subject's references wrapped in a Proxy - a read outside its declaration fails; a rule
  declared to read nothing must give the same findings with the records its subject refers to hidden ("vanish",
  1,000+ comparisons); a read outside the subject's references needs `peers` (or is RXN-018/AST-001's
  referrer-side read). `tests/property/validation-scope.test.ts` (3, P-11): 20 kinds of edit aimed at a hub
  species - its name, aliases, notes (with `[[links]]`), review, classes, kind, deletion and restoration; a
  referrer's role, participant, lifecycle, `instanceOfId`/scope, reversibility, deletion; new `reverse_of`
  relationships; neighbours renamed to the hub's name, linking to it, listing it twice - on the ADV-012 shape (one
  hub, every reaction) and the like-water shape (several hubs, one relationship per reaction), as raw recipes so
  a hub deletion leaves dangling references; after every edit the incrementally kept findings equal `validateAll`.
  Fault-injected by hand to confirm the tests bite (RVW-002 not declaring `review`; RXN-013 not declaring
  `instanceOfId`; REL-006 declaring the wrong field; REF-001 declaring nothing): each fails at least one of the
  audit, the hub property test or the older fixture walk (`incremental.test.ts`). Note the hub property test
  alone does not catch the RXN-013 fault (it needs an instance-of chain, which 24 random edits rarely build); the
  audit and the targeted RXN-013 test do.
- **Doubled `scopeOf` call: not removed.** Field-aware scoping does not remove it (the store calls `scopeOf`, then
  `validateTouched` computes it again); removing it needs a `Validator` contract change or a memo keyed on
  argument identity. After this change one `scopeOf` on the hub costs 0.02 ms (dense) to 0.57 ms (ADV-012 hub
  without `previous`, where all 1,000 referrers are walked) - 0.1-4% of the 16 ms budget. Left as the Phase 9
  candidate.
- `npm run check`: 70 files, 1165 tests (was 67 / 1135), green.
- **Tooling note (not fixed; not this step's)**: `npm run bench` fails on this machine (`spawnSync npx.cmd
  EINVAL`: Node 24 refuses to spawn a `.cmd` without a shell); `scripts/run-bench-cli.mjs` needs `shell: true` on
  win32. `bench:baseline`/`bench:compare` use the Node API and are unaffected; individual benches were run with
  `npx vitest bench --run --no-file-parallelism <file>`.

**Commit 2 — `phase-2.12: re-measure; Gate B packet`.** `npm run bench:baseline` (3 fresh-process runs, median
p99) and `npm run bench:compare`; `tests/perf/baseline.json` regenerated; `docs/reviews/gate-b-packet.md`
rewritten (median-p99 verdict for all 35 entries, §0 answered with the reviewer decisions of 2.9-2.12, ADV-013
status, remaining misses with causes). No source change. The hub benches (`commit.bench.ts`, `budgets.mjs`,
`perf-kit.ts`'s `afterHubEdit`) were extended in commit 1 so they measure what the store does (previous state
given): the old "no previous state" entries stay, as the safe-fallback measurement.

Hub scenarios, median p99 (ms), before = the 2.11 baseline:

| Scenario | Before | After | 16 ms Commit row |
|---|---|---|---|
| Commit dispatch, like-water hub (1,000 referrers), notes edit | 70.86 | 3.27 | pass |
| `validateTouched`, ADV-012 hub (1,000 referrers), rename, previous given | 32.15 (no-previous call) | 0.96 | pass |
| `validateTouched`, like-water hub, rename, previous given | 32.82 (no-previous call) | 1.89 | pass |
| `validateTouched` either hub, no previous state (fallback) | 32.15 / 32.82 | 31.22 / 31.03 | n/a (50 ms row: pass) |
| `validateTouched` either hub, `review` changed (a field RVW-002 reads) | not measured | 33.97 / 37.10 | n/a (50 ms row: pass) |
| Commit dispatch, like-water hub, `review` changed | not measured | **67.47** | n/a (50 ms row: **fail**) |

**Judgement on the "Commit on a record with >= 500 referrers" row.** The two hub scenarios (ADV-012 hub,
like-water hub, edited the way the store edits them) now meet the plain 16 ms Commit row by 5-17x, so the row is
no longer needed *for them*: PERFORMANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md §2 marks it a backstop (kept for Phase 9). It is not
dead, though: an edit to a field a referrer rule reads (or creating/deleting the hub) still re-validates every
referrer whole, and the whole-commit version of that on the like-water hub misses even 50 ms by 17.5 ms (+35%).
Cause (timed in a commit-shaped loop, one-off script, not committed): `validateTouched` ~50 ms over 1,023
in-scope records (~45-50 us each: REF path walking and L1 re-parse, per the 2.11 profile), `commitFindings` ~3 ms,
`scopeOf` ~1.7 ms, index apply 0.1 ms. Making that cheaper needs per-rule re-runs on context-only referrers
(the finding store replaces per subject, so a finding-merge contract) or a faster path walker: Phase 9.

**Every failing entry on median p99 — one** (the entry above). Every entry the 2.10 packet failed now passes.
`bench:compare` flagged 4 entries over +20% (undo+redo `dense` +21%, rejected commit `dense` +24%, `scopeOf`
twice `dense` +42%, route search `dense` +21%), all far under budget and none a hub-validation entry; treated as
noise (baseline spreads 9-45%), not re-run.

### Step 2.13 — Per-rule referrer re-validation, class-hub benchmarks, bench fixes, Gate B packet (2026-09-28)

**Commit 1 — `phase-2.13: class-hub benchmarks` (measure before fixing).** `tests/perf/class-hub.bench.ts`: whole
commits on the design-tier `hub_explosion` class hub (every one of its 1,400 species lists class 0, found
structurally by `perf-kit.ts`'s `mostReferencedClass`): rename, notes edit, and moving it under another parent
(a spare root class is added so the move alternates `[]` / `[root]` without closing a cycle; every generated
class descends from class 0). Each edit is checked to be accepted, so the bench never times a rejection.
Budgets: rename and notes against the 16 ms Commit row; the parent move against the >= 500-referrer backstop row
(SPC-006 reads class ancestry of every species referrer). `tests/perf/baseline.json` regenerated (3 fresh-process
runs, median p99) with Edge closed and nothing else heavy running. The run was **noisier than 2.12's** (25 of 38
entries over 25% spread; several run-1 outliers 5-10x the other two, e.g. the notes edit 449 / 92 / 97 ms), so
read the medians with that in mind.

**Reviewer decisions taken in this step** (from the step's instructions; recorded here so the Gate B packet can
attribute them exactly):
- Approved an additive `FindingStore` method that replaces a subject's findings for a set of rule IDs only, and
  per-rule re-validation of referrers, with the fallback that if it needed more than an additive change it was not
  to be made (it did not).
- Accepted per-rule re-runs for **peers** as well as referrers (a reading of the `peers` contract that I proposed
  and applied in the first version of commit 2; ADR-032 flags it).
- Approved an optional `ValidationRule` field for the fields a rule reads on its peers (my proposal, first called
  `watchedFields`; named `peerReads` to match `referencedReads`), declared on SPC-006 so a class rename or notes
  edit does not re-run SPC-006 on every listing species, with the read audit extended to check it.
- **Exit rule for Gate B, final** (recorded in `docs/TEST_STRATEGY.md` §6 as the general performance rule for gates):
  correctness green (the ADR-031/032 property tests, the read audit, `npm run check`); ordinary edits (rename,
  notes) on the like-water hub and the class hub under 50 ms median p99; anything over its 16 ms target, and the
  structural edits (class parent move, review-status change), recorded as documented Phase 9 exceptions with their
  numbers; no further rounds for Phase 2. Also `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §1 item 7: a step that adds or changes a hot
  path adds or updates its benchmark in the same step.

**Commit 2 — `phase-2.13: per-rule re-validation for referrers and peers (ADR-032)` (119f337).**
`FindingStore.replaceForRules(subjects, ruleIds, findings)`; `Validator.validateTouchedByRule(state, indexes,
touched, previous, known)` returning a `TouchedValidation` (whole subjects + per-rule groups + `all`) and
`commitTouched`; the command store makes that one call per commit, which also removes the doubled `scopeOf` call
(closed, no longer a Phase 9 candidate). Rule: touched records and referents are whole; a referrer or a peer named by
an L3 rule has only the affected rules re-run; an affected L2 rule keeps a record whole (an L2 ERROR gates its L3
rules); a per-rule subject with an L1/L2 ERROR in the store is skipped. `ValidationRule.peerReads` makes `peers`
field-aware: declared on SPC-001 (`species: names`), SPC-005 and CLS-002 (`class: names`), SPC-006 and CLS-001
(`class: parentIds`), HIER-001 (each hierarchy kind's parent field). **SPC-006 is now declared** for
`referencedReads` too (`class: parentIds`): its reads go through the class-ancestry index, so the read audit now
treats a hierarchy walk over a record the subject refers to (or any other) as a read of that kind's parent field;
undeclared and unverified before, safe now because the audit fails a wrong declaration (fault-injected). Tests: 6
`replaceForRules` store tests; scoping tests for per-rule referrers and peers (a referrer's findings from rules that
did not run survive; an L2-ERROR referrer is left alone; class-hub rename/alias/notes re-run nothing on species, a
parent move re-runs SPC-006 alone); the P-11 hub property test now also has review-status edits and class-hub edits
(rename incl. a twin's name, notes, parents incl. cycles, review, deletion, species class lists, other classes'
parents); `incremental.test.ts` runs its fixture walk on both commit paths; the audit gains a `peerReads` trace
(reads of records the rule does not refer to: direct fetches, name-index lookups, hierarchy walks; it refuses
lookups through other records' reference fields). Fault-injected by hand, each caught: no L1/L2-error gate, L2 rules
allowed to be partial, `replaceForRules` not removing, wrong `referencedReads`/`peerReads` declarations (SPC-001,
CLS-002, HIER-001 x2, SPC-006). `npm run check`: 70 files, 1,188 tests (was 70 / 1,165); property tests at
`ORN_PROPERTY_RUNS=200`: 7 files, 71 tests.

**Commit 3 — `phase-2.13: bench fixes, docs, Gate B packet`.**
- **`npm run bench` on Windows fixed** (`scripts/run-bench-cli.mjs` runs vitest's own entry file with
  `process.execPath`: no `.cmd`, no shell). `npm run bench`, `bench:baseline` and `bench:compare` all ran on this
  machine in this step.
- **Hot-path benchmarks updated (IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN §1 item 7):** `validateTouchedByRule` entries for both species
  hubs (rename, and review status changed, per rule) using `perf-kit.ts`'s `knownFindings`, alongside the
  whole-subject `validateTouched` entries (kept: that API still exists). 42 entries.
- **Stale fragment removed from this file** (the interrupted-session note after the step 2.11 section).
- **Phase 4 note** (`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`, `PERFORMANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md` §3): the compute worker must always call
  `validateTouchedByRule`/`validateTouched` with the previous state (without it every referrer of a touched record is
  re-validated whole: ~31-47 ms on a hub).
- Edge was closed and nothing else heavy ran during the baseline runs. **This session's runs were noisier than 2.12's**
  (31 of 42 entries over 25% spread in the final baseline; run 2 was slow across the board): read the medians, and see
  the packet's noise note.

Hub and class-hub scenarios, median p99 (ms) of 3 fresh-process runs (before = this step's part-1 baseline, whole
commits; the 2.12 figure in brackets where it differs):

| Scenario | Row | Before | After | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Class hub (1,400 species), rename | 16 | 97.0 | 16.5 | over 16 by 0.5 (< 50): Phase 9 exception |
