### Phase 3 contract decisions (reviewer, 2026-09-28)

Docs-only record of the reviewer's decisions on the contracts fit scan above. No source, contract, dependency or benchmark change; step 3.1 not started. Each amendment lands in the step the scan named, through CLAUDE.md §7 (contracts, docs and Amendments list in that step's commit).

- **Approved as proposed:** Q2, Q3, Q4, Q5, Q6, Q7, Q8, Q9 and Q1 (a)–(f), each landing in the step the scan named.
- **Q10:** approved `withVersionOffset` in `src/test-support` (not `minVersion`); the core runner is unchanged. It has its own self-test (shifted versions and step reports map back exactly; every runner error code passes through). Boot (3.3) also tests a real version-0 document against the real runner: `INVALID_VERSION` → `OpenResult` `'migration_failed'`, read-only, nothing written.
- **Q1 asset write: REJECTED "blob first, then the record".** One transaction per ChangeSet stays (PERSISTENCE §5, INV-20): `writeChanges(changeSet, blobs?)` takes the blobs of asset records created in that ChangeSet and writes records + meta + `assetBlobs` in one Dexie transaction; any failure writes nothing. Blobs of deleted asset records are kept (undo needs them); an orphan-blob sweep is a Phase 9 Diagnostics action (reported, user-confirmed), never automatic. Lands in 3.1 (signature and tests); used by 3.7 and 3.11.
- **Q11:** approved as ADR-033 with guards: `applyRemote` and `reload` are allowed only on a read-only (follower) store; `applyRemote` on a writable store is refused as a value (INV-28: never two writers); a follower has no write queue attached (skipping `cause: 'remote'` is a second guard, not the main one); re-validation uses `validateTouchedByRule` WITH the previous state; a revision that is not exactly current + 1 triggers a full reload; `reload` clears history; promotion = reload, then become writable. Lands in 3.4.
- **Q12:** approved `TaskOptions { signal?, onProgress? }` in the io (and persistence) contracts, plus a type-level test under `tests/` asserting `TaskOptions` stays assignable to `RpcCallContext`. Pending import batch: at most one; a new preview or a cancel discards it; apply takes `batchId` + plan and is refused as a value when the batch is unknown or the live revision differs from the revision the preview was built on ("data changed since the preview; run it again"). Lands in 3.6/3.7; the stale-revision refusal is part of 3.7's INV-21 tests.
  **Correction (reviewer, step 3.5 review, 2026-09-29):** the direction above is reversed and does not typecheck (`RpcCallContext.signal`/`.progress` are required; `TaskOptions`'s are optional and `TaskOptions` has no `progress` property at all, only `onProgress`). The correct, useful direction — approved — is `RpcCallContext` assignable to `TaskOptions`, so a Phase 4 compute-worker method already holding a real `RpcCallContext` can pass it straight through to one of these pure functions without adapting it. `tests/types/task-options.test.ts` (step 3.5) already asserts the correct direction; this line only fixes the record. The original bullet is left as written above, not deleted (CLAUDE.md §7).

**Addendum (reviewer, 2026-09-28, after step 3.1) — change-channel message, to Q3/Q11.** `ChangeMessage = { fromRevision, revision, changedIds }`. `fromRevision` is the revision the database was at before the writer's transaction (a transaction that coalesced several ChangeSets names the whole span). A follower applies the message only when `fromRevision` equals its current revision; otherwise it reloads fully (ADR-033: `reload`). Lands in 3.4 (contract type, tests, PERSISTENCE.md §10, and the 3.4 Must cover cell, already updated).

### Step 3.1 — Dexie adapter, serial write queue, SaveStatus, P-01 on IndexedDB (2026-09-28)

**Delivered** (`src/persistence`, every new source file under 190 lines)
- `db.ts`: `OrnDb` (Dexie, stores from `DB_STORES`, DB `orn` v1) built by `createOrnDb({ indexedDB, IDBKeyRange })`; the
  browser globals are never read (ESLint bans `indexedDB`, `IDBKeyRange`, `window`, `document` in `src/persistence`).
  `DB_VERSION_STEPS` is the list of released versions with optional `upgrade` functions: the upgrade scaffold.
- `coalesce.ts`: a pending batch = which records were touched + the newest state + revision + blobs; the latest value wins.
- `write-queue.ts`: serial queue, `SaveStatus`, retry 250 ms / 1 s / 4 s → `failed` (pending kept), quota handling,
  revision-gap guard, read-only refusal, `close()`; storage-agnostic (`persist(batch)` is injected).
- `derived-cache.ts`: `derivedCache` put/get, LRU eviction, 100 MB soft cap, emergency eviction for quota.
- `adapter.ts`: `createDexieAdapter({ db, clock, … })`: one transaction per write over records + meta + asset blobs,
  `getAssetBlob`, `putDerived`/`getDerived`, the two meta markers, `getStatus`/`subscribeStatus`, `flush`, and
  `readCommitted` (raw rows + meta read in ONE read transaction; boot in 3.3 validates each row, P-01 loads with it).
- `events.ts`: `Scheduler` (injected timers; the tests' fake timers are a manual scheduler) and `PersistenceEvent` diagnostics.
- Test support: `src/test-support/persistence-kit.ts` (fresh fake-indexeddb per test, manual scheduler, transaction spy,
  mutation log, fault injector, seed/load pair).
- `dexie` 4.4.6 (dependency), `fake-indexeddb` 6.2.5 (dev); `npm audit`: 0 vulnerabilities. Node 24.21.0.

**Contract amendments applied** (CLAUDE.md §7; the approved list from "Phase 3 contract decisions"; entry under Amendments)
- Q1 (c): `DerivedCacheRow`, `DerivedCacheInput`, `DerivedPutResult`, `putDerived`, `getDerived`.
- Q1 (d): `setLastCleanShutdownAt`, `setLastIntegrityCheckAt`.
- Q1 asset write: `AssetBlobWrite` and `writeChanges(changes, next, blobs?)`. **Reading of the shorthand to confirm:** the
  decision text writes `writeChanges(changeSet, blobs?)`; the adapter needs the new record values, which a ChangeSet does
  not carry (only Immer patches), so `next` stays and `blobs` is the third, optional parameter.
- Q1 asset write, consequence to confirm: `putAssetBlob` is REMOVED from `PersistenceAdapter`. A standalone blob write
  is exactly the "blob first, then the record" path the reviewer rejected (it cannot be atomic with the record).
- Q2: `getStatus(): SaveStatus` (pending-writes rule in the doc comment). The `deletablePeriodicSnapshotIds` half lands in 3.2.
- No schema change, no `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump, no migration, no fixture change.

**Decisions**
- *Coalescing and INV-20.* The plan says "one transaction per ChangeSet … coalescing per record". Implemented as: every
  transaction is atomic and lands the database on a real committed revision; ChangeSets that wait together (same tick,
  or while a write or backoff is running) share ONE transaction that ends at the newest revision. An idle queue writes
  each ChangeSet on its own (the tests count the transactions). If the reviewer wants strictly one transaction per
  ChangeSet even for queued ones, that is a change to `resolveWrites`/`mergeBatches` only.
- *A failed batch is kept, never skipped.* Writing a later ChangeSet while an earlier one failed would leave a database
  state memory never had. After `failed`, the next `writeChanges` or `flush()` starts a fresh retry cycle over
  everything pending. `writeChanges` resolves when its write cycle ends (durable or `failed`); callers read `getStatus()`.
- *Revision-gap guard.* A ChangeSet that does not follow the previous revision is refused (exception + status `failed`):
  a ChangeSet the queue never saw would leave its record changes out of the database for good. The first ChangeSet
  establishes the revision unless `initialRevision` is given (boot will).
- *One microtask of grace* before a write cycle starts, so a command loop in one tick makes one transaction.
- *Quota* (ADV-040 stage 1): evict the least recently used HALF of the derivedCache rows, retry at once (no timer), repeat
  until nothing is left to free, then `failed`. Snapshot deletion (stage 2) is 3.2. A cache write that fails is a value
  (`DerivedPutResult`), never SaveStatus.
- *`DexieAdapter` is a `Pick` of `PersistenceAdapter`* plus `flush` and `readCommitted`. Not yet implemented, each in the
  step that needs it: `open` (3.3), `replaceAll` (3.3, migration), snapshots (3.2/3.3), editor sessions and uiState (3.3
  reads them at boot), `storageEstimate` and `requestPersistentStorage` (Phase 5/9 wiring). The clean-shutdown setters write
  directly; the caller must check `getStatus()` first (that is what the pending-writes query is for).
- *Read-only* is a constructor flag for now (status `read_only`, writes refused); promotion to writable is 3.4.
- `test:coverage` now also covers `src/persistence` with an 80% lines threshold (`vitest.config.ts`, package script).

**Tests** (`npm run check`: 77 files, 1,265 tests; before this step 70 files, 1,188)
- Unit: coalesce (8), write queue (21), derived cache (11), events (4).
- Integration `tests/integration/persistence-write-path.test.ts` (18) and `persistence-db.test.ts` (3): INV-20 (one read-write
  transaction over records + meta + assetBlobs per write, a coalesced burst is one, meta rewritten only when changed, one
  read transaction for `readCommitted`, asset record + blob in one transaction, a failed write stores neither);
  ADV-038 integration part (a row IndexedDB cannot clone aborts the transaction mid-ChangeSet: reopening shows the last
  committed revision, no partial record, no meta change; retry with backoff on the fake scheduler; the pending batch lands
  whole with the next ChangeSet); ADV-040 write-path part (eviction frees room → saved; nothing to evict → failed with
  quota named, memory kept, serialising the in-memory state still works, recovers on `flush`); DB upgrade scaffold
  (newest step = `DB_VERSION`/`DB_STORES`, every store and index exists, a test-only version 2 keeps rows and runs its
  upgrade once). Mutation-checked by hand: removing the transaction, the delete path, "latest wins" or the kept-batch
  merge each fails several tests.
- Property `tests/property/persistence-roundtrip.test.ts` (12), P-01 IndexedDB variant: both schema fixtures, the demo,
  every generator shape (tiny), the initial-tier dense dataset (1,000 reactions), random small datasets, random command
  sequences saved as one coalesced burst, and saved ChangeSet by ChangeSet with undo^n / redo^n, reopening the database
  each time. Coverage of `src/persistence` (focused run): 99% lines, 92% branches.

**Benchmark** (`tests/perf/persist.bench.ts`, run once and alone; `bench:baseline` NOT run, `baseline.json` untouched;
design-tier "like water" state, 5,000 reactions; mean / p99 in ms; informal, no §2 row):
- enqueue + coalesce, ordinary edit (1 record): 0.0006 / 0.0008
- enqueue + coalesce, hub edit (replaceReferences on the 1,000-reaction hub, ~1,000 records): 0.17 / 1.26
- flush on fake-indexeddb, ordinary edit (our overhead, not real IndexedDB): 22.3 / 30.9 (100 samples)
- flush on fake-indexeddb, hub edit (our overhead, not real IndexedDB): 17,353 / 19,145 (5 samples, so p99 is the max)

The flush numbers are fake-indexeddb's own scaling, not ours: a bare Dexie `bulkPut` on fake-indexeddb with the same row
counts (no ORN code) measured 21.6 ms for one row into 25,000 rows and 28.2 s for 1,000 rows (1.2 s into 2,000 rows);
its cost per put grows with the store size. Real IndexedDB is traced at Gates E/F. The synchronous enqueue is what the
main thread pays per commit: microseconds for an ordinary edit, well under a millisecond for a hub edit. The bench file
takes about 3 minutes, almost all of it the hub-edit flush.

No timing or budget test failed in any run (ADV-013 and the rest passed first time; nothing else was running), so there is nothing to record or re-run.

### Step 3.2 — Snapshots: create, list, restore, delete, retention, periodic policy (2026-09-28)

**Delivered** (`src/persistence`, every new source file under 130 lines)
- `snapshot-codec.ts`: `buildSnapshot` (state → `full` DatasetDocument as deterministic JSON through the strict schema →
  fflate zlib; a state that does not serialise is refused, never stored) and `decodeSnapshot` (bounded inflate, cap 200 MB;
  bad stream, bad JSON, not an ORN dataset are values). zlib rather than gzip because fflate's gzip header carries the
  current time, which would make identical states differ. Pure and structured-clone-safe, ready for the compute worker.
- `snapshot-retention.ts`: newest 5 periodic kept; mandatory reasons and manual snapshots never pruned; the list of
  deletable periodic snapshots (oldest first) for the quota status.
- `snapshot-policy.ts`: `createPeriodicPolicy` (30 min of activity or 200 revisions, injected clock; activity starts with the
  first ChangeSet after the previous snapshot, so an idle gap then one edit does not fire) and `startPeriodicSnapshots`
  (one at a time, a failed one is reported and retried at the next ChangeSet).
- `snapshot-store.ts`: create (build, then one `snapshots`-only transaction that also prunes older periodic snapshots and
  reports them), list (newest first, summaries only), read, delete, all in the write queue's order via `runOrdered`.
  A row is the summary plus the compressed bytes as a Blob, so `DB_STORES` and `DB_VERSION` are unchanged.
- `restore.ts`: `restoreSnapshot` (see Decisions). `adapter.ts` now implements the four snapshot methods.
- `write-queue.ts`: quota stage 2 (`deletableSnapshotIds` provider, list refreshed after each task), and tasks no longer
  restart a failed batch (`retryAllowed`).
- `fflate` 0.8.3 (dependency); `npm audit`: 0 vulnerabilities.

**Contract amendments applied** (CLAUDE.md §7; Amendments entry below)
- Q1 (f): `readSnapshot` returns the tolerant `MigratableDocument`. **To confirm:** it returns `SnapshotReadResult`
  (`{ ok: true, summary, doc } | { ok: false, code: 'NOT_FOUND' | 'CORRUPT', message }`), not the bare document, because
  a corrupt snapshot is a data condition and errors across module boundaries are values (CLAUDE.md §6).
- Q2: `SaveStatus.deletablePeriodicSnapshotIds`.
- Q9 (restore side): restore takes the core `MigrationRunner` and `Pick<CanonicalStore, 'getState' | 'getRevision' |
  'isReadOnly' | 'replaceState'>`, and a `Pick<PersistenceAdapter, 'createSnapshot' | 'readSnapshot' | 'deleteSnapshot'>`
  (all existing types; new: `RestoreSnapshotResult` in the persistence contracts). `WriterLock` for boot is 3.3; io's
  `SnapshotPort` is 3.5/3.7 (the adapter's `createSnapshot` already satisfies it structurally).
- Also new in the contracts: `SnapshotReadResult`, `RestoreSnapshotResult`. `DexieAdapterOptions` gains a required `ids: IdFactory`
  (snapshot IDs `snp_<ulid>` and timestamps) and an optional `generator`.
- No schema change, no `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump, no migration, no fixture change.

**Decisions**
- *Restore order and failure.* read → migrate → strict L1 parse → `pre_restore` snapshot → `replaceState` (L1–L3, history
  cleared). Every failure is a value and changes nothing. If `replaceState` refuses (an L2/L3 ERROR in the snapshot's
  state), the `pre_restore` snapshot made for it is deleted again, so a refused restore leaves no mandatory snapshot behind.
  If the live data changes while the `pre_restore` snapshot is written, it is taken again (3 attempts, then
  `SNAPSHOT_FAILED`): the pre-restore snapshot always holds exactly what the restore replaced.
- *Persistence of the restored state* is not awaited inside `restoreSnapshot`: `replaceState` notifies subscribers with a
  `replace` ChangeSet and the write queue persists it like any other; callers `flush()` (the tests do).
- *Quota stage 2* is a status event only (no UI): `SaveStatus.failed` carries `deletablePeriodicSnapshotIds`, oldest first,
  only for quota failures and only when periodic snapshots exist. Deleting one refreshes the list but never retries the
  write (a task must not trigger a doomed retry); a new ChangeSet or `flush()` does.
- *Snapshot build validates through the strict schema* (as export does), which costs a full L1 pass (see the benchmark);
  the alternative, a bare `JSON.stringify`, would risk storing a snapshot that cannot be restored.
- *Older-snapshot tests* use a fake runner over a stored document in an "older shape" (the envelope requires
  `schemaVersion >= 1`, as the runner does, so a version-0 document cannot exist); `withVersionOffset` and the real-runner
  boot tests are 3.3's. A newer-version snapshot is tested through the real `createMigrationRunner()` (`NEWER_VERSION`).

**Tests** (`npm run check`: 85 files, 1,337 tests; before this step 78 files, 1,273)
- Unit: codec (12: summary, determinism, compression, refusal of an unserialisable state, garbage / truncated / flipped-byte /
  not-JSON / not-a-dataset input, inflate cap, unknown keys kept), retention (4), policy and periodic wiring (9), write queue
  tasks and quota stage 2 (6 + 5 new).
- Integration `persistence-snapshots.test.ts` (12: create/list/read/delete, one transaction over `snapshots` only, ordering
  after a pending ChangeSet, read-only refusal, retention with pruned IDs reported, damage reported as CORRUPT four ways,
  ADV-040 snapshot stage) and `persistence-restore.test.ts` (12: ADV-045 snapshot-restore part with history cleared and the
  restored state persisted and "undo restore", migration through an injected runner, and every failure changing nothing:
  NOT_FOUND, CORRUPT, MIGRATION_FAILED, NEWER_VERSION, INVALID twice, REJECTED, READ_ONLY, SNAPSHOT_FAILED, and the
  live-data race twice). `persistence-markers.test.ts` was split out of the write-path file (no test changed).
- Property `snapshot-roundtrip.test.ts` (10): fixtures, demo, every generator shape (tiny), initial-tier dense, random
  datasets, random command sequences; build is deterministic.
- Coverage of `src/persistence` (focused run): 99.7% lines, 91.7% branches.

**Benchmark** (`tests/perf/snapshot.bench.ts`, run once and alone; `bench:baseline` NOT run; design tier, dense, no
IndexedDB; SLOW options, 5 samples, so p99 is the maximum; mean / max in ms; informal, no §2 row):
- build: serialise (with L1) + compress: 788 / 859
- restore without the store: decode + migrate + L1 parse + state: 699 / 743
- restoreSnapshot, whole (decode, L1, `pre_restore` build, `replaceState` L1–L3): 3,489 / 3,671
About 2 s of the whole restore is `replaceState`'s full validation. All of this runs on the main thread until Phase 4 hosts
it in the compute worker; the "no main-thread task > 50 ms" row is judged at Gate E.

**Timing-sensitive tests.** `npm run check` ran twice on the final code; no timing or budget test failed (nothing else was running).

**Open items**
- Snapshot build and restore are 0.7 to 3.5 s on the main thread at the design tier until Phase 4 (worker hosting); a Gate E item.
- `restoreSnapshot` does not wait for the restored state to reach IndexedDB; the app wiring (Phase 5) calls `flush()` where it matters.
- Q1 (a), (b), (e), Q4, Q10 land in 3.3 as planned; `open`, `replaceAll`, the boot use of `pre_migration` snapshots are 3.3.

Decisions and notes recorded with the verdict:
- **Open item (Phase 5).** The app reports a restore (and an import) as done only after `flush()` resolves with status `saved`; until then it shows "saving".
- **Phase 4 note.** `replaceState` runs `validateAll` on the main thread. From Phase 4 the compute worker validates the replica, and `replaceState` (and ADR-033's `reload`) must accept precomputed findings, so the main thread only swaps the state and rebuilds indexes. Recorded in IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md's Phase 4 note and in PROJECT_STATE openItems. No code change to `replaceState` now.
- **`replaceState` measured in parts** (design tier, `dense`, seed 2 replacing seed 1, median of 5 runs in one process, ms): `freeze` 0 (already frozen); core index build 500; `validateAll` 1,319; ChangeSet + replacement patches 15; `commitFindings` over both states' refs 124. Sum about 1,960. `hub_explosion`: index build 460, `validateAll` 1,389, ChangeSet + patches 14, `commitFindings` 122. So `validateAll` is about two thirds of the call, the index build about a quarter, the rest about 7%. Whole `replaceState` calls measured 2.0 to 2.4 s at steady state on both shapes; a few calls came back much faster (0.6 to 0.8 s, cause not established, possibly JIT and GC state), so the parts, not those calls, are the numbers to plan with. - **Decision on the revision-gap open item (replaces it).** A refused gap sets status `failed` with its own reason, `'revision_gap'`. Recovery is a user-visible "save everything now": `replaceAll` of the in-memory state in one transaction through the queue's ordering, after which the queue continues from that revision. Built in 3.3 (API only; the button is Phase 5).

### Step 3.3 — Boot sequence (2026-09-28)

**Delivered** (`src/persistence`, every new source file under 190 lines)
- `boot.ts` (`runBoot`, run once by `adapter.open()`), `boot-load.ts` (per-record L1 → state, quarantine rows, raw cap),
  `boot-migrate.ts` (the older-schema path), `boot-checks.ts` (open-failure mapping, tolerant uiState, bounded `isJsonValue`),
  `boot-ports.ts` (what boot needs from the adapter). Boot takes an injected `WriterLock` (`lock.ts`: `alwaysGrantedLock`
  until 3.4) and an injected `MigrationRunner`. The order and every branch are in PERSISTENCE.md §4 (rewritten).
- `aux-stores.ts`: quarantine, import batches, editor sessions, uiState, asset-blob IDs, meta markers; every write through
  `runOrdered`. `replace.ts`: `writeAllRecords` (`replaceAll`) and `writeRawDocument` (rollback), each one transaction.
- `write-queue.ts`: `setReadOnly` (boot decides after the queue exists; 3.4 uses it for promotion), `pendingRevision`, `resync`,
  and the `revision_gap` failure reason. `adapter.ts` now implements the whole `PersistenceAdapter` except `storageEstimate` and
  `requestPersistentStorage` (Phase 5/9 wiring), plus `setReadOnly`.
- `snapshot-codec.ts`: `buildRawSnapshot` (the `pre_migration` snapshot of data in an OLD shape, which cannot pass the strict
  schema); `decodeSnapshot` now only requires the version to be a number (see Decisions). `snapshot-store.ts`: `createRawSnapshot`.
- Test support: `version-offset.ts` (Q10, with its own self-test), `synthetic-migration.ts` (synthetic 0 → 1 step, canonical JSON),
  `persistence-kit.ts` gains `stubLock` and `savedDatabase`. Frozen fixtures: `tests/fixtures/migrations/synthetic-v0.orn.json` and
  `expected/synthetic-v1.canonical.json` (generated once from `minimal-valid`, committed, never edited).

**Contract amendments applied** (CLAUDE.md §7; Amendments entry below)
- Approved (Q1 a, b, e; Q4; Q10): `addQuarantine`, `listQuarantine`, `deleteQuarantine`; `ImportBatchRow`, `saveImportBatch`,
  `listImportBatches`; `listAssetBlobIds`; `OpenResult` gains `readOnlyReason: 'migration_failed'`, `detail` and `bootReport`
  (`BootReport`, no `singleTab`: that is 3.4); `withVersionOffset` in `src/test-support` with its self-test.
- Needed by the step and recorded here for confirmation: (1) `setLastCleanShutdownAt(at: string | null)`: `null` marks a session as
  running (a timestamp alone cannot say "this session has not ended"); (2) `SaveStatus.failureReason?: 'revision_gap'` (the step 3.2
  review decision); (3) `addQuarantine` returns the IDs that were new (not a count), so boot can report how many of them were
  truncated; (4) `QuarantineRow.rawTruncated?: { originalChars }` and `QUARANTINE_RAW_MAX_CHARS` (64 Ki characters, the LIMITS
  convention), because "truncation reported" needs a place to be reported; (5) `replaceAll`'s semantics (queue order, resync,
  refusal of a stale state) written into its doc comment.
- No schema change, no `DB_STORES`/`DB_VERSION` change, no `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump, no migration, no fixture change.

**Decisions**
- *Migration order.* The runner is the arbiter of stored versions. Boot builds the tolerant document from the stored rows and runs
  the migration IN MEMORY first (pure, writes nothing). A version the runner refuses outright (INVALID_VERSION, NEWER_VERSION) is
  reported with nothing written and no snapshot. Otherwise the `pre_migration` snapshot is written (raw, exactly the stored rows)
  BEFORE the first write to `records`, then strict parse → L1–L3 → `replaceAll`. Any failure after the snapshot restores the stored rows
  from it (today a no-op in effect, because `replaceAll` is one atomic transaction; it stays correct if that ever changes, and a
  failing restore is reported next to the original error). The plan's order is "snapshot → migrate"; the difference is that the pure
  migration runs first so a refused version leaves no mandatory snapshot behind.
- *A migration ends at `revision + 1`*: the migrated state is a different state, so observers (followers, 3.4) see a new revision.
- *Quarantine is a copy, not a move.* The offending row stays in `records` (PERSISTENCE.md §4 said "move"; the reviewer's brief says
  nothing is deleted, and a raw copy capped at 64 Ki characters would lose the rest). The quarantine ID is derived from the row's ID,
  so a row that is still bad at the next boot is not quarantined again. Rows that never load stay in `records` until Diagnostics
  (Phase 9) offers repair or removal.
- *Crash check* uses `lastCleanShutdownAt`: `null` while a session runs (boot writes it after reading the previous value), a time
  after a clean shutdown. A `null` or unreadable value at boot is a suspected crash, and only then are editor-session IDs offered.
  A database from before the marker existed (no row) is not treated as a crash.
- *A tab without the lock* loads the state (current schema) and writes nothing: no quarantine rows, no markers, no uiState resets
  (it reports them). If the stored data needs a migration it opens read-only without data (`state: null`); following changes and
  promotion are 3.4.
- *Newer schema* releases the lock it took, so a newer app in another tab can become the writer. A non-integer version above the
  target (1.5) is INVALID_VERSION (the runner checks integrality first), not "newer".
- *Boot returns the L1–L3 findings* in the `bootReport`; the app can hand them to the store (`validateInitial: false` plus
  `commitFindings`). The store still builds its own indexes (about 0.4 to 0.5 s at the design tier): a Phase 4 item (workers).
- *`decodeSnapshot` is tolerant about the version* (a number; whether the runner can use it is the runner's call). Before, a
  snapshot with `schemaVersion` 0 read as CORRUPT; now restore reports the runner's own reason. Needed so a `pre_migration` snapshot of a
  synthetic version 0 can be restored on rollback; a real database never holds a version below 1.
- *The write queue continues from the loaded revision*: boot calls `resync` inside an ordered task, so the gap guard is armed from
  the first ChangeSet. A gap is recovered with `replaceAll(state, revision)` ("save everything now", API only).
- *Editor sessions and uiState writes go through the queue too* (SONNET_HANDOFF §18.8); a read-only tab's calls reject.
- P-03 framework form and the migrations-framework evidence: see Tests.

**Tests** (`npm run check`: 91 files, 1,414 tests; before this step 85 files, 1,337)
- `version-offset.test.ts` (9): versions, the steps' own view and reports map back; INVALID_VERSION, NEWER_VERSION, MISSING_STEP,
  STEP_FAILED, STEP_CONTRACT pass through; offset 0 equals the real runner; input not mutated.
- `migrations-framework.test.ts` (6): P-03 framework form: the frozen v0 fixture is refused by the real runner alone; output is byte-equal
  to the committed expected file, identical across runs and input key orders, input untouched, nothing dropped (INV-23: every input
  leaf is in the output or in `provenance.legacy.unmapped`), strict-schema valid and equal to the minimal fixture apart from the moved field.
- `persistence-boot.test.ts` (24): fresh and current databases, the queue continuing from the stored revision, damaged meta (4 kinds), INV-23
  quarantine (state without the bad rows, issues, raw values, rows not removed, truncation reported, no duplicates at the next boot, a
  read-only tab writes nothing), the writer lock and its release, AST-002, the crash check, tolerant uiState, IndexedDB unavailable (4 reasons).
- `persistence-boot-migration.test.ts` (18): ADV-041 (disk part): newer schema, and every version the real runner refuses, read-only with nothing
  written and no snapshot; ADV-045 (migration part): the migrated database, its `pre_migration` snapshot, a store from the boot result with no
  undo or redo, the queue continuing, a second boot finding it current, a tab without the lock; six failure paths (throwing step, strict
  schema, L2 error, write fails once, write and restore both fail, snapshot cannot be written), each ending read-only with the stored rows as before.
- `persistence-recovery.test.ts` (14): `replaceAll` (one transaction, blobs and snapshots untouched, queue order, stale refusal, failure, read-only),
  revision-gap recovery end to end, Q1 (a), (b), (e), editor sessions, uiState, the `null` marker. `write-queue-resync.test.ts` (5).
- Hand-checked mutations: deleting quarantined rows, dropping the rollback, and not setting read-only after a refusal each fail tests.
- Coverage of `src/persistence` (focused run): 99.4% lines, 88.9% branches; `test:coverage` thresholds met.

**replaceState split** was recorded in the step 3.2 review (no change here).

**Benchmark** (`tests/perf/boot.bench.ts`, run once and alone; `bench:baseline` NOT run; design tier, dense; fake-indexeddb; SLOW options,
5 to 9 samples; mean / max in ms; informal):
- bulk read from fake-indexeddb (`readCommitted`; not our code): 383 / 408
- per-record L1 + state (`assembleState`, our code): 371 / 426
- index build (`coreIndexEngine.build`): 449 / 584

The parts sum to about 1.98 s; the rest of `open()` (about 0.5 s) is opening a new database handle, the asset and uiState checks and the

### Step 3.3 review (reviewer, 2026-09-28)

Verdict: step 3.3 ACCEPTED with one reversal.

- **REVERSED: boot quarantine is a MOVE, not a copy.** A row failing L1 at boot is removed from `records` and written to `quarantine` with its FULL raw value (no cap) in ONE transaction, through `runOrdered`. Reason: `records` must always equal the in-memory state; otherwise `replaceAll`, a restore or a recovery silently deletes the kept row later. The 64 KB cap applies only to display and to import quarantine (3.6, where the source file still exists); `rawTruncated` stays false for boot rows. The quarantine ID stays derived from the row ID. Supersedes the step 3.3 decision "Quarantine is a copy, not a move" and the PERSISTENCE.md §4/§7 wording that went with it.
- **Approved as reported:** `setLastCleanShutdownAt(null)` = session running; `addQuarantine` returns IDs; `QuarantineRow.rawTruncated`; the migration runs in memory before the `pre_migration` snapshot; `decodeSnapshot` accepts any numeric `schemaVersion`; a lockless tab needing migration opens read-only with `state: null` (it reloads when the writer's change arrives, 3.4).
- **Confirm or add:** a test that the `pre_migration` snapshot holds the ORIGINAL stored rows at the OLD `schemaVersion` (not the migrated document), and that a failed migration restores exactly them.
- **Phase 4 note:** the precomputed-findings note is extended: the store must also accept prebuilt indexes, so boot does not build indexes twice (about 0.45 s at the design tier).
- **Timing tests out of `npm run check`** (determinism, TEST_STRATEGY §1): wall-clock budget assertions move into `npm run test:timing`, which `npm run check` and `npm run test:coverage` do not run; it runs at gates next to `bench:baseline`.

Follow-ups done in the review commit ("phase-3: step 3.3 review follow-ups"):

- **Boot quarantine is a move.** `assembleState` returns `rejected: RejectedRow[]` (`{ recordKey, quarantine }`); `boot.ts` calls `ports.moveToQuarantine(rejected)` (replaces `addQuarantine` in `BootPorts`; the adapter's public `addQuarantine` stays, it is for imports). `moveToQuarantine` (`aux-stores.ts`) is one `rw` transaction over `records` + `quarantine` inside `runOrdered`: `bulkPut` the quarantine rows, `bulkDelete` the record rows. The raw value is the row exactly as stored, not a JSON round trip (IndexedDB keeps more than JSON: a Date, NaN); when it is not plain JSON the issues say so. `capRaw` is gone (no caller: the import cap comes back with 3.6). A quarantine row already stored under the derived ID (a copy from a pre-review boot) is kept when its raw value is the same, replaced by the full value when it is a truncated copy, and a different value is stored under its own ID (hash of the value appended): nothing stored is overwritten with less. A tab without the lock leaves bad rows out of its state and writes and moves nothing. `BootReport.quarantineTruncated` is removed (always 0 now); `newlyQuarantined` counts the rows moved. Docs: PERSISTENCE.md §4 step 4, §4 OpenResult text, §7; Amendments entry below.
  Tests (`persistence-boot.test.ts`, the INV-23 block rewritten, 10 tests): bad rows gone from `records` and `records` equal the state; full raw values with issues; a 100 kB row kept whole and no row truncated; a Date/NaN row kept as stored with the note; ONE transaction over `records` + `quarantine` (transaction log and mutation log: one quarantine write, one records delete); a fault on `records` leaves both stores as they were (a crash between is impossible) and the next boot completes the move; a reboot does not quarantine again and the rows are unchanged; a later `replaceAll` leaves the quarantine rows; a truncated leftover is replaced by the full value; a different value under the same derived ID is kept beside the new one; a read-only tab moves nothing.
- **Migration snapshot tests** (`persistence-boot-migration.test.ts`, +2, one strengthened): the `pre_migration` snapshot's rows deep-equal the ORIGINAL stored rows (sorted by ID), `schemaVersion` 0, old-shape meta (`name`, not `title`), revision 4, while the live rows are the migrated ones; `writeRawDocument` (the rollback function) from that snapshot puts back exactly the original rows, `schemaVersion`, meta and revision over the migrated data; the failed-step test now also compares the restored rows with the snapshot's rows and the original rows. The existing failure-path tests (six) already compared rows before and after.
- **`npm run test:timing`.** New `vitest.timing.config.ts` (includes only `tests/timing/**`, serial); `vitest.config.ts` excludes `tests/timing/**`, so `npm test`, `npm run check` and `npm run test:coverage` never run them. Moved: `tests/adversarial/gate-b-performance.test.ts` → `tests/timing/gate-b-performance.test.ts` (git mv, unchanged; ADV-012 performance part and ADV-013), and three other wall-clock assertions found by searching for `performance.now`/`Date.now`/elapsed in all tests, now in `tests/timing/core-bounded-work.test.ts` with their original titles and assertions: the INV-22 (Phase 1 part) `parseRichText` bounded-work cases (5), the Base64 "enforces the cap and stays linear on a maximum-size payload" test, and the INV-22 (Phase 1 part) 5,000-deep hierarchy chain. Their functional parts stay in `check` under titles without a time claim (richtext: "terminates with a non-empty tree"; primitives: "enforces the cap on a maximum-size payload"; hierarchy: "walks a 5,000-deep chain without recursion"), so nothing functional left `check`. The files are still `*.test.ts` under `tests/`, so the gate-matrix title scan finds the INV/ADV IDs. The step 2.8/2.10 entries and `docs/reviews/gate-b-packet.md` still name the old path (history, not edited).

The other property files keep `TIMEOUT_MS` (30 s, or `ORN_PROPERTY_RUNS` seconds).

### Step 3.4 — Writer lock, change channel, follower mode, promotion (2026-09-28)

**Delivered**
- `src/core/commands` (ADR-033): `store.applyRemote(change: RemoteChange)` and `store.reload(state, revision)`, both for a READ-ONLY store only (`NOT_FOLLOWER` as a value on a writable one); `remote.ts` (`planRemote`: the mirrored state as Immer patches). `applyRemote`: needs `fromRevision` = current revision (`STALE_REVISION` otherwise), incremental indexes, `validateTouchedByRule` WITH the previous state, no ERROR gate, no history entry, ChangeSet `cause: 'remote'`. `reload`: adopts the state at the revision storage holds (not +1), rebuilds indexes, `validateAll`, clears history, no gate. `ChangeSet.cause` gains `'remote'`; the core index engine accepts a remote ChangeSet as a forward span (the writer's transaction may cover several revisions), every other cause keeps the exact +1 guard.
- `src/persistence`: ports `WriterLock` (`acquire` → acquired / held_elsewhere / unsupported, `whenAcquired`, `release`), `ChangeChannel { post, subscribe, close }`, `ChangeMessage { fromRevision, revision, changedIds }`; `web-locks.ts` (`createWebLocksWriterLock(locks)`, `createBroadcastChangeChannel(factory)`: the browser objects are handed in, nothing reads a global); `change-message.ts` (`parseChangeMessage` validates and bounds what another tab sends; `changeMessageFor`); the adapter posts after each persisted transaction (`announce`), `replaceAll` posts `'all'`; `follower.ts` (the controller), `follower-read.ts` (`interpretCommitted`: a follower's full read, L1 per row, rejects reported and never quarantined), `adapter.readChanged / loadForFollower / promote`; `write-wiring.ts` (`attachWriteQueue`); `boot.ts` takes `{ promotion }` (the same sequence again as the writer) and reports `bootReport.singleTab`.
- Test support: `tab-fakes.ts` (in-memory exclusive lock hub, broadcast hub with deliver / drop / duplicate / inject), `tab-kit.ts` (a tab: adapter + lock + channel + store + follower + wiring, as the app will wire it), `tab-rig.ts` (fake and real rigs, bounded `settle`).

**Contract amendments applied** (CLAUDE.md §7; Amendments entry below; ADR-033 written)
- Approved and landed: Q3 (`WriterLock.whenAcquired`, acquire result `'unsupported'`, `ChangeChannel`, `ChangeMessage` with `fromRevision`), Q4 `bootReport.singleTab`, Q11 as ADR-033 with all its guards.
- Needed by the step, recorded for confirmation (see REVIEW REQUIRED): `ChangeMessage.changedIds` may be `'all'`; `ChangeChannel.subscribe` hands the listener `unknown`; `WriterLock.release()` resolves when the lock is really free; `CommandErrorCode 'NOT_FOLLOWER'`; `ChangeSet.cause 'remote'` with the index engine's forward span; `RemoteChange`; the fresh-database boot result now carries a `bootReport`; `attachWriteQueue`; `PersistenceEvent 'channel_error'`.
- No schema change, no `DB_STORES`/`DB_VERSION` change, no `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump, no migration, no fixture change.

**Decisions**
- *Message semantics.* `fromRevision` is the revision the database was at before the transaction, tracked by the adapter (boot's `continueFrom`, every persisted transaction, `replaceAll`); with no boot it is `revision − count`. A coalesced transaction is ONE message over the whole span. Nothing is posted before the transaction is durable or when it fails; a failed cycle's retry posts once with the same span; a write that ends `failed` posts nothing and the next success names the whole gap. A channel that throws is a `channel_error` event, never a failed write.
- *`'all'`.* `replaceAll` (boot migration, "save everything now") rewrites everything, so a list of IDs would be wrong for a follower at the old revision (recovery re-saves at a revision the follower may not have). Also used beyond 2,000 changed IDs, so a message and the follower's read stay bounded. A follower reloads fully on `'all'` (unless the message is older than its revision).
- *Follower rules.* Strictly in order, one at a time; a message whose `revision` is not newer is ignored (the follower may already have read past it); `fromRevision` ≠ current → full reload; the read is ONE transaction over the rows, `meta.revision`, `meta.schemaVersion` and `datasetMeta`; a database at another revision or schema version → full reload (never a wrong apply); a row failing L1 or a meta failing its schema → reported, full reload; `applyRemote` `STALE_REVISION` / `INTERNAL` → full reload; `NOT_FOLLOWER` (a misconfigured store) → `apply_refused`, nothing applied. A full reload leaves rows that fail L1 out of the state and reports them (no loop: it happens once). Backlog beyond 100 messages → one full reload. Malformed messages are ignored and reported (a junk flood cannot cause reloads); the next real message heals a follower that missed one.
- *Promotion.* `whenAcquired` resolves → the queue becomes writable and boot runs again as the writer (migration if needed, quarantine MOVE, markers, crash check: the old writer's editor sessions are offered when it did not shut down cleanly) → the store reloads from that result while still read-only → the tab becomes writable → the app attaches the write queue. A refused boot (newer schema, failed migration, damaged meta) gives the lock back and leaves the tab read-only and following, with `promotion_failed`. A store handed a state of nothing, or refusing the state, also fails the promotion (queue read-only again, lock released). Read-only becomes switchable only through promotion (`adapter.promote`).
- *No Web Locks.* `acquire()` answers `'unsupported'` when there is no `LockManager` or `request` throws: the tab runs as the writer, `bootReport.singleTab` is true (also for a fresh database, whose boot result now carries a `bootReport`).
- *A lockless tab that needs a migration* opens read-only with `state: null` (3.3); when the writer's migration posts its `'all'` message the follower loads (a `loaded` event, the app builds the store and `attach`es it).
- *`release()` waits until the lock is really free.* Real Web Locks free the lock a moment after the holder's callback settles; a `release()` that resolved earlier let the same tab's next `acquire()` see `held_elsewhere` (found by the real-`navigator.locks` test).
- *ERROR gate.* None on a mirror (the fit scan's wording): the writer accepted the state and it is durable; errors become findings in the follower's finding store, and `applyRemote`/`reload` return them.

**Tests** (`npm run check`: 100 files, 1,533 tests; before this step 90 files, 1,419 after Part A)
- `src/core/commands/remote.test.ts` (15): both refused as a value on a writable store (state, revision, indexes, findings, subscribers untouched; the guard follows the read-only flag); one commit and a coalesced span (create, update, add-then-drop, meta) mirrored with indexes, findings and revision equal to a fresh build; harmless deletes of unknown IDs; `STALE_REVISION` cases; no ERROR gate; an ID that changed kind is moved; the index engine takes a remote span incrementally (previous handle goes stale) and a non-remote gap is still rebuilt; `reload` (any revision, history cleared, bad state and bad revision refused, no gate); promotion order.
- `tests/property/follower.test.ts` (2): after random command sequences (undo and redo included) applied to a follower as random coalesced spans, and with random full reloads, the follower's state, revision, indexes and findings equal the writer's, and it never gains history.
- `tests/integration/persistence-follower.test.ts` (15): ADV-039 integration part: the main scenario (follows, cannot write, is promoted on close, writes from the adopted revision) on in-memory fakes AND on the real `navigator.locks` and `BroadcastChannel`; a burst is one transaction, one message, one apply; creates/deletes/meta; a dropped message (gap reload); duplicate, older and moved-database messages; malformed messages (10 kinds); a stored row failing L1 (reported, full reload, follower wrote and quarantined nothing, the row untouched); `replaceAll` → reload; backlog; promotion (undelivered changes, reload before writable, boot as the writer moves a bad row to quarantine and reports the crashed old writer, refused promotion, a lockless tab that needed a migration).
- `tests/integration/persistence-follower-guards.test.ts` (7): INV-28: attaching a write queue to a follower is refused; a remote ChangeSet is never written even when wired; a follower handed a writable store applies nothing; one lock holder at any time (three tabs); no Web Locks → single writer with `singleTab` (existing and fresh database); a granted lock is not a warning.
- `tests/integration/persistence-change-messages.test.ts` (10): message per transaction, the coalesced span, nothing before durable or on failure, retry, the gap after a failed write, `'all'` beyond the bound and for `replaceAll`, a throwing channel, no channel, read-only tab, an unbooted adapter.
- `src/persistence`: `follower.test.ts` (20: reload failures, store refusals, promotion failure modes, close), `follower-read.test.ts` (10), `write-wiring.test.ts` (5), `change-message.test.ts` (20), `web-locks.test.ts` (10: fake LockManager, real `navigator.locks` promotion and abandoned wait, fake and real BroadcastChannel).
- Hand-checked mutations (all caught by a named test): applyRemote or reload allowed on a writable store; `fromRevision` not checked; remote ChangeSets written; wiring attached to a read-only store; the writer never announces; `replaceAll` does not announce; a gap ignored; rows failing L1 applied; the database revision not checked; the tab writable before the store reloads; promotion skipping the queue; the single-tab flag dropped; malformed messages trusted. One mutation survived at first (the index engine's remote span: the fallback rebuild gives the same answers), so a test now pins the incremental path.
- Coverage of `src/persistence` (focused run): 99.2% lines, 90.7% branches; `follower.ts` 100% lines. `npm run test:coverage` (core + persistence) passed on its first run: 1,533 tests, 98.9% lines, 91.6% branches, no timing failure (the wall-clock tests are no longer in it, step 3.3 review). `npm run check` passed on the final code three times (the runs before it failed only on lint, file size and a typing error I fixed; no test failed on timing).

**Benchmark** (`tests/perf/follower.bench.ts`, run once and alone; `bench:baseline` NOT run, six entries added to `budgets.mjs` with no budget; design tier, `hub_explosion`; mean / p99 in ms; informal). The fake-indexeddb read is reported apart from our own work:
- read the changed rows on fake-indexeddb (not our code): ordinary edit (1 row) 0.461 / 1.384; hub edit (~1,000 rows) 78.3 / 123.0
- L1 parse of those rows (our code): ordinary 0.0074 / 0.0175; hub 18.96 / 30.02
- `applyRemote` (our code: patches, incremental indexes, `validateTouchedByRule` with the previous state): ordinary 1.246 / 2.390; hub edit (~1,000 records) 394.9 / 439.7 (8 samples)
- So one message costs, of our own work, about 1.3 ms for an ordinary edit and about 414 ms for the hub edit (`replaceReferences` on the 1,000-reaction hub, as in `persist.bench.ts`). For context, measured once alone with a throwaway bench (not committed): the WRITER's own commit of that same edit costs about 480 ms each way (dispatch + undo 957 ms mean, 5 samples), so the follower pays about what the writer paid. Main thread until Phase 4.

**Open items**
- Phase 5 wiring (not built, no UI): create the store with `readOnly: follower.isReadOnly` and `follower.attach` it; `attachWriteQueue` after a writer boot and after `promoted`; the read-only and single-tab banners; handling of `loaded`, `promotion_failed`, `reload_failed`, `row_rejected`. The real-browser (Playwright) halves of ADV-039 are Gate E.
- A tab whose promotion was refused does not ask for the lock again (boot gave it back), so it is not promoted when the next writer closes. Decide in Phase 5 (a retry, or "reload to retry").
- Phase 4: `reload` and the follower's reads go to the compute worker; `reload` takes precomputed findings and prebuilt indexes (already in the Phase 4 notes); the hub-edit apply (about 400 ms) is a Phase 4/9 candidate.
- Constants chosen here (reviewer to confirm): 2,000 IDs per message, 512 characters per ID, follower backlog 100, 20 reported rejected rows per reload.
- `src/core/commands/store.ts` is 342 lines (limit 400, warning above 300).

**REVIEW REQUIRED**
1. `ChangeMessage.changedIds` is `readonly string[] | 'all'` (the approved shape had only the list): `'all'` for `replaceAll` and beyond 2,000 IDs. Without it a recovery `replaceAll` cannot be told to followers.
2. `ChangeChannel.subscribe` hands the listener `unknown` (validated by `parseChangeMessage`), not `ChangeMessage`: another tab is not trusted.
3. `applyRemote` and `reload` have no ERROR gate (as the Q11 fit scan said); `reload` and `promote` can therefore adopt a durable state that has errors, and report them as findings.
4. Promotion order: the write QUEUE becomes writable first (boot's markers, quarantine move and migration go through it), then boot runs, then the STORE reloads while still read-only and has no write queue attached, then the tab becomes writable. This is how I read "promotion = reload, then writable".
5. `WriterLock.release()` resolves when the lock is really free; `acquire()` answers `'unsupported'` also when `request` throws.
6. The fresh-database boot result now carries a `bootReport` (all empty, `singleTab` set), and `BootReport.singleTab` is a required boolean.
7. New public helper `attachWriteQueue` (guards 2 and 3) and `PersistenceEvent 'channel_error'`.

### Step 3.4 review (reviewer, 2026-09-29)

Verdict: step 3.4 ACCEPTED. All seven REVIEW REQUIRED items above are approved as reported: `changedIds` may be
`'all'`; `ChangeChannel.subscribe` hands out `unknown`, validated by `parseChangeMessage`; no ERROR gate on
`applyRemote`/`reload`; the promotion order (queue writable → boot as writer → store reload while read-only →
writable); `release()` waits for the lock to be really free and `acquire()` answers `'unsupported'` when `request`
throws; `bootReport` is always present with a required `singleTab`; `attachWriteQueue` and the `channel_error` event.
Refused-promotion retry (open item) stays a Phase 5 decision.

Follow-ups done in the review commit ("phase-3: step 3.4 review follow-ups"):

1. **Promotion window.** No existing test asserted that a command dispatched on the store is refused while the write
   queue is already writable (inside the gated `adapter.promote()`) but the store has not yet reloaded — the closest
   existing coverage (`tests/integration/persistence-follower.test.ts`, "the store reloads while still read-only, and
   only then does the tab become writable") only checks a `'remote'`-cause reload notification, not an attempted
   writer-style commit. Added: `tests/integration/persistence-follower.test.ts`, "while boot runs the write queue
   writable again, the store still refuses to commit, until reload finishes (PERSISTENCE.md §10 promotion order)" —
   gates `adapter.promote()` behind a manually-released Promise so the test can dispatch on the store while boot is
   provably still pending, confirms `READ_ONLY`, then releases the gate and confirms the tab becomes writable and can
   commit. No code change: the guard was already correct (`follower.ts`'s `readOnly` local variable, read live by the
   store's injected `readOnly: follower.isReadOnly` on every `dispatch`, flips to `false` only after `store.reload`
   returns inside `promote()`); only the gap in test coverage is closed.
2. **Capped lists report their true total (fixed a real gap).** Checked all four approved constants (2,000 changed
   IDs per message, 512 characters per ID, follower backlog 100, 20 reported rejected rows) against "wherever a list
   is capped, the total count is still reported": the first two are validity/mode-switch bounds (an over-long message
   becomes `'all'`; an over-long ID makes the whole message malformed), not a partial-keep-of-a-list, so nothing to
   report a count for. The backlog-100 overflow already discards the queue and reloads fully (`reason: 'backlog'`),
   not a partial keep. The 20-reported-rejected-ROWS cap in `follower.ts`'s `reload()` already paired the shown list
   with `rejectedRows: loaded.rejected.length` (the true total). But `handle()`'s single-message apply path capped
   the ISSUES of the one rejected row to the same 20 by a bare `.slice(0, MAX_REPORTED_REJECTS)`, with no trailer and
   no total — silently dropping issues beyond the 20th for a badly malformed row (CLAUDE.md §1.5). Fixed: extracted
   the cap-with-trailer logic `quarantineRow` (boot/import quarantine) already used ("… and N more issue(s)") into a
   shared `capIssues` (`src/persistence/boot-load.ts`), used by both `quarantineRow` and `follower.ts`'s
   `row_rejected` emission, so a follower's issue report caps exactly the way quarantine already does. Test:
   `src/persistence/follower.test.ts`, "a row that fails L1 with more than 20 issues reports all of them capped, with
   the true total named" (a row with 25 wrong-typed array entries; confirms exactly 21 issues reported, the last one
   matching `"… and N more issue(s)"`).
3. **Structural hub edit: writer-side benchmark added.** `tests/perf/follower.bench.ts` gains a describe block
   ("commit — writer side (design tier, hub_explosion): replaceReferences on the 1,000-reaction hub") next to the
   `applyRemote` hub-edit entry: `hub` and `other` swap roles each sample via real `store.dispatch` calls (`from`'s
   record is never deleted by `replaceReferences`, only its referrers rewritten, so the same store can be dispatched
   to indefinitely — no store pool needed). `tests/perf/budgets.mjs` gains the matching entry (`budgetMs: null`,
   informal, tier `design`/`hub_explosion`). Run once alone (`npx vitest bench run tests/perf/follower.bench.ts
   --no-file-parallelism`; `bench:baseline` NOT run): **699.7 ms mean / 717.0 ms p99 (5 samples)**. The same run's
   `applyRemote` hub-edit entry (unchanged code) read 636.1/667.8 ms mean/p99 in this run, against 394.9/439.7 ms
   committed in step 3.4 — workstation noise (PERFORMANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md §9 documents spreads over 100% on this
   machine for slow entries), not a regression; the step 3.4 entry is left as committed. Recorded in
   `docs/PROJECT_STATE.json` openItems as a Phase 9 exception candidate, numbers to be confirmed in the Gate C
   packet.

Tests after this review: `npm run check` — 100 files, 1,535 tests (2 more than step 3.4's 1,533: the promotion-window
integration test and the capped-issues unit test).

### Step 3.5 — Export: `.orn.json` and `.ornpkg`, scopes, dependency closure (2026-09-29)

**Delivered** (`src/io`, every new file under 145 lines)
- `contracts.ts` (additive): `AssetBytesProvider(assetId, sha256) => Promise<Uint8Array | undefined>`; `MissingReference`;
  `ExportResult { bytes, counts, missingAssets, missingReferences }`; `ExportBuildResult` (`ExportErrorCode` `'CANCELLED'
  | 'SERIALIZE_FAILED'`); `TaskOptions { signal?, onProgress? }`.
- `export-scope.ts`: `selectScope(state, options)` — the reference closure behind every scope, driven entirely by
  `REFERENCE_SPECS` through `ReferenceEngine.extract` (never a per-kind/per-field special case). `full` is everything;
  `chemistry` is layers 1-2 whole plus a REVERSE pass pulling in any asset that attaches to something in it (assets
  forward-reference what they attach to, never the other way, so a forward closure from chemistry alone finds nothing
  new, INV-29); `curriculum` and `presentation` are their own layer whole plus a forward closure of whatever they
  reference; `subset` is exactly the given records plus a forward closure when `includeDependencies`, otherwise every
  reference leaving the subset is reported in `missingReferences` (never dropped or silently kept).
- `export-document.ts`: `buildScopedDocument` — `selectScope`'s included IDs applied to the state, reusing the
  existing deterministic `stateToDocument`/`serializeDocument` (records sorted by ID, keys in schema order, INV-19).
- `export-json.ts`: `buildOrnJsonExport` — the scoped document, `assets: 'embed'` adds base64 `embeddedAssets`
  (chunked `btoa`, no call-stack overflow on the 20 MB asset cap), a missing provider result is reported and never
  thrown, an aborted signal returns a value with no partial bytes at two points (before/after the asset loop),
  progress once per embedded asset plus once for the final serialise.
- `export-package.ts`: `buildOrnPackageExport` — fflate zip: `manifest.json` (`{ format: 'orn.package', schemaVersion,
  files: [{ path, sha256 }] }`, `dataset.json`'s own SHA-256 via `crypto.subtle.digest` plus one entry per asset file),
  `dataset.json`, `assets/<sha256>.<ext>` (written when `assets` is `'embed'` or `'package'`). Determinism (INV-19):
  every zip entry carries one FIXED modification time — the ZIP/MS-DOS format's own epoch (1980-01-01; Unix's 1970
  epoch is rejected by fflate's DOS-date range check) — and a fixed entry order (manifest, dataset, assets sorted by
  sha256), so the same state and clock give byte-identical bytes on every run. Same abort/progress/missing-asset
  handling as the JSON builder.
- `index.ts`: barrel.
- `vitest.config.ts`, `package.json`: `src/io` added to the coverage include list and thresholds (80% lines, matching
  `src/persistence`).

**Contract amendments applied** (CLAUDE.md §7; approved list from "Phase 3 contract decisions"; entry under Amendments)
- Q7: `AssetBytesProvider` and `ExportResult` as approved (a function port beside `ExportOptions`, never inside them,
  so options stay structured-clone-safe).
- Q9 (the part step 3.5 needs): `AssetBytesProvider` lives in io, no persistence import; `SnapshotPort`/`ImportBatchSink`
  are not needed by export and stay for 3.6/3.7.
- Q12 (the part step 3.5 needs): `TaskOptions { signal?, onProgress? }` added to `src/io/contracts.ts`; a type-level
  test (`tests/types/task-options.test.ts`) asserts `RpcCallContext` (from `src/workers/rpc/contracts.ts`, not
  imported by `src/io` itself) is assignable to `TaskOptions` — see "Reading beyond the approved list" below for why
  this is the direction that actually holds. The persistence-side half of Q12 (snapshot build, boot) and the pending
  import-batch shape stay for 3.6/3.7.
- No schema change, no `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump, no migration, no fixture change.

**Reading beyond the approved list, both flagged REVIEW REQUIRED**
1. *Scope closures.* PERSISTENCE.md §8 named the layers for `chemistry`/`curriculum` but left the exact closure
   algorithm and `presentation`'s composition unstated ("Where §8 leaves a scope's closure ambiguous ... choose the
   reading that produces a file which imports cleanly ... and list it as REVIEW REQUIRED" — IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §2
   step 3.5). Chosen reading, applied uniformly and always on for the four named scopes (never gated by any option,
   since `ExportOptions` has none for this): every named scope's output must have no dangling reference, so
   `curriculum`'s "with the chemistry records they map, optional" is read as "always included" and `presentation`
   (unstated in §8) gets the same forward-closure treatment. PERSISTENCE.md §8 updated in this commit with the exact
   algorithm; `src/io/export-scope.ts`'s doc comment states it too.
2. *`TaskOptions`/`RpcCallContext` assignability direction.* Q12's approved text says `TaskOptions` is "structurally
   identical" to `RpcCallContext` "minus the required-ness"; the master prompt for this step asked for a type-level
   test that "TaskOptions stays assignable to RpcCallContext". Taken literally that fails to typecheck and would be
   pointless if it did: `RpcCallContext.signal`/`.progress` are required and `TaskOptions` has no `progress` property
   at all (it has `onProgress`, a different name), so no `TaskOptions` value can ever satisfy `RpcCallContext`. The
   direction that is both true and useful is the reverse — `RpcCallContext extends TaskOptions` — since it lets a
   Phase 4 compute-worker method, which already holds a real `RpcCallContext`, pass it straight through to one of
   these pure functions without adapting it (works because `TaskOptions`'s fields are all optional: any richer object
   with a compatible `signal` satisfies it regardless of its other properties). `tests/types/task-options.test.ts`
   asserts that direction; the literal reading is noted here for the reviewer instead of silently substituted.

**Tests** (`npm run check`: 105 files, 1,573 tests; before this step 100 files, 1,535 tests)

### Step 3.6 — Import part 1: caps, safe parser, format detection, `.ornpkg` unpack, migration hook, tolerant envelope, per-record L1 quarantine (2026-09-29)

**Delivered** (`src/io`, every new file under 175 lines)
- `json-caps.ts`: `scanJsonCaps` — a single linear, string/escape-aware pass over the RAW TEXT (no recursion,
  no parse tree) tracking nesting depth and each string literal's length, stopping at the first violation.
  Runs BEFORE `JSON.parse`, so a hostile 60 MB / depth-100 / 5 MB-string file is rejected without ever
  touching the call stack or building an object graph (ADV-032 io part).
- `safe-json.ts`: `parseJsonSafely` (the scan, then `JSON.parse`, then sanitise) and `sanitizeUnsafeKeys` — an
  iterative-in-spirit, depth-bounded rewrite (recursion is safe here specifically because the scan already
  proved depth ≤ 64, CLAUDE.md's non-negotiable is UNBOUNDED recursion) that renames `__proto__`/
  `constructor`/`prototype` at ANY depth to `__unsafe_key__…`, disambiguated against any key already at that
  name (never a silent overwrite either way — see the bug this caught, below) and reported (`path` +
  original key). `JSON.parse` itself is already safe (`CreateDataProperty`, never `[[Set]]`, so it never
  touches the prototype chain even for a literal `"__proto__"` key); the danger is entirely in code
  downstream that might later do `obj[key] = value` with an untrusted key — sanitizing here means nothing
  downstream ever sees one of the three names again.
- `format-detect.ts`: `looksLikeZip` (byte-level `PK` magic-number sniff, container detection one level above
  format detection) and `detectFormat` — a discriminated result over the parsed top level: `orn.dataset`,
  `legacy_v6` (MIGRATION.md §4.1: `compounds[]`/`reactions[]`/`schemaVersion: 5`, checked first, exact),
  `legacy_v4` (any reaction lacking a `participants` array), or a refusal with a reason, never a guess.
- `asset-magic.ts`: `magicBytesMatch` — real magic-byte signatures for png/jpeg/gif/webp, a text sniff for svg
  (`<svg`/`<?xml`), against a declared media type.
- `package-unpack.ts`: `unpackPackage` — fflate `unzipSync` with a `filter` that rejects (and reports) unsafe
  entry names (absolute, `..`, a backslash), duplicates and paths outside `manifest.json`/`dataset.json`/
  `assets/…`, checks each entry's DECLARED size against its own cap before fflate even attempts to inflate
  it, and a running total against the package cap. (fflate's own `unzipSync` additionally pre-allocates the
  OUTPUT buffer at the zip's declared size and decompresses into that fixed buffer — confirmed by reading
  `inflateSync(…, { out: new u8(su) })` in `node_modules/fflate` — so even a maliciously mislabelled entry
  cannot inflate past what it declared, a second, independent bound.) `manifest.json` is required;
  `dataset.json`'s SHA-256 must match the manifest or the whole import is refused; a file not in the
  manifest, or a manifest entry with no file, is reported; an asset whose bytes do not hash to the
  manifest's declared value is reported separately (`assetHashMismatches`), never fatal on its own.
- `asset-resolve.ts`: `resolveAssets` — cross-references recovered bytes (by SHA-256) against parsed `asset`
  records; a hash with no record, or bytes that fail the magic-byte check against the record's declared
  media type, is reported (`RejectedAsset`) and left out — the asset RECORD still imports and shows AST-002
  (missing blob) later, never silent, never thrown.
- `import-envelope.ts`: `parseDatasetEnvelope` — the tolerant envelope (a locally-defined, looser schema than
  the exported `DatasetEnvelopeSchema`: `schemaVersion` accepts ANY number, not `min(1)`, so a version-0 or
  negative file gets the migration runner's own `INVALID_VERSION` explanation instead of a flat envelope
  rejection — the same reading step 3.3's review gave `decodeSnapshot`) → record-count cap (≤ 200,000,
  checked on the envelope) → the injected `MigrationRunner` (a newer version refused with an explanation,
  ADV-041 file part; an older one migrated; `meta` rides alongside the migration, not lost between the
  envelope check and the runner — see the bug this caught, below) → per-record L1 (`validator.parseRecord`
  keyed by each row's OWN `.kind` field, matching boot's convention: the envelope's grouping is
  informational, never authoritative) → quarantine (raw JSON text capped at 64 Ki characters, `rawTruncated`
  when cut, matching `QuarantineRow`'s existing convention).
- `import-source.ts`: `parseImportSource` — the top-level orchestrator. Byte size checked against the LARGER
  package cap before anything is read; a 4-byte magic-number peek decides the JSON vs `.ornpkg` path (the
  only read before the path-specific cap applies); the JSON path then checks its own 50 MB cap before
  `.text()` is ever called. An `.orn.json`'s `embeddedAssets` (base64, keyed by `assetId`) are decoded and
  re-keyed by their record's declared SHA-256 so `resolveAssets` treats them exactly like a package's
  `assets/<sha256>.<ext>` files — one shared verification path either way; malformed base64 is skipped, never
  thrown. `TaskOptions`: an aborted signal returns a value with no partial result at every phase boundary;
  progress is reported once per phase (read, parsed/unpacked, envelope, assets).
- `hash.ts`, `l1-issues.ts`: tiny shared helpers (`sha256Hex`, `issueOf`) — `l1-issues.ts` exists because io
  must not import persistence (`src/persistence/boot-load.ts` has its own copy of `issueOf`; a one-line
  function, not worth a shared core module for).
- `index.ts`: barrel additions.

**Contract amendments applied** (CLAUDE.md §7; approved list from "Phase 3 contract decisions"; entry under Amendments)
- Q5: `IMPORT_LIMITS.maxAssetBytes` (20 MB, matching SECURITY.md §3).
- Q6 (the unsafe-keys part only; preview/plan fields land in 3.7): nothing new needed — `unsafeKeysRenamed`
  is reported directly on `ImportParseResult`, this step's own result type (`ImportPreview.unsafeKeysRenamed`
  is 3.7's).
- Q12 (the import functions part): `TaskOptions` used throughout `src/io/import-*.ts`.
- Beyond the approved list, recorded here: `ImportFormat` renamed from the placeholder `'legacy-orn-html'`
  (wrong — the legacy app is HTML, but only its exported JSON is ever imported) to `'legacy_v6' | 'legacy_v4'`
  (unused before this step, confirmed by grep, so the rename is additive in effect); `QuarantinedItem` gains
  `raw`/`rawTruncated` (PERSISTENCE.md §7 always required them; the step 3.5-era type predates any importer
  needing them); new types `UnsafeKeyRename`, `DetectFormatResult`, `ParsedAsset`, `RejectedAsset`,
  `PackageEntryIssue`, `ImportRejectionCode`, `ImportParseResult`, `ImportParseOutcome` (all `src/io/contracts.ts`).
  No schema change, no `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump, no migration, no fixture change.

**Two real bugs found and fixed while writing tests (nothing silent, CLAUDE.md §1.5)**
1. `sanitizeUnsafeKeys`'s disambiguation only checked for a collision on the RENAMED branch, not the ordinary
   one: a benign key arriving AFTER a renamed dangerous key (in the JSON's own key order) would silently
   overwrite it. Fixed by disambiguating on every insert, whichever branch it came from. Caught by
   `safe-json.test.ts`'s collision test, which failed with a swapped value before the fix.
2. `parseDatasetEnvelope` passed only the STRIPPED envelope (`format`/`schemaVersion`/`records`) to the
   migration runner, silently dropping `meta` — any real migration step that reads or rewrites `meta` (the
   synthetic test step does) would see `undefined` and throw. Fixed by carrying `meta` alongside the document
   handed to the runner and reading it back from the migrated output. Caught by
   `import-envelope.test.ts`'s "older schema version is migrated" test.

**Tests** (`npm run check`: 113 files, 1,662 tests; before this step 105 files, 1,573 tests)
- `json-caps.test.ts` (8): depth/string caps, escape-aware scanning, ADV-032 io part (depth-100, 5 MB string
  rejected fast).
- `safe-json.test.ts` (9): ADV-035 renaming at any depth with path + original key, `Object.prototype` proved
  unchanged even after the sanitised value is spread/`Object.assign`-merged, the disambiguation bug's own
  regression test, cap propagation, `INVALID_JSON` for text that passes the scan but fails to parse.
- `format-detect.test.ts` (10), `asset-magic.test.ts` (14), `asset-resolve.test.ts` (4): format/container
  detection including the v6-checked-before-v4 ordering; real magic bytes for every media type incl. svg's
  text sniff, rejecting cross-type bytes and empty buffers; hash-with-no-record and magic-mismatch reporting.
- `package-unpack.test.ts` (15): a valid package; missing manifest; dataset.json hash mismatch or missing;
  an unreadable zip; unsafe names (absolute, drive letter, `..`, backslash) reported while the rest still
  imports; an oversized declared entry rejected; a file not in the manifest; a manifest entry with no file;
  an asset hash mismatch; an unexpected top-level path; a manifest that is not readable JSON.
- `import-envelope.test.ts` (11): envelope-shape rejection, good records passing L1, a bad record quarantined
  with issues/raw value and the rest still importing, raw-text truncation at the 64 Ki cap, envelope grouping
  being informational (a record found by its OWN kind field), the record-count cap, ADV-041 file part (newer
  refused with an explanation), an older version migrated (with `meta` carried through), a version with no
  registered path to the target, and version-0's own distinct path through the now-loosened envelope check.
- `import-source.test.ts` (19): real `.orn.json`/`.ornpkg` files exported by step 3.5's own builders and
  re-imported (round-trip confidence across the two steps); a genuinely matching embedded asset recovered
  end to end; malformed embedded-asset base64 skipped; ADV-035 through the full pipeline; ADV-041 file part
  through the full pipeline; v6/v4 legacy detection; unknown-format refusal; ADV-032 io part at the
  orchestrator level (a Blob whose `.slice(0,4)` is allowed but whose FULL content throws if ever read,

  branches. `npm run test:coverage` (core + persistence + io) passed on its first run: 96.75% statements,

### Step 3.6 review (reviewer, 2026-09-29)

Verdict: step 3.6 ACCEPTED.

**a. Approved as reported:** the loosened envelope `schemaVersion` (the runner explains bad versions); `ImportFormat` `'legacy_v6' | 'legacy_v4'`; `QuarantinedItem.raw`/`rawTruncated`; the new result types; the depth-bounded recursion in `sanitizeUnsafeKeys` (depth proven ≤ 64 by the pre-scan).

**b. `.ornpkg` entry-count cap: it did NOT exist; added.** `IMPORT_LIMITS.maxPackageEntries = 65_535` (the most a classic zip can hold: its entry count is 16 bits, the exporter never writes zip64; a first draft of 200,002 was unreachable — a 200k-entry classic zip wraps its count). Counted in `unpackPackage`'s entry filter, which throws at the first entry over the cap, so the whole package is refused (`PACKAGE_INVALID`, "more than N entries") before anything is inflated. `unpackPackage` takes an optional `limits.maxEntries` so the test reaches the cap without a 65,536-entry archive. Tests: `src/io/import-review-followups.test.ts` "item b" (the constant; a 12-entry zip refused at cap 11 and read at cap 12; a normal package unaffected).

**c. Malformed embedded-asset base64: it was skipped silently; now REPORTED.** Every unusable `embeddedAssets` entry becomes a `RejectedAsset` with a reason: malformed base64, base64 over the 20 MB asset cap, an ID with no asset record, an entry with no string `assetId`/`base64`, an `embeddedAssets` that is not an array. Found on the way, same function: embedded bytes were keyed by the RECORD'S declared SHA-256 without ever being hashed, so wrong bytes were accepted as verified (SECURITY.md §3 says "SHA-256 verified"). They are now hashed; a mismatch is a `RejectedAsset`. The step 3.6 test that asserted `rejectedAssets` stays empty for malformed base64 encoded the silent behaviour; it now asserts the report (the reviewer's decision changed the expected behaviour; nothing weakened). Tests: `import-source.test.ts` (updated), `import-review-followups.test.ts` "item c" (3).

**d. `legacyPayload` complete: confirmed, test added.** `parseJsonSafely` returns the whole sanitised parse (caps are checked, never applied as truncation). Test: a 3,000-compound, depth-55, 900,000-character-string legacy v6 export comes back `toEqual` the input; with an unsafe key it differs only by that key's rename (reported). The contract comment on `legacyPayload` now says "COMPLETE".

**e. Record listed under the wrong envelope group: it imported (own kind wins) but the mismatch was NOT reported; now reported.** `ImportParseResult.kindMismatches: { id, envelopeKind, ownKind }[]` (new type `KindMismatch`; empty for legacy formats). Tests: "item e" (2).

**f. Test timeouts (resource limit, not an assertion):** `vi.setConfig({ testTimeout: 30_000 })` added to `src/core/schema/schema-mutations.test.ts`, `src/core/validation/reads-audit.test.ts`, `tests/property/generator-tiers.test.ts` and `tests/property/export-persistence-roundtrip.test.ts`. No assertion changed. **Decision replacing the timing open item:** these four files build large datasets and lose their 5 s default only when the machine is contended; a per-file 30 s ceiling is the resource limit, it never hides a slow assertion (wall-clock budgets stay in `tests/timing`, docs/TEST_STRATEGY.md §1).

**Contract change beyond the approved list (CLAUDE.md §7), recorded under Amendments:** `IMPORT_LIMITS.maxPackageEntries`, `KindMismatch`, `ImportParseResult.kindMismatches`.

### Step 3.7 — Import part 2: conflict analysis, merged replica, preview, apply (2026-09-29)

**Delivered** (`src/io`, every new file ≤ 190 lines; `src/core`: two small additive functions)
- `canonical-json.ts`; `import-conflicts.ts` (`analyzeConflicts`); `import-request.ts` (`resolveRequest`: defaults, refusals as values); `import-merge.ts` (`buildMergedReplica`: merge and replace_all, `import_as_copy` ID map, remap + rich-text rewrite, asset staging plan); `import-validate.ts` (`validateMerged`, capped finding list + summary); `import-preview.ts` (`assemblePlan`); `import-apply.ts` (`applyImport`); `import-session.ts` (`createImportSession`: preview / revise / apply / cancel, pending batch); `import-ports.ts`.
- `src/core/text/richtext.ts`: `rewriteRichTextLinks(source, mapId)` — the parser now records where each link's ID starts (offsets mapped back through joined paragraphs and bullets), so only links the PARSER sees are rewritten (a `[[…]]` inside `_{ }` or a malformed one is literal and untouched). `parseRichText` was refactored to share that code (line splitting by offset); its 65 tests pass unchanged. `src/core/references/richtext-links.ts`: `rewriteRecordRichTextLinks(record, idMap)` over `RICH_TEXT_PATHS`.
- `src/persistence/coalesce.ts`: `checkBlobs` accepts a blob for an asset record the ChangeSet created OR updated (see the asset-bytes decision below).

**Decisions worth confirming**
1. *Assets — the current contracts CAN carry the bytes from apply to the write queue without io importing persistence,* so I did not stop. `AssetStagePort` (io, a function-shaped port) is filled just before `replaceState`; `replaceState` notifies the write queue synchronously; the existing `WriteWiringOptions.blobsFor` reads what was staged. Integration test: one write transaction holds `records`, `meta` and `assetBlobs`. The one persistence change: `checkBlobs` also accepts UPDATED asset records (`replace`/`replace_all` of an asset whose SHA-256 differs would otherwise be refused with a RangeError). Consequence, disclosed in the preview (`assets.overwritten`): the bytes stored under that asset ID are overwritten and snapshots do not hold bytes, so "Undo import" cannot restore them.
2. `revise` takes the FULL desired request and recomputes everything from the pending parse against the CURRENT live state (a new `batchId`, the old one discarded); copy IDs already handed out are reused.
3. `apply` compares the submitted plan with the pending one on mode, conflicts, excluded, copy map and decisions (not on the derived preview).
4. `import_as_copy` always renews the nested IDs of the copied record too (they are globally unique; keeping them would clash with the live record's).
5. `replace_all` takes the file's dataset meta; a file with no usable meta is refused (`INVALID_META`). `merge` keeps the live meta and does not import the file's.
6. In-file repeated IDs: first kept, repeats quarantined with a reason. An identical record may be given any resolution; `identical_skip` only when it really is identical.
7. `decisions` stays empty in 3.7 (the legacy importer supplies them from 3.11); legacy formats are refused as `LEGACY_NOT_CONVERTED`.

**Contract amendments** (CLAUDE.md §7; approved list Q6, Q9, Q12 plus what the step needed; entry under Amendments)
- `IdConflict.identical`; `ImportPreview` gains `findingSummary`, `richTextLinksRewritten`, `unsafeKeysRenamed` (Q6), `kindMismatches`, `rejectedAssets`, `packageIssues`, `assets`, `mergedRecordCount`, `canConfirm`, `blockers`; `ImportPlan` gains `excluded` and `copyIdMap` (Q6); new types `FindingSummary`, `ImportAssetSummary`, `ImportPlanRequest`, `ImportPreviewOutcome`/`ImportPreviewErrorCode`, `ImportApplyResult`/`ImportApplyErrorCode`, `ImportBatchReport`, constant `IMPORT_PREVIEW_MAX_FINDINGS`; ports `ImportStorePort`, `SnapshotPort`, `ImportBatchSink`, `AssetStagePort` (Q9, Q12); core `rewriteRichTextLinks` / `rewriteRecordRichTextLinks` (Phase 3 note 2); `checkBlobs` created-or-updated. No schema change, no `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump, no migration, no fixture change.

**Tests** (`npm run check`: 123 files, 1,746 tests; before this step 114 files, 1,672)
- `richtext-rewrite.test.ts` (7), `richtext-links.test.ts` (3), `canonical-json.test.ts` (3), `coalesce-updated-blob.test.ts` (3).
- `import-session.test.ts` (preview: INV-21 nothing changes, cancel, one pending batch, legacy refused, abort; ADV-042 all four resolutions incl. `import_as_copy` rewiring, determinism and rich-text count; request refusals; ADV-030 block-then-exclude) and `import-apply.test.ts` (snapshot-before-replaceState order, one replaceState, history/redo cleared, applied state equals the merged replica and its findings the previewed ones, replace_all, INVALID_META, STALE_REVISION before and during the snapshot, PLAN_MISMATCH, UNKNOWN_BATCH after apply, SNAPSHOT_FAILED, REJECTED with the snapshot removed, READ_ONLY, report failure reported, in-file duplicates and quarantine rows); `import-adv031.test.ts` (2).
