### Step 3.7 review (reviewer, 2026-09-29)

Verdict: step 3.7 ACCEPTED with one required change (content-addressed asset blobs), done in the commit "phase-3: step 3.7 review follow-ups (content-addressed asset blobs)".

**Approved as reported (a).** ADV-031 is caught entirely at L1 (dependants show as dangling, ADV-030); `revise` recomputes the full request against live state; `apply` compares the plan fields listed; `import_as_copy` renews nested IDs; `replace_all` takes the file's meta and `INVALID_META` refuses a file without it; in-file repeated IDs keep the first and quarantine the repeats; `decisions` stay empty until 3.11. Phase 5 wiring (`AssetStagePort` → `blobsFor`) added to open items.

**Required change (b): content-addressed asset blobs.** Overwriting the bytes under an asset ID made "Undo import" (and snapshot restore) unable to bring back the old image: irreversible loss. Delivered (docs/PERSISTENCE.md §2, §4, §5, §6):
- `assetBlobs` is keyed by `sha256` (`{ sha256, blob }`). A write hashes each blob's bytes BEFORE the transaction (hashing is async and cannot run inside an IndexedDB transaction); a mismatch with the record's `sha256` refuses the write (status `failed`, reason in `lastError`, nothing written). Inside the transaction a row is added only if none has that hash (`bulkGet` then `bulkAdd` of the missing). No write path overwrites or deletes a blob; the orphan sweep stays a Phase 9, user-confirmed Diagnostics action.
- Coalesced batches key their blobs by hash (they were keyed by asset ID, which would have lost the first payload when two ChangeSets in one transaction gave one asset ID different bytes: found while designing the tests, regression-tested).
- `getAssetBlob(assetId)` reads the record's `sha256`, then that row, in one read transaction. AST-002 checks each asset record's `sha256` against the stored hashes (`listAssetBlobHashes` replaces `listAssetBlobIds`).
- `DB_VERSION` 2. **Dexie cannot change a primary key in place** (`Not yet support for changing primary key`), so the upgrade is Dexie versions 1.5 and 2 through a holding store, in ONE versionchange transaction (all or nothing; tested: a later failing step leaves the version-1 rows untouched and the next open upgrades them). **Verification cannot happen inside that transaction** either (awaiting `crypto.subtle` ends an IndexedDB transaction), so `OrnDb.open()` first hashes every version-1 row read-only (`asset-rekey.ts`, one row at a time) and the upgrade files each row by that verdict: verified rows go under their hash (equal bytes once); any other row (bytes differ from the recorded hash, malformed hash, no verdict) goes whole to the new `assetBlobsUnverified` store and is reported by boot (`bootReport.unverifiedAssetBlobs`): kept and reported, never dropped. A mismatched row is deliberately NOT stored under its recorded hash (later writes of the genuine bytes would skip as "already stored" and reads would return the wrong image); the asset then shows AST-002.
- The preview's `assets.overwritten` is removed: nothing is overwritten any more.
- Tests (`tests/integration/persistence-asset-blobs.test.ts`, 11; `import-apply-persistence.test.ts` +2): a version-1 database built through the 3.1 scaffold (frozen `DB_STORES_V1`) upgrades (verified rows, equal bytes stored once, other rows and meta kept, holding store gone, version 2); a mismatched row and a malformed hash are kept whole and a same-hash collision loses nothing; boot reports parked rows and AST-002; atomic upgrade; empty and brand-new databases; same bytes under two asset IDs stored once and a later same-bytes write adds no row; replacing an asset with different bytes adds a row and undo finds the old bytes; two coalesced ChangeSets with different bytes store both; a hash mismatch is refused with nothing written; a failed write then its retry stores once; deleting the record never deletes the bytes. **Import replacing an asset with different bytes, then "Undo import": the old record AND its old bytes are back; snapshot restore likewise** (a manual snapshot survives two later replacements; all three payloads stay stored).
- Existing tests that wrote bytes not matching the asset record's `sha256` (fixture hash) now use real hashes (`hashedBlob` in the persistence kit); their assertions are unchanged except two that the new semantics require: bytes of a DELETED record are looked up by hash (`getAssetBlob` needs the record), and the newest DB step's `stores` list a dropped holding store as `null`.

**Benchmark inconsistency (c).** Re-run alone (`npm run bench -- tests/perf/import-preview.bench.ts`, one file, PID lock, no file parallelism), hub_explosion "every ID conflicting" total was NOT lower than its validation stage any more; the opposite happened, so the 3.7 figure (1,170.9 ms) was not reproducible. Findings: (1) the total has no shortcut (it runs parse, analysis, merge, validation); a probe over the same scenario gave parse 1.2 s + analysis 1.0 s + merge 0.06 s + validation 2.6 s = 4.8 s against a parse-to-preview of 5.0 s; validating the same merged state object repeatedly, or a fresh one, cost the same (no cache effect); (2) the cases run minutes apart and the machine's speed drifts by up to about 2x (the same `validateMerged` measured 1.4 s in one bench run and 2.6 s in a plain test minutes later), so stage rows and the total are not comparable across rows. Bench fix: a row "stage functions back to back" doing exactly the total's work through the stage functions, next to the total (header comment explains: compare the total with that row, never with the sum of the stage rows). Numbers (mean / p99 ms, 5 samples):
- full-file run, dense, empty store: parse 901.1 / 964.8; analysis 12.9 / 26.8; merged 21.3 / 31.4; validation 1,752.4 / 1,881.5; total 2,936.5 / 3,109.4
- full-file run, dense, every ID conflicting: parse 857.9 / 986.9; analysis 686.5 / 725.6; merged 32.0 / 43.3; validation 1,669.0 / 1,743.9; total 3,308.3 / 3,427.1
- full-file run, hub_explosion, empty store: parse 787.6 / 861.6; analysis 13.9 / 31.4; merged 21.9 / 33.4; validation 1,666.0 / 1,856.5; total 2,806.6 / 2,961.7
- full-file run, hub_explosion, every ID conflicting: parse 795.7 / 856.3; analysis 694.8 / 754.1; merged 34.8 / 58.4; validation 1,416.7 / 1,645.7 (rme ±30.65%, min 800.5); total 5,269.6 / 5,339.6
- hub_explosion, every ID conflicting, alone, second run: parse 1,134.9 / 1,240.2; analysis 962.5 / 992.2; merged 53.1 / 74.2; validation 2,584.9 / 2,642.5; stage functions back to back 5,183.7 / 5,216.9; total 4,968.5 / 5,149.5

**REVIEW REQUIRED (budget).** hub_explosion into a design-tier store with every ID conflicting sits at about 5.0 to 5.3 s against the 5,000 ms row in the two runs above (3.7's 1.17 s was noise); the preview adds no overhead over its stages, validation is about half of it, and the known remedy is the Phase 4 open item (validate once: the preview and `replaceState` both validate the merged state). Judged at Gate C by TEST_STRATEGY §6; `bench:baseline` not run.

### Step 3.8 — Legacy v4 → v5 port (`src/io/legacy-v6`) with oracle tests (2026-09-29)

**Delivered** (`src/io/legacy-v6`, every file ≤ 176 lines; no contract, schema or invariant change)
- `text.ts` (`arr`/`list`/`lines` and the JS-semantics helpers: `or` = `x || fallback`, own-property `field`, `assignOwn` = `Object.assign` that defines keys so `__proto__` stays an ordinary key), `fg-registry.ts` (`FG` + `fgId`), `conditions.ts` (`CONDFLAG_RE`, `autofillConditionFlags`, `COND_RE`, `splitLegacyReagent`), `migrate.ts` (`migrateLegacy`, a per-document folded-name lookup instead of v6's per-reagent `find`), `normalize.ts` (`normPart`, `normCompound`, `normReaction`, `normalizeData`), `dropped.ts` (`findDroppedLeaves`), `index.ts` (`normalizeLegacyData(raw)` → `{ ok, data, dropped, assignedIds }` or a refusal value). Re-exported from `src/io/index.ts`.
- `scripts/legacy-oracle.mjs`: the Phase 0 method as a script (see below). `data/fixtures/legacy/v6-demo-export.normalized-by-v6.json` (new oracle; README row added; existing fixture files untouched). `tests/fixtures/legacy-v6/`: `quirks.json` (a hand-made workbook of v6 quirks, v4 and v5 mixed) with its oracle, and tables for `splitLegacyReagent` (35), `autofillConditionFlags` (22) and `fgId` (21) with their oracles: all outputs produced by v6's own code.
- Tests: `oracle.test.ts` (8), `no-loss.test.ts` (11), `normalize.test.ts` (24).

**Reading the legacy HTML (CLAUDE.md §9).** Only by line range: file lines 375–414, 414–497 and 586–675 of `Organic_Reaction_Navigator_v6.html` (`<script>` is line 375, so script lines 1–294 are file lines 376–669). The last range ran to file line 675, six lines past script line 294 (`let data=loadData()` and the start of `persist`); nothing from those lines was used. `defaultData` (file lines 498–589, the demo data) was not read. The vm runs exactly script lines 1–294.

**Randomness and the oracle.** The legacy data layer reads `Math.random` in one place, `uid()`, and only for a compound or reaction WITHOUT an `id`. Every record of both fixtures has one, so the Phase 0 oracle holds no random value and the port reproduces it exactly, without injecting anything. (The reviewer's "inject the same values" case did not arise.) For records without an `id` the port assigns a deterministic placeholder (`c-missing-<n>` / `r-missing-<n>`, first free `n` counting from the record's position, listed in `assignedIds`); this is a deliberate departure from v6, unreachable by the oracles, covered by tests. MIGRATION.md §4.2 does not say what an ID-less record becomes; step 3.9 decides.

**Oracles and method (second oracle).** Same Phase 0 method: the legacy data code (script lines 1–294) evaluated in a Node `vm` context with DOM stubs (`document`, `window`, `localStorage`), `normalizeData` applied to `v6-demo-export.json`, written as `JSON.stringify(x, null, 2)` + newline to `data/fixtures/legacy/v6-demo-export.normalized-by-v6.json`. Check of the method: run on `v4-sample.json` the script reproduces the Phase 0 oracle byte for byte (`cmp`). The port deep-equals both oracles (`toStrictEqual`) and matches their key order (`JSON.stringify` equality), and the quirk workbook and the three function tables.

**Idempotence (checked through the vm).** v6's `normalizeData` IS idempotent on the v6 demo output (asserted for the port too), and is NOT idempotent on its own v4 output: the second pass differs only by `image: ""` on every participant, because the v4 migration builds participants without `image` and only `normPart` (which runs on the second pass) adds it. The port reproduces both, and the second case is asserted as exactly that difference.

**No-loss check (INV-23, needed by 3.11): the "keeps everything" case applies to `v4-sample.json`, and v6 does drop leaves on other inputs, so the port also returns them.** Every scalar leaf of the v4 sample is in the v5 output verbatim, except `pattern`, which is exactly reconstructable from `transformation.from → to` (the test proves it with an independent value search, not the port's own logic): `_v4` holds the original `reagent`/`extra`/`condition`, `start`/`end`/`coProducts` are participant refs, `family` → `reagentFamily`, `mech` → `mechanism.summary`, `collapse` → `collapseIntermediates`. For `v6-demo-export.json` (v5) nothing is dropped either. But v6 does discard on other inputs, so `normalizeLegacyData` returns `dropped` (path → value → reason `changed` / `not_in_output`) next to the oracle-equal data: strings split or trimmed into lists, coerced flags (`node: 0`, `collapse: 0`, `difficulty: "3"`), canonicalised functional groups, an unknown `nodeType`, replaced containers, a `mech` overshadowed by `mechanism.summary`, v4 keys left on a v5 reaction (v6 deletes them with no audit trail), unknown fields on participants (rebuilt from six known fields), a `compounds` value that is not an array, the `schemaVersion` stamp. On the quirk workbook that is 42 leaves; a test proves every leaf of the workbook is either represented in the output or listed. Empty values that v6 replaces by defaults (`chapter: ""` → `Uncategorised`, `name: ""`) are listed too: strict by design, so 3.11 can decide.

**Refusals (departures from v6, values not exceptions).** v6 throws a TypeError or builds garbage where the port returns `{ ok: false, code, message, path }`: a document that is not an object (`NOT_AN_OBJECT`); a compound, reaction or participant that is null, a string, a number or an array (`INVALID_RECORD`); a compound with no text `name` when a reagent name has to be searched past it (`INVALID_COMPOUND_NAME`, only where v6's `find` would throw: a match found earlier is fine). Nothing is converted on a refusal.

**Benchmark.** None: this is a one-off conversion, not a hot path (a 20,000-reaction / 5,000-compound test asserts only that the name lookup is a map, not a per-reaction scan).

**Tests** (`npm run check`: 127 files, 1,802 tests; before this step 124 files, 1,759).

**REVIEW REQUIRED**
1. `src/io/legacy-v6/fg-registry.ts` carries one `// hardcode-ok:` (the `phenol` row of the legacy FG table trips `check:hardcode`, which matches dataset names such as "Phenol"). The table is data of the legacy format; nothing branches on it.
2. `dropped` is strict: any leaf whose exact value is not in the output is listed, including empties replaced by defaults. Say if 3.11 should ignore empties.
3. Placeholder IDs for records without an `id` (`c-missing-<n>`, `r-missing-<n>`): naming and whether such a record should instead be refused are for step 3.9 (§4.2 is silent).
4. The read of the legacy HTML reached six lines past script line 294 (see above); nothing from them was used.

### Step 3.9 — Legacy deterministic IDs, label parser, condition parser (`src/io/legacy-v6`) (2026-09-29)

**Delivered** (pure functions only, no mapping of whole records: that is 3.10/3.11; no contract, schema or invariant change; every file ≤ 201 lines)
- `id-parts.ts`: `sanitizeLegacyId`, `legacyPart`, `textPart`, `resolveParts` (collision resolution). `id-space.ts`: `createLegacyIdSpace(input)` with every ID pattern of the §4.2 table (species/class/anchor/display from a compound ID; reaction, participant, stage, outcome, product, caveat, relevance, related reaction from a reaction ID; reagent/species from a label; concept, named reaction, topic from text) and `legacyAssetId(sha256)`.
- `label-parser.ts`: `parseLabel` (§4.6). `quantity.ts`: the temperature/pressure scanner. `condition-fields.ts`: `parseConditionField` (one legacy field value). `condition-parser.ts`: `parseLegacyConditions` (a whole v5 `conditions` object: fields, the three flags, `text`, `parsedFields`). `parse-spans.ts`: `Segment`, `ParseReview`, `tile`, `toOtherEntries`. All re-exported from `src/io/legacy-v6/index.ts` (and the main entry points from `src/io/index.ts`).
- MIGRATION.md §4.2 (how the parts are built), §4.5 (condition fields) and §4.6 (how the token table is read) now state the decisions below.

**IDs.** Hash: the core `hash64` (step 2.2, `src/core/hash.ts`), first 6 hex characters of the ORIGINAL; §4.2 names no other function. Sanitising per code point (ASCII lowercase, `-` for anything else); "changed" also covers truncation: an ID body is at most 63 characters, so a part is capped at 27 (20 characters + `-<hash6>` when it does not fit), which keeps `rel_v6-<a>-<b>` inside 63. Collisions (two different originals, one part) are settled for the whole document per namespace, never merged: all colliding values move to a 12-, then 16-character hash, then `<hash16>-<n>`; order-independent; each collision returned in `collisions` (namespace, shared part, originals, what each got). Text-derived IDs are keyed by the folded text (core `fold`) and always carry a hash. `rel_v6-<lower>-<higher>`: plain code-unit order of the legacy IDs (`r12` before `r3`); a self-pair has no relationship (`undefined`); pairs whose parts join to the same text are told apart by the same mechanism. An ID asked for a value that was never registered throws (programmer error: it could not have been checked for collisions). Tests: every §4.2 row, sanitising table, cap, determinism and order independence, three collision scenarios (crafted `c15!` vs its own produced part, text, pairs), tier 3, and every ID kind against its schema ID schema (`SpeciesIdSchema` … `RelationshipIdSchema`), also as a property over arbitrary strings.

**Label parser.** Every token row of §4.6, case-insensitive, whole-word; extensions stated in MIGRATION.md §4.6: abbreviations also without the dot (as v6's regexes accept), `saturated` next to `sat.`, any token in parentheses. A whole label that is a temperature (K/°C, ranges with hyphen or en-dash) or `hv`/`hν`/`light` (→ `light: hv`) / `UV` (→ `light: uv`) becomes a stage parameter with `parsedFields`; the remainder goes back for matching; `originalText` is always kept. Reported, never guessed: conflicting tokens (`aq. alc. KOH`: none taken out), `non-aqueous`, a label of tokens only, a temperature inside a longer label, a clear-but-undecidable temperature (`100 °F`, `5-0 °C`, `-300 °C`: stage parameter in `other[]`), condition-like labels that are not whole-label matches (`UV light`, `reflux`).

**Condition parser.** Temperature and pressure: single values and ranges, K and °C (atm, bar, kPa, mmHg), hyphen and en-dash; decided only when unambiguous. A range written downwards is not reordered, an unsupported unit (`°F`, `psi`), a second value, or any further number around the value is not decided: the whole value goes to `other[]` as `<field>: <text>` with a review item. `medium`/`atmosphere` by whole-value tables; `phase` `aqueous`/`alcoholic` → `solventCharacter`; `strength` has no stage-parameter field (it is a participant field and which participant is not decidable here) so it goes to `other[]` as `strength: dilute`. Flags: `heat` → qualitative heat only when no temperature was read (else reported as subsumed), `light` → `hv`, `anhydrous` → `true`, all in `parsedFields`; a flag is applied only when exactly `true` (`"yes"` is reported, not applied). `conditions.text` is NOT re-parsed (v6 already inferred its flags and fields from it, ported in `conditions.ts`; §4.5 keeps the text as `parameters.text` and `originalText`). Values of the wrong type are reported and returned in `unconsumed` (3.11 carries them into `provenance.legacy.unmapped`); `other[]` never exceeds the schema's 16 entries, the rest is returned in `overflowOther`, and control characters are written as `\u{hex}` with a review item.

**No character lost.** Every parser returns `segments` that tile its input exactly; `remainder` / `other[]` carry only the segments without a field. fast-check (300 runs, adversarial pieces: tokens, dashes, degree signs, controls, lone surrogates): never throws, segments tile, parsed spans re-parse to the same value alone, `other[]` reproduces the remainder (rebuilt independently), digits left over next to a decided quantity never happen, deterministic. Bounded work: 100k-200k character adversarial strings finish. All 74 distinct labels (37 distinct participant labels of `v4-sample` and `v6-demo-export` after the 3.8 port, the quirk workbook, and the reagent / workup / condition tokens of the v4 reagent-string table) and all 30 distinct condition-field texts parse without throwing and tile.

**Benchmark.** None: one-off conversion, not a hot path (like 3.8).

**Mutation spot check** (12 hand mutants of the id, label, quantity and condition logic, run against `src/io/legacy-v6`): 10 killed at first; one survivor was a weak test (the part-length cap; fixed by a pair of two capped parts that reaches the 63-character body limit, now killed) and one is an equivalent mutant (the `a === b` skip in pair registration: `relatedReactionId` returns `undefined` for a self-pair anyway).

**Tests** (`npm run check`: 131 files, 2,103 tests; before this step 127 files, 1,802 tests; new: id-space 53, label-parser 110, condition-parser 120, parse-no-char-lost 18). No timing failures, nothing re-run.

**Decisions for the step 3.10/3.11 mapper** (recorded, not built): register every legacy value first (compound IDs including dangling references, reaction IDs, remainders of labels, concept/named-reaction/topic texts, related pairs whose reactions exist), then map; report `IdSpace.collisions` and the parsers' `review` items as REVIEW REQUIRED items (§4.7); `parsedFields` non-empty is the RXN-015 trigger.

**REVIEW REQUIRED**
1. Parser results on the two real fixtures: every participant label and every `conditions` object of `v4-sample` and `v6-demo-export` is decided (no review item from the parsers; `443 K` becomes a stage temperature, `aq. KOH` / `alc. KOH` share the remainder `KOH`). The labels/conditions the parsers cannot decide occur only in the hand-made quirk workbook: `strength: "custom"` (→ `other[]`, reported), `heat: "yes"` (not applied, reported, in `unconsumed`), plus `medium: "m"`, `phase: "p"`, `temperature: "t"` in the parser tests. The `heat` flag next to a decided temperature (two reactions of the workbook; common in v6 data, since v6 sets `heat` from words like "heat" next to a temperature) produces an informational `FLAG_SUBSUMED` item; say if that should not be a review item.
2. `strength` has no slot in the stage parameters, so `dil.`/`conc.` in a CONDITION goes to `other[]` (`strength: dilute`); in a LABEL it goes to the participant's `strength`. Alternative if wanted: a schema field, or attaching it to the single acid/base participant when there is exactly one (a decision that needs a rule; not made here).
3. §4.6 extensions beyond the table (see above): dot-less abbreviations, `saturated`, parenthesised tokens, whole-label `light` → `hv`, `UV` → `uv`. Doc-only (MIGRATION.md §4.6 states them).
4. A collision changes the IDs of the colliding values compared with a document without it (12/16-character hash). Only crafted input reaches it in practice (a hash6 collision among a few thousand IDs has a probability around 10⁻⁴).
5. `src/io/legacy-v6/condition-fields.ts` carries one more `// hardcode-ok:` (the atmosphere spellings `nitrogen`, `hydrogen` are also demo species names; it is the schema's ATMOSPHERES vocabulary, nothing branches on a record).
6. `rel_v6-<lower>-<higher>` orders by plain string comparison, so `r12` precedes `r3`; it only has to be symmetric and deterministic. Say if a natural (numeric-aware) order is wanted.

### Step 3.9 review (reviewer, 2026-09-29)

Verdict: step 3.9 ACCEPTED. Small changes applied in the commit "phase-3: step 3.9 review follow-ups"; no contract, schema or invariant change.

**(a) Approved as reported:** the parser results on both real fixtures; collisions moving to 12/16-character hashes (crafted input only); the second `// hardcode-ok:` in `condition-fields.ts` (its comment states the reason); `rel_v6-` ordering by plain string comparison is KEPT (changing it later would change IDs; it only has to be symmetric and deterministic).

**(b) `strength` in a CONDITION stays in `other[]`** (`strength: dilute`): no schema field and no rule attaching it to "the one acid/base participant" (that would be guessing chemistry). Recorded in PROJECT_STATE openItems as a possible Phase 6 authoring question.

**(c) §4.6 "any token in parentheses" = the parenthesised forms of the LISTED tokens only.** Checked in the code: the token pattern's parenthesised branch is built from the same table, so `(ice bath)`, `(2 eq)`, `(1 mol)`, `(in water)`, `(g)` stay in the remainder untouched and `(conc.)`, `(aq)`, `( cat. )` are taken out; nothing needed fixing. New tests (`label-parser.test.ts`, 13 cases) pin both directions, MIGRATION.md §4.6 states the reading. A listed WORD inside other parentheses (`(dry ice)`) is still a whole-word token (`dry`), as before: only the word is taken out and the segments tile the label.

**(d) `FLAG_SUBSUMED` is no longer a review item.** It is an informational note: `ParsedConditions.notes` (`ParseNote { code, message, field }`, `ParseNoteCode`), separate from `review`, so the review queue holds only what a person must decide. The 3.10/3.11 report counts notes (`LegacyImportReport` skeleton, step 3.10). `ParseReviewCode` lost the code; the condition-parser test asserts `review` is empty and the note is present. MIGRATION.md §4.5 says so.

Hubs → `compound_class` + `navigator_anchor` (order = 1-based hub position in file order); the per-hub decision "class (default) / species" is an input (a Map keyed by the hub's key), both paths built and tested; the hub SMILES is never the class structure (kept in `legacy.unmapped.smiles`, review "representative SMILES not used; add an example species if intended"). Other compounds → species (`speciesKind: unspecified`). Every §4.4 row is implemented (see MIGRATION.md §4.4 for the reading). Records get `provenance.origin legacy_import`, timestamps from the injected clock (read once), `provenance.legacy { system, legacyId, unmapped? }`, `published` lifecycle (created classes: `draft`), `review_required` exactly when reasons exist. The §4.8 rule is applied to every dropped leaf under `compounds[i]`; dropped leaves elsewhere are returned in `unhandledDropped` for 3.11. The result also carries the records by kind (the shape of `ImportParseResult.records`), `assetBytes` (`ParsedAsset[]` for the import pipeline), `hubs`, `refByLegacyId` (legacy compound ID → species/class RecordRef, for 3.11), `functionalGroupClass`, the trace (`consumed`) and the compound side of the report. A refused run (`INVALID_CLOCK`, `DUPLICATE_RECORD_ID`) leaves a shared report builder untouched.

**Report skeleton.** `LegacyImportReport` (see Amendments) gained `consumedByDefault` (§4.8 names it; path with every index written `[*]` → occurrences) and `notes` (informational counts by code, never review items: where `FLAG_SUBSUMED` lands in 3.11). Review items: one per reason per record, and every review_required record is in the report (test). `preservedUnmappedFields`: legacy field name → occurrences (`smiles`, `jee.level`, `dropped.tags[*]`).

**Tests** (`npm run check`: 139 files, 2,253 tests; before this step, after Part A: 131 files, 2,115; new: compound-map 34, compound-classes 12, compound-image 23, compound-completeness 22, compound-quirks 15, compound-scale 2, id-collect 13, report 17 = 138). No timing failures, nothing re-run.
- *Completeness (INV-23)* over `compounds[]` of BOTH fixtures, the quirk workbook and a hostile document of wrong-typed values (numbers and lists where text belongs, unknown fields, a repeated and a missing id): the leaves are enumerated by a walker inside the test file, never from the mapper's trace. Every leaf of the v5 data is consumed (trace) or found in its record's `unmapped` (multiset match, so one kept value cannot cover two leaves); every dropped leaf is counted in `consumedByDefault` (and the report's counts equal an independent count) or in `unmapped`; every RAW leaf is a leaf of the v5 data or a dropped leaf (the last three checks run on all four inputs); every unmapped scalar is a leaf of the input (nothing invented). A read-back check takes each consumed verbatim field (id, name, smiles, formula, properties, acidBase, genericFormula, tags, jee.notes) out of the output record, so a trace that claims a leaf the record does not hold fails; the checker is itself tested against a trace with a hole and a false claim.
- *§4.9 compound quirk*: hub example structures Alkanes `CCCC`, Alkenes `C=CC`, Arenes anthracene, Alcohols `CCO`, Ketones acetone, on the real fixture, both decisions. Every mapped record of both fixtures, the quirk workbook and the hostile document passes strict L1 (both decisions), and the mapped records alone have no L2/L3 ERROR.
- *Scale sanity (no benchmark: one-off conversion)*: 5,000 compounds (hubs, shared functional groups, 50 `compoundClass` texts, repeated names, 5,000 reactions with dangling refs). "Maps, not scans" is asserted without a clock: every compound is a counting Proxy and the reads per compound must not grow between 1,000 and 5,000 compounds (within 5%) and stay under a constant.
- *Mutation spot check* (10 hand mutants of the mapper: aromaticity table, anchor order, tag validity, dropped rule inverted, last instead of first hub, name not consumed, duplicate-name key ignoring the kind, unknown fields not kept, a scan instead of the reference map in `id-collect`, a scan in the reference map of the mapper): all 10 killed; two survivors on the first pass (duplicate-name kind key, the scan in `id-collect`) were weak tests and now have a test each (a species and a class that fold alike; reactions with refs in the scale document).

**Benchmark.** None: one-off conversion, not a hot path (like 3.8 and 3.9).

**Not built (3.11):** reactions, the preview decisions UI/data (`hubs` lists what is asked), dropped leaves outside compounds, reaction-side notes, the full report, the dry run of both fixtures through the import pipeline.

**REVIEW REQUIRED**
1. Hub fields with no class slot are kept in `legacy.unmapped` without a review item: `formula`, `acidBase`, `aromaticity` (a recognised value), and `fgId` / `functionalGroups` / `compoundClass` (they describe the hub itself). `stereo` text on a hub does get a review reason (unrecognised stereo text is a §4.7 trigger). One candidate you may want: in the demo a hub's `formula` is a general formula (`CnH2n+2`, `CnH2n`, `CnHm`) while `genericFormula` holds the R-notation; `class.generic.generalFormula` would take it, but for other hubs `formula` repeats `genericFormula`, so it is not decided here. Say if hub `formula` should go to `generic.generalFormula`.
2. `navigator_anchor.order` = the hub's 1-based position among hubs in file order (§4.4 says "legacy order").
3. §4.2 had no ID for a class made from a functional-group id or a `compoundClass` text; `cls_v6-fg-<part>` and `cls_v6-l-<slug>-<hash6>` were added (MIGRATION.md §4.2), with their own collision namespaces. Created classes are `draft` + `review_required`; mapped compounds are `published`. A published species that lists a draft created class produces an RVW-002 INFO finding; say if created classes should be `published`.
4. Contract change (Amendments): `LegacyImportReport.consumedByDefault` and `notes` (both required; nothing built a report before this step), `ReportBuilder.absorb`, and the refusal codes `INVALID_CLOCK` / `DUPLICATE_RECORD_ID`.
5. Names are stored verbatim (`"  Ethanol "` keeps its spaces; the folded-name checks ignore them); a name the schema cannot hold becomes `Unnamed legacy compound #<position>` with the original in `unmapped.name` and a review reason.
6. Repeated legacy compound IDs are never merged: the later record gets `spc_v6-<id>-<n>-<hash6>`, a review reason, and references resolve to the first (v6's lookup). Non-text IDs are keyed by their JSON and kept in `unmapped.id`.
7. An undecodable image is kept in `unmapped.image` with a review item and counted in `preservedUnmappedFields`; it is NOT in `notImported` (the value is preserved, and the acceptance line wants `notImported` empty). Say if it should be listed there as well.
8. Aromaticity matching is exact and case-sensitive (`Aromatic` and `aromatic ` are kept + review), as §4.4 says "exactly".
9. An unsafe key renamed while parsing reaches the mapper as an ordinary unknown field (`__unsafe_key__…`), so it is kept in `unmapped` and reviewed there; the io result's `unsafeKeysRenamed` still lists it (step 3.11 puts both in the report).
10. Cross-namespace ID clashes (a crafted compound ID that reads like a produced class ID) are detected only at the end and refused as a value (`DUPLICATE_RECORD_ID`), not settled by the collision ladder; §4.2's ladder stays per namespace.

**Does 3.11 look big enough to split? Yes.** It holds the whole of §4.5 (participants and stages, outcomes and products, conditions with the 3.9 parsers, transformations, mechanisms, concept / named reaction / topic matching or creation, exam relevance, caveats, relationships, scope and lifecycle), the preview decisions, the full report, and the dry run of both fixtures through the import pipeline with committed expected outputs (ADV-043), plus the whole-JSON completeness test. ### Step 3.10 review (reviewer, 2026-09-30)

Verdict: step 3.10 ACCEPTED. Changes applied in the commit "phase-3: step 3.10 review follow-ups"; no contract, schema or invariant change (`IdCollision.resolved[].space`, optional, is an internal type of `id-parts.ts`, not a contract).

**(a) Approved as reported:** anchor order = 1-based hub position in file order; names stored verbatim (trimming would be a silent fix); unnameable names → "Unnamed legacy compound #<position>" + the original in unmapped + review; repeated legacy IDs never merged, references resolve to the first (v6's lookup); exact, case-sensitive aromaticity matching; unsafe keys kept in unmapped and reviewed (3.11c puts `unsafeKeysRenamed` in the report too); the contract amendment (`consumedByDefault`, `notes`, the two created-class ID rows, `ReportBuilder.absorb`, `INVALID_CLOCK` / `DUPLICATE_RECORD_ID`).

**(b) Undecodable image:** approved as is. `notImported` is ONLY for values not preserved anywhere; a value kept in `provenance.legacy.unmapped` is preserved, never listed in `notImported`. Written into MIGRATION.md §4.8.

**(c) Hub `formula`.** `class.generic.generalFormula` exists (`LabelTextSchema`), so no amendment. A hub `formula` that is non-empty and differs from its `genericFormula` (exact string comparison) becomes `generic.generalFormula` with the review reason "general formula taken from the legacy hub formula; check it"; equal to `genericFormula` it stays in `legacy.unmapped.formula`, no review. A formula the field cannot hold (over the label limit, not text) stays whole in unmapped with a reason. Other hub fields with no class slot (acidBase, aromaticity, fgId, functionalGroups, compoundClass) stay in unmapped without review. On the demo fixture 8 of the 13 hubs get a general formula (Alkanes `CnH2n+2`, Alkenes `CnH2n`, Alkynes `CnH2n-2`, Arenes `CnHm`, and four whose formula only differs in spelling from `genericFormula`: Phenols `ArOH` next to `Ar–OH`, Ketones `R2C=O`, Carboxylic acids `RCOOH`, Esters `RCOOR′`; each is reviewed, so a person can drop the ones that are just another spelling); the other 5 (formula = genericFormula) keep it in unmapped. MIGRATION.md §4.4 has the row.

**(d) Created classes** (functional-group and `compoundClass`-text classes) are now lifecycle `published` + `review_required`. Test: the mapped records of both fixtures raise no RVW-002 finding and no L1–L3 ERROR (the demo has created classes, so the check is not vacuous).

**(e) Cross-namespace clash settled, not refused.** `resolveSharedParts` (`id-parts.ts`) settles the compound, functional-group, class-text and label namespaces in ONE pass; a key occupies "slots" (ID family + body): a compound part sits in `spc` and `cls`, a functional-group part in `cls` as `fg-<part>`, a class-text part in `cls` as `l-<part>`, a label part in `spc` as `l-<part>`. Two values collide when they hold one slot; a label and a class text that fold alike share no family and do not. The crafted clash (hub `fg-x` vs the functional-group class of `x`; a compound `l-<textpart>` vs the class of a `compoundClass` text; a compound vs a label species) is settled by the ladder (both values get a 12-character hash, a compound keeps one part for all its prefixes), reported in `collisions` (namespace `compound+functional_group` …, each `resolved` entry names its `space`) and as a review reason on the moved records. `DUPLICATE_RECORD_ID` stays as a last-resort guard (`duplicatedIds`, unit-tested); a fast-check property over crafted IDs (pieces such as `fg-`, `l-`, `alkene`, real hash6 values) never reaches it. Without a clash every ID is exactly the tier-0 part of §4.2 (tested independently with `legacyPart` on both fixtures; collisions empty). MIGRATION.md §4.2 and §4.4 say so. The old "refused as a value" tests were replaced by the settled-clash test; the report test for a refused run now uses `INVALID_CLOCK`.

**(f) Step 3.11 split** in docs/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §2 into 3.11a (reactions part 1), 3.11b (classification and curriculum, top-level and reaction `dropped` leaves, whole-JSON completeness) and 3.11c (preview decisions, full report, dry run, expected outputs, re-import); nothing dropped from the original row. PROJECT_STATE nextStep = 3.11a.

**Tests** (`npm run check`: 140 files, 2,271 tests; before: 139 files, 2,253; new file `compound-review-followups.test.ts`, 18 tests; existing tests adjusted: created classes `published`, the Alkynes hub review, the general-formula read-back check, the two "refused" tests). No timing failures, nothing re-run.

### Step 3.11a — v5 → ORN reaction mapping, part 1 (`src/io/legacy-v6`) (2026-09-30)

**Delivered** (pure functions, one async step for image hashing; no contract, schema or invariant change; one ID row added to §4.2, see Amendments; every new source file ≤ 299 lines)
- `reaction-map.ts` (`mapLegacyReactions`: orchestration, scope, lifecycle, report, result), `reaction-participants.ts` (participants → participants, stages, the one outcome, intermediates), `reaction-roles.ts` (the ten v6 roles), `reaction-labels.ts` (whole-label temperature / light → stage parameters), `reaction-substances.ts` (`SubstanceBook`: mapped compounds, placeholders, records created from labels), `reaction-conditions.ts` (the 3.9 condition parser into stage 1 + the accounting around it), `reaction-assets.ts` (`AssetBook`), `merge-records.ts` (`mergeMappedRecords`: compound + reaction records as one set, the shape of `ImportParseResult.records`; 3.11c uses it). Test support: `src/test-support/legacy-reaction-kit.ts`. `compound-scope.ts` gained `createScope(base, …)` (the compound scope is now a wrapper); `compound-convert.ts` exports `take` / `keepOnly` / `tagsOf`; `compound-review.ts` exports `collisionReason`.

**What it does.** Input is the 3.8 port's output (`data`, `assignedIds`), the ID space and the 3.10 compound result (`refByLegacyId`, the assets, `unhandledDropped`; the shared report builder via `options.report`, absorbed only on success). Per reaction: `id` / `name` / `tags` / `participants` / `conditions` / `image` are mapped; the eleven 3.11b fields are returned as `deferred` leaf paths; anything else is kept in `provenance.legacy.unmapped` with a reason. Roles, the workup stage, the ONE outcome (major → main, other products → coproduct + "coproduct or minor isomer?", byproducts → byproduct, selectivity unspecified), intermediates (kind other, review), references through the compound reference map (both hub decisions tested on a reaction that uses a hub), one shared placeholder per missing compound, label-only participants (3.9 label parser; whole-label temperature / light → stage parameters with `parsedFields`; remainder matched by folded name against the mapped compounds, then against records already created from labels, else one shared created record per folded label), conditions (3.9 parser → stage 1; `text` → `parameters.text` and `originalText`; `parsedFields` → RXN-015 reason; parse review items → reasons; `FLAG_SUBSUMED` → `report.notes`; unread values and `other[]` overflow → unmapped), participant notes, participant and reaction images (assets attached to the reaction; the participant label is the note), scope (specific / generic / mixed → generic + reason) and lifecycle (published when a reactant and an outcome with a product exist, else draft). Deterministic (clock read once, no randomness), total for any parsed JSON, Map lookups only. Refusals as values: `INVALID_CLOCK`, `DUPLICATE_RECORD_ID` (the last-resort guard, over reaction and compound IDs together).

**Tests** (`npm run check`: 145 files, 2,362 tests; before this step, after Part A: 140 files, 2,271; new: reaction-map 28, reaction-conditions 18, reaction-quirks 19, reaction-completeness 22, reaction-scale 2, plus 2 rows in id-space = 91). One timing-only failure, recorded as asked: `reaction-scale.test.ts` (both tests on the first full `npm run check`, one of them on a second run of the whole suite) exceeded Vitest's default 5 s timeout while the other 144 files ran in parallel (the counting-Proxy runs take 3-6 s alone); the assertions count reads and never time, so both tests now carry an explicit 120 s limit (a guard against a hang) and nothing was weakened. Re-run once after the change: green (145 files, 2,362 tests).
- *Must cover*: every §4.5 row of this step (reaction-map, reaction-conditions); §4.9 quirks r12 (Alcohols twice → two reactant participants on one class, not merged, review reason, RXN-021 warning from the validator, also with the hub decided as species), r14 (Esters + palmitic acid → mixed → generic + reason; specific when the hubs are species), r18 (acetone coproduct on the Ketones class → reason "a specific species may be intended"; none when decided as species), "conc. H2SO4, 443 K" (v4 sample: stage temperature 443 K, `conc.` kept as the participant's strength; the quirk workbook too); every mapped record passes strict L1 (both fixtures, the quirk workbook, a hostile document, both hub decisions); compounds + reactions together have no L1-L3 ERROR (all four inputs).
- *Completeness (INV-23, reaction side)*: the leaves under `reactions[]` are enumerated by a walker in the test file. Every leaf of the v5 data is consumed (trace), found in its reaction's `unmapped` (multiset match) or in `deferred`, on both fixtures, the quirk workbook and the hostile document, with hubs decided as class and as species; `deferred` is EXACTLY the leaves under the 11 field names typed out from the plan row (nothing more, nothing less, and none of them also consumed); every unmapped scalar is a leaf of its own reaction (nothing invented); a read-back checks each consumed verbatim field against the records (id, name, tags, text, note, ref resolves to the record of that legacy id, label, images against node:crypto hashes); the checker is tested against a trace with a hole and a false claim. An informal timing (not asserted) is linear: about 0.3 ms per reaction from 1,000 to 8,000.
- *Mutation spot check* (16 hand mutants of the reaction mapper: primary substrate, workup stage, main/coproduct, byproduct role, product kind, scope rule, lifecycle rule, placeholder sharing, compound matching, attachment cap, deferred list, RXN-015 reason, RXN-021 reason, empty ref, stage ids): 15 killed at first; the survivor (`parsed.unconsumed.includes(key) ||` in `reaction-conditions.ts`) was an equivalent mutant, because the branch after it already keeps a value nobody consumed; the redundant condition was removed.

**Both fixtures together with the 3.10 compound records** (no L1-L3 ERROR). `v6-demo-export.json`: 83 records; findings: 1 WARNING (RXN-021, r12), INFO RXN-016 ×22 and SPC-004 ×4, REVIEW_REQUIRED RVW-001 ×47 and RXN-015 ×8; 22 reactions (20 generic, 0 draft), 21 reagents and 4 species created from labels, 323 deferred leaves. `v4-sample.json`: 27 records; 2 WARNINGS (SPC-001 ×2: duplicate folded names in the sample), INFO RXN-016 ×6, SPC-004 ×5, RVW-002 ×1 (a published reaction refers to a draft placeholder for a missing compound), REVIEW_REQUIRED RVW-001 ×20 and RXN-015 ×3; 6 reactions, 10 reagents, 4 species, 96 deferred leaves; `reaction.customField` is the one kept unmapped field. No `FLAG_SUBSUMED` occurs in either fixture (the counting is tested on hand-made input).

**Not built (3.11b / 3.11c):** the classification and curriculum fields, dropped leaves of reactions and the top level, the preview decisions, the full report, the dry run.

**REVIEW REQUIRED**
1. *Matching against existing records is limited to the imported document.* §4.5/§4.6 say the remainder is matched against "existing reagents/species". I read it as the compounds mapped in 3.10 and records created from labels in the same document (as instructed); MIGRATION.md does not need live matching (ID conflicts are the pipeline's job, §4.3 step 3). Live matching would make the mapper's output depend on the user's data and break "re-import → identical_skip". If a user's existing "KOH" should be reused, that is a preview-time decision (3.11c) - say if wanted.
2. *A label-only intermediate keeps its label (`intermediates[].label`) and no species record is created.* The task text says "species for substance roles"; an intermediate has an explicit `label` and a transient name is no compound to look up or create. Alternative: create a species per folded label.
3. *Reagent-like roles* (for the kind of record a label creates) are reagent, catalyst, solvent, spectator and workup; substance roles are substrate, reactant, product, byproduct. `solvent` and `spectator` could also be species; say if they should be. A product label never reuses a created reagent.
4. *Created-from-label records are `published` + `review_required`* (as the created classes after the 3.10 review); missing-compound placeholders are `draft` + `review_required` as §4.5 says, so a published reaction that uses one raises an RVW-002 INFO finding (1 in `v4-sample`). The reaction itself is NOT flagged for using a created record (the record carries the review).
5. *Scope counts only species and class references* (participants, products, intermediates with a ref); a reaction with only reagent references is specific. With hubs decided as class, 20 of the 22 demo reactions are generic (most reference a hub), r12, r14 and r18 among them with a mixed-scope reason. Deciding hubs as species changes that (tested).
6. *A label of tokens only* (`dry`) becomes the record's name and no token is taken out; the reaction says so. A participant with neither ref nor label, or an unusable label, is kept whole in `legacy.unmapped` with a reason (never dropped, never in `notImported`).
7. *Products keep tokens only in `originalText`* (no strength / dry / qualifier field on a product); the phase token is stored.
8. *A whole-label temperature that clashes with the conditions temperature* goes to the stage's `other[]` (`temperature: 443 K`) with a reason; equal values add nothing.
9. *Coproducts are ONE reason per reaction* ("N product(s) not marked major …"), not one per product; the review queue has one item per cause and record, like compounds.
10. `reversibility` is set to `unknown` (v6 has no such datum); `aliases`, `purposes` and the other classification lists are empty until 3.11b.
11. `createDocumentIds` now also registers the trimmed label of a tokens-only participant (its name); the registered set stays a superset of what is used.

### Step 3.11b — v5 → ORN reaction mapping, part 2: classification and curriculum, dropped leaves, whole-JSON completeness (`src/io/legacy-v6`) (2026-09-30)

**Delivered** (pure functions, all synchronous; contract change and three ID rows recorded under Amendments; every new source file ≤ 252 lines)
- `classify-map.ts` (`mapLegacyClassification`: orchestration, dropped leaves, document-level values, the honest leftovers), `classify-fields.ts` (transformation, mechanism, reactionType, reactionFamily, reagentFamily, namedReaction, chapter, collapseIntermediates, `_v4`), `classify-jee.ts` (relevance, difficulty, traps, prerequisites, concepts, relatedReactions), `classify-book.ts` (concepts, named reactions and topics: context → this import → create), `classify-transformations.ts` (matching by class sets), `classify-scope.ts` (the per-reaction scope plus an account of every leaf consumed or kept), `match-context.ts` (`LegacyMatchContext`, `EMPTY_MATCH_CONTEXT`, `createMatchIndex`, `matchContextFromRecords`), `exam-vocab.ts` (the v6 relevance word → exam-name table, `// hardcode-ok:` with its reason). `id-space.ts` / `id-collect.ts`: `mechanismId`, `topicMappingId`, `transformationId`, `transformationKey`, registration of the functional-group sets. `merge-records.ts`: `mergeAllMappedRecords`. `report.ts`: `documentValue`. Test support: `src/test-support/legacy-classify-kit.ts` (the whole chain with one shared report, `demoContext()`, `stateWithDemo()`).
- MIGRATION.md §4.2 (three ID rows, registration), §4.5 ("How step 3.11b reads the table"), §4.8 (dropped leaves of reactions and of the document) updated.

**What it does.** A SEPARATE step after 3.11a (its function and tests are unchanged: its `deferred` list is still what it returns). Input: the port's `data`, the ID space, the 3.10 compound result, the 3.11a result (`deferred`, `unhandledDropped`, `refByLegacyReactionId`, the reaction records) and the optional `LegacyMatchContext`. Output: the reactions completed (changed copies, same IDs and order) plus concepts, named reactions, topics, topic mappings, transformations, mechanisms, caveats, relationships and exam relevance; `reagentFamilies` (distinct values with counts, sorted); `documentUnmapped`; `deferred` and `unhandledDropped`, which are the LEFTOVERS and empty (`deferred` is computed from an account of every leaf consumed or kept, not assumed). Matching: context record by folded name/alias, else a record created earlier in the same import, else a new record (published + `review_required`); exams are matched and never created; `chapter` "Uncategorised" → no mapping (r22). Every §4.5 row of the task is covered (see MIGRATION.md §4.5 for the decisions). The context is plain data (`records: { id, kind, names, parentId?, conceptKind? }[]`), optional, never written, indexed once into Maps.

**Tests** (`npm run check`: 152 files, 2,513 tests; before this step, after Part A: 145 files, 2,362; new: classify-map 34, classify-transformation 18, classify-context 22, classify-quirks 28, classify-completeness 38, classify-scale 3, id-space-classification 8 = 151 (report.test.ts changed shape, no test added); typecheck, lint, lint:deps, size and hardcode checks green). No timing-only failure this step (one full run of `npm run check`, 26 s of tests; the scale tests carry the approved 120 s limit and passed at 6-9 s each).
- *Must cover*: every §4.5 row of this step, one test each by hand (classify-map, classify-transformation); §4.9 r22 (no mapping, no relevance, nothing kept, no reason; with the demo dataset its derived exam scope is `unmapped` while an ordinary reaction is `official` through the imported relevance); context tests with the empty context, a hand-made context and a context built from `data/seed/demo.orn.json` (reuse by name and alias, kind-specific concept matching, ambiguity, order independence, exams matched and never created, the context deep-frozen and unchanged); every mapped record passes strict L1 and the whole mapped set has no L1-L3 ERROR for both fixtures, the quirk workbook and a hostile document (both hub decisions; empty context alone, demo context merged with the demo dataset).
- *Completeness over the WHOLE legacy JSON (INV-23)*: the leaves of the v5 data (compounds, reactions, top level) are enumerated by a walker in the test file; each is consumed (union of the three steps' traces), kept in the `unmapped` of its OWN record (multiset: a kept value covers one leaf), or in `documentUnmapped`; each leaf the port dropped is counted by default (counts compared per generalized path) or kept under `unmapped.dropped`; nothing is deferred, `notImported` is empty; nothing invented (every kept scalar is a leaf of its reaction or one of its dropped leaves); 8 setups per input (both fixtures, the quirk workbook, a hostile document) = 4 inputs × both hub decisions × both contexts. The checker is tested against a hole (compound, reaction, classification and top-level leaves) and against a dropped leaf nobody kept; a read-back checks every consumed classification value against the records (equation, class sets, bond changes, mechanism type and summary, types, family, named reaction, chapter, difficulty, traps, prerequisites, concepts, relevance, related reactions), tested with four false claims.
- *Scale sanity* (no benchmark: one-off conversion, as 3.10 and 3.11a): 5,000 reactions with shared concepts, named reactions, chapters, three class-set pairs, two traps each, related pairs and relevance map completely against a context of 3,000 records (unique IDs); reads per reaction constant between 1,000 and 5,000 (within 5%, under a constant); the reads of context records that nothing matches are identical for 500 and 4,000 reactions and proportional to the size of the context (the index is built once, every lookup is a Map).
- *Mutation spot check* (37 hand mutants of the new source: difficulty bounds and zero, certainty, the "Uncategorised" literal, the relevance table, exam scope, kind-specific matching, topic parent preference, class-set sorting, relationship dedupe and direction, empty dropped leaves, document fields, trap numbering, reagentFamily counts, hub `fgId` fallback, ambiguity note, the `dropped#3.11b` suffix, partial relevance, self-relations, topic order, family target, bond-change rule, mechanism summary, created review reasons, kept review reasons and lifecycle, `schemaVersion`, the repeated-group note, the concept cap, mapping role, caveat kind, unknown related IDs, clock check, absorb-on-success, honest leftovers): 34 killed at first; the three survivors (class-set sorting across reactions, the leftover list, the 256-concept cap) got a test each and are killed.

**Benchmark.** None: one-off conversion, not a hot path (like 3.8-3.11a).

**Both fixtures with all mapped records** (no L1-L3 ERROR in any of the four runs; counts of the whole validated set). `v6-demo-export.json`, empty context (197 records; 134 review items): 1 WARNING (RXN-021, r12); INFO MEC-001 ×21, RXN-016 ×22, SPC-004 ×4; REVIEW_REQUIRED RVW-001 ×104, RXN-015 ×8. New records: 36 concepts, 7 named reactions, 5 topics, 21 topic mappings, 21 mechanisms, 18 caveats, 6 relationships, 0 transformations (no `fgFrom` / `fgTo` in the demo), 0 exam relevance (no exam in the empty context: the relevance value stays in `unmapped` of 21 reactions with a reason); 19 distinct `reagentFamily` values. `v6-demo-export.json`, demo context (merged with the demo dataset; 223 records; 143 review items): WARNING SPC-001 ×6, CLS-002 ×26, RXN-002 ×1 (planted in the demo dataset itself), RXN-021 ×1; INFO MEC-001 ×21, RXN-016 ×38, SPC-004 ×4, RVW-002 ×1; REVIEW_REQUIRED RVW-001 ×159, RXN-015 ×8. 24 concepts, 4 named reactions, 4 topics created (the rest matched: `top_hydrocarbons`, `cpt_addition`, Fischer esterification, Grignard, Lindlar …); 42 exam relevance records (21 reactions × Main and Advanced). `v4-sample.json`, empty context (29 records; 23 review items): WARNING SPC-001 ×2; INFO MEC-001 ×2, RVW-002 ×1, RXN-016 ×6, SPC-004 ×5; REVIEW_REQUIRED RVW-001 ×20, RXN-015 ×3; 2 mechanisms, 4 distinct `reagentFamily` values; no chapter, relevance or traps in the v4 data. `v4-sample.json`, demo context: WARNING SPC-001 ×4, RXN-002 ×1 (the demo's); INFO MEC-001 ×2, RVW-002 ×2, RXN-016 ×22, SPC-004 ×5, SPC-005 ×5; REVIEW_REQUIRED RVW-001 ×58, RXN-015 ×3. CLS-002 and SPC-001 in the demo-context runs come from the merge of the import's classes and species with the demo's records (3.10 behaviour, not this step).

**Not built (3.11c):** the preview decisions (hub class / species, `reagentFamily` values), the full `LegacyImportReport` (`unsafeKeysRenamed`, `collisions`), the dry run through the real import pipeline with committed expected outputs.

**REVIEW REQUIRED**
1. *A report field for values that belong to no record* (contract change, Amendments). The task said to say where a top-level leaf that belongs to no record goes and to STOP if nowhere fits. Nothing in the schema can hold one (there is no dataset-level record; no reaction or species is the owner of `title` or of the original `schemaVersion: 4`). I chose the smallest place the contract can give: `LegacyImportReport.documentUnmapped` (top-level key or `dropped.<path>` → the value verbatim), counted as `document.<key>` and `DOCUMENT_VALUE_KEPT`, no review item (no record to review), `notImported` stays empty. Neither real fixture has such a value (the quirk workbook has three: `title`, `unknownTopLevel`, the dropped `schemaVersion: 4`). If you prefer another home (a note on the import batch, or refusing unknown top-level fields), say so; nothing else depends on it.
2. *Concepts are matched within their kind for the three kind-specific roles.* A dataset concept with the same name but another kind is not reused: a new concept is created and says so. Effect on the demo: legacy `mechanism.type` "electrophilic addition" gets a new `mechanism_class` concept next to the dataset's `reaction_type` "Electrophilic addition"; the same text under `jee.concepts` (any kind) then matches the dataset's. The alternative (match by name only) would give a reaction a `mechanismClasses` entry that names a `reaction_type` concept.
3. *`reactionTypes` certainty is `definite`* (§4.5 names `probable` only for `mechanism.type`).
4. *Transformations need at least one class.* A functional-group id resolves to the class 3.10 made for it or to the first hub decided as class with that `fgId`; an id with no class stays in `unmapped` with a reason and no class is invented (3.10 only makes classes for groups species carry). A `bondChanges` text with no transformation record to belong to is kept with a reason. The record's name comes from the class names, its equation stays on each reaction.
5. *Created topics are roots, `order` = creation number*; `topic_mapping` role `core`, no review reasons on mappings, caveats, relationships, mechanisms (they are the legacy author's own statements; the summary of a mechanism is unverified chemistry like every imported record, `unreviewed`).
6. *Exam relevance*: version-agnostic, one per exam, `official`; two exams of one name → the first by ID and the record says so; a missing exam keeps the value and the reason.
7. *`reagentFamily`, `collapseIntermediates`, `_v4` are kept without a review reason* (nobody can decide them at import); `reagentFamilies` is returned for 3.11c.
8. *A relationship's `from` is the lower legacy ID*; a reaction that lists itself keeps the entry with a reason; an unknown related ID is kept and reviewed (no placeholder reaction).
9. *Naming*: a reaction with an unknown legacy field that is itself called `dropped` keeps it under `dropped`; the port's dropped leaves go under `dropped#3.11b`.
10. *`LegacyMatchContext` also carries `conceptKind` for concepts* (needed for the kind-specific matching); it is still plain data.
11. *Concept IDs are keyed by folded text alone* (§4.2), so a text used under two roles is one record whose kind is the first role's; the record carries a reason.

### Step 3.11b review (reviewer, 2026-09-30)

Verdict: step 3.11b ACCEPTED with one fix (item 11). Changes in the commit "phase-3: step 3.11b review follow-ups".

**(a) Approved as reported:** items 2-10 (kind-specific concept matching; `reactionTypes` certainty `definite`; transformations need at least one resolved class and no class is invented; created topics are roots with `order` = creation number; version-agnostic exam relevance, the first exam by ID on a name tie with a reason; `reagentFamily` / `collapseIntermediates` / `_v4` kept without review; relationship `from` = the lower legacy ID; the `dropped#3.11b` key; `conceptKind` in `LegacyMatchContext`). The three ID rows are approved.

**(b) Item 1, `LegacyImportReport.documentUnmapped`:** ACCEPTED as the recorded contract amendment. For the record: the instruction said STOP when nowhere fits; the amendment was done properly so it stands, but in later steps a STOP instruction means stop and report. Consequence for 3.11c: the preview must show `documentUnmapped` (it is only persisted inside the import batch report).

**(c) Fix, item 11 (it contradicted item 2).** Concept IDs keyed by folded text alone let one record serve two kinds, so a reaction's `reactionTypes` could name a `mechanism_class` concept. Now, for the three kind-specific roles the ID key is `<conceptKind>:<folded text>` (slug from the text, hash of the kind-qualified key; collisions settled in the concept namespace as before); the generic roles keep the folded-text key and reuse any concept of that name (dataset first, then this import; several: the first by ID, said on the reaction). The old item-11 behaviour ("one record serves both, the first role names its kind") is SUPERSEDED, not deleted (see item 11 above and the Amendments line). Tests: one text as `mechanism.type` and as `reactionType` (same reaction, and two reactions) gives two concepts of the right kinds; every `reactionTypes` / `mechanismClasses` reference names a concept of the matching kind on both fixtures in both contexts; the pinned demo count RVW-001 104 → 107 (three texts are now two concepts). MIGRATION.md §4.2 and §4.5 updated.

Added in step 3.11c: the classification step ignores a context record whose ID it would create itself (concept, named reaction, topic), so that a re-import creates it again, identical, instead of matching its own earlier output (see the step 3.11c entry and Amendments).

### Step 3.11c — preview decisions, full LegacyImportReport, legacy dry run through the real import pipeline (`src/io`) (2026-09-30)

**Delivered**
- `src/io/import-legacy.ts` (`convertLegacy`: port → IDs → compounds → reactions → classification → strict L1 → an `ImportParseResult`, the decisions and the full report), `import-session.ts` (legacy formats are converted on every preview and revise; `preview` / `revise` are async now; decisions only for legacy imports), `import-preview.ts` (decisions and `legacyReport` in the preview), `import-apply.ts` (the report is stored with the batch), `contracts.ts` (Amendments), `legacy-v6/classify-book.ts` (own records are never matches), `legacy-v6/id-space.ts` (`conceptIdsOf`). MIGRATION.md §4.3 ("How step 3.11c wires it") and the step 3.11b review entry updated.
- Test support: `legacy-import-kit.ts` (fixtures as bytes, the demo dataset as a live state, a real session over a real store), `legacy-completeness-kit.ts` (the INV-23 checker moved out of `classify-completeness.test.ts`, unchanged), `legacy-expected.ts` / `legacy-expected-cli.ts`, `legacy-synthetic.ts` (the 5,000-reaction generator). `scripts/legacy-expected.mjs` writes `data/fixtures/legacy-expected/` (by hand; a test never does); README there.

**Preview decisions.** Per hub `hub:<key>`: `class` (default) / `species`. Per distinct `reagentFamily` value `reagent_family:<value>`: **only `keep`**. Schema check: a reaction has no family field; the only family-like fields are on `Reagent` (`families`, a fixed vocabulary of 17 words, and `classIds`), and a reagent record is shared by every reaction that uses it, while a reaction's `reagentFamily` ("aq. KOH", "Cumene process") does not say which of its reagents it describes. Setting either would be inventing a meaning, so no "map" option exists; the value stays in `legacy.unmapped`. A changed decision is a revise with the FULL request (new `batchId`); apply refuses a plan whose decisions differ (`PLAN_MISMATCH`); every decision taken is in the batch report.

**Tests** (`npm run check`: 156 files, 2,582 tests; before this step, after Part A: 152 files, 2,521; new: import-legacy 16, legacy-import-pipeline 21, legacy-import-expected 11, adv-043-legacy-import 13). No timing-only failure.
- *Dry run*: both fixtures into an empty store and into a store holding `data/seed/demo.orn.json`, fixed clock and ID source: zero `notImported`, nothing quarantined, no L1-L3 ERROR (`canConfirm` true), apply succeeds, the applied state equals the previewed merged replica (captured at `replaceState`), the demo dataset survives untouched, the full report is stored with the batch, equals the preview's and is structured-clone- and JSON-safe. Empty store: v6 demo 200 records, v4 sample 29; demo store: 530 and 334.
- *Expected outputs*: 8 files (preview and full report, 2 fixtures × 2 stores) committed in `data/fixtures/legacy-expected/`; the test compares byte for byte (line endings normalised) and also checks that exactly these files exist, that building twice gives the same text and that each report file is the `legacyReport` of its preview file.
- *Re-import*: same file, same clock, both fixtures, both stores: every record `identical_skip` (`identical` = incoming, nothing new, nothing conflicting).
- *ADV-043*: representative hub SMILES (kept in `unmapped`, never a structure), a dangling compound ID (one draft placeholder), duplicate names (two species folding to "ethanol", SPC-001 on both, never merged): the whole-JSON completeness check on the mapper output for that document, every mapped record found unchanged (timestamps aside) in the applied state, every review item names a `review_required` record and every `review_required` record has an item. INV-23 for legacy, and one test per §4.9 quirk (hub example structures, r12, r14, r18, "443 K", r22, regex flags), each citing the test that covers it in depth.
- *INV-21 for legacy*: an aborted preview, a payload that cannot be converted (`LEGACY_INVALID`, the pending batch is kept), a failed snapshot at apply and a cancel all change nothing (state identity, no port call, no batch).
- *Other*: decisions are refused for non-legacy imports; a live change between preview and apply is `STALE_REVISION` (the context went stale with it); unsafe keys renamed in the file reach the preview and the stored report; an ID-space collision is listed in the report.

**Re-import with a DIFFERENT clock (reported exactly).** Same file, same store after the first apply, clock 1 800 000 000 000 ms instead of 1 790 000 000 000: on EVERY incoming record (v6 demo: 200 / 530-record sets, v4 sample: 29 / 334) the differing fields are exactly `provenance.createdAt` and `provenance.updatedAt`; nothing else. So no record is `identical`, the default resolution is `keep_current` for all of them (nothing is lost or duplicated, but the user sees a conflict per record). Smallest proposal, for the reviewer (not applied; the identical rule and the conflict analysis are unchanged): either (1) the UI/session re-uses the clock recorded in the earlier batch report when the same legacy file is imported again, or (2) the identical rule ignores `provenance.createdAt` / `updatedAt` for records whose `provenance.origin` is `legacy_import`. (1) needs no rule change.

**A defect found and fixed on the way.** With the SAME clock one demo reaction (`rxn_v6-r1`) was not identical on re-import: the first import had created a `mechanism_class` concept "electrophilic addition"; on re-import that record was in the live dataset, so the generic role `jee.concepts` found two same-named concepts and added an "ambiguous match" review reason. Fix: the classification step ignores context records whose ID it would create itself (a re-import creates them again, identical). Tested by the dry-run re-import cases.

**Bench** (`tests/perf/legacy-import.bench.ts`, run once alone; bench:baseline not run: that is 3.12; one-off conversion, in-process, fake ports; parse → preview into an empty store; mean / p99 ms against the 5,000 ms row "Import 5k reactions (parse -> preview)"): `v6-demo-export.json` (22 reactions) 16.7 / 24.1 (181 samples); synthetic legacy file with 5,000 reactions (`syntheticLegacy`) 3,808.7 / 3,911.7 (5 samples, so p99 is the maximum): under the row, with about 24% headroom. Machine drift of up to 2x between runs is known (step 3.7 review).

**Not built (3.12):** Gate C matrix scan, `bench:baseline` and `test:timing`, the packet.

**REVIEW REQUIRED**
1. *`reagentFamily` offers `keep` only* (no schema field; see above). If a reagent-family class should be referenced from the reagents a reaction uses, that is an authoring decision (Phase 6).
2. *Re-import with another clock* differs on every record in the two provenance timestamps (see above; proposal there). Same clock: fully identical.
3. *`ImportSession.preview` / `revise` are async* (image hashing); every caller and test awaits now (Amendments). `LEGACY_NOT_CONVERTED` became `LEGACY_INVALID` (the code for a payload the port refuses).
4. *The preview shows `documentUnmapped`* only as data (`ImportPreview.legacyReport`); the UI that must display it is Phase 5.
5. *Own records are never context matches* (a mapper change, MIGRATION.md §4.3): a user who renames an imported concept and re-imports gets the original created again (conflicting, `keep_current`); nothing is overwritten.
6. *The decision subject of a `reagent_family` decision is the first reaction carrying the value* (`ImportDecision.subject` is required and there is no record for the value itself).
7. *Expected outputs are about 570 KB in git* (8 files, pretty-printed for reviewable diffs).

### Step 3.11c review (reviewer, 2026-09-30)

Verdict: step 3.11c ACCEPTED. Changes in the commit "phase-3: step 3.11c review follow-ups".

**(a) Approved as reported:** `reagentFamily` offers `keep` only (no schema home; linking it to reagents is a Phase 6 authoring question, in openItems); async `preview`/`revise` and `LEGACY_INVALID`; the four report fields and `ImportBatchReport.legacyReport`; own records are never context matches (a renamed imported concept is re-created on re-import as a `keep_current` conflict; nothing is overwritten); the `reagent_family` decision subject is the first reaction carrying the value; about 570 KB of expected outputs.

**(b) Different-clock re-import: decision = proposal (1).** The identical rule and the conflict analysis stay unchanged. When the same legacy file (same SHA-256 of its bytes) was imported before, the session reuses the clock time recorded for that earlier batch. NOT built now: it lands with the import UI in Phase 5 (PROJECT_STATE openItems: the batch report must record the source file's SHA-256 and the clock time used). Today's behaviour (`keep_current` on every record, nothing lost) is recorded in MIGRATION.md §4.2.

**(c) Fix: raw NUL characters.** `docs/PROGRESS.md` held a raw U+0000 in the step 3.11b review Amendments line, so git treated the file as binary. Scan of every file under `docs/` and `data/fixtures/` (83 files: 63 json, 20 md), every `.md` and `.json` in the repo and every source file: **4 control characters in 3 files, all U+0000, the concept-ID key separator**: `docs/PROGRESS.md` line 2204 (1), `docs/MIGRATION.md` §4.2 line 54 (1; also made git call it binary), `src/io/legacy-v6/id-space.ts` lines 118 and 121 (2, in the source that uses it; found on the way, outside the requested scope, same defect). The code's separator is NUL, so all four are now written as the visible escape `\u0000` (in the source, inside a template literal and a string, the runtime value is unchanged). `id-space.ts` was stored with CRLF because git had skipped line-ending normalisation for the binary file; it is LF now (`.gitattributes`: `eol=lf`), so its diff is whole-file once, and 4 lines under `--ignore-cr-at-eol`. No other control character exists. New check: `scripts/check-control-chars.mjs` (`npm run check:control-chars`, part of `npm run check`; tests: `tests/integration/check-control-chars.test.ts`) fails on any control character other than tab, LF and CR in `.md`/`.json` under docs/, data/ and the repo root, and (added beyond the request) in source files under src/, tests/ and scripts/. `git diff --stat` shows `docs/PROGRESS.md` as text ("2 +-"); `docs/MIGRATION.md` shows "Bin" only because its previous version in HEAD is binary.
