### Step 4.1 — RPC library (`src/workers/rpc`) (2026-09-30)

**Done.** No new dependency. Client, host, ports, restart policy, retry-once, transferables, worker events, the four worker APIs typed as clients, ADR-034, the dependency rules, tests over an in-memory port pair and over real `worker_threads`, a bench.

- **Library.** `client.ts` (`createRpcClient`: a plain object built from the method specs, never a Proxy), `host.ts` (`createRpcHost`), `retry.ts` (`callWithRetry`), `helpers.ts` (`RpcHostError`, `withTransfer`, `taskOptionsFrom`, `messageOf`), `browser-port.ts` (`browserWorkerPort`, `workerScopePort`), `index.ts`. Test support: `src/test-support/rpc-memory-port.ts` (clones every message with `structuredClone`, so every param and result in the in-memory tests is proven cloneable), `rpc-node-port.ts` (`worker_threads` adapters, `spawnTestWorker`), `rpc-test-api.ts` (`TestApi`, `TEST_SPECS`), `tests/fixtures/rpc/worker.ts`.
- **Rules held.** A response after a cancel is dropped and reported (`dropped_response`), never delivered. A crash (`error`, `messageerror`, `exit`) fails every pending call with `WORKER_CRASHED`, then restarts; `maxRestarts` (default 3) counts restarts in a row and is reset by a successful response; beyond it the client stays down and every call answers `WORKER_CRASHED` as a value. A timeout answers `TIMEOUT`, terminates and restarts the worker (a synchronous task cannot see a cancel, D1) and fails the other pending calls. `callWithRetry` retries once, after the restart and the hook, only when the first answer was `WORKER_CRASHED` and the spec says idempotent (never after `TIMEOUT` or `CANCELLED`). More than `maxInFlight` (16) calls answer `BUSY`; the host has its own cap (64). Host exceptions answer `INTERNAL` with the message only. A param that cannot be cloned answers `BAD_REQUEST` before anything is sent.
- **Worker fixture: tsx worked.** The real-thread tests run the real host, TypeScript with `@/` imports, in a `worker_threads` thread started with `execArgv: ['--import', 'tsx']` (tsx is already a dev dependency; resolves the tsconfig paths; about 0.9 s per thread start on this workstation). No `.mjs` host was needed.
- **Progress lost silently (recorded as asked).** `RpcCallContext` is assignable to `TaskOptions` only because every `TaskOptions` field is optional; the context has `progress()`, `TaskOptions` has `onProgress`. Passing a context straight through compiled and never forwarded a single progress report. `taskOptionsFrom(ctx)` converts; the RUNTIME tests (`host.test.ts`, `rpc-worker-threads.test.ts`) prove that progress sent by a function taking `TaskOptions` (`countWithTask`) reaches the caller's `onProgress` through the real client and host. No Phase 3 caller was affected: nothing hosted io in a worker yet.
- **Tests.** In-memory (fake timers, no sleeps): `client.test.ts` 24, `host.test.ts` 9, `retry.test.ts` 5, `browser-port.test.ts` 2; real threads `tests/integration/rpc-worker-threads.test.ts` 7 (crash by process exit, hang → timeout → terminate → restart, a busy synchronous task cannot see a cancel and ends by timeout and termination, cooperative cancel, BUSY, transfer of a 1 MB ArrayBuffer detached on the sender, progress); type tests `tests/types/rpc-contracts.test.ts` 2 (`RpcClient<Api>` exists for all four APIs and answers `RpcResult`; `RpcMethodSpecs` names every method). `npm run check`: 165 files, 2,661 tests (was 159 / 2,612), dependency rules green.
- **Coverage.** `src/workers` joins the 80% lines threshold (`vitest.config.ts`, `test:coverage`): 99.2% lines. `npm run test:coverage` run once: thresholds met.
- **Dependency rules** (`.dependency-cruiser.cjs`): `workers-not-persistence`, `services-not-workers` (test files exempt), `worker-entry-chem|layout|search|compute` (each entry imports its own service and core only; compute also io). A deliberate violation failed `npm run lint:deps` with 4 errors (persistence, io and workers → persistence, workers/chem → io) and was removed.
- **Bench** (`tests/perf/rpc.bench.ts`, real `worker_threads`, one run alone, other terminals closed; informal, no budget row; `budgets.mjs` entries added; not `bench:baseline`, not `bench:compare`): small payload mean 0.048 ms / p99 0.131 ms; ChangeSet-sized (about 8 KB echoed) 0.094 / 0.276 ms; about 1 MB (10,000 records echoed, cloned both ways) 47.7 / 54.7 ms. The 1 MB round trip is over 50 ms on the receiving side: state-sized transfers are the D3 measurement of steps 4.2c/4.2d, and large buffers go by `transfer`, not by clone.
- **Amendments** (CLAUDE.md §7; see the Amendments list): the approved Q5 (a)-(g), Q7 `WorkerEvent`, Q8 rules, plus one consequence of "as a value": `RpcClient` methods answer `RpcResult<T>` instead of a bare promise (REVIEW REQUIRED 1). The four worker API method lists are unchanged (their steps change them).

**REVIEW REQUIRED**
1. `RpcClient` calls answer `RpcResult<T>` (`{ ok: true, value }` or `{ ok: false, error }`) and never reject. The approved text said "answers WORKER_CRASHED as a value"; a bare `Promise<T>` cannot do that, so the client type changed (nothing used it yet). Alternative: reject with an `RpcError`.
2. Default timeouts (ADR-034): chem and layout 2 s; compute and search values are proposals (about ten times the budget row, because a timeout costs a restart and the worker's state).
3. `callWithRetry` does not retry after `TIMEOUT`, only after `WORKER_CRASHED` (ADR-034). D4 says "retry once after a restart"; a timeout also restarts.
4. `maxRestarts` (3) counts restarts in a row and resets on a successful response; a worker that crashes on every start stays down. There is no timeout on the `onRestart` hook itself: a hook that awaits something that never resolves holds every other call (calls made through `raw` inside it have their own timeouts).
5. Cancel: the host sends NO answer for a cancelled call (the client has already answered `CANCELLED`); `dropped_response` therefore only appears if a host misbehaves or a message crosses a cancel.

**Open items.** The browser adapters (`browserWorkerPort`, `workerScopePort`) are unit-tested against stand-ins only; their real-browser test is Phase 5 (D7). `rpc.bench.ts` entries have no baseline until the Gate E baseline (`bench:compare` reports them as new).

### Step 4.1 review (reviewer, 2026-09-30)

**Verdict: ACCEPTED.** Decisions on the five REVIEW REQUIRED items of step 4.1 and one note:
1. `RpcClient` methods answer `RpcResult<T>` and never reject: approved (errors are values, CLAUDE.md §6).
2. The ADR-034 default timeouts: approved as proposed; the REVIEW REQUIRED mark is removed.
3. No retry after `TIMEOUT`, only after `WORKER_CRASHED`: approved (a call that timed out would most likely time out again). D4 in IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN §2 and ADR-034 now say so.
4. `maxRestarts` counting restarts in a row: approved, with follow-ups a and b below.
5. No answer from the host for a cancelled call: approved.
6. ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md is CRLF (mixed) on disk: Windows checkout only; nothing to do.

Follow-ups, done in commit "phase-4.1: review follow-ups" (`src/workers/rpc`):
- **a. Restart-hook timeout.** New option `restartHookTimeoutMs` (default 30 s). On expiry the client emits `restart_hook_timeout`, the calls waiting for the restart answer `WORKER_CRASHED`, and the worker is terminated and restarted again, so the failed restart counts towards `maxRestarts`. A hook that is still running when its worker is replaced holds a `raw` client bound to its worker generation: its late calls answer `WORKER_CRASHED` and never reach the new worker.
- **b. `gave_up`.** New `WorkerEvent` `{ type: 'gave_up', reason: 'restart_cap' | 'start_failed', restarts }`, emitted when the client goes down for good (restart cap, or no worker can be created, also at the first start).
- **c. Timeout victims.** Already the behaviour (a timeout terminates the worker, so the OTHER pending calls answer `WORKER_CRASHED`, not `TIMEOUT`); the existing client test asserted it for a raw call. New test in `retry.test.ts`: the timeout of one call, an idempotent victim through `callWithRetry` is retried on the restarted worker and succeeds.
- **Tests.** `client.test.ts` +5 (hook timeout with waiting call and a second attempt; default 30 s boundary and an in-time hook untouched; hook timeouts reach the cap and give up; stale hook cannot reach the new worker; `gave_up` for cap and start failure), `retry.test.ts` +1; one existing assertion extended (`gave_up` after the repeated-timeout cap). Contract additions (CLAUDE.md §7): see the Amendments list.

### Step 4.2a — Store accepts precomputed findings (`src/core/commands`, `src/core/validation`) (2026-09-30)

**Done.** `replaceState` and `reload` take optional precomputed findings (Phase 4 contract decisions Q3). No worker, no dependency, no schema change. Without the parameter the behaviour is unchanged (the existing store, remote, INV-12 and INV-21 tests are green unchanged).

- **`RULE_SET_ID`** (`src/core/validation/rule-set.ts`): `rs_` + `hashParts` of the sorted (rule ID, level) pairs of the L1 schema rule (`SCHEMA`, level 1) and every registered L2 and L3 rule. Computed at load, never hand-edited; the rule order and severities do not change it; adding, removing, re-levelling or renaming a rule does. `RULE_SET_LEVELS` maps rule ID to level for the store's check.
- **`PrecomputedFindings`** (`contracts.ts`): `{ findings, ruleSet, revision, recordCounts }`. **`checkPrecomputed`** (`src/core/commands/precomputed.ts`, pure, linear in the findings): the value has the shape; `ruleSet === RULE_SET_ID`; `revision` is the revision the store will adopt (`replaceState`: current + 1; `reload`: the argument); `recordCounts` equal the new state's per kind (and name no unknown kind); every finding is well formed, has a known severity, is about a record that exists in the new state, is level 1-3 and of a rule that the rule set has at that level (a finding that says a rule is at another level is refused too); no finding ID twice. The first failing check is named in the message. Refusal is `CommandErrorCode 'FINDINGS_MISMATCH'`, before the index build (a refusal costs nothing), and the store is unchanged.
- **Store.** With `precomputed`, `replaceState` keeps its ERROR gate on the precomputed findings (same `VALIDATION_FAILED` value as the in-process path, tested equal); `reload` keeps ADR-033 (no gate); indexes are built on the main thread as before (D2). No fallback: without `verifyPrecomputed` the store never calls `validateAll` for these calls (counted in a test). The option `verifyPrecomputed` (default false, tests only) also runs `validateAll` and refuses `FINDINGS_MISMATCH` if the result differs (compared as a set, order-insensitive). `NOT_FOLLOWER` and `READ_ONLY` come first, unchanged, whatever `precomputed` says.
- **Tests.** `rule-set.test.ts` 7; `precomputed.test.ts` 45 (every refusal reason once, for `replaceState` and for `reload`, each proving state, revision, index revision, finding store (same array), history, `canUndo` and subscribers unchanged; verify option; ERROR gate; reload without gate; guards); `tests/property/precomputed-findings.test.ts` 9 (fixtures, demo, six shapes at the initial tier, random command sequences with undo and redo: `replaceState` and `reload` with precomputed = `validateAll`, `verifyPrecomputed` on, leave state, revision, ChangeSet, history and finding store equal to the in-process path; no state produced a repeated finding ID, so the unique-ID check never refused valid `validateAll` output). Kit: `src/test-support/precomputed-kit.ts`. `npm run check`: 168 files, 2,728 tests (was 165 / 2,661 at the end of 4.1; +6 from the 4.1 follow-ups, +61 here). On this workstation the default 16 Vitest workers make heavy tests time out under load (different tests every run, each green alone); the recorded run used `--maxWorkers=4`.
- **Bench** (`tests/perf/precomputed.bench.ts`, design tier dense, run once alone, 5 samples; informal, no budget row; `budgets.mjs` entries added; not `bench:baseline`, not `bench:compare`), mean / p99 ms: `replaceState` in-process 2,851.4 / 2,895.6, precomputed 1,003.3 / 1,198.0; `reload` in-process 2,787.8 / 2,801.2, precomputed 992.1 / 1,194.8. **About 35% of the old main-thread cost remains** (2.8x faster). What is left is the index build (0.5-1.2 s in a separate measurement, D2) and the finding-store commit (0.07-0.19 s); the ~0.9-2.8 s of `validateAll` leaves the main thread once 4.2b exists. The timed part excludes producing the findings (the worker's job).
- **Not built.** Q10 (b) (clearing L4 findings of vanished subjects in the commit step): the plan's 4.2a "Must prove" cell names it, but the Q10 scan lands it in 4.3b, where L4 findings first exist, and this step's specifics do not list it (REVIEW REQUIRED 1). `applyRemote` is unchanged (4.2d, only if adopted).
- **Amendments** (CLAUDE.md §7): see the Amendments list. Docs: ARCHITECTURE.md §2.4 sentence, ADR-034 bullet.

**REVIEW REQUIRED**
1. Q10 (b) stays with 4.3b (see above). Confirm, or move it into 4.2b.
2. `checkPrecomputed` also refuses a finding whose level differs from its rule's level in the rule set, and a finding with an unknown severity or a malformed shape. Stricter than the approved list, all cheap; a worker built from the same rule set can never trip them.
3. The `SCHEMA` (L1) rule is part of the rule set, so a change to L1 finding IDs or levels would change `RULE_SET_ID`; Zod schema changes alone do not (they bump `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION`).
4. Completeness of the precomputed findings (none missing) is not checkable cheaply; it rests on the worker's replica (4.2b). Only `verifyPrecomputed` proves it, in tests.

**Open items.** PROJECT_STATE openItems marked "-> step 4.2a": (a) "Phase 4: replaceState runs validateAll on the main thread": the store side is closed; waits for 4.2b (the producer) and 4.2d (boot, indexes built once); (b) "Step 3.7: preview validates the merged replica and replaceState validates it again": the store side is closed; waits for 4.2b and 4.2c (the worker returns the merged state and its findings).

### Step 4.2b — Compute worker core (`src/workers/compute`) (2026-09-30)

**Done.** The replica, its handlers, the main-thread sync, the method specs, tests at every level, a bench. No new dependency, no schema change. `adoptPending` is NOT here (moved to 4.2c, step 4.2a review); the import, export and snapshot methods keep their old shape and answer `NOT_READY` as a value until 4.2c.

- **Replica** (`replica.ts`, pure and synchronous). `load(state, revision)` builds the full index engine, runs `validateAll`, fills its own `FindingStore`, and answers `PrecomputedFindings` (findings, `RULE_SET_ID`, revision, record counts): exactly what `replaceState` and `reload` take. A refused load (`BAD_REQUEST`: bad revision, missing tables) leaves the old replica untouched. `apply(changes)` follows the store's commit step: Immer patches, the index engine's `apply`, `validateTouchedByRule(next, indexes, touched, previous, replica.findings)`; `previous` is never omitted. The revision rule is the core index engine's (one step ahead; a `'remote'` ChangeSet any forward span); otherwise the VALUE `{ ok: false, code: 'REPLICA_STALE', have }` and nothing changes. A ChangeSet whose patches cannot be applied throws (`INTERNAL`), leaves state and revision as they were and rebuilds the consumed index handle. `validateAll(revision)` and `findRoutes(revision, query)` answer the same refusal for another revision; before the first load every call is `NOT_READY`. `findRoutes` times its budget with the injected `nowMs` (no `Date.now`); the call's abort signal replaces `query.limits.signal`.
- **Contract change** (`src/workers/rpc/contracts.ts`, see Amendments): `loadReplica` answers `PrecomputedFindings`; `applyChanges`, `validateAll`, `findRoutes` answer `ReplicaAnswer<...>` (`{ ok: true, ... }` or `ReplicaStale`); `findRoutes` takes `{ revision, query }`; `WorkerEvent` gains `replica_status`. Specs (`specs.ts`): idempotent `loadReplica`, `validateAll`, `findRoutes`, `importPreview`, `buildExport`; NOT idempotent `applyChanges`. Timeouts as in ADR-034.
- **Host** (`handlers.ts`, `host.ts`, `entry.ts`): `startComputeHost(port, clock)` is the real host over the real handlers; `entry.ts` is the browser entry (creating the worker is Phase 5, D7).
- **Sync** (`sync.ts`, main thread). `createReplicaSync({ factory, store, ... })` builds the client itself (so `onRestart` can be wired), subscribes to the store, forwards every ChangeSet (commit, undo, redo, replace, remote) in order, one at a time. **Reloads instead of forwarding** (one `loadReplica` of the store's CURRENT state, the queue dropped) when the worker answers `REPLICA_STALE` or refuses in any other way (`INTERNAL`, `BAD_REQUEST`...), or when more than **100 ChangeSets or 2,000 touched records** are waiting (the bound, chosen from the bench below). After a crash or timeout the client restarts the worker and the `onRestart` hook loads the store's current state before any other call; the sync then waits for that hook instead of re-sending the apply (never retried), and drops queued ChangeSets already inside the loaded state. A hook that fails, or a worker that crashes during the hook, starts over (the client's own cap and hook timeout apply). Status: `status()` value (`state`, `reason`, `replicaRevision`, `storeRevision`, `queued`, `reloads`), `settled()`, and a `replica_status` WorkerEvent on every change of state (`reloading` at creation, `in_sync`, `down`). `down` = the client gave up (`gave_up`) or the worker refused a reload; the queue is dropped, the store subscription ends, and later commits are not an error.
- **Tests** (+40: 168 files / 2,728 tests -> 172 / 2,768). `replica.test.ts` 16, `sync.test.ts` 15 (in memory; every message cloned), `tests/property/replica.test.ts` 5 (P-07 style: replica == store (state, revision, every index answer, findings vs the store AND vs `validateAll`) after random command sequences with undo, redo and a replaceState, plus a non-vacuity test that all four causes are reached; P-11 style on the ADV-012 and like-water hub shapes with the same raw-recipe edits as `validation-scope.test.ts`, moved to `src/test-support/scope-edits.ts` without changing any assertion; the findings `loadReplica` answers accepted by `replaceState` and `reload` with `verifyPrecomputed` on, the ERROR gate behaving as in process), `tests/integration/compute-worker-threads.test.ts` 3 (a real `worker_threads` thread running the real compute host under tsx: follows the store; an induced crash under `validateAll` (the thread exits when that request arrives, via a port wrapper in the fixture only) restarts, reloads and retries once; an induced crash under `applyChanges` reloads and does not repeat the apply), `tests/types` +1. "Previous never omitted": a spy validator records `previous` for commit, undo, redo, replace and remote ChangeSets (all `validateTouchedByRule`, all defined, `validateTouched` and `scopeOf` never called) with a negative control. Every param and result passes `structuredClone` (the in-memory port clones every message; the replica tests clone explicitly too). Five hand mutants (skip rule removed, `previous` dropped, no wait after a crash, off-by-one bound, remote span refused) were each killed. `npm run check` as ONE command at the default cap (4 workers): 172 files, 2,768 tests, Vitest 149 s (whole check 3 min 30 s).
- **Bench** (`tests/perf/compute-replica.bench.ts`, run once alone; informal; `budgets.mjs` entries added; not `bench:baseline`, not `bench:compare`), design tier, mean / p99 ms. Worker code (no thread): applyChanges ordinary edit 1.12 / 2.32; hub record edit (rename of the 1,000-referrer hub, ONE record touched, previous passed) 16.5 / 21.8; hub edit (`replaceReferences`, ~1,020 records touched) 448.4 / 465.6; `validateAll` dense 298.0 / 307.7 and hub_explosion 254.2 / 262.0 (budget row < 3 s: met by 10x); load (index build + validateAll) dense 831.3 / 850.7. Real thread: applyChanges round trip, ordinary edit 1.56 / 5.36; `loadReplica` of the whole design-tier state 1,308 / 1,327; the sender's blocking part of that clone (`v8.serialize`) 56.8 / 190.8. **Drift:** an earlier run of the same file the same session (also alone) read about 2.3x slower on every entry (ordinary 2.42 / 4.73, hub edit 1,044 / 1,085, validateAll 712 and 591, load 1,979, thread loadReplica 3,097, serialize 108.9); the backlog bound uses the range. A scratch breakdown of the hub edit (not committed): patches about 140 ms, part-1 index apply about 120 ms, full index apply about 420 ms, `validateTouchedByRule` about 300-460 ms. **D2 not measured:** the core indexes are class instances with private fields and Maps of a lineage, not plain data; reaching in would change a contract.
- **Not built.** `adoptPending` (4.2c); import, export, snapshot methods (4.2c, 4.2d); creating the sync and a status banner in the app (Phase 5); Q10 (b) (4.3b).
- **Amendments** (CLAUDE.md §7): see the Amendments list. Docs: ADR-034 bullet.

**REVIEW REQUIRED**
1. `findRoutes` takes `{ revision, query }` instead of a bare `RouteQuery` (the plan says "as they are", and also "refused when the revision asked is not the replica's"; a bare query has no revision). Results of `loadReplica`, `applyChanges`, `validateAll`, `findRoutes` changed as described above.
2. The replica keeps the FULL index engine (route search needs the cross-record indexes; the store keeps the part-1 engine). It makes a hub edit cost about 300 ms more in the worker. Alternative: maintain the part-1 indexes and build the full ones lazily per revision for `findRoutes`. Kept because it is simple and off the main thread; the hub edit is the rare case.
3. Backlog bounds: 100 ChangeSets AND 2,000 touched records, from the bench. The count alone would have been wrong (a hub edit is about 1,000 times an ordinary one).
4. A reload the worker refuses (not a crash) ends in `down`, not in a retry loop. `down` is final for a sync; how the app recovers or shows it is Phase 5.
5. `importPreview` and `buildExport` answer `NOT_READY` (a value) until 4.2c.
6. The real-thread tests start `startComputeHost`, the function the browser entry calls; `entry.ts` itself needs `self`. Only the port differs (and, for the induced crash, the fixture's port wrapper).
7. `in_sync` means "following": ChangeSets may still be queued (`queued`, `settled()` says when caught up).

**Open items.** See PROJECT_STATE openItems (Phase 9 exception 5 closed on the worker path; the D3 clone measurements; app wiring in Phase 5).

### Step 4.2c — Import in the compute worker, snapshotBuild, the snapshot bytes entry point, adoptPending (2026-09-30)

**Done.** The import session runs in the compute worker; main's apply orchestrator drives it through ports; the persistence adapter has ONE bytes entry point; the replica adopts a pending state instead of receiving the merged state twice. No new dependency, no schema change. Export stays `NOT_READY` (moved to 4.2e, step 4.2b review). Everything a-e of the master prompt is built and tested; nothing was left over.

- **Worker import host** (`src/workers/compute/import-host.ts`, `ids.ts`). `importPreview({ source: Blob, fileName, request?, ids })`, `importRevise({ batchId, request, ids })`, `importCancel`, `importPrepareApply({ batchId, plan, baseRevision })`. The unchanged Phase 3 `createImportSession` runs over a store view of the REPLICA; one call at a time; the pending batch (at most one) lives in the worker. A revise or prepare against a replica that is not at the revision the preview was built on is a value (`STALE_REVISION`, batch consumed). The `IdFactory` is rebuilt per call from `{ nowIso, seed }` (`idFactoryFrom`, mulberry32; bad data is `BAD_REQUEST`); the worker has no clock or RNG of its own for IDs. Legacy files use the same session. The file crosses as a Blob (structured clone shares its bytes; a Blob cannot be in a transfer list), the asset buffers of `importPrepareApply` are transferred.
- **io** (`src/io/import-prepare.ts`, `import-apply.ts`, `import-session.ts`). `ImportSession.prepareApply(batchId, plan, liveRevision)` is the pure half of `apply` (UNKNOWN_BATCH, PLAN_MISMATCH, STALE_REVISION (consumes), BLOCKED; nothing written, batch stays pending), `consume`, `pendingBaseRevision`. The report split into `buildReportDraft` (everything but the time and the snapshot ID) + `completeReport`, `quarantineDraftsOf`, `storeImportReport`; `applyImport` uses them (Phase 3 tests unchanged and green). `ImportSessionDeps` write ports are optional: a session without them cannot `apply` (REJECTED as a value).
- **Replica pending state** (`replica.ts`): `prepare(state, revision)` (index build + `validateAll` once, keeps the result as PENDING, answers `PrecomputedFindings`), `adopt(revision)` (swap; refusals `NO_PENDING` / `PENDING_REVISION` / `REPLICA_STALE` as values), `drop()`, `pendingRevision()`. A load or an ordinary apply drops it.
- **snapshotBuild** (`snapshot-build.ts`): `snapshotBuild({ revision, reason, exportedAt })`, bytes of the replica, transferred; `REPLICA_STALE` as a value. A worker cannot import persistence, so the few lines of `buildSnapshot` are repeated here; a test proves byte equality with the codec.
- **The bytes entry point: `storeSnapshotBytes(input)`** on the persistence adapter (`SnapshotBytesInput { bytes, reason, revision, schemaVersion, note? }`, result `{ ok: true, summary } | { ok: false, code: 'STALE_REVISION' | 'BAD_BYTES', message, current? }`). Stores the bytes as they are in the write queue's order; `revision` must be the adapter's current revision (new `WriteQueue.currentRevision()`: the newest revision the queue was given). Bytes from `snapshotBuild` read back with `readSnapshot` and `restoreSnapshot` restores the same state (tests).
- **Apply orchestrator** (`src/workers/compute/import-flow.ts`, ports only): settle the sync, `importPrepareApply`, STALE check, `snapshotBuild('pre_import')`, `storeSnapshotBytes`, UNKNOWN_BATCH check (cancel or a newer preview while the snapshot was written), STALE check, `sync.expectAdoption(revision + 1)`, asset stage, ONE `store.replaceState(merged, 'import', precomputed)`, batch report and quarantine rows. Every failure deletes the snapshot (when stored), clears the expectation and sends `dropPending`; a store refusal changes nothing (Phase 3 behaviour). It answers the same `ImportApplyResult`. A worker crash on the prepare call is `UNKNOWN_BATCH`, on the snapshot call `SNAPSHOT_FAILED`; nothing changed either way.
- **Sync** (`sync.ts`): `expectAdoption(revision)` / `clearAdoption()`; on the `replace` ChangeSet at the announced revision it sends `adoptPending` instead of forwarding the ChangeSet (no second whole-state transfer); a refusal or failure makes it reload.
- **Tests** (+9 files, +92: 172 files / 2,768 tests -> 181 / 2,860). `tests/integration/compute-import.test.ts` (11): the SAME scenario in process (Phase 3 session, twin store, same ID script) and through the worker, plans, results and final states equal: ADV-042 (identical_skip, keep_current, replace, import_as_copy), ADV-030 (blocked, BLOCKED refusal, exclude offenders, apply), ADV-043 (v6 demo export and v4 sample, legacy report, nothing not imported), ADV-045 import part (history cleared, redo unavailable, `snapshotBuild` bytes read back with `readSnapshot` and restored to the same state), adoption (one `loadReplica`, no `applyChanges` for the import, replica == store: state, revision, findings, indexes; a refused adoption reloads). `compute-import-failures.test.ts` (23): INV-21 at every failure point (snapshot not stored, stale, damaged bytes, store refuses the findings, `replaceState` throws, a commit while the snapshot is stored, a cancel while it is stored, `snapshotBuild` fails, replica stale): live state (same reference), revision, history, stored snapshots, batch reports, persisted state and replica all as before, no pending state left; STALE_REVISION (apply and revise), cancel, PLAN_MISMATCH, BLOCKED, crash between preview and apply and under `snapshotBuild` (UNKNOWN_BATCH / nothing changed), structuredClone of every result, per-call IDs, parse refusals and CANCELLED as values, asset bytes transferred (detached in the worker; a failed apply ends such a batch), report not stored. `compute-import-threads.test.ts` (2): a REAL `worker_threads` thread, a small import end to end, and a thread that exits on the prepare request. `tests/property/import-worker-roundtrip.test.ts` (27): P-02 full through the worker (export in process until 4.2e), fixtures, demo, six generator shapes, json replace_all / merge and pkg. `persistence-snapshot-bytes.test.ts` (4), `src/io/import-prepare.test.ts` (4), `replica-pending.test.ts` (7), `snapshot-build.test.ts` (5), `ids.test.ts` (9). A mutation check (adoption disabled, snapshot delete removed) failed 7 of the first 34 tests, then was reverted.
- **Bench** (`tests/perf/compute-import.bench.ts`, run once alone; informal except the §2 row; `budgets.mjs` entries added; not `bench:baseline`, not `bench:compare`), design tier, mean / p99 ms. Worker code (no thread): `importPreview` parse -> preview total, dense into an EMPTY store 3,245.5 / 3,540.3 (row < 5 s: met), hub_explosion into a design-tier store with every ID conflicting 1,532.9 / 1,630.7 (met; the Gate E watch item: about 1.5 s, was 1.17 s in process in step 3.7, within run-to-run drift); `importPrepareApply` (the merged state's replica) 2,334.6 / 2,467.4 (dense) and 1,364.3 / 2,115.6 (hub_explosion, rme 55%); `snapshotBuild` 473.0 / 514.0. Real thread (dense, empty store): `importPreview` round trip 1,718.0 / 1,847.1, `importPrepareApply` round trip 1,440.5 / 1,531.0 (the merged state and findings come back), and the receiving side of the merged state measured as `v8.deserialize`: **130.9 / 150.8 ms** (blocks the caller; D3). **Drift:** the worker-code rows ran before the thread rows and read slower (prepare 2.3 s in the worker code, 1.4 s in the thread round trip that includes it); the machine drifts by about 2x over a run, so rows are not comparable across describes.

**REVIEW REQUIRED**
1. `snapshotBuild` takes `exportedAt` (main's wall-clock time). The plan says `{ revision, reason }`; the worker's clock is monotonic (`performance.now`), and the document carries an export time. Smallest alternative: drop it and stamp the epoch (would make snapshots carry a wrong date).
2. `importRevise` takes `ids` (the plan says `{ batchId, request }`); a revise mints a new batch ID, which needs the per-call data.
3. `importPreview` takes `source: Blob` and `fileName` separately, as the master prompt wrote it (the old stub took an `ImportSource`).
4. A batch that carried asset bytes is used up by `importPrepareApply`'s transfer (the buffers left the worker): after a failed apply of such a batch the user previews again. A batch without assets stays pending after a failed apply, as in Phase 3. Alternative: copy the buffers instead of transferring them.
5. `snapshot-build.ts` repeats the body of `buildSnapshot` (workers do not import persistence). Alternative: a shared package for the codec (a new layer). The duplicated constants are the generator (`organic-reaction-navigator` 0.1.0, the adapter's default) and zlib level 6; the equality test guards the second, not a changed default generator.
6. `storeSnapshotBytes` checks the zlib header only; it does not inflate (up to 200 MB). A wrong `schemaVersion` or a truncated stream is found by `readSnapshot`/restore, as for any stored snapshot.
7. The main thread's receive of the merged state costs about 131 ms mean (151 ms p99) at the design tier (v8.deserialize proxy), on top of the 57-191 ms serialise part of every whole-state send (step 4.2b). Not optimised (D3); judged at Gate E.
8. `prepareApply` builds the merged state's replica (index build + `validateAll`, about 1.4-2.3 s in the worker) although the preview already validated the merged state with the part-1 engine: preview and prepare validate twice in the worker (off the main thread). Alternative: keep the preview's findings and the full index engine from the preview stage (a bigger change to `validateMerged`).

**Open items.** See PROJECT_STATE openItems (D3 receive-side numbers; Phase 5 wiring of the flow and its ports; 4.2d restore and boot; 4.2e export).

### Step 4.2c review (reviewer, 2026-09-30)

**ACCEPTED.** Decisions on the eight REVIEW REQUIRED items of step 4.2c:
1. `snapshotBuild` takes `exportedAt` from main: approved. 2 and 3 (`importRevise` takes `ids`; `importPreview` takes a Blob and `fileName`): approved.
4. Transferring asset buffers (a batch with assets is used up by prepare): approved, on one condition (follow-up a).

- **a.** A failed apply of a batch whose asset buffers were transferred now says so: the result's message ends with "The file data of this preview was handed over to the failed apply and is gone; run the preview again." (`PREVIEW_AGAIN`, `import-flow.ts`; the code stays the real reason), and every later `importPrepareApply` or `importRevise` on that batch answers `UNKNOWN_BATCH` with "the file data of this preview was handed over to an apply that failed and is gone; run the preview again" (`import-host.ts`, one remembered batch ID, forgotten at the next preview). ### Step 4.2d — Snapshot decode and restore, boot and follower in the compute worker (2026-09-30)

**Done: a, b, c, e and f in full; d partly (follower reload and promotion built; the hub-edit `applyRemote` variant measured, qualifies, NOT built).** No new dependency, no schema change.

- **Restore in the worker (a).** `snapshotDecode({ bytes, id, schemaVersion, baseRevision })` (`src/workers/compute/snapshot-decode.ts`, `handlers.ts`): the same bounded inflate and envelope check as the codec, the summary's schema version against the document's (CORRUPT), migration by the worker's injected runner (`ComputeHandlerOptions.runner`, default the app's), strict L1, duplicate IDs, then `replica.prepare(state, baseRevision + 1)` (index build and `validateAll` once). Answers `{ ok: true, state, precomputed, steps }` or the Phase 3 codes (`CORRUPT`, `MIGRATION_FAILED` with the runner's error, `INVALID` with the same capped issue list), `REPLICA_STALE`, `BUSY`. New persistence entry point **`readSnapshotBytes(id)`** (`SnapshotBytesReadResult`; `readSnapshot` now uses it, behaviour unchanged). Main side **`createComputeRestore`** (`restore-flow.ts`, ports only): read bytes, settle the sync, decode, `pre_restore` through `snapshotBuild` + `storeSnapshotBytes`, at most 3 attempts when the live data changed (the decode is redone for the new revision, because the pending state is prepared for one revision), `expectAdoption`, ONE `replaceState(state, 'restore', precomputed)`. A store refusal removes the `pre_restore` snapshot, clears the expectation and sends `dropPending`. One pending slot: a prepared import refuses a decode, a decoded snapshot refuses `importPrepareApply` (`BUSY`, mapped to `REJECTED` in both flows).
- **Boot (b).** `BootPorts.validate?(state, revision): Promise<PrecomputedFindings>` (`boot-validate.ts`); default the in-process `validateAll`, so every existing caller and test is unchanged (205 boot and persistence tests unchanged and green). `open()` builds no index when the port answers; the migrated path awaits it before `replaceAll` and keeps its ERROR gate. New store creation option **`StoreOptions.precomputed`** (checked by the same `checkPrecomputed`; a mismatch is a `RangeError`, a programmer error because boot already refused a bad answer). `bootReport` gains `precomputed` and `validation { where: 'worker' | 'main' | 'none', reason }`. Compute implementation **`createBootReplica`** (`boot-port.ts`) over `loadReplica`; `createReplicaSync({ adopt })` starts from the replica boot loaded (no second `loadReplica`), and `ReplicaSync.noteLoaded(revision)` lets a later validation (follower reload, promotion) tell the sync that the reload's ChangeSet must not be forwarded. `recordCountsOf` and `precomputedFor` moved into core (`src/core/commands/precomputed.ts`) so persistence and the worker share them. Promotion runs the same boot with the same port.
- **Worker unavailable (c).** Start failed, gave up, crash, refusal or a wrong answer: boot validates in process, `bootReport.validation` says `main` and why, and a `validation_fallback` WorkerEvent is emitted. The data always opens.
- **Follower (d, reload part).** `FollowerOptions.validate`: a full reload validates in the worker and calls `store.reload(state, revision, precomputed)`; a failure or a wrong answer falls back in process with a `validation_fallback` FollowerEvent; `loaded` carries `precomputed`. Promotion reloads with `bootReport.precomputed`.
- **Amendments recorded below.** `SnapshotDecodeParams/Answer`, `snapshotDecode`, `ImportPrepareRefused` gains `BUSY`, `WorkerEvent.validation_fallback`, `readSnapshotBytes`, `BootPorts.validate`, `BootReport.precomputed/validation`, `StoreOptions.precomputed`, `FollowerOptions.validate`, `FollowerEvent.validation_fallback` and `loaded.precomputed`, `ReplicaSyncOptions.adopt`, `ReplicaSync.noteLoaded`, core `recordCountsOf` / `precomputedFor` / `checkPrecomputed` exports.
- **Tests** (189 files, 2,941 tests, check green). `tests/integration/compute-restore.test.ts` (8): the SAME scenario through the in-process `restoreSnapshot` (twin store) and through the worker: result, state, revision, history cleared, findings, stored snapshots, persisted state; adoption (one decode, one build, one `adoptPending`, no `loadReplica`, no `applyChanges`); structuredClone; ADV-045 migration part (injected runner, steps reported, the real runner on a newer version); the `pre_restore` retry (one change, three changes). `compute-restore-failures.test.ts` (19): INV-21 at every failure point (NOT_FOUND, CORRUPT in four forms, MIGRATION_FAILED, INVALID with capped issues and duplicates, NEWER_VERSION, store refuses (VALIDATION_FAILED), READ_ONLY, snapshot cannot be stored / damaged / stale three times, store refuses the findings, `replaceState` throws, `snapshotBuild` cannot serialise, a crash while decoding, a stopped sync, BUSY both ways): state (same reference), revision, history, stored snapshots, persisted state, replica (no pending state, equals the store). `snapshot-decode-equality.test.ts` (19): the guard for the repeated decode code (values, messages, size cap, issue cap). `compute-boot.test.ts` (17): boot through the port equals in-process (three datasets, quarantine), store from `precomputed`, migrated boot and its gate, indexes counted (in process 1 + 1; through the port 0 on main in `open()`, 1 in the worker, 1 in the store), five fallbacks, adoption (one `loadReplica` in all). `compute-follower.test.ts` (5): reload through the worker (one load per reload, no `applyChanges` for the reload's ChangeSet), a worker that is gone, a wrong answer, promotion through the port, promotion with a worker that is gone. `compute-restore-threads.test.ts` (3): a REAL `worker_threads` thread: boot (one thread, one load), restore end to end, a thread that exits while decoding. `tests/integration/snapshot-builder-equality.test.ts` and `tests/types/restore-result.test.ts`. Mutation checks: `noteLoaded` disabled failed the follower reload test; the sync's start-from-boot disabled failed the boot adoption test (both reverted). ADV-041 disk part and INV-28 guards unchanged (their suites untouched and green).
- **Bench** (`tests/perf/compute-restore.bench.ts`, run once alone; `budgets.mjs` entries added (20); informal; not `bench:baseline`, not `bench:compare`), design tier, mean / p99 ms. The machine read slower than at Gate C in this run (the in-process hub `applyRemote` read 450 where Gate C had 200): rows are comparable inside this run only. Restore: Phase 3 in-process restore (all on main) **1,407.6 / 1,454.8**; through a real thread, end to end (wall clock) **3,345.4 / 3,456.0**. Main-thread part of the worker restore: send the request (`v8.serialize` of the bytes) 0.16 / 0.38, receive the decoded state with its findings (`v8.deserialize`) **107.1 / 121.1**, `replaceState` with the findings (index build on main) **250.8 / 369.5**: about **358 ms** against 1,408 in process; the `pre_restore` snapshot bytes come back as a transferred buffer (about 0). Boot: bulk read (fake-indexeddb, not ours) 173.1 / 183.5; per-record L1 on main 140.8 / 337.0; serialise the assembled state for the worker 235.0 / 642.8; serialise the RAW rows and meta instead 190.8 / 601.3; receive the findings 20.6 / 63.4; in-process index build + `validateAll` 1,910.0 / 1,963.4; the store from the boot result with the findings 647.8 / 976.9, without 2,017.0 / 2,105.4; whole `open()` in process 3,062.0 / 3,388.8, through a thread (wall clock) 4,502.6 / 4,770.3. Main-thread total of a boot through the port: 173 + 141 + 235 + 21 + 648 = about **1,218 ms**, against about 3,062 (`open()`) + 648 (store) = 3,710 ms before. Follower reload: in process 2,042.9 / 2,121.5, with the worker's findings 676.8 / 748.9, plus L1 141, send 235, receive 21: about **1,070 ms** against about 2,190. Hub-edit `applyRemote` (like-water hub, ~1,000 records): validation in process **449.6 / 522.2**; the validation replayed as data (what main would still do) **157.2 / 223.2**; receive of that result 5.4 / 20.6: about **163 ms, a drop of 64%**.
- **Phase 9 exceptions 6, 7 and 9, re-stated (main-thread numbers).** 9 (snapshot build / restore, Gate C 395 / 1,621): restore now about 358 ms on main (index build 251, receive 107), was 1,408 in this run's in-process measurement; the snapshot build costs main only the stored bytes (built in the worker, transferred). 7 (follower `applyRemote`, Gate C 200): unchanged code, 449.6 / 522.2 in this run (machine drift: about 2x Gate C); with the worker's validation the lower bound is about 163. 6 (writer `replaceReferences` on the hub, Gate C 216): the writer's `dispatch` still validates on main; not touched by 4.2d.
- **applyRemote with the worker's validation: qualifies (−64% against the 50% rule), NOT built.** It needs a two-phase store API (plan the remote ChangeSet, let the worker apply it and answer the `TouchedValidation`, then commit with that validation) and a sync expectation like adoption so the replica does not receive the ChangeSet twice: a contract change to `applyRemote`, `applyChanges` and the sync, larger than what was left of this session. Proposed smallest amendment: `CanonicalStore.prepareRemote(change) -> { changes } | error` (pure) and `applyRemote(change, validation?)` where `validation` (`TouchedValidation` stamped with the revision) is checked (scope equals the touched refs, subjects exist, levels) and replaces `validateTouchedByRule`; `applyChanges({ changes, withValidation: true })` answering the validation; `ReplicaSync.noteApplied(revision)`. Left for a follow-up step of the reviewer's choosing.

**REVIEW REQUIRED**
1. `BootPorts.validate` returns `PrecomputedFindings`, not bare findings (the master prompt said findings): the port must carry `ruleSet`, `revision` and `recordCounts` so a worker of another build is refused by `checkPrecomputed`; the default in-process port stamps them itself.
2. `snapshotDecode` takes `id` and `schemaVersion` besides `bytes` (and `baseRevision`): they make the worker's refusals read exactly like `readSnapshot`'s (a document whose version differs from its summary is CORRUPT, messages name the snapshot).
3. `createCanonicalStore({ precomputed })` throws a `RangeError` on a mismatch (a constructor cannot answer a value; boot and the follower check first and fall back, so it is only reachable by a caller that skipped the check).
4. `ImportPrepareRefused` gained `BUSY` (one pending slot shared by an import batch and a decoded snapshot); the import flow maps it to `REJECTED`.
5. Observation 3 (raw rows or the assembled state to the worker): raw rows would move per-record L1 (about 141 ms) off main, but serialising them costs about the same as serialising the state (191 vs 235, both noisy), and the writer must still run L1 on main to know which rows to quarantine (or the worker must return the rejected rows). Recommendation: keep the assembled state (the port unchanged); the saving is about 141 of about 1,218 ms.
6. The receive of the decoded state (107 ms) and the findings, and the 250 ms `replaceState` index build stay on main (D2, D3); judged at Gate E.
7. The hub-edit `applyRemote` variant qualifies and is not built (see above); it needs the reviewer's decision on the amendment.
8. A worker restore is slower end to end than the in-process one (3.3 s against 1.4 s on this run) because the thread also builds the indexes and validates (the main thread is free for about 2.9 of those seconds); the user-visible wait is longer, the UI stays responsive. Judged at Gate E.

**Open items.** See PROJECT_STATE openItems (the `applyRemote` variant; Phase 5 wiring of `createComputeRestore`, `createBootReplica` and the follower's `validate`; 4.2e export).
