### Step 4.2e — Export in the compute worker (`src/workers/compute`) (2026-09-30)

**Done: a, b, c, d in full; e (no background command or monitor left running).** No new dependency, no schema change. Part A (the 4.2d review record, commit 57a297f) is above.

- **Contract (a).** `buildExport(params): Promise<Blob>` is replaced by `exportPlan({ options, format, revision })` -> `ExportPlanAnswer` (`{ ok: true, assets: { assetId, sha256 }[] }` or `REPLICA_STALE`) and `exportBuild({ options, format, revision, assets: { assetId, sha256, bytes }[], exportedAt })` -> `ExportBuildAnswer` (`ExportBuildResult` or `REPLICA_STALE`). `ExportFormat = 'orn.json' | 'ornpkg'` is new: the plan must know the format, because `.orn.json` reads bytes only for `assets: 'embed'` and `.ornpkg` for `'embed'` and `'package'` (`readsAssetBytes`). Blob is gone from the contract; the result bytes are a `Uint8Array`, transferred (a view onto part of a larger buffer is copied first). Both calls are idempotent (`COMPUTE_SPECS`: plan 30 s, build 60 s).
- **Worker side** (`export-flow.ts`, `handlers.ts`). `planExport` runs the io layer's own `selectScope` over the replica's state and lists the asset records in the closure, in ID order. `buildExportBytes` runs the SAME `buildOrnJsonExport` / `buildOrnPackageExport` as the in-process export, with a provider that is a map lookup over the transferred bytes (an asset main did not send answers `undefined`, so the exporter reports it in `missingAssets`, never thrown). Cancel and progress go through `taskOptionsFrom(ctx)`. `exportedAt` is main's, validated like `snapshotBuild`'s (`assertUtcInstant`, shared). The generator stamp is the snapshot builder's `SNAPSHOT_GENERATOR` (the app's own).
- **Main side (b)** (`export-main.ts`, ports only). `createComputeExport({ sync, store, assetBytes, ids })`: settle the sync, plan for the store's revision, read the listed bytes through the injected `AssetBytesProvider` (persistence's `getAssetBlob` satisfies it once its Blob is read; the real-thread test does exactly that), copy each buffer (so a buffer the provider keeps is never detached) and transfer the copies with the build. A read that throws counts as no bytes and shows up in `missingAssets` (the result has no place for the reason). `REPLICA_STALE` (a commit landed between settle and call) plans again, at most `MAX_EXPORT_ATTEMPTS` = 3 times, then `REJECTED`. `CANCELLED` only when the caller's signal is aborted; a `CANCELLED` from a stopped client is `REJECTED` (a bug the tests found: it read as a user cancel). The export needs only the live state: a failed save does not block it (ADV-040).
- **Tests (c)** (+3 files, +48 tests: 189 / 2,941 -> 192 / 2,989; `npm run check` green as one command). `tests/integration/compute-export.test.ts` (35): INV-19 through the worker, bytes equal to the in-process export for both formats and six scopes (full, chemistry, curriculum, presentation, subset with and without closure) over the minimal fixture, the demo and six generator shapes (tiny), and over the maximal fixture plus five synthetic assets with `assets` none, embed and package (one asset without bytes); what the plan asks main for (only the closure's assets, none when the format reads none); `missingAssets`, a throwing provider, `missingReferences` equal to the in-process ones; structuredClone of every parameter and result; a buffer the provider keeps is not detached; two worker calls per export. `compute-export-failures.test.ts` (11): cancel (already aborted, during main's reads, during the worker's build with the store, revision, history and replica unchanged, the worker-side `CANCELLED` value and progress once per asset plus once for the file), `REPLICA_STALE` (both calls; NOT_READY before the first load; a commit between plan and build gives the newer revision; a replica that keeps moving is `REJECTED` after 3 attempts), ADV-040 (the save fails on quota; the export still builds the unsaved edit, byte-equal to the in-process one), a stopped sync and a crash while building. `compute-export-threads.test.ts` (2): the one real `worker_threads` test of an `.ornpkg` export with assets (bytes read from the real adapter's `getAssetBlob`, unzipped and compared, equal to the in-process export), and an `.orn.json` with embedded assets with a cancel. `tests/property/import-worker-roundtrip.test.ts`: P-02 full is now entirely through the worker, export AND import (27 cases, both exports through `createComputeExport`). New test support: `src/test-support/asset-kit.ts` (synthetic assets: PNG signature, seeded incompressible filler, real SHA-256) and `compute-export-kit.ts`; `compute-rig.ts` logs `exportPlan` and `exportBuild`.
- **Bench (d)** (`tests/perf/compute-export.bench.ts`, run once alone; 11 `budgets.mjs` entries; informal; not `bench:baseline`, not `bench:compare`), design tier, mean / p99 ms, budget row "Export full dataset" < 2,000 ms. In the worker's code (`exportBuild` of the real handlers, `assets: 'none'`): dense `.orn.json` 162.8 / 229.9, `.ornpkg` 405.7 / 417.3; hub_explosion `.orn.json` 148.4 / 185.7, `.ornpkg` 382.8 / 398.3. Real thread, end to end (settle, plan, build, result back), dense: `.orn.json` 153.8 / 172.4, `.ornpkg` 413.0 / 426.5. All well under the row; the machine read faster in this run than at step 3.5 (dense `.orn.json` 393.2 there, `.ornpkg` 994.1), so compare rows inside this run only: the thread adds no measurable cost over the worker's code. The first asset-bearing export: the dense design-tier state plus 20 synthetic assets of 1 MiB each (20 MiB in total; PNG signature then seeded pseudo-random filler, incompressible like a real image; real SHA-256; `withSyntheticAssets`). In the worker's code (bytes already there): `.ornpkg` with assets 'package' 990.8 / 1,013.4, `.orn.json` with assets 'embed' 1,341.8 / 1,402.9. Real thread, end to end with the bytes read on main: `.ornpkg` 1,040.7 / 1,060.9, `.orn.json` 1,355.7 / 1,369.9 (5 samples each). What main does alone (read 20 assets through the provider and copy each for the transfer): 9.2 / 62.3. So moving the bytes costs about 50 ms over the build, and both asset-bearing exports stay under the 2 s row at 20 MiB.
- **Does this close the step 3.5 openItem on asset-bearing export benches? Yes, for the measurement it asked for** (asset-bearing export at scale, both formats, the provider called); it uses synthetic bytes, not real images, and the IndexedDB read is not in it (fake-indexeddb is not our code). The openItem is closed with that caveat, the reviewer to confirm.
- **Amendments recorded below.** `ComputeWorkerApi.buildExport` replaced by `exportPlan` / `exportBuild` (`ExportFormat`, `ExportPlanParams`, `ExportPlanAnswer`, `ExportBuildParams`, `ExportBuildAnswer`), Blob dropped from the contract.
- **Test timeouts (resource limits, TEST_STRATEGY §1).** `tests/integration/compute-export-threads.test.ts` sets `THREAD_TEST_TIMEOUT_MS` = 60 s (the same limit as the other real-thread tests) from the start; no earlier limit was changed. `compute-export.test.ts` carries a 60 s per-file limit like the P-02 files.

**REVIEW REQUIRED**
1. `ExportFormat` is a new parameter of BOTH calls (the master prompt's signatures had only `options` and `revision`): without it the plan cannot know whether a `.orn.json` (embed only) or an `.ornpkg` (embed and package) reads bytes, and the build cannot pick the exporter. The names `'orn.json'` and `'ornpkg'` are the file kinds.
2. The plan's list depends on the format and `assets`: for `assets: 'none'` (or `'package'` for `.orn.json`, which writes no bytes) it is empty, so main reads nothing.
3. A provider that throws is treated as "no bytes" and reported in `missingAssets` without its message (the result type has no field for it). Say if the reason should be kept (an `ExportResult` addition).
4. Main copies every asset's bytes before transferring them (about 9 ms for 20 MiB) so a buffer the provider or a cache keeps is never detached; the alternative is a documented "the provider must hand over ownership".
5. The stale-replica retry (3 plans) and the `REJECTED` message when a replica keeps moving.
6. The generator stamp of a worker-built export is the snapshot builder's constant (`SNAPSHOT_GENERATOR`), not the adapter's option: an app that sets a custom `generator` on the adapter would get the default in worker-built files.
7. Step 3.5's openItem closed by synthetic 20 MiB (see above).

**Open items.** See PROJECT_STATE openItems (Phase 5 wiring of `createComputeExport` and its provider; the generator stamp; no baseline for the new bench).

### Step 4.2e review (reviewer, 2026-09-30)

**ACCEPTED.** Decisions on the seven REVIEW REQUIRED items of step 4.2e:
1 and 2. `ExportFormat` on both calls and the format-dependent plan: approved.
3. A provider that throws: the reason must be kept (CLAUDE.md §1.5). Follow-up a.
4. Copying asset bytes before transfer: approved. 5. Three plans then `REJECTED`: approved.
6. Worker-built files must carry the same generator as main's: follow-up b.
7. The step 3.5 asset-bench openItem is closed on synthetic bytes; real IndexedDB reads and real images are seen in the Gate E/F traces (noted on the closed item in PROJECT_STATE).

**Follow-ups done (commit "phase-4.2e: review follow-ups"; CLAUDE.md §7 amendment recorded below).**
- **a. Missing assets carry a reason.** `ExportResult.missingAssets` entries are now `MissingAsset = { assetId, sha256, reason?: 'no_bytes' | 'read_failed', message? }` (additive; `src/io/contracts.ts`). `no_bytes`: the provider answered `undefined`. `read_failed`: the provider threw or rejected; `message` is the error's text, capped at `MISSING_ASSET_MESSAGE_CAP` = 500 characters (a non-error value gives "the provider threw a non-error value"). One helper, `readAssetBytes` (`src/io/export-assets.ts`), serves both exporters, so the in-process export has the same behaviour (before, a throwing provider made the in-process export reject; now it finishes and reports). Through the worker, main reads with the same helper: bytes it got are sent in `assets`, a read that failed is sent in the new optional `ExportBuildParams.unreadable` (`assetId`, `sha256`, `message`), and the worker's provider rethrows that message so the exporter records it exactly as in process; a `no_bytes` asset is simply left out. Tests: `src/io/export-assets.test.ts` (helper: bytes, `undefined`, rejection, synchronous throw, non-error values, cap at and over 500; both exporters finish on a throwing provider), `tests/integration/compute-export-reasons.test.ts` (both formats through the worker: one `no_bytes`, one `read_failed`, one 900-character message capped, one good; `missingAssets` and the file bytes equal the in-process export's); the two Phase 3 "provider cannot supply bytes" tests now expect `reason: 'no_bytes'`.
- **b. The generator is main's.** `snapshotBuild` and `exportBuild` take `generator: { app, version }` (`SnapshotBuildParams`, `ExportBuildParams`, type `DocumentGenerator`), like `exportedAt`. `SNAPSHOT_GENERATOR` is deleted; `buildSnapshotBytes` takes the generator as a required argument; `createComputeImport`, `createComputeRestore` and `createComputeExport` take `generator` in their deps and pass it on (Phase 5 gives them the value it gives the adapter). Tests use a `TEST_GENERATOR` that is NOT the adapter's default (`src/test-support/compute-rig.ts`), so a worker that kept a constant of its own would show. `tests/integration/snapshot-builder-equality.test.ts` passes the same generator to both sides (codec and worker builder) and checks the decoded document carries it; `snapshot-build.test.ts` checks the handler's bytes carry the generator main sent; `compute-export-reasons.test.ts` checks both export formats do.

### Step 4.3a — Chem adapter and SVG sanitiser (`src/chem`, `src/workers/chem`) (2026-09-30)

**Done: a to g in full (no background command or monitor left running).** Part A of the session (the 4.2e review record and its two follow-ups) is above, commit 3cd59ec. One new dependency, no schema change, no `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump.

- **Dependency (a).** `@rdkit/rdkit` **2026.3.6**, exact in `package.json` (the package's own version string is `2026.03.6`, RDKit release 2026.03.6, BSD-3-Clause), lockfile updated. `npm audit`: **0 vulnerabilities**. The glue is 105,843 bytes, the WASM 7,333,095 bytes. Loaded lazily (a dynamic import on the first call) from the package's own file: in the browser `src/chem/rdkit/wasm-url.ts` imports `@rdkit/rdkit/RDKit_minimal.wasm?url` (a local build asset, no CDN), under Node the glue reads the file next to itself. **dependency-cruiser rule `rdkit-only-in-adapter`**: nothing outside `src/chem/rdkit/` may import `@rdkit/rdkit` (checked by adding a throwaway violation in `src/chem` and in `src/workers`: both reported, then removed; `lint:deps` clean).
- **Eval scan of the glue (a).** Source, `RDKit_minimal.js`: `eval(` **0**; `new Function` **2** (Emscripten embind builds its call wrappers with it: `craftInvokerFunction` and the method caller); `fetch(` 2 and `XMLHttpRequest` (the WASM loading fallbacks, used only with a URL the caller gives); `require("node:fs")` and `require("node:crypto")` only in the Node branch; no `importScripts`, no `import(`, no absolute URL. The same two `new Function` sites are in the lazy chunk of a scratch Vite build. **`new Function` is not allowed by `script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'`**, so the CSP of SECURITY §4 may stop RDKit at start-up; not testable in Node. See REVIEW REQUIRED 1. Scratch Vite build (not committed): the worker entry bundles to 147 KB, RDKit is its own lazy 64 KB chunk and the WASM a separate hashed 7.3 MB asset the worker references by URL; Vite's **lib mode inlines the WASM as base64 (9.98 MB)**, so the 4.6 build must use the app/worker build, not lib mode.

**What RDKit MinimalLib does here (measured, real RDKit in Node).** A WASM trap (`RuntimeError: memory access out of bounds`, a stack overflow) is reachable well inside the 20,000-character cap: an unbranched chain of 500 atoms works, 700 traps writing the SMILES (parse itself works), 1,000 traps at once; `NCC(=O)` x 400 (2,801 characters) traps; a C++ exception (`WebAssembly.Exception`, no message) came from drawing an 800-heavy-atom amide chain. Some inputs HANG: 4,000 characters of chained cyclohexane rings (`C1CCCCC1` x 500) ran past 25 s, and the depiction of 10,001 disconnected `C` atoms (20,000 characters) also runs past the limit. So the size caps are not a bound on RDKit's work: the 2 s limit with terminate-and-restart is the defence, and traps must be caught. `get_mol` returns `null` for a structure it cannot read (it throws only in rare cases). Logging goes to stderr/console unless switched off (`disable_logging()` is called at load).

- **Adapter (b)** (`src/chem/rdkit/`: `adapter.ts` 186 lines, `facts.ts`, `draw.ts`, `scope.ts`, `smiles-atoms.ts`, `elements.ts`, `errors.ts`, `types.ts`, `loader.ts`, `wasm-url.ts`; plus `src/chem/depiction-options.ts`, `lru.ts`, `cached-toolkit.ts`). `createRdkitToolkit({ load })` implements `StructureToolkit`; the module is loaded on the first call, concurrent first calls share one load, `ready()` never rejects and a failed load is not remembered. **Caps and format BEFORE RDKit**: SMILES and CXSMILES 20,000 characters, molfile 1,000,000 (`LIMITS`), empty or unknown format, and the declared format is checked because `get_mol` sniffs its input (a "SMILES" with a line break or a brace, a "molfile" without `M  END`, are `invalid`). **`ParseOutcome` reasons (Q7)**: `invalid` (RDKit cannot read the structure, an over-cap input; the only reason that means the structure is bad), `unavailable` (no module, a WASM trap, or a failure while WORKING on a structure RDKit did read, such as drawing it), `timeout` (only the RPC client says it, below). A trap or a JS `RangeError` drops the module (the next call loads a fresh one, measured 44 ms in a real thread). **One helper frees every RDKit object** (`withHandle`, a `finally`): `scope.test.ts` proves with a fake module that it frees on success, on a throw, on an early return inside the callback, with nested handles (inner first), never frees twice, hides no failing `delete()` on a normal path and does not let a failing `delete()` replace an exception already propagating (after a trap `delete()` can trap too); the adapter tests assert `fake.leaked()` is empty after every operation and every failure path.
- **Facts, and what is left undefined (b).** `canonicalSmiles` is RDKit's canonical isomeric SMILES **with atom map numbers kept** (MinimalLib cannot strip them: `ignoreAtomMapNumbers` has no effect, so a mapped structure and its unmapped twin have different canonical SMILES; matters for CHEM-009 in 4.3b). `formula` is a Hill formula the ADAPTER computes from RDKit's own JSON (atomic numbers and implicit hydrogens; all components summed, no charge, no isotopes) because MinimalLib has no formula call; undefined if an atom is a dummy or not in the table. `charge` is the sum of formal charges from that JSON. `heavyAtomCount` is `get_num_atoms(true)`. `specifiedStereocentres`, `unspecifiedStereocentres`, `hasDoubleBondStereo` come from `get_stereo_tags()` (`(?)` = not specified; a specified double bond means E or Z). `aromatic` is "any aromatic atom" from the JSON's representation extension. `atomMaps` are read from RDKit's own canonical SMILES (MinimalLib has no accessor), ascending with repeats kept. **Left undefined, never guessed, with a warning that names the fact:** any fact RDKit does not give for that structure (a stereo-tag exception leaves the three stereo facts undefined; a missing representation extension leaves `aromatic` undefined; a dummy atom leaves `formula` undefined; unreadable JSON leaves formula, charge and aromatic undefined). **`warnings` is only the adapter's own per-fact notes (at most 10)**: MinimalLib gave no parse warnings (`set_log_capture` returned an empty buffer while RDKit still wrote its message to stderr), so RDKit's own warnings are not reported. For the demo, 64 of the 65 species with a structure parse and give EVERY fact with no warning (asserted); the 65th is the invalid SMILES, and one species has no structure.
- **Depiction (b).** `depict` always returns sanitised SVG or a failure value; the key is `hash(format, value, options, toolkit, version)`. RDKit draws atom map numbers as part of the atom label and has no option to hide them, so with `showAtomMaps: false` the structure is drawn again from RDKit's canonical SMILES without the `:n` (a molfile's coordinates are then lost; only when the structure has maps). A highlight by `highlightAtomMaps` finds its atoms through the SMILES and RDKit's `_smilesAtomOutputOrder`; if they cannot be located the depiction is `unavailable`, not drawn wrongly. Bad options are a programmer error (`RangeError`; the worker host answers `BAD_REQUEST`).
- **Chem worker (c)** (`src/workers/chem/`: `handlers.ts`, `host.ts`, `entry.ts`, `specs.ts`, `client.ts`). Handlers over the adapter behind the cache; the shape of every param is checked at the wire (`BAD_REQUEST`), the content is the toolkit's. Specs: `parse` and `depict` idempotent, **2,000 ms** each. `createChemService(client)` (main side) turns a call the worker could not answer into a value: `TIMEOUT` -> `timeout` (not retried, D4), everything else -> `unavailable` with the code; a crash under a call is retried once. Restart is the RPC client's: a call past its limit terminates the thread and starts a new one, which loads the WASM again on its first call. **Cache**: two LRU caches of 500 entries (facts, depictions) in the worker, key hash(kind, format, value, options, toolkit, version); a hit also compares the whole input, so a hash collision cannot answer for another structure (tested with a hash forced to collide); `invalid` and `refused` are cached, `unavailable` and `timeout` never. The worker entry is `entry.ts`; creating it in the app is Phase 5 (D7).
- **Sanitiser (d)** (`src/chem/svg/`: `sanitize.ts` 185 lines, `tag.ts`, `attributes.ts`, `values.ts`, `whitelist.ts`, `text.ts`), rules in SECURITY §2. One linear pass, the open-element stack capped at 256 (deeper is refused, no recursion), no DOMParser. The output is written from whitelisted parts with escaping; it always starts with its own `xmlns`, is idempotent and no string in it contains `href`, `url(`, `expression`, `javascript` or an `on...=` in any case (a second, independent check on every attribute value and text node, on top of the value patterns). Refusals are values with a reason: `too_large` (over **2 MiB**, the message says so), `declaration` (DOCTYPE or any other `<!`), `too_deep`, `no_svg`. Everything else it drops is counted in the report. RDKit's own output needs `stroke-miterlimit` (found by the real-RDKit test on 1-bromopropane, added to the whitelist).

**Tests (e)** (+11 files, +282 tests: 194 / 3,000 -> 205 / 3,282 after Part A; `npm run check` green as ONE command).
- `src/chem/svg/sanitize.test.ts` (62: RDKit-shaped output kept and counted, style, attribute values, text and entities, structure, refusals at the exact caps, the whitelist itself, the second check); `tests/adversarial/adv-033-svg-sanitizer.test.ts` (78, the corpus below); `tests/property/svg-sanitizer.test.ts` (5: never throws, output bounded and inert by an independent checker, idempotent, deterministic, cap exact; 300 runs over arbitrary and assembled strings).
- **Corpus (ADV-033, depiction part)**: script (any case, with type, namespaced, inside metadata), foreignObject with html and iframe, image, use, anchors with javascript, animate/set of handlers and hrefs, style elements, on* in any case on every element, href and xlink:href to anything and on the root, xml:base, namespace tricks, style with url() (any case, with a comment inside, with escapes, as numeric references), expression, -moz-binding, behavior, @import, a declaration that ends the attribute, DOCTYPE (internal subset, XXE, billion laughs, entity declared inside, lower case, public), unknown entities, numeric references to forbidden characters, CDATA (with markup, unclosed), comments (conditional, hiding markup, unclosed), processing instructions, unclosed and malformed tags (crossed, doubled brackets, tag-in-attribute, quotes hiding the end of a tag, slashes for spaces, null byte and newline in a name), many roots, text around, nesting depth 10,000, a 5 MB input, and seven worst-shape inputs at the cap (deep unclosed nesting, unclosed comments, unclosed attributes, entity soup, 140,000 groups, a 1.8 MB path, 400,000 entities) that finish and are inert. Every accepted output is checked by `src/test-support/svg-inert.ts`, which does not share the sanitiser's scanner.
- `src/chem/rdkit/adapter.test.ts` (28, fake module: lazy loading, caps before RDKit, formats, every reason, traps, the stage that decides the reason, depiction through the sanitiser, keys, atom maps, SMARTS), `scope.test.ts` (9), `facts.test.ts` (21), `src/chem/cache.test.ts` (24: LRU, cache rules, option checks), `cache-collision.test.ts` (2), `src/workers/chem/handlers.test.ts` (19: in-memory port with `structuredClone` of every message, `BAD_REQUEST`, cache use, the service's `timeout` and `unavailable`, one retry after a crash), `tests/types/rpc-contracts.test.ts` (+1).
- **Real RDKit in Node** (`tests/integration/chem-rdkit.test.ts`, 28, RDKit loaded once for the file): **every species of the demo with a structure (65 of 66) parses, except the one with the invalid SMILES (`spc_invalid-smiles-demo`), which answers `invalid`, also for depict; the other 64 depict and its sanitised drawing keeps EVERY drawing element RDKit produced**, counted independently of the sanitiser from the raw SVG: kept elements = drawing elements outside comments and metadata, dropped elements = the elements inside `<metadata>`, comments dropped = every comment (1), processing instructions = the XML declaration (1), and nothing else dropped (no style, no text); the adapter's depiction equals the sanitised raw drawing exactly. Canonical SMILES is a fixed point for every species. Hand-checked facts (formula, charge, heavy atoms, aromatic, stereo, atom maps, a CXSMILES, a molfile), depiction options (maps shown, hidden, highlighted), SMARTS, a 20,000-character SMILES accepted and 20,001 refused, ten malformed SMILES all `invalid`, and a real WASM trap (`invalid` never, `unavailable` always, a new module loads and goes on).
- **Real thread** (`tests/integration/chem-worker-threads.test.ts`, 5, `worker_threads` under tsx, real RDKit): facts and a sanitised drawing end to end; **a call over the 2 s limit answers `timeout` after at least 2,000 ms, the thread is terminated, and the next call runs in a NEW thread that loads the WASM again** (events: `timeout`, `restart`); a hung depiction stops the same way; a trap inside the thread is `unavailable` with no restart; an over-long structure is refused in the thread.
- **Hand mutants of the new source (36 run in three rounds): 35 killed, 1 equivalent** (the unknown-entity fallback in `text.ts` is unreachable because the entity pattern only matches names the table has). Three survivors of the first round led to tests: the redundant forbidden-content guard, "`unavailable` and `timeout` are never cached" and the hash-collision safety.

**Benchmarks (f)** (`tests/perf/chem.bench.ts`, 11 `budgets.mjs` entries, run once alone; informal: the design doc has no chem row; not `bench:baseline`, not `bench:compare`), mean / p99 ms, real RDKit:
- WASM init: `loadRdkit()` in process 21.30 / 29.53 (141 samples); **cold real thread (spawn under tsx, load, first parse) 367.6 / 382.2 (9)**; **first parse after a restart** (a call limited to 50 ms terminates the thread, then a new thread answers; the figure includes the first thread's own start and the 50 ms) 781.5 / 793.6 (5), which is about 364 ms for the restarted thread once its 50 ms and the first thread's 368 ms are taken off: **the WASM is re-initialised after a restart at about the cold cost**. Thread start and the tsx transpile are most of it (in-process init is 21 ms); the browser worker is bundled, so its cold start is a different, unmeasured number (Gate E).
- Typical demo species, in process, no cache: parse ethanol 0.0442 / 0.1361; depict + sanitise ethanol (drawing 1,988 characters) 0.3489 / 0.5563; parse benzoyl peroxide (the largest demo species) 0.2866 / 0.4911; depict + sanitise benzoyl peroxide (11,203 characters) 1.4570 / 2.2391.
- 20,000-character SMILES (10,001 disconnected `C`): parse in process 431.6 / 451.6 (7); **depict through a real thread runs into the 2 s limit and answers `timeout`: 2,385.6 / 2,395.7 (3; the limit plus thread start and restart)**.
- Sanitiser: ethanol's drawing 0.0699 / 0.1383; benzoyl peroxide's 0.4100 / 0.5856.

**REVIEW REQUIRED**
1. **CSP and `new Function`.** RDKit's glue calls `new Function` (embind) in two places; `script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'` does not allow it. Also, `vite.config.ts` injects the CSP as a `<meta>` tag, and a meta policy is not applied to a dedicated worker (its policy comes from its own script's response headers). Both must be checked in the 4.6 build smoke; if RDKit needs `'unsafe-eval'`, that is a security decision (SECURITY §4 says the CSP is `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` only), not one to make silently. Recorded in SECURITY §4 as open.
2. **Contract changes (Amendments below).** `ParseOutcome` failures carry `reason` (Q7, approved); `depict` answers `DepictOutcome` (a value with `refused` as a fourth reason) instead of a bare `Depiction`, and `matchesSmarts` answers `SmartsOutcome` instead of `boolean | 'invalid_smarts'`, because neither could say "invalid structure", "toolkit unavailable" or "drawing refused" (CLAUDE.md §6: errors across a module boundary are values); and `ChemWorkerApi.checkAll` is removed now (Q2 approved its replacement in 4.3b; a host could not implement a method that assumes state in a stateless worker). `StructureToolkit.ready()` resolves when loading has finished, successfully or not.
3. **An input that traps RDKit is `unavailable`, not `invalid`** (the toolkit crashed; the structure may be fine), so CHEM-001 does not fire for it and it is checked again, and crashes again, on every check (about 30-45 ms each, nothing is cached). If a deterministic crash should count as a bad structure, that is a decision for 4.3b.
4. **Declared format is enforced** (a SMILES with a line break or a brace, a molfile without `M  END`, are `invalid`) because RDKit sniffs its input. Not in the master prompt; small and cheap; say if it should go.
5. **`formula` is computed by the adapter** (Hill, from RDKit's JSON), `atomMaps` from RDKit's canonical SMILES, `canonicalSmiles` keeps map numbers, `warnings` holds only the adapter's per-fact notes (see above). The stored `species.formula` strings of the demo (`C2H5OH`, `CH3COOH`) are not Hill and are not compared with it here; CHEM-002/003 (4.3b) decide what to do with that.
6. **Hiding atom maps redraws from the SMILES** (a molfile's coordinates are lost, only when it has maps).
7. Real-browser start-up of the chem worker (WASM fetch from the build asset, cold init in a bundled worker) is not measured here.

**Open items.** See PROJECT_STATE openItems (the CSP question, Phase 5 wiring of the chem worker and `createChemService`, no baseline for the new bench).

### Step 4.3a review (reviewer, 2026-09-30)

**Verdict: ACCEPTED.** Decisions:
1. **CSP and RDKit's `new Function`** (reviewer decision; ADR-035, SECURITY §4 updated). The page CSP stays exactly as SECURITY §4 says: no `'unsafe-eval'` anywhere on the page. `new Function` is accepted only inside the chem worker, from RDKit's embind glue (never from data; the worker has no DOM and no storage); our own code keeps the ESLint ban. Step 4.6's build smoke adds a check that fails if `new Function`, `Function(` or `eval(` appears in any built chunk other than RDKit's own. Phase 5 (Gate E e2e) proves in a real browser that the page CSP is enforced and the chem worker still starts; if a host ever applies a header CSP to workers, the chem worker script alone gets a policy that allows it, never the page (openItem for Phase 5). **Follow-up a (done):** each worker entry calls `z.config({ jitless: true })` before anything else (`src/workers/zod-jitless.ts`, imported first by the compute and chem entries; a test checks the setting and the import order; the layout and search entries do the same when they exist).
2. `DepictOutcome`, `SmartsOutcome`, `checkAll` removed now, `ready()` semantics: approved.
3. Inputs that trap or hang RDKit are `unavailable` / `timeout`, never `invalid`: approved, with a change carried into 4.3b: they must not be re-run on every check (a hang costs 2 s plus a restart). See 4.3b item e.
4. Declared-format enforcement: approved.
5. Adapter-computed Hill formula, `atomMaps` from the SMILES, map numbers kept in `canonicalSmiles`, warnings = the adapter's notes: approved. Formula comparisons in 4.3b compare ATOM COUNTS, never strings (4.3b item d).
6. Redraw from SMILES when hiding atom maps: approved.
7. Real-browser start-up of the chem worker: Gate E.

### Step 4.3b — L4 checks (`src/chem/checks`, `src/workers/chem`, `src/core`) (2026-09-30)

**Done: a, b, c, d, e, f, g, h, i (CHEM-006 NOT built: stopped on that rule, see REVIEW REQUIRED 1).** Part A of the session (the 4.3a review record, ADR-035, the jitless follow-up) is above, commit 56a0a66. No new dependency, no schema change, no `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump. `npm run check` green as ONE command: 212 files, 3387 tests, 198.8 s. No background command or monitor left running.

- **Catalogue (a).** `L4_RULES` (`src/chem/checks/rules.ts`): ID, default severity, owner (`worker` or `service`), `implemented`. A test proves it equals the nine CHEM entries of `EXTERNAL_RULES` and the severities of VALIDATION_RULES §3; another that L4 IDs are not in `RULE_SET` / `RULE_SET_ID`. `toFinding(ruleId, subject, draft)` builds a level-4 finding with core's `makeFinding` (stable ID hash of rule, subject, path).
- **Rules as built (a).** CHEM-001: RDKit cannot read the notation. CHEM-002: RDKit reads it without sanitising but sanitisation fails (new `problem: 'unreadable' | 'sanitisation'` on `invalid` parse outcomes; the adapter reads a failed structure a second time with `sanitize: false`). A structure that traps or hangs RDKit gets neither. CHEM-003: element COUNTS of the entered formula (`formula.ts`: condensed forms, groups and brackets, Unicode subscripts, dot hydrates with multiplier, an unambiguous charge suffix) against the derived formula; unreadable formula, a sign after digits (`Al3+`, `NH4+`, could be a count) or an underivable formula is NOT COMPARED (in the subject's status), never a finding. CHEM-004: entered charge against net formal charge. CHEM-005: per outcome, only when every atom-contributing participant (DOMAIN_MODEL §6.2 roles, `flags.contributesAtoms` overrides, a spectator never) and every product is a species with a structure and a coefficient and the outcome has one set of reactants; coefficients count; INFO. CHEM-007: classes with a SMARTS probed against each species with a structure; stated classes include ancestors; both directions as suggestions, the "listed but does not match" direction only for classes the species lists itself. CHEM-008: maps used by bond changes and arrows against the maps of the step's structures; a step with an unreadable structure is not compared. CHEM-009: groups by RDKit canonical SMILES (map numbers kept) on main.
- **Worker API (b).** `ChemWorkerApi` gains `checkSpecies({ species })`, `checkReaction({ outcomes })` (one balance request per outcome), `checkMechanism({ steps })` (one item per step, with its structures and every map use with its path) and `matches({ probes })`. At most 64 items a call (`MAX_CHECK_BATCH`, shape checked at the wire: `BAD_REQUEST`). The worker is stateless; every answer names the toolkit (name, version). CHEM-007's class SMARTS travel in `matches` probes from the service. `ChemService` gains the four methods answering `CheckCall` (the answer, or `timeout` / `unavailable`). Specs: idempotent, 2,000 ms.
- **Service (c).** `createL4Service` (`src/workers/chem/l4`, ports only: `state`, `findings` (replaceForRules), `chem`, `schedule` (the debounce)). From each ChangeSet: a created species, or an updated one whose check signature changed (structure, formula, charge, listed classes: a note or name edit re-checks nothing), is re-checked, and CHEM-009 is refreshed for its OLD and its NEW canonical group only (derived map, in memory); a created or updated reaction or mechanism re-checks that record; a deleted species leaves its group. **Batch size: 32** items per call (species, outcomes, steps, probes). A result is applied only after a synchronous freshness check against the record as it is NOW (the request signature must equal the one sent); otherwise it is dropped and counted (`staleDropped`). `checkAll()` queues everything (boot, reload); the CHEM-009 map is built from the species that were checked, so it must run first.
- **Formula rule (d).** Counts, never strings (see above); "not compared" is visible in `statusOf`.
- **Not-checkable inputs (e).** `unavailable` (per item, trap) or `timeout`: no CHEM-001, status `not_checked` with the reason and `remembered: true`; the input (structure; for reactions and mechanisms the request) plus the toolkit version is kept (2,000 entries, oldest first, main memory, this session) and is not sent again until it changes. A batch that times out is resent item by item; the item that times out alone is the culprit (cost: one 2 s timeout for the batch, one for the culprit, then never again). A worker that did not answer at all (down) is `not_checked`, NOT remembered; a toolkit version of `not loaded` is never remembered. Status per subject: `pending | checked{notCompared} | not_checked{reason, message, remembered} | none`, with `subscribe`, for Phase 5.
- **Q10 (b) (f).** `clearLevel4` / `vanishedRefs` (`src/core/validation/level4.ts`) called in the store's commit step for commits, undo/redo, `replaceState`, `reload` and `applyRemote`; an L1-L3 commit never writes level 4 (`commitFindings` only levels 1-3; tested, including the referrer path).
- **Adapter fix found on the demo.** RDKit's read removes explicit hydrogens, mapped ones included (`[H:2][OH:1]` has only map 1), so CHEM-008 called every proton-transfer map absent (16 false findings on two demo mechanisms). A line notation with map numbers is now read a second time with `removeHs: false` and `atomMaps` come from that (`mapped-hydrogens.ts`); `canonicalSmiles` is unchanged.

**Tests (g).** Files 212, tests 3387 (was 206 / 3,284 after Part A). New: `formula.test.ts` (32), `rules.test.ts` (6), `level4.test.ts` (6, Q10 b), `tests/integration/chem-checks.test.ts` (25, real RDKit, every built rule fires on a minimal case and is silent on a clean one; every request and answer through structuredClone), `tests/integration/l4-service.test.ts` (10, real store and RDKit: the demo's invalid species is CHEM-001, ADV-036 unit part, what an edit re-checks, Q10 b through the store, L4 never changes data or blocks, L1-L3 findings equal `validateAll`, the demo findings doc is current), `src/workers/chem/l4/service.test.ts` (17, scripted worker: counted re-checks, batches 32/32/6, CHEM-009 old and new group only, stale drop for a species, a deletion and a reaction, not-checkable memory, hang fallback, worker down, toolkit version, bounded memory), wire and timeout tests in `handlers.test.ts` (+3), real-thread tests in `chem-worker-threads.test.ts` (+2: a trap inside a batch; a hanging batch through the service, culprit remembered), adapter tests (+2). One expectation changed, not weakened: `cache.test.ts` lists the extra `#nosan` read of an invalid structure. **Hand mutants: 29 run (26 + 3 after adding tests), 29 killed** (one first-round survivor was a no-op mutant and was replaced; two survivors led to tests: inherited class not reported, stale reaction result), plus 2 for Q10 b.

**Demo findings (h)** (`docs/reviews/demo-chem-findings.md`, generated by `node scripts/demo-chem-findings.mjs`, REVIEW REQUIRED, nothing verified, no data edited): CHEM-001 1 (`spc_invalid-smiles-demo`), CHEM-003 1 (`spc_palmitic-acid`: entered C16H32O2, the structure has 15 carbons), all others 0. CHEM-005 compares nothing on the demo (no reaction has coefficients on all contributing species); every reason is listed.

**Benchmarks (i)** (`tests/perf/chem-checks.bench.ts`, 4 `budgets.mjs` entries, run once alone, informal, no bench:baseline, no bench:compare), real RDKit, no cache, mean / p99 ms: demo's 65 species in batches of 32, worker code only 30.19 / 42.79 (100); whole demo through the service (65 species, 27 reactions, 3 mechanisms, merge) 42.60 / 64.79 (71); whole demo in a fresh real thread (spawn under tsx, WASM load included) 1,474.26 / 1,598.64 (5); **the generator gives its species NO structures**, so the design tier was benched with 1,400 synthetic distinct alcohols: 3,205.56 / 3,265.78 (5), 44 calls of 32.

**REVIEW REQUIRED**
1. **Stopped on CHEM-006.** "Stereo descriptors disagree with the structure": descriptors are free text ("2R", "Z", "cis"); the rule does not say how they are compared (a locant cannot be mapped to an atom without naming; RDKit's legacy CIP labels can differ from the descriptor a student writes correctly; `achiral` is legitimate for an E alkene). Not built; it is in the catalogue as `implemented: false` with the reason, never raises a finding. Smallest proposal: compare only the multiset of R/S letters and of E/Z letters found in the descriptors with RDKit's CIP labels, ignoring locants and words, and say "not compared" otherwise.
2. **CHEM-002 reading**: a structure readable without sanitising is CHEM-002 (and not CHEM-001). Contract change (Amendments): `ParseOutcome` invalid failures carry `problem`.
3. **Records that refer to a changed record are not re-checked** (item c says "only"): a reaction whose species changed structure keeps its CHEM-005 until the reaction is edited or `checkAll` runs; a species keeps CHEM-007 after its class changed its SMARTS. Smallest proposal: on a species structure change also re-check the reactions that refer to it (`referrersOf`, bounded), and on a class SMARTS or parent change the species that list it.
4. **CHEM-005 interpretation** (contributing participants by role table, one set of reactants per outcome, per outcome not per reaction).
5. **Adapter fact change**: `atomMaps` now includes maps on hydrogens RDKit removes (second read).
6. **Remembering a trap by the toolkit's answer**: an item after a trap in the same batch that fails only because the module could not be reloaded would also be remembered; rare, visible in its status.
7. The demo findings (palmitic acid, the invalid record) are for the human reviewer; nothing is marked verified.

**Open items.** See PROJECT_STATE openItems (Phase 5 wiring of the L4 service: store.subscribe to `onChange`, `checkAll` after boot and reload, the debounce, statuses in the UI; the not-checkable memory is per session).

### Step 4.3b review (reviewer, 2026-09-30)

**Verdict: ACCEPTED.** Decisions:
1. **CHEM-006:** build it now with the proposed reading, cautiously (follow-up a).
2. CHEM-002 reading and `ParseOutcome` `problem`: approved.

### Step 4.3b review follow-ups (2026-10-01)

**Done: a, b, c.** No new dependency, no schema change, no `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump. Commit "phase-4.3b: review follow-ups (CHEM-006, referrer re-checks, palmitic acid)".

- **a. CHEM-006** (`src/chem/checks/stereo.ts`, pure; `species.ts` calls it). Compares ONLY the multiset of R/S letters and of E/Z letters in the species' descriptors with RDKit's CIP labels (new additive fact `StructureFacts.cipCounts`, from the same `get_stereo_tags` call). A descriptor is read only in the form `[locant]R|S|E|Z` (comma, semicolon or slash lists, optional parentheses); anything else is words. "Not compared" (in `statusOf(...).notCompared`, never a finding): words-only or other forms, lower-case r/s in a descriptor or in the structure, unspecified stereocentres in the structure, R/S naming FEWER centres that fit inside the structure's, and E/Z counts that differ (RDKit does not list a double bond whose geometry is not given, so an unequal count may be an unspecified bond: only an equal count with different letters is a finding). A finding (WARNING, path `stereo.descriptors`) only for a certain disagreement: a different letter at equal count, more R/S centres named than the structure has, a letter it lacks. The request carries `stereoDescriptors` when the species has any, so they are part of the species' check signature (a descriptor edit re-checks that species). `L4_RULES` has every rule `implemented`; `VALIDATION_RULES.md` §3 has the dated reading.
- **b. Referrer re-checks** (`src/workers/chem/l4/fanout.ts`, `service.ts`). On a species created, deleted, or whose check signature changed: the reactions that refer to it (`referrersOf`); on a class created, deleted, or whose SMARTS or `parentIds` changed (read from the ChangeSet's patches): the species that list it or a descendant (`referrersOf` filtered to `classIds`, plus `descendantsOf('class')`). Bounded: the first **500** (`RECHECK_CAP`) per change are queued at once, the rest wait in an ordered backlog and are queued behind them as the queue empties; never dropped, shown as `pending`, removed if the record is deleted meanwhile. New port `L4Ports.indexes` (the store's `getIndexes()`); new stats `referrerQueued`, `maxQueuedAtOnce`, `backlog`. A queued reaction whose plan equals the one already answered is not sent to the worker again (a formula edit costs no worker call for its reactions). **Deliberate narrowing, for the reviewer:** mechanisms are NOT queued: `mechanismPlan` reads only the structures embedded in the mechanism's own steps, so a species record cannot change its answer. `cause: 'replace'` is `checkAll`'s job.
- **c. Palmitic acid data correction by the reviewer** (not by the implementer's judgement): `spc_palmitic-acid` SMILES `CCCCCCCCCCCCCCCC(=O)O` (16 carbons, hexadecanoic acid) in the demo builder (`src/test-support/demo/species.ts`); `data/seed/demo.orn.json` and `data/fixtures/demo-invalid.orn.json` regenerated by `node scripts/build-demo.mjs` only (one line each). The record stays REVIEW REQUIRED; nothing is marked verified. `docs/reviews/demo-chem-findings.md` regenerated by `node scripts/demo-chem-findings.mjs`: the CHEM-003 finding on palmitic acid is gone (CHEM-001 on `spc_invalid-smiles-demo` is the only finding); otherwise only the CHEM-006 wording and the palmitic question (now the correction note). No demo species has stereo descriptors, so CHEM-006 compares nothing on the demo. Other committed outputs that held that SMILES: the two legacy fixtures carry their own correct 16-carbon string and were not touched; `demo.test.ts` expectation made exact; `tests/integration/l4-service.test.ts` expectations updated (see tests).

**Tests.** `stereo.test.ts` (10), real-RDKit CHEM-006 cases in `tests/integration/chem-checks.test.ts` (agree, disagree, not compared x7), `facts.test.ts` (+1), `fanout.test.ts` (9: reactions of a species, formula edit queued but not sent, deleted/created species, mechanism not queued, SMARTS / parent / name edits, created or deleted class, the 500 cap with 1,200 reactions counted: 500 at once, 700 behind, a deletion leaves the backlog, every other one checked, calls of at most 32), two real-RDKit referrer tests in `l4-service.test.ts`. Expectations changed, not weakened: the demo findings per rule (CHEM-003 gone), the `replaceState` test keeps a CHEM-002 species instead of the palmitic CHEM-003. **Hand mutants: 5, 5 killed** (no cap; no promotion from the backlog, which never ends; parent edits ignored; mechanisms instead of reactions; no unchanged-plan skip).

### Step 4.4 — Layout worker (`src/layout`, `src/workers/layout`) (2026-10-01)

**Done: a, b, c, d, e, f, g.** `npm run check` green as ONE command: 221 files, 3,572 tests, 126.8 s (vitest 103.4 s; was 214 / 3,412 after Part A). Part A of the session (the 4.3b review record and its three follow-ups) is the commit before this one. Dependency added: **elkjs 0.12.0** (exact, `npm audit` 0 vulnerabilities). No schema change, no `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump. No background command or monitor left running (two stray vitest processes left by stopped commands were killed).

- **Dependency and scan (a).** elkjs 0.12.0, its bundled build `lib/elk.bundled.js` (1.6 MB, loaded lazily) runs inside OUR worker; ELK's own worker is not used. Scan of `elk.bundled.js`, `elk-api.js` and `elk-worker.js`: `eval(` 0, `new Function(` 0, `Function(` 0, string-argument `setTimeout` 0; the only text match was `new Function$lambda$0$Type`, a GWT class name in `elk-worker.js` (not used). So ADR-035's RDKit-only exception is not touched; no STOP was needed. Licence: EPL-2.0 OR GPL-3.0-or-later (EPL-2.0 is the usable choice). dependency-cruiser rule `elk-only-in-adapter` (only `src/layout/elk/` may import `node_modules/elkjs/`), proved with a throwaway violation (removed).
- **Contracts (CLAUDE.md §7; Amendments).** `LayoutResult` gains `fallbackReason?: FallbackReason` (`'timeout' | 'elk_error' | 'engine_unavailable'`, set exactly when `engine` is `'fallback'`; Q7) and `notes: readonly string[]` (what the engine could not honour, never silent); `MAX_LAYOUT_NODES` = 2,000 and `MAX_LAYOUT_EDGES` = 4,000; `LayoutWorkerApi` gains `layoutFallback({ request, reason })`; `LayoutRequest`'s "sorted" is now stated for nodes AND edges, strictly ascending by `<`. **`cacheKey` changed from the contract's text** (REVIEW REQUIRED 1): it now hashes everything that changes the answer, because "node ids + sizes + edge ids" alone lets two requests with different edge endpoints, hints, pins or `previous` share a cached layout.
- **Request checks.** `checkRequest` (`src/layout/validate.ts`, shared by the worker and the service): `TOO_LARGE` over the caps; `BAD_REQUEST` for unsorted or duplicate ids, an edge to a missing node, non-finite sizes (at least 1), spacing, seed (unsigned 32-bit integer), hints (integer 0 to 10,000) or positions, unknown algorithm or direction. In the worker both are `BAD_REQUEST` (the too-large message starts `TOO_LARGE:`); the service refuses both as values before any call.
- **ELK adapter (b)** (`src/layout/elk/`). `LayoutEngine` over a fresh ELK instance per layout (no state shared between calls). Layered: direction, node/layer/edge spacing, seed (`elk.randomSeed`), zero padding. Radial and stress: ring radius and desired edge length are `spacing.layer` plus the largest node dimension (ELK measures them between centres). **Seed** is passed to ELK as `elk.randomSeed`; on stress it changes the layout (tested), on layered and radial it is passed and no effect was seen.
  **Hints, measured on elkjs 0.12.0** (the table is at the top of `map-request.ts`, proved by `tests/integration/layout-elk.test.ts`):
  - layered `layerHint`: honoured as the ORDER of layers (ELK partitions; a lower hint is never to the right of a higher one), not as exact layer numbers; no note. `layerChoiceConstraint` did nothing in 0.12.0 and is not used.
  - layered `orderHint`: honoured as a PREFERENCE (children listed in hint order, `considerModelOrder` = `PREFER_NODES`; `NODES_AND_EDGES` let edge order override the hints); no note. Not used when `previous` is (noted).
  - layered `previous`: honoured as the order inside layers (interactive crossing minimisation with the previous positions); a node without one takes the mean of its positioned neighbours, else the far end; a `previous` that names no node of the request is noted.
  - `pinned`: NOT honoured by any ELK algorithm (layered has no fixed positions; stress's `fixed` option did not hold in 0.12.0), ignored AND noted with a count.
  - layered edge `priority`: honoured (`layered.priority.direction`); ignored and noted by radial and stress.
  - radial: `layerHint` only chooses the root of each tree (noted); `orderHint`, `previous` ignored (noted). stress: `layerHint`, `orderHint`, `previous` ignored (noted).
  - `direction` applies to layered only (radial and stress have none; not a hint, not noted).

  **Two ELK facts found and handled.** (1) ELK radial throws "The given graph is not a tree!" for any graph with a cycle, a parallel edge or a loop, and overlaps nodes on a forest: the adapter builds a spanning tree (BFS per component, root = lowest `layerHint`, else most connected, ties by id), joins several components under ONE virtual size-0 centre node (dropped from the answer), gives ELK only tree edges and draws the rest straight, all counted in `notes`. (2) Radial and stress ignore node sizes, so wide nodes overlapped (measured on the first run): `spread.ts` scales the centres apart by the smallest factor that separates every pair by the node gap (every edge then drawn straight, noted); coincident centres or a factor over 20 are an ELK failure (the worker answers with the fallback, `elk_error`; seen once on the hub_explosion precursor explorer with radial).
- **Fallback (c)** (`src/layout/fallback/`, pure). Cycle breaking: iterative DFS, roots and edges in id order, back edges reversed. Longest-path layering over the DAG in Kahn order; `layerHint` places a node EXACTLY on its layer (an edge between two hinted nodes may point backwards); empty layers are squeezed out. **Barycentric ordering: 8 sweeps** (`SWEEPS`, four downward, four upward, alternating), stable; after every sort the nodes carrying an `orderHint` are put back into the slots hinted nodes occupy in (hint, id) order, so among hinted nodes of a layer the hint decides. Complexity: barycentres O(V + E) per sweep, plus an O(m log m) sort per layer (so a sweep is O(V + E + V log V); not strictly O(V + E)). Positions from node sizes and spacing (layers a layer gap apart, nodes a node gap apart, layers centred on the widest; LEFT and UP mirror RIGHT and DOWN); straight border-to-border routes, a four-point loop for a self-loop; parallel edges draw on top of each other. No two node boxes overlap, by construction, and tested (every direction, odd sizes, 400 / 800, the caps). It ignores `pinned`, `previous` and edge `priority` and the algorithm (it is always layered); each is noted. `seed` is accepted and unused.
- **Worker and service (d).** `src/workers/layout`: `entry.ts` imports `zod-jitless` first (ADR-035) and starts `startLayoutHost`; `layout` answers ELK's result or the fallback with `elk_error` (ELK threw; a note names what it said) or `engine_unavailable` (ELK could not load); `layoutFallback` answers the fallback alone for the reason given. Specs: both idempotent, 2,000 ms. `createLayoutService` (ports only: an `RpcClient<LayoutWorkerApi>`): per-call limit 2 s (override per call); on TIMEOUT the RPC client has terminated and restarted the worker and the service asks the NEW worker for the fallback (`'timeout'`); other failures (crash twice, busy, not ready) get the fallback with `'engine_unavailable'`; if that fails too the result is `{ ok: false, code: 'UNAVAILABLE' }`, never a main-thread layout. A newer request for the same view cancels the older (`CANCELLED`, result dropped; a running ELK still finishes in the worker). **Cache: LRU of 32** results by `cacheKey`, memory only (persistence is Phase 5); cached: ELK answers and the `elk_error` fallback; NOT cached: `timeout` and `engine_unavailable` fallbacks. **Caps: 2,000 nodes, 4,000 edges** refused as values (`TOO_LARGE`).
- **Test-support.** `src/test-support/layout-kit.ts` (sorted requests, a seeded random graph, validity checks, the projection mapper with fixed 120 x 40 node sizes), `layout-projections.ts` (demo and design tier navigator, reaction explorer, precursor explorer), `layout-fakes.ts` (a fake ELK), `spawnLayoutWorker` in `rpc-node-port.ts`, fixture `tests/fixtures/layout/worker.ts` (modes `real`, `busy` = an uninterruptible synchronous loop, `throw`).

**Tests (e).** Files 221, tests 3,572. New: `src/layout/layout.test.ts` (checks, key, LRU, routes), `src/layout/fallback/fallback.test.ts` (19), `src/layout/elk/elk.test.ts` (fake ELK: mapping, radial tree, spread, failure modes), `src/workers/layout/layout.test.ts` (18: in-memory port with every message cloned, the specs, handlers, refusals, cache and LRU, supersede, the timeout path with a fake engine, no worker able to answer, a crash retried once, two crashes), `tests/integration/layout-elk.test.ts` (40, REAL elkjs: valid layouts for three algorithms on chains, cycles, loops, parallel edges, disconnected graphs, empty and single; every hint row of the table; the seed; **determinism property: the same request gives byte-identical positions and routes from fresh instances and from a reused one, ELK 60 runs and the fallback 150 runs, durationMs excluded**; the key moves with every real change), `tests/integration/layout-projections.test.ts` (35: the real demo and design-tier projections, three algorithms, valid and structured-clone safe, deterministic, the fallback valid and fast), `tests/integration/layout-worker-threads.test.ts` (5, a REAL thread: a layout with real ELK for all three algorithms; **the timeout path with a thread that cannot be interrupted: TIMEOUT at 500 ms, the thread terminated, a NEW thread answers the fallback `timeout`, the 8 s loop is not waited for**; `elk_error`; refusals never reach the thread; the worker's own BAD_REQUEST). Every param and result passes `structuredClone` (the in-memory and real ports clone every message). **Hand mutants: about 21 run, all killed except one pair that is equivalent** (the two `signal.aborted` re-checks in the service: the RPC client already answers a cancelled call `CANCELLED` and drops its response). Survivors found and fixed with tests: node spacing across a layer, `settleHints` (killed only when both calls are removed, as intended), the `engine_unavailable` reason after two crashes, BAD_REQUEST of `layoutFallback`.

**Benchmarks (f)** (`tests/perf/layout.bench.ts`, 14 `budgets.mjs` entries, run once alone with `npm run bench`; no `bench:baseline`, no `bench:compare`), mean / p99 ms, worker code in Node, real ELK, a fresh instance per call, no cache, 10 samples:
- ELK layered, design tier 400 nodes / 465 edges (dense precursor explorer): 549.7 / 620.4. hub_explosion reaction explorer 400 / 486: 573.5 / 620.4. **The row (< 300 ms for <= 400 nodes) is MISSED on this machine.** (Earlier single cold runs read 233 to 297 ms; this machine's timing moved by 2x between runs, so the figure is judged on the bench run.)
- ELK layered, design tier dense navigator 60 nodes / 800 edges: 3,558.3 / 3,915.7 (over the 2 s limit: in production this projection would time out and fall back; the Phase 5 Scene mapping should lay out edge BUNDLES, not 800 raw edges). Demo reaction explorer 49 / 70: 87.3 / 98.1.
- ELK layered synthetic 400 / 800: acyclic 1,629.4 / 1,697.4; with cycles, loops and parallel edges 5,242.8 / 5,956.0.
- Radial (informal): dense precursor explorer 613.0 / 660.1; synthetic 400 / 800 with cycles 413.7 / 459.6. Stress (informal): 40 / 80 720.0 / 762.5; 100 / 200 4,230.8 / 4,667.6 (stress is all-pairs; 150 nodes took about 11 s in a scratch run, and its iteration-limit option had no effect).
- Fallback: 400 / 800 with cycles, loops, parallel edges 4.46 / 7.36; design tier precursor explorer 3.14 / 5.45; 2,000 / 4,000 (the caps) 22.5 / 32.1.
- One round trip through a real worker thread (thread and ELK already started, no cache), demo reaction explorer 49 / 70: 123.1 / 161.9 (about 36 ms over the same layout in process).
- ELK option measurements (scratch, not adopted, quality is a decision): on the 400-node hub_explosion projection default 590-820, thoroughness 3 about 460-490, thoroughness 1 about 380-420, polyline routing about 415-425, polyline plus thoroughness 3 about 300-370 ms; on the dense navigator default about 3,300-3,600, thoroughness 3 about 1,900-2,100, thoroughness 1 about 1,300-1,400, polyline plus thoroughness 3 about 1,650-1,700 ms.

**REVIEW REQUIRED**
1. **`cacheKey` text changed** (above): the contract's "sorted node ids + sizes, sorted edge ids" cannot distinguish requests that differ in edge endpoints, hints, pins or `previous`; a cache hit would return a layout for a different graph. Smallest change taken: hash all of them (not `requestId`). Approve, or say what else.
2. **The ELK layered row is missed** (about 550-575 ms mean at 400 nodes against < 300 ms) and a dense navigator projection (60 nodes / 800 raw edges) takes about 3.5 s, over the 2 s limit. No quality option was changed on my own. Options, measured above: lower `elk.layered.thoroughness`, polyline edge routing; or a Phase 9 exception; and in Phase 5 lay out bundles rather than raw edges. Please decide.
3. **Stress is all-pairs**: 100 nodes about 4 s, over the limit. A node cap per algorithm (say stress at most 60) is not in the plan; without it a stress request on 100 or more nodes always times out (restart plus fallback, uncached). Decide a cap or accept.
4. **Radial** gives the fallback (`elk_error`) when ELK puts two nodes on the same spot (seen once on a real projection). Acceptable, or a better overlap pass later?
5. **`pinned` is not honoured by any algorithm** (listed in notes); `previous` and `orderHint` only by layered; see the table above. Confirm this is enough for Phase 5.
6. **Part A deviation:** mechanisms are not queued by species changes (see the follow-ups section above).
7. Licence: elkjs is EPL-2.0 OR GPL-3.0-or-later.

- **a. ELK layered defaults** (`src/layout/elk/map-request.ts`): `elk.edgeRouting` = `POLYLINE`, `elk.layered.thoroughness` = 3; recorded in ADR-007 as a dated line with the reason. Layered only (radial and stress untouched; tested). Determinism and validity tests re-run green.
  **Defect found by the determinism property after the change, not caused by it:** ELK stress with `seed` 0 was different on every call (ELK reads a raw `elk.randomSeed` of 0 as "from the clock"; measured: 8 distinct layouts in 8 runs; seed 1 and 7 gave 1). Fixed: the seed reaches ELK as `elkSeed(seed) = 1 + seed % 2,147,483,646` (never 0, inside a Java int; seeds above 2^31-3 wrap and can share a layout). Tests: `elkSeed` unit test, and a real-ELK test that seed 0 and the largest seed are byte-identical over 5 runs for all three algorithms.
- **b. Bundled mode** (`src/test-support/layout-kit.ts`): `projectionLayoutEdges(projection, 'raw' | 'bundled')`, and `projectionRequest(projection, { edges: 'bundled' })`: one edge per `EdgeBundle` (the bundle's id and endpoints) plus every edge in no bundle. Tests (`tests/integration/layout-projections.test.ts`): count identity, no raw edge of a bundle remains, no two edges share an ordered pair, sorted, valid real-ELK layouts.
- **c. Stress cap** (`src/workers/layout/service.ts`, `MAX_STRESS_NODES` = 40 in `src/layout/contracts.ts`, additive): a stress request over 40 nodes is laid out with ELK layered and the result says so in `notes` (engine `'elk'`, no fallbackReason), cache hits included. Tests in `src/workers/layout/layout.test.ts` (40 stays stress; 41 becomes layered with the note once, also from the cache; radial and layered of any size untouched).

### Step 4.5 — Search worker (`src/search`, `src/workers/search`) (2026-10-01)

**Done: a, b, c, d, e, f, g (h not needed: nothing left over).** `npm run check` green as ONE command: 228 files, 3,675 tests, Vitest 101.0 s, whole command 2 min 6 s (was 221 / 3,580 after Part A). Dependency added: **minisearch 7.2.0** (exact, MIT, `npm audit` 0 vulnerabilities; recorded in docs/DEPENDENCIES.md). Scan of every `.js` / `.cjs` in the package (dist cjs, es, umd and its sources): `eval(` 0, `new Function` 0, `Function(` 0, string-argument `setTimeout` / `setInterval` 0, so ADR-035 is not touched and no STOP was needed. dependency-cruiser rule `minisearch-only-in-adapter` (only `src/search/engine/` imports it), clean on the tree. Commit A (the 4.4 review follow-ups) is the commit before this one. No schema change, no `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump. No background command or monitor left running.

- **Contracts (CLAUDE.md §7; Amendments).** Q6 as approved: `SearchDocument.scopeRanks` (exam version id to rank 0 official ... 3 outside; a record the version does not map has no rank), `lifecycle`, `SearchQuery.examVersionId`, `SearchIndexUpdate.fromRevision`, `apply` answers `ApplyAnswer`, `serialize()` / `loadSerialized({ json, revision })`. Beyond Q6 (REVIEW REQUIRED 1): `SearchDocument.aliases` (so a hit can say it matched an alias; `names` no longer holds aliases), `textTruncated`, `facets.scopes` defined (distinct scope names, best first), `SearchHit.score` rounded to 9 decimals, the limits `SEARCH_TEXT_CAP` 4,000, `MAX_QUERY_CHARS` 1,000, `MAX_QUERY_WORDS` 16, `MAX_SEARCH_LIMIT` 200, and the old `SearchService` interface became `SearchEngine` (the index itself); `SearchWorkerApi` gains `serialize` and `loadSerialized`, `apply` answers a value, `search` is `NOT_READY` before a load.
- **What each document contains (b).** The header of `src/search/document/builder.ts` and SEARCH §6 have the full list. Every kind: title, names, aliases, entered formula (species, class, reagent), structure text (species, class), tags, plain text of its OWN rich text and fields, lifecycle, review status. Species: names, aliases, formula, structure, isotope note, stereo descriptors and note, properties, acid-base, study and general notes. Class: formula text and general formula (as the formula), generic structure, R-group note, description, notes. Reagent: names, aliases plus every profile's label and aliases, formula text, profile behaviours, description, notes. Reaction: name, aliases, generalised equation, the original wording, labels and notes of participants, stages, variants, outcomes, products and intermediates, reversibility, selectivity, limitations, notes. Named reaction, concept, transformation, mechanism (variants and steps), caveat (title, rule, statement, trigger, explanation), route (description, step notes), topic, reference (title, authors, edition, publisher, identifier, year, notes). Not indexed: relationships, exams, exam versions, topic mappings, exam relevance, assets, presentation records (tested on the demo, which has presentation records).
  **Does a document contain text of OTHER records? No.** A reaction does not carry its participants' names (ids stay ids), a species does not carry its classes' names. So a rename, an alias or a note on a record refreshes that one document, and renaming the 1,000-referrer hub is one document (tested: a reaction's document is byte-identical after its participant is renamed). What a document does read from elsewhere is DERIVED, not text: `scopeRanks` / `facets.scopes` (core's `scopeOf` per exam version), `facets.topicIds` (mapped topics and their ancestors), and for a reaction `facets.reagentFamilies` (the families of its reagent participants). Those are what item d refreshes. Consequence (REVIEW REQUIRED 2): searching "ethanol" finds the species, not the reactions that use it; that is the navigator's job (SEARCH §3 lists no such field).
- **MiniSearch configuration (c)** (`src/search/engine/config.ts`, `query.ts`). Fields name, alias (boost 4, prefix ON, fuzzy by length), formula (3, exact), structure (2, exact, the trimmed text as ONE case-sensitive term), tags (2) and text (1) (no prefix, fuzzy by length). Terms come from core's `tokens()` (folded words, adjacent pairs, whole phrase joined), body text word by word so it does not triple the index. Fuzzy: 1 edit at 5+ characters, 2 at 9+, none shorter (characters, not UTF-16 units). Prefix, fuzzy and exactness are set per query GROUP (names and aliases; formulas; tags and text) through query-combination options, because MiniSearch applies them per query, not per field. Ranking: score (rounded to 9 decimals), exam-version scope rank (only when the query names a version), published before draft, folded title, id: a total order. Kinds and facets filter (any value within a facet, all facets; an empty list is no constraint; with an exam version the scope facet is read under it). No words with a filter lists what the filter admits (a palette prefix alone); no words and no filter lists nothing.
  **Facts found and handled.** (1) MiniSearch's `discard` leaves old postings until a vacuum, so document counts, and with them scores, would depend on the history of updates and searches: removal uses `remove` with the stored document, so the index is a function of its documents. (2) BM25's average field length drifts by a few ulps with the add/remove history: scores are rounded to 9 decimals, which lets an incrementally maintained index and a fresh one agree on hits AND order. (3) A first draft let the joined form of a multi-word query be fuzzy; "Reaction 41" then also matched every "reaction" (two edits): the joined form is now names and aliases only, prefix, no fuzzy.
- **Incremental updates (d)** (`src/workers/search`). `apply` with `fromRevision`; the worker answers `{ ok, revision, upserted, removed }` or `REVISION_MISMATCH` with `have` (-1: nothing loaded); everything is validated before any change (a malformed update changes nothing). The main-side sync (`sync.ts`, ports only: state, the full indexes, the store revision, the search service, a debounce `schedule`) rebuilds the change's own records and, via `fanout.ts`, every document whose derived data another record's change moved: a topic mapping or exam relevance (the target before AND after, the old one read from the inverse patches), a reagent whose families changed (its reactions), a prerequisite field, a relationship or any mapping (every concept: a concept's derived scope depends on the direct scope of what it is a prerequisite of), an exam version whose units or exam changed, a topic deleted or re-parented (everything). Field changes are found by comparing the old record (from the inverse patch) with the new one, because the store's patches replace whole records. Dependent documents are queued 500 at a time (`REFRESH_CAP`), the rest in a backlog behind them, never dropped; only documents that differ from the one last sent (a hash is remembered per record) are sent; an update that changes no document still advances the index revision (the warm-boot cache is keyed by it). `replace` and `remote` changes, a mismatch and any worker failure lead to ONE full load of the current state (the previous index keeps answering until it lands); a failing worker leaves the sync `down` with the reason and the next trigger loads again. `serialize` / `loadSerialized` carry one JSON text (MiniSearch's export, the documents, the format version, the configuration id); a text that does not load is `BAD_INDEX` as a value and leaves the engine as it was. The service supersedes per channel (a newer query cancels the older: `CANCELLED`).
  **Hand mutants:** 11 mutations of the fan-out rules (old target, new target, concept fan-out for a mapping, for a relationship, for a prerequisite field, reagent families, exam version units, topic parent, field-change detection forced false and forced true, direct records skipped), all KILLED. Two mutants first SURVIVED and taught something: "exam record created" (the rule was dead: `scopeOf` never reads an exam record, only its id, so the rule was removed) and "topic created" (a new topic reaches nothing, whatever points at it is a change of its own: narrowed to deletion).
- **Palette grammar (e)** (`src/search/palette/parse.ts`, pure): plain text; `r: s: c: g: n: m: t:` narrow to reaction, species, class, reagent, named reaction, mechanism, topic; `>` commands (text lower-cased, spaces collapsed). Case and whitespace tolerant, a full-width colon counts; only the first prefix is read; reads at most 1,064 characters; cuts to 16 words and 1,000 characters and reports `truncated`.

**Tests (f).** `src/search/engine/engine.test.ts` (29: C2H5OH = C₂H₅OH, Wolff-Kishner = wolffkishner, α = alpha, the query parts agree with core `tokens()` for random input, prefix on names only, fuzzy by term length at 4, 5, 8, 9 characters, formulas and structures exact, the order and its tie-breaks, any load order and updates give the same answers, what a hit matched, filters and facets, revisions and refusals, serialise round trips and nine kinds of bad index, structuredClone of every param and result), `src/search/palette/parse.test.ts` (18, fast-check: never throws, within limits, linear), `src/search/document/builder.test.ts` (14: kinds, contents, no text of other records, the cap including a surrogate pair, scope ranks against `scopeOf`, topics, reagent families), `src/workers/search/search.test.ts` (19: the worker over RPC with every message cloned, refusals as values, NOT_READY, supersede, ordinary and curriculum updates, **ADV-019 search half**, **ADV-058 search part**, the 500 cap with a backlog of 1,040 reactions, mismatch and one full load, a crashed worker, a failing worker), `src/workers/search/fanout.test.ts` (10), **`tests/property/search-incremental.test.ts` (3, THE property: after random command sequences of renames, aliases, tags, lifecycle, notes, creations, deletions, mappings, relevance, coverage, topic edits, prerequisites, families, undo, redo and a replaceState, the incrementally maintained index holds exactly the documents of a full build and answers a generated query set of about 200 queries (titles, prefixes, typos, aliases, formulas, structures, facets, exam versions) with the same hits, order and scores as an index rebuilt from scratch; flushing after every step or only at the end gives the same index; the walk is not vacuous: every edit kind, undo, redo and replace are reached)**, `tests/integration/search-worker-threads.test.ts` (2, a REAL thread: boot from a store, edits flow, answers equal an in-process index, supersede, a second thread booted alike, the serialised index loads in another thread, NOT_READY before a load, an `apply` mismatch).

**Benchmarks (g)** (`tests/perf/search.bench.ts`, 15 entries in `tests/perf/budgets-search.mjs`, run once alone with `npm run bench`; no `bench:baseline`, no `bench:compare`), mean / p99 ms, worker code in Node, the design tier dense shape (5,000 reactions, 2,000 other entities, 10 topics, two exam versions, formulas on the species); this machine moves by 20 to 40 percent between runs:
- Queries (row: < 50 ms p95, judged on p99): name prefix "Reaction 41" 27.8 / 42.5; fuzzy name "Reactin 4100" 4.8 / 7.7; exact formula 0.16 / 0.95; palette-prefixed "r: Reaction 4100" 25.9 / 48.2. Informal worst cases: a prefix every reaction matches ("reacti") 33.2 / 58.0 (73.7 in another run); fuzzy over every species ("specias") 2.6 / 5.8.
  Why the prefix rows are slow: the synthetic names are "Reaction N", so the joined-pair terms of core's `tokens()` ("reaction41", "reaction42", ...) give 5,000 distinct terms that start with "reaction", and a prefix on "reaction" scores all of them. Real names are far more varied; this is the adversarial case. The two prefix rows pass on p99 with a thin margin (a first version, before the joined form lost its fuzzy match, read 58 ms p99 on "Reaction 41").
- Full builds (SEARCH §5 row: < 1.5 s): the index from the documents (worker) 177.0 / 218.6, so the row is met with a large margin. Every document built from the state on the MAIN thread: 869.8 / 934.9 with the indexes built too, 192.6 / 210.8 with the indexes ready (the builder and `scopeOf` alone). serialize 131.6 / 157.3; loadSerialized 126.9 / 164.1 (about the same as a build here, the synthetic documents are small).
- Incremental: the worker takes one document (apply) 0.18 / 0.59; the sync end to end, an ordinary edit (change analysis, document build, hash, in-process engine) 0.13 / 0.43; the rename of the 1,000-referrer hub 0.15 / 0.68 (documents hold no other record's name, so it is one document, the same cost); a curriculum edit that moves every document (an exam version's coverage), 6,998 documents rebuilt and hashed 500 at a time, only the changed sent: 302.8 / 379.3 in all, about 22 ms per slice of 500.

**REVIEW REQUIRED**
1. **Contract additions beyond Q6** (above): `aliases`, `textTruncated`, `facets.scopes` shape, the limits, `SearchService` renamed `SearchEngine`, `serialize` / `loadSerialized` on the worker API. Approve, or say what else.
2. **A document never holds another record's text**, so a reaction is not found by its participants' names (searching "ethanol" finds the species). It keeps renames to one document and makes the 1,000-referrer hub rename cost nothing. If a view needs "reactions of ethanol" by search, say so: it would be a field of the reaction document and a fan-out from every rename of a participant.
3. **Derived formulas** (SEARCH §3 says "entered or derived"): documents carry the ENTERED formula only; a formula derived from a structure needs the chem worker and its facts; wiring that is Phase 5.
4. **Prefix applies to every query word**, as SEARCH §3 says ("prefix search on names"), not only the last. It is what makes broad prefixes cost about 30 ms on the adversarial synthetic names. Prefix on the last word only would cut that to about 10 ms and is the usual type-ahead rule; it changes behaviour ("eth acid" would no longer find "ethanoic acid"). Decide.
5. **Fuzzy and transpositions:** edit distance 1 at 5 to 8 characters, as specified, counts a swap of two letters as two edits, so "Reactoin" does not find "Reaction". A Damerau distance is not in MiniSearch.
6. **Main-thread cost of a full load:** building every document on the main thread is 193 ms with the indexes ready (870 ms with the indexes built too) at the design tier, above a frame, for a boot, a `replace`, a `remote` change or a mismatch; a curriculum edit that moves everything is about 22 ms per slice of 500 (over 16 ms). Options for Phase 5/9: a smaller `REFRESH_CAP` (250), or build the documents in the compute worker, which already holds the replica and the indexes (the builder is pure and takes ports; the sync would then ask the worker for the update). Not done: the 4.5 text asks for a main-side sync.
7. **The sync needs the FULL indexes** (`ReadonlyIndexes`: topics, relationships), which the store does not expose (it keeps `CoreIndexes`); the test rig keeps them as the app will, applying each ChangeSet with `indexEngine.apply`. Phase 5 must provide the same port.
8. **Scores are rounded to 9 decimals** in `SearchHit.score` (needed for incremental = rebuild, see above).

**Open items.** Phase 5: create the search worker and service in the app (D7); provide the sync's ports (state, full indexes, revision, a debounce); wire the warm-boot cache (`serialize` after a quiet period keyed by revision, `loadSerialized` at boot, `BAD_INDEX` falls back to a load); call `sync.reload()` from the client's `onRestart` hook; show `sync.status()` (down, backlog) in Diagnostics. Step 4.6: the build smoke must reference `src/workers/search/entry.ts` and check minisearch's chunk has no `eval` or `new Function`; every `SearchWorkerApi` method already has an in-process test (`engine.test.ts`, `search.test.ts`).
