Also accepted: the build smoke (CSP exact, four worker chunks, the WASM a separate hashed asset, `new Function` only in rdkit-glue: the build half of ADR-035); coverage with chem, layout and search under the 80% lines threshold; npm audit 0; the property suite at 200 runs 179/179; test:timing 10/10; bench:compare 198 entries, none over +20% p99, no baseline rewrite.

**Decisions.**
1. **Real-thread tests (REVIEW REQUIRED 1):** the scheduling is fixed, no timeout is touched. Built in the follow-up commit (`phase-4.6: review follow-ups`): `vitest.config.ts` has two projects. `main` runs the 223 other files; `real-threads` runs the 9 files that start a real thread (`tests/integration/*-threads.test.ts` x8 and `worker-crash-recovery.test.ts`) with `sequence.groupOrder: 1` (after `main`), `fileParallelism: false` and `maxWorkers: 1`; `cache: false` turns Vitest's results cache off, so file order no longer depends on an earlier run. Evidence: with the nine files marked failed in the results cache, the unfixed config reproduced 2 chem timeouts in one full run; with the fix, three `npm run check` runs from that same starting state were green (232 files, 3,728 tests; Vitest 175.5 s, 197.6 s, 195.1 s, longer than the 119.6 s of step 4.6 because the real-thread files now run alone). A handbook note alone was rejected: a check that stays red until a cache is deleted is not acceptable. Option 3 (a first call that tolerates a cold load) is a product question, not a test fix: decision 6.
2. **Title-based completeness test (REVIEW REQUIRED 2):** accepted as a regression guard, not as proof. Known limitation: generic names (search, load, apply, layout, parse, depict, matches) pass on any title with that word. The recording client is not built. `importCancel` exercised through the main-side flow's cancel is an in-process test and is enough.
3. **The six title renames are approved** (Part 0.3: titles only; no test body, assertion or ADV/INV/P id changed).
4. **The Phase 9 exception list after Phase 4** is packet section 8: the seven items (class-hub parent move; review-status change on the like-water hub; writer `replaceReferences` on the 1,000-reaction hub; follower hub `applyRemote`; snapshot build and restore, main-thread parts; `prepareApply` double validation; ELK layered at 400 nodes), this run's numbers next to the Gate C ones. `validateTouched` without previous stays closed on the worker path. The Phase 4 benches get their baseline at the Gate E `bench:baseline`.
5. **`src/app/main.tsx`'s side-effect import of the worker factories** is approved as temporary; it creates no worker (the factories are lazy). Step 5.1 replaces it with real use (D7).
6. **New item for the Phase 5 plan review:** the 2 s limit of the chem and layout workers also covers the engine's start-up in a fresh thread (step 4.6 report: RDKit's start-up has to fit the 2 s window). In a real browser on a slow device a cold load may not fit: the first call times out, the worker restarts, and three such restarts in a row end in `gave_up` (ADR-034). The plan review decides whether start-up gets its own limit (candidate: a warm-up call sent on creation and in `onRestart` with a load timeout, so the 2 s limit covers calculations only; a contract amendment, CLAUDE.md §7); step 5.1 measures the real-browser cold start. Nothing is built now.
7. **Environment:** the first full run of step 4.6 was on battery with Edge open and 7 real-thread tests timed out. Full checks, gates and phase closes run plugged in, heavy apps closed.

**Contracts fit scan (read-only; nothing applied).** Q11 was done without touching package.json or the lockfile: `npm install --ignore-scripts` in a temp folder outside the repo, then a text scan of the published code (not a browser run).

| Q | answer (fits / gap) | gap and smallest amendment (landing step) |
|---|---|---|
| Q1 Indexes on main | gap | The store maintains only `coreIndexEngine` (`getIndexes()` returns `CoreIndexes`: no `navigatorRollup`, participations, `topicsOf`, `displayOf`). The navigator needs `navigatorRollup()` through `ProjectionContext.indexes`; the search sync needs the full `ReadonlyIndexes` (its port); the L4 service needs only `referrersOf` / `descendantsOf` and is already served by `getIndexes()` (fits). The full engine does NOT support the one-step-back rollback: `apply` calls `build` whenever `changes.revision !== core.revision + 1` (src/core/indexes/full.ts), so a rejected commit on a full-engine store would rebuild (about 0.5 s); its handles are one mutable lineage (an older handle throws "Stale index handle"); and the derived tables take about 300 ms of the 448 ms hub edit (open item 58) against the 16 / 50 ms commit rows. Option A (store takes the full engine) therefore breaks the commit rows on hub edits. Smallest amendment (option B, no core change): an app-level `DerivedIndexes` service in `src/app/services`: builds `indexEngine.build(state, revision)` lazily on the first read; `store.subscribe` queues ChangeSets; a read applies them in order (a `replace` / `remote` cause, a gap, or a queue over a bound -> one `build`); `current()` returns the handle for the store's revision and callers never keep it past the call. Rejected commits are never announced, so no rollback exists on this path. Cost: the hub edit's 300-450 ms lands on main at the first read after it, off the commit path; 5.1a benches it and the reviewer judges. Landing: 5.1a. |
| Q2 Store and findings for React | fits | `getState()` and `getRevision()` keep their identity until a commit (frozen Immer state); `subscribe(listener)` fires after each commit, with the ChangeSet as an argument that a `useSyncExternalStore` subscriber ignores; `canUndo()` / `canRedo()` are booleans (valid snapshots). `FindingStore.counts()` returns one frozen object per change (no scan on read; the recount is a scan of all findings per change, not measured), and includes `review_required`; `bySubject` is cached; `all()` without a filter is cached (with a filter it returns a new array: not a snapshot). Notes: `historyLabels()` builds new arrays on every call (read on demand for a tooltip, never as a snapshot); L4 results change the finding store WITHOUT a ChangeSet, so React needs a second subscription (finding store). No amendment; selector hooks are built in 5.1c (`src/ui/hooks`). |
| Q3 Projections | gap | `ProjectionRequest` (view, focus, expansion, filters, limits with signal) fits the UI. But `navigatorProjection.build` never reads `request.expansion` or `request.focus` (src/core/graph/navigator/build.ts: its header says "Not built yet: hub expansion/focus, degree caps and overflow"; it shows every real hub plus Unclassified and one arrow per outcome product, truncated at `maxEdges`): hub expansion DOES NOT EXIST in the navigator projection, although GRAPH_SEMANTICS §3 and §11 define it and plan step 5.5a needs it. A memo "by request and the revisions of the records read" does not exist either: `Projection.key` is documented as "request + revision of every dependency record" but `projectionKey(request, revision)` hashes the store's global revision, so it changes on every commit, and no read-set is recorded anywhere. Smallest amendments: (1) a core step before 5.5a that gives the navigator projection expansion (an expanded hub reveals its member species and their reactions as nodes, with `hidden` counts; the degree cap and overflow node for a hub with 1,000 reactions; collapse; a bench; the request and `ExpansionState` types stay as they are; GRAPH_SEMANTICS §3 gets a dated line); (2) the memo is Phase 5's, in the navigator projection hook (5.5a): key = request hash; rebuild only when a ChangeSet touches a record kind the view reads (reaction, species, reagent, class, concept, named_reaction, navigator_anchor, entity_display, caveat, topic mapping and exam records for `scopeOf`, review status); after a rebuild keep the previous `Projection` object when it is structurally equal, and key the layout and scene memos on a CONTENT hash of the projection, not on `Projection.key`. The 5.5a counter test then uses an edit of a kind the view does not read (for example a reference or a presentation record). Landing: (1) a new core step ahead of 5.5a, to be placed at the contract decisions; (2) 5.5a. |
| Q4 LayoutRequest and LayoutResult against the Scene | fits (one gap) | Sizes (`width` / `height` from the injected measurer), `layerHint` / `orderHint`, `previous`, `direction`, `seed`, spacing, one edge per bundle (the edge id is the bundle id), the 2,000 / 4,000 caps, sorted input, `positions` (top-left), `edgeRoutes` per edge id, `bounds`, `notes`, `fallbackReason` and `cacheKey` all fit; `pinned` is unused by any algorithm. Gap: self-loops. Nothing says what the engines do with `from === to` (the fallback drops them, `src/layout/fallback/graph.ts`; the ELK mapping does not say). Smallest amendment: the builder leaves self-loop edges OUT of the `LayoutRequest`, draws the loop glyph itself beside its node, and counts them in the Scene (ADV-005); a comment in `src/layout/contracts.ts`. Also: edges carry no label size, so ELK reserves no room for arrow text; label positions are the Scene's job (route midpoint plus offset) and overlaps are possible (accept; polish in Phase 8, or add an optional `labelSize` later). Landing: 5.3. |
| Q5 Scene contracts | gap | (a) `SceneNode.state` holds `selected`, `focused`, `dimmed`, `onPath`: selection and focus in the node would rebuild the Scene and re-render the node layer on every click, against "hover and focus on an overlay layer, the node layer does not re-render" (PERFORMANCE §6; 5.4a test "a selection change re-renders the overlay only"). Smallest amendment: remove `selected` and `focused` from `SceneNode.state` (they live in the selection store and are drawn by the overlay layer; keyboard focus uses `aria-activedescendant` on the canvas composite so no node element changes); keep `dimmed` (scope, a projection fact) and `onPath` (Phase 7 routes). (b) The self-loop glyph: `SceneEdge` has `points`, `from`, `to` but no loop shape; the builder sets `points` to the loop path and a self-loop is drawn by `from === to` (document it). (c) Relationship edge labels fit `above` / `below` (`FormattedText`). (d) `Scene.revision` is undefined in the contract: define it as the store revision of the projection it was built from (informational); layer memoisation keys on content (`projectionKey` + `layoutKey` + display options), never on the revision. (e) `depictionKey` cannot equal `Depiction.key`: that key hashes the toolkit name and version, known only after a worker call. Define `depictionKey` as the hash of (structure format, value, `DepictionOptions`) computed in core, and let the main-side depiction cache key add the toolkit version once known. (f) Truncation, Unanchored and layout notes have no place in `Scene`: add additive `Scene.truncation: Truncation or null`, `Scene.notes: readonly string[]` (layout notes) and `Scene.layoutEngine` with `fallbackReason`; the Unanchored list is read from the `Projection` by the feature (ids can number thousands; never copied into the Scene). Landing: contract decisions, then 5.3. |
| Q6 Persistence for the UI | fits (gaps in the plan) | uiState: `bootReport.uiState` gives every valid entry at boot (first paint without async reads), `getUiState` / `setUiState` per key, `uiStateValidators` at adapter creation, damaged entries reset and listed in `uiStateReset`: fits. `setUiState` runs through the write queue's `runOrdered`, which REJECTS when the tab is read-only (follower, newer schema): the panels store must treat that as "not persisted in a read-only tab" (a status, never thrown); no amendment. derivedCache: caller keys, `JsonValue` values, LRU by `lastUsedAt`, 100 MB soft cap, `put` / `get` do not use the write queue (they work in a read-only tab), failures are values (`quota`, `error`) and reported through `onEvent`: fits for layouts, the search index (one JSON text, keyed by revision; old rows age out by LRU) and depictions. `getStatus()` and `pendingRevisions` fit `beforeunload`. `OpenResult` maps: `read_write` -> writer; `read_only` + `other_tab_is_writer` -> follower banner; `read_only` + `newer_schema` / `integrity_failure` / `migration_failed` -> read-only with the reason and `detail`, never promoted (E11); `unavailable` -> session-only (no adapter: the store runs in memory and says so). Gaps: (1) wiring that exists in `src/persistence` but is in no plan step: `startPeriodicSnapshots` (PERSISTENCE §9), the clean-shutdown marker (`setLastCleanShutdownAt`, best effort on `pagehide`), the integrity marker, `requestPersistentStorage`, `storageEstimate`: add to 5.1a (writer only). (2) `bootReport` fields with no banner in 5.1c: `crashSuspected`, `newlyQuarantined` / `quarantinedCount`, `assetIdsWithoutBlob`, `unverifiedAssetBlobs`, `migrationSteps` (nothing silent, CLAUDE.md §1.5): add a one-line notice for each to the status line or banners in 5.1c; the full pages stay Phase 9 (E9). |
| Q7 Engine start-up | gap | Chem: `entry.ts` passes `loadRdkit` to the host; `createRdkitToolkit` loads RDKit lazily at the first call (`ready()`), so the first `parse` / `depict` pays the 7.2 MB WASM fetch, compile and init. Layout: `load.ts` does `await import('elkjs/lib/elk.bundled.js')` at the first `layout` (about 1.6 MB). The 2 s clock (`CHEM_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS`, `LAYOUT_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS`) starts when the client sends the call (client.ts `setTimeout` at send), so the cold load is inside the first call's 2 s; a timeout terminates the worker, the next one is cold again, three in a row end in `gave_up` (ADR-034). Smallest amendment (Q7 candidate, recommended): an additive idempotent method `warmUp` on `ChemWorkerApi` and `LayoutWorkerApi` (params `{}`, answers `{ ready: boolean }`; the chem handler awaits `toolkit.ready()`, the layout handler loads ELK), with its own spec limit `ENGINE_LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS` (value for the reviewer: 20,000 ms until 5.1c measures a real cold start); the chem and layout services send it on creation and gate real calls behind it (a failed warm-up answers `unavailable` with its message), and the client's `onRestart` hook sends it again (the hook is awaited and bounded by `restartHookTimeoutMs`, 30 s). A dated ADR-034 line and a contract Amendment entry. Alternative: a `firstCallTimeoutMs` field in `RpcMethodSpec` (first call after a start) is smaller but a hanging structure as the first call would then hold the long limit. `maxInFlight` 16: the 17th call is answered `BUSY` (it maps to `unavailable`), and one timeout restarts the worker and fails EVERY call in flight (each is retried once, D4); a depiction burst (every visible species at LOD 3) must therefore queue on main with a small cap (4 in flight, leaving room for L4 batches), as 5.4b plans; no contract change. Landing: warm-up in 5.1b (amendment at the contract decisions); queue cap in 5.4b; measurement in 5.1c. |
| Q8 Main-side services and their ports | gap (providers) | Providers that exist: replica sync (factory, store: `getState` / `getRevision` / `subscribe`), boot replica (factory; its `validate` goes to `createDexieAdapter({ validate })` and `FollowerOptions.validate`), adapter (`createOrnDb` with `indexedDB` + `IDBKeyRange`, `createWebLocksWriterLock` over `LockManagerLike`, `createBroadcastChangeChannel`, clock, `IdFactory`, `migrationRunner`, `realScheduler`), follower, restore (`RestoreSnapshotsPort` = the adapter), export (`AssetBytesProvider` over `getAssetBlob`), chem service (client + `CHEM_SPECS`), L4 (`state`, `findings` from the store options, `indexes` = `store.getIndexes()`, `chem`, `schedule`), layout service (client), search sync and service. Ports with NO provider today: (1) `ReadonlyIndexes` for the search sync and the navigator (Q1); (2) `Clock` and `RandomSource` for production: `src/core` only defines the types and `createIdFactory(clock, rng)`, so `src/app` writes both (Date, `crypto.getRandomValues`); (3) the `schedule` debounce providers of L4 and search (`realScheduler`-style timers in `src/app`); (4) `ImportStorePort` and `AssetStagePort` wiring (shown only in tests/integration/import-apply-persistence.test.ts); (5) a persistence port for `createLayoutService` (memory LRU 32 only; 5.5b needs get / put by key; additive `LayoutServiceOptions.persist`); (6) `SearchSync` has no way to start from a warm-loaded index (`needsLoad` is true at creation, so a `loadSerialized` at boot is followed by a full load): additive `adopt(revision)` (Q9); (7) an in-process IMPORT for a down compute worker (restore and export have theirs; `createComputeImport` answers `REJECTED`, so "Load demo" on a first run with the worker down has no path; E7 says no second code path): offer the existing in-process import session as a value, like restore. The adapter comment still names `createComputeBootValidator` (it is `createBootReplica().validate`): fix the comment. Landing: 5.1a (1-3), 5.1b (4-7). |
| Q9 Search UI | gap | `PaletteQuery` (`mode: search` with `text`, `kinds`, `truncated`; `mode: command`) maps to `SearchQuery` { text, kinds, limit } plus `examVersionId` and `facets` from UI state: fits. A hit (`SearchHit.ref`, `title`, `matchedField`) gives the `RecordRef`; "focus" is a navigator selection (record with no node: opens in the inspector and says so, 5.6a): fits. Derived formulas: gap in three places. `DocumentPorts` has no derived-formula input (the builder reads the entered formula only, builder.ts line 25); `SpeciesCheckResult` carries `canonicalSmiles` but no formula (the toolkit's `StructureFacts.formula` is not returned by `checkSpecies`); and a derived formula arrives asynchronously with no ChangeSet, so the sync's fan-out never fires. Smallest amendment: additive `SpeciesCheckResult.formula?`; the L4 service remembers it per species (`derivedFormulaOf(ref)`, notifying its subscribers); `DocumentPorts.derivedFormulaOf` and an additive `SearchDocument.derivedFormula` (indexed like `formula`: boost 3, exact); `SearchSync.refresh(ids)` called when facts arrive. Landing: contract decisions, then 5.1b (L4 + sync) and 5.8 (UI). |

Missing: nothing forbids `src/ui`, `src/render` or `src/features` from importing `src/persistence`, `src/workers` or `src/io` directly (E1: services reach React through the provider only). Nothing restricts `Worker`, `SharedWorker`, `indexedDB`, `IDBKeyRange`, `BroadcastChannel` or `navigator.locks` to `src/app` (the globals rules cover `src/core` and `src/persistence` only). Import and export UI folder: SONNET_HANDOFF §2 lists none. Recommended: `src/features/data-exchange` (import, export, undo import), added to the §2 list and ARCHITECTURE §2.3 with a dated line; the first-run screen is app shell (`src/app`) and may use it (app -> features); the palette's `> export` and `> load demo` go through a command registry that `src/app` fills, because `feature-isolation` forbids `search` importing `data-exchange`. Landing: 5.1a (rules); folder at the contract decisions. |
| Q11 CSP against the planned libraries | gap (verified by text scan) | Method: a scratch `npm install --ignore-scripts` in a temp folder OUTSIDE the repo (repo package.json and lockfile untouched), then a text scan of the published code for `<style>` elements, `nonce`, `eval(`, `new Function`, `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, `innerHTML`. Versions seen: react-dialog 1.1.23, react-dropdown-menu 2.1.24, react-context-menu 2.3.7, react-popover 1.1.23, react-tooltip 1.2.16, react-toast 1.2.23, react-scroll-area 1.2.18, react-select 2.3.7, react-visually-hidden 1.2.11, cmdk 1.1.1, @tanstack/react-virtual 3.14.13, d3-zoom 3.0.0, zustand 5.0.15, @fontsource/ibm-plex-sans and -mono 5.3.0 (the latest at scan time, not pins). Result: no `eval(` or `new Function` in any of them. BLOCKERS for `style-src 'self'` (E3): (1) `react-style-singleton` (`document.createElement('style')`, nonce only if `get-nonce` finds one) used by `react-remove-scroll`, which Radix Dialog (modal, the default), Popover, Select and, through react-menu, DropdownMenu and ContextMenu (modal by default) render: a modal layer injects a `<style>` element that the page CSP blocks; (2) Radix ScrollArea and Select render a `<style>` with `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`. Clean: Tooltip, Toast, VisuallyHidden (inline `style` objects, set through the CSSOM, which `style-src` allows), react-focus-scope, react-dismissable-layer, react-portal, cmdk itself (but `Command.Dialog` is a Radix modal Dialog), TanStack Virtual, d3-zoom (d3-selection ships `selection.html`, unused), zustand, Fontsource (CSS with relative `woff2` urls; import the latin subset files only). Smallest amendment for 5.2: no Select, no ScrollArea (native `<select>` and `overflow`); Dialog, Popover, DropdownMenu and ContextMenu with `modal={false}`, and the focus trap built from `@radix-ui/react-focus-scope` (`trapped`) with `react-dismissable-layer`, plus our own scroll lock as a class set through the DOM; cmdk's `Command` inside our own Dialog, not `Command.Dialog`. Not verified: behaviour in a browser (a text scan only); the exact pins; React setting `style` as a CSSOM write. 5.2's e2e (zero `securitypolicyviolation`) is the proof. Landing: 5.2. |

**Plan observations (reviewer to decide; the plan text above is unchanged).**

1. Seeding comes after its first use. ADV-012's e2e part (5.5a) needs a 1,000-reaction dataset in the browser, but the import UI is 5.9a and the first run offers only the demo. Either the e2e seeds IndexedDB from test code (Q12, recommended, no production code) or a minimal "Import file" moves ahead of 5.5a. I would take the seed helper.
2. The navigator has no hub expansion in core (Q3). The plan lists "hub expand and collapse" inside 5.5a as UI work, but the projection ignores `expansion`, degree caps and overflow. It needs its own core step with a bench before 5.5a, or 5.5a becomes two steps and the user's first look at the app slips.
3. Step 5.1b is two steps. Chem + L4 + layout + search sync + warm cache + the start-up limit is one session; the three whole-state flows (import, restore, export) with their ports and the one-operation rule are another. Split at the line between the engines and the flows. 5.1a is also near the limit once periodic snapshots, markers and the lint rules (Q6, Q10) are added; consider moving the lint rules to a tiny step 5.0.
4. Missing from the plan: periodic snapshots, the clean-shutdown and integrity markers, persistent-storage request and storage estimate (Q6), and notices for `crashSuspected`, quarantined rows and asset problems from the boot report (nothing silent, CLAUDE.md §1.5).
5. E7 says "no second code path" for Load demo, but a down compute worker leaves import with no path (`createComputeImport` answers `REJECTED`). Restore and export offer an in-process fallback; import should too, as a value, or the first run can strand a user whose worker cannot start. The cost is one more flow test in 5.1b.
6. The CSP result changes 5.2 (Q11): Radix Dialog, Popover, Menu, Select and ScrollArea inject `<style>` in their default form. Put an E3 spike at the start of 5.2 (render each planned primitive in the production build with the CSP and assert zero violations) before building the design system on them; the page is cheap to throw away, the primitives are not.
7. Cross-engine risk is left to the end. Firefox and WebKit appear first in 5.10a: module workers, WASM, Web Locks, BroadcastChannel and IndexedDB blobs differ there. Run a two-spec smoke (boots, demo loads, worker parses) in all three engines from 5.1c, and keep the full suite in 5.10a.
8. The e2e CSP proof is weaker than ADR-035 reads (Q13 b): a `<meta>` CSP does not reach workers. Add the header project, or change SECURITY §4 to say the worker half is a build-smoke check only.
9. Persisting depictions in `derivedCache` (PERFORMANCE §8: 100 MB soft cap) is in no step; 5.4a says LRU in memory only. Either say Phase 9 owns the persistent cache (my preference: the memory LRU is enough for Gate E) or add it to 5.4a with a quota test.
10. Undo and redo in the top bar (5.8, E9) can never be enabled in Phase 5: the only commits are import and Undo import, and `replaceState` clears the history (ADV-045). Show them disabled with the reason ("nothing to undo; editing arrives in Phase 6", E8) and take the store-binding test to Phase 6, or keep a small test with a synthetic commit.
11. The search remedy named in 5.1b ("a remedy proposal" for the 193 ms document build) cannot be "build documents in the compute worker" without an amendment: the `worker-entry-compute` rule forbids `src/search`. Say that in the proposal; moving the pure builder under core, or lifting that rule for the builder only, are the two options.
12. 5.5a asks the user to try the app with only the demo and no import, no search and no inspector. That is fine for a navigator check, but the inspector (5.6a) is what makes clicks useful. Consider reordering 5.6a before the user's look, or keep the checkpoint and tell the user what is not there yet.
13. The 80% coverage line for `src/render/scene` and `src/app/services` joins in 5.3, but `src/app/services` is created in 5.1a. Add it in 5.1a; add `src/features` and `src/ui` as informal figures only (component code, jsdom).
14. A bundle has no step. GRAPH_SEMANTICS §11 draws parallel edges as one path with a count badge and says expanding the bundle fans them out; the overflow node "opens a virtualised list". 5.3 builds `bundleSize` and the overflow glyph, but nothing in 5.5 to 5.7 selects a bundle, lists its reactions (inspector) or fans it out. Add both to 5.5b (selection and inspector list) and say whether the fan-out itself waits for Phase 7.
15. "No chemistry in components" (non-negotiable 4) is only a convention today. `check:size` and `check:hardcode` already cover `.tsx`, but nothing stops `src/ui`, `src/render` or `src/features` from importing the chem toolkit or the validation rules. Fold a rule into the Q10 dependency-cruiser change: those folders may import `src/chem` and `src/search` types only.

**REVIEW REQUIRED (for the Phase 5 contract decisions).**
1. Q1: option B (an app-level derived-indexes service) instead of the store taking the full engine; its main-thread cost after a hub edit is to be benched in 5.1a.
2. Q3: navigator hub expansion is missing in core and needs a step before 5.5a; the memo rule (rebuild by record kinds, structural sharing, content-keyed downstream memos).
3. Q5: remove `selected` / `focused` from `SceneNode.state`; additive `Scene.truncation` / `notes` / layout engine; `depictionKey` meaning.
4. Q7: the `warmUp` method and `ENGINE_LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS` (proposed 20,000 ms) for the E4 start-up limit.
5. Q9: derived formulas (`SpeciesCheckResult.formula`, `SearchDocument.derivedFormula`, `SearchSync.refresh` / `adopt`).
6. Q10: the dependency-cruiser and ESLint additions, and `src/features/data-exchange` for import and export.
7. Q11: Radix modal layers, Select and ScrollArea inject a `<style>` element under the CSP; the proposed replacements (non-modal parts plus our own focus trap and scroll lock).
8. Environment: the first `npm run check` of this session ran on battery with Edge open and failed 10 real-thread tests (cold RDKit load over 2 s: chem-worker-threads 7, worker-crash-recovery 1, search-worker-threads 1, compute-import-threads 1; 4 files); after the user plugged in it passed as one command (232 files, 3,728 tests, Vitest 111.8 s). Edge was still open and idle (9 processes). No timeout, test or rule was changed.

### Phase 5 contract decisions (reviewer, 2026-10-02)

Docs only: no source, test, dependency, contract, configuration or benchmark change. The plan review (35443d5, 22b8cc1) is ACCEPTED. Each amendment lands, through CLAUDE.md §7, in the step named; nothing is applied now. Step numbers are the NEW ones (old 5.1b = new 5.1b engines + new 5.1c flows; old 5.1c = new 5.1d). The review entry above keeps the old numbers as history.

- Q1 Option B: app-level `DerivedIndexes` service in `src/app/services` (lazy build, ChangeSets queued and applied on read, one build on replace / remote / gap / a queue over its bound, `current()` never kept past the call). No core change. 5.1a benches the first build, an ordinary-edit apply and a hub-edit apply at the design tier. Phase 9 candidate: derived tables over the store's own core handle.
- Q2 Fits. A second subscription for the finding store; `historyLabels()` on demand; 5.1d measures `FindingStore.counts()` after a commit at the design tier.
- Q3 (1) New core step 5.4c "Navigator scale and expansion" before 5.5a; semantics fixed from the step 5.0 fact sheet. (2) Additive `ProjectionDefinition.readsKinds`, proved by a property test; pure `sameProjection` in core/graph; the hook keeps the previous Projection object when equal; memos key on object identity; no content hash. Lands: 5.4c (readsKinds), 5.5a (hook).
- Q4 Self-loop edges stay out of the LayoutRequest; glyph beside the node; the Scene counts them; label overlaps accepted until Phase 8. Lands 5.3.
- Q5 (a) `selected` and `focused` leave `SceneNode.state`; the focus mechanism is decided in 5.7 with evidence. (b)(c)(d) as proposed. (e) `depictionKey` through an injected port `depictionKeyOf(ref)`; `src/render` never imports `src/chem` at run time. (f) Only `Scene.truncation` is added. Lands 5.3.
- Q6 Clean-shutdown marker (writer only) in 5.1a; `requestPersistentStorage` in 5.1c; one plain boot-report line each in 5.1d; a refused `setUiState` is a status. Not in Phase 5: periodic snapshots (Phase 6, step 6.1) and `storageEstimate` (Phase 9).
- Q7 Additive idempotent `warmUp` with `ENGINE_LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS` = 20,000, sent before the first real call; `onRestart` re-warms only if warmed; `firstCallTimeoutMs` rejected; depiction queue (4 in flight) approved. Lands in step 5.0 (item b).
- Q8 Clock, RNG and schedulers in `src/app` (5.1a); port wiring, `SearchSync.adopt` and the in-process import as a value (5.1b, 5.1c); `LayoutServiceOptions.persist` (5.5b); the stale adapter comment (5.1a).
- Q9 As proposed (`SpeciesCheckResult.formula?`, `derivedFormulaOf`, `DocumentPorts`, `SearchDocument.derivedFormula`, `SearchSync.refresh(ids)`): contract, L4 and sync in 5.1b; UI proof in 5.8.
- Q10 In step 5.0: import and export UI in `src/features/data-exchange`; first-run screen is app shell; palette commands come from a registry that `src/app` fills.
- Q11 No Select, no ScrollArea, cmdk's `Command` inside our own Dialog. 5.2 starts with a throw-away CSP spike deciding between native `<dialog>` + `showModal()` (reviewer's preference) and Radix Dialog `modal={false}` + `react-focus-scope`; dated ADR-013 line. Menus, Popover, Tooltip: Radix, non-modal.

### Phase 5, step 5.0 — Boundaries and engine warm-up (2026-10-02)

Session start: AC power checked (Win32_Battery BatteryStatus 2, on AC). `npm run check` as ONE command, before (Part A commit, no source change): 232 files, 3,728 tests, Vitest 187.8 s. After: 233 files, 3,741 tests (+1 file, +13 tests: 11 in `src/workers/rpc/warm-up.test.ts`, 2 from the Phase 4 completeness test, which now also needs a title naming `warmUp` for chem and layout), Vitest 187.2 s. `build:smoke`: passed. No schema change, no dependency change.

**(a) Boundaries (configuration only).** dependency-cruiser: `presentation-not-persistence-workers` (ui, render, features: `src/persistence` and `src/workers` as types only), `presentation-engines-types-only` (chem, layout, search, io as types only, except the allow-list `src/search/palette/parse.ts` and each of `src/(chem|layout|search|io)/contracts.ts`), `presentation-no-validation-rules` (`src/core/validation/rules`, not even as a type). Tests are excepted. ESLint (src and tests, one config): `no-implied-eval`, `no-new-func`, `no-script-url` as errors; `Worker`, `SharedWorker`, `indexedDB`, `IDBKeyRange`, `BroadcastChannel` (globals and `window.` / `self.` / `globalThis.` members) and `navigator.locks` allowed only in src/app, src/workers, src/test-support and tests; src/persistence keeps its own stricter rule (indexedDB and IDBKeyRange are injected there). Each rule was proved with a throw-away violation (not committed): value imports of persistence, workers, chem and a rules file from src/ui, src/render and src/features failed; the type imports, the palette parser and a contracts.ts did not; every confined global, `navigator.locks`, `window.Worker`, `globalThis.indexedDB`, `setTimeout('x')`, `new Function` and a `javascript:` literal failed in src/ui and src/core, and passed (except the eval rules) in src/app. Existing violations: only four `javascript:` string literals in tests (src/core/schema/primitives.test.ts twice, schema.smoke.test.ts, tests/property/svg-sanitizer.test.ts), which are hostile test inputs on purpose; `no-script-url` is switched off for test files in one config block (no behaviour change). No source violation. `src/features/data-exchange/` added (empty, .gitkeep); SONNET_HANDOFF §2 and §18, ARCHITECTURE §2.2 (dated line) and ADR-017 (dated line) updated.

**(b) Engine warm-up (contract amendment, Q7; see Amendments).** `warmUp(params: {})` on `ChemWorkerApi` and `LayoutWorkerApi`; `ENGINE_LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS` = 20,000, `WarmUpParams`, `WarmUpResult` in `src/workers/rpc/contracts.ts`; spec `warmUp: { idempotent: true, timeoutMs: ENGINE_LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS }` in both spec files. Handlers: chem awaits `toolkit.ready()` and reports `engine_unavailable` when the toolkit is still "not loaded"; layout calls the new optional `LayoutEngine.warmUp()` (the ELK adapter loads ELK); both time themselves with an injected clock (`createChemHandlers(toolkit, clock)`, `startChemHost(port, load, clock)`; the browser entry passes `performance.now`). Main side: `createWarmUpGate` (`src/workers/rpc/warm-up.ts`), one per client, whose `onRestart` is the client's hook; `createChemService(client, gate)` and `LayoutServiceOptions.gate`. Warm-up is sent before the first real call and the calls wait behind it; `ok: false` is kept (chem: `unavailable` with the message; layout: the fallback layout, `engine_unavailable`; no restart); a warm-up TIMEOUT is an ordinary RPC timeout (restart, counts towards maxRestarts, `gave_up` a status; the gate goes back to cold and the next call tries again); `onRestart` re-warms only if the engine had been warmed. Layout's `layoutFallback` is not behind the gate (it needs no ELK). Decision for the reviewer: an engine that cannot load stays unavailable for the life of the gate (a page reload retries); retrying per call would reload the WASM on every call.

Tests (`src/workers/rpc/warm-up.test.ts`, in-memory port, injected loader, fake timers; mutating the gate made 8 and 2 of the 11 fail): a load of 5 s (chem) and 6 s (ELK) with the 2 s call limit: no timeout, no restart, the next call answers, one warmUp; calls made while loading wait behind ONE warmUp; a failing loader: values, no restart, no second warm-up, the loader runs once; a loader past the limit: TIMEOUT at 20 s, restart, the new worker is not re-warmed (never warmed), the third try ends in `gave_up` (restart_cap), then `unavailable`; `onRestart` re-warms (new worker receives warmUp, then parse); handlers: idempotent (loader once), `loadMs` from the injected clock (250), every param and result passes `structuredClone`; layout: ELK that cannot load answers the fallback twice with one warmUp, ELK past the limit answers the new worker's fallback (`timeout`), `onRestart` re-warms. `tests/adversarial/phase4-dod.test.ts` is unchanged and now sees warmUp named in titles.

Existing test lines changed (the approved product change, note E4; no assertion about the 2 s CALCULATION limit was loosened): (1) `src/workers/chem/handlers.test.ts`: the specs test expects the new `warmUp` entry; `rig()`, `hangingService()` and the crash tests build a gate and give the client `onRestart: gate.onRestart`; the fake hosts (`never` api, the two crash hosts) answer `warmUp` with `{ ok: true, loadMs: 0 }` so only the CALL hangs; the "runs into its limit" test now counts requests other than warmUp on the new worker (the re-warm is the only one that reaches it). (2) `src/workers/layout/layout.test.ts`: the specs test expects `warmUp`; `rig()` builds a gate; the "no worker can answer" test now runs on fake timers (20 s warm-up limit, then the 30 ms fallback call) and still expects UNAVAILABLE. (3) `tests/types/rpc-contracts.test.ts`: the spec and `keyof ChemWorkerApi` type tests list `warmUp`. (4) Real-thread files (`chem-worker-threads`, `layout-worker-threads`, `worker-crash-recovery`) and the two chem benches create a gate and wire `onRestart`; the real-thread tests that time a calculation limit (`a hung depiction is stopped the same way`, the 500 ms busy layout, and the L4 batch with a hanging structure) call `warm()` first. (5) `tests/fixtures/chem/worker.ts` passes a clock.

Cold warmUp in a real thread (Node worker_threads under tsx, informal, run once alone, three runs): chem loadMs 88-91 ms (spawn + load about 490-550 ms); layout loadMs 197-202 ms (spawn + load about 325-330 ms); a second warmUp is 0.3-7 ms. These are Node numbers (no WASM fetch); 5.1d measures the browser.

**(c) Navigator fact sheet** (read-only, throw-away script, nothing committed; default limits: 400 nodes, 800 edges; build time = median of 30 builds on an indexed state, ms):
- demo: 27 reactions, 15 hubs, 2 unanchored reactions, 35 candidate edges (34 on real hubs), 25 distinct ordered real-hub pairs, projection 16 nodes / 35 edges / 6 bundles (15 edges bundled, largest 4), truncation null, 0.16 ms.
- initial tier, dense, 60 anchors asked (the generator has only 50 classes, so 50 hubs): 1,000 reactions, 1,502 candidates, 1,026 distinct ordered hub pairs (largest pair 6), projection 50 nodes / 800 edges / 144 bundles (317 edges bundled, largest 5), truncation max_edges, 2.55 ms. Without anchors: 0 hubs, all 1,000 reactions Unanchored on the Unclassified hub, 1,502 candidates, 800 edges, ONE bundle of 800 (a self-loop pair), max_edges, 2.29 ms.
- initial tier, hub_explosion, 50 hubs: 1,000 candidates, 1 hub pair (all 1,000 between the same two hubs), projection 800 edges in ONE bundle of 800, max_edges, 1.87 ms.
- design tier (5,000 reactions), dense, 60 anchored hubs: 7,492 candidates, 1,702 distinct ordered hub pairs (largest pair 752), projection 60 nodes / 800 edges / 115 bundles (475 edges bundled, largest 63), max_edges, 4.71 ms (max 7.7).
- design tier, hub_explosion, 60 anchored hubs: 5,000 candidates, 1 hub pair of 5,000, projection 800 edges in ONE bundle of 800, max_edges, 2.48 ms.
Answers in words. (1) Bundles when edges are cut: `bundleEdges` runs on the ADMITTED edges only, after the cut at maxEdges; edges are admitted in (reaction, outcome, product) ID order, so which pairs survive depends on IDs, not on importance; a pair with one admitted edge is no bundle at all. (2) A bundle does NOT know its full count: `EdgeBundle` is `{ id, from, to, edgeIds }` with the admitted edge IDs only (design/dense: largest bundle 63 against a real pair size of 752). (3) `hidden` on a node is `{ outgoing, incoming }`, the number of DROPPED candidate edges seen from that admitted node (an edge dropped by maxEdges is counted at both admitted ends, one dropped edge adds 1 to each; an edge to an unadmitted hub counts only at the admitted end); the sum over nodes is 2 x dropped (design/dense 13,384 = 2 x (7,492 - 800)); it says nothing per hub pair and is not capped to a pair. (4) The roll-up index answers, without materialising edges (no `toEdge`, no hashing): per hub, `candidatesFrom(hub)` and `candidatesTo(hub)` (sorted lists whose lengths are the unfiltered outgoing and incoming counts) and the unanchored reaction list; per hub pair there is NO index, a count is a scan of one hub's list filtered on the other hub (the cost of that list, up to 5,000 in hub_explosion); filters (topic, scope, text) are applied per reaction in `build`, so the roll-up's counts are unfiltered upper bounds unless the caller filters the candidates itself.

Open for the reviewer: (a) the unavailable-for-the-gate's-life decision above; (b) `ENGINE_LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS` stays 20 s until 5.1d has a browser number; (c) the services' clients MUST be created with `onRestart: gate.onRestart` (step 5.1b wires it; nothing in src/app creates them yet). No REVIEW REQUIRED item blocks 5.1a.

The changed test lines are accepted: no assertion about the 2 s calculation limit was loosened.

It is retried ONLY on a user action, never by a timer and never per call: the gate gains `retry()` (back to cold), the services expose it, AppStatus shows "unavailable" with a Retry. Finding: at 1,000 reactions the hub overview is already cut at 800 edges, a bundle does not know its real size (63 shown for a pair of 752), which edges survive depends on IDs, and all arrows of a hub onto itself become one self-loop bundle. Direction for step 5.4c (its prompt fixes the details; added to the 5.4c plan row): the overview is built per ordered hub PAIR. One bundle per pair with its FULL count after the request's filters (a per-pair table in the roll-up index, kept incrementally, or a counted scan if the bench allows); at most a small number of edges materialised per bundle, ranked by exam scope, then selectivity, then name; the limits count pairs (drawn paths) and nodes, not raw edges; when pairs exceed the limit the largest pairs are kept and the truncation says how many pairs and arrows are not shown; `hidden` is exact; a hub-to-itself pair is a loop with a count. The inspector reads the full list of a pair from the index. Build stays under 16 ms p99 at the design tier.
8. The check now takes about 187 s: accepted.

### Phase 5, step 5.1a — App services, part 1 (headless) (2026-10-02)

Session start: AC power checked (BatteryStatus 2). Part A (the step 5.0 review) is commit 5ca4479. `npm run check` as ONE command, before (after Part A, no source change): 233 files, 3,741 tests, Vitest 136.2 s. After: 245 files, 3,824 tests (+12 files, +83 tests), Vitest 145.3 s. `npm run test:coverage` green with the new threshold (whole run 97.74% lines, 90.41% branches). No schema change, no contract change (`contracts.ts` files and `src/core/schema` untouched), no dependency change; no Amendments entry.

**What was built (`src/app/services`, plain TypeScript: no React, no DOM, no global; a test greps the source for forbidden globals and runs in Node).** `createAppServices(env): Promise<AppServices>` takes everything from `AppEnv`: `indexedDB` and `IDBKeyRange` (either absent: session-only), `locks` (absent: no Web Locks, `singleTab`), `channel` (a BroadcastChannel factory; absent: nobody is told), `workers` (`compute` required, the other three optional and unused until 5.1b), `clock`, `random`, `timers` (a persistence `Scheduler`), `migrationRunner`, `generator`. It never throws for an environment problem (IndexedDB missing or refusing, a worker that cannot start, a channel that cannot be made): the answer is a value and a status. The production providers (`systemClock` from Date, `createCryptoRandom(crypto)`, `systemTimers`) are in `src/app/providers.ts`; tests inject fakes (`src/test-support/app-env-kit.ts`: fake-indexeddb, an in-memory lock manager and BroadcastChannel shared by tabs, the compute host over the in-memory port pair).

Files: `contracts.ts` (AppEnv, AppServices, results), `boot.ts` (`decideMode`, `emptyState`, `noticesOf`, `openStorage`), `status.ts` (AppStatus store and the event log), `derived-indexes.ts`, `replica.ts` (boot replica, sync, retry), `writer.ts`, `follower.ts`, `create.ts`; each under 200 lines.

**Public surface of AppServices:** `store` (the one canonical store), `status` (get, subscribe), `log` (get, dropped, subscribe), `indexes` (DerivedIndexes: current, stats, dispose), `bootReport`, `persistence` (the adapter, undefined when session-only), `replicaSync()`, `pendingWrites()` (synchronous), `saveEverythingNow()`, `retryReplica()`, `shutdown()`, `dispose()`.

**Modes (OpenResult -> mode).** read_write -> `writer` (write queue attached, `singleTab` kept); nothing stored -> an empty writer (an empty dataset with a deterministic id and times from the injected factory); read_only with other_tab_is_writer -> `follower`; read_only with newer_schema, integrity_failure or migration_failed -> `read_only` with the reason and detail, an empty store, never promoted and no follower (nobody holds or waits for the lock); unavailable (or no IndexedDB in the env, or one that refuses to open) -> `session_only` (a writable store in memory, no adapter, `save: null`, the log says nothing is saved).

**AppStatus fields (ONE immutable value; the identity changes only when a field's value changes).** stage (booting, ready, disposed), mode, reason, detail, singleTab, save (state, lastSavedRevision, pendingRevisions, lastError, failureReason, deletablePeriodicSnapshotIds; null when nothing is saved), replica (absent, in_sync, reloading, down; reason; revision), validation (where: worker, main with its reason, none), promotion (not_applicable, waiting, promoted, refused), reloadFailure, rejectedRows, followerReloads, notices (crashSuspected, quarantinedRows, assetsWithoutBytes, unverifiedAssetBlobs, migrationSteps, for the 5.1d lines). The event log is a ring of the newest 200 entries (`EVENT_LOG_LIMIT`), `dropped()` says how many it let go; follower events that carry a whole state are summarised, never stored.

**Boot.** `BootPorts.validate` is `createBootReplica().validate`; the store is created with `bootReport.precomputed` (no second validation); `bootReport.validation` goes into the status. The replica is loaded ONCE: the sync adopts the boot client (`createReplicaSync({ adopt })`; tests count one `loadReplica` and one worker). A worker that cannot start, gives up (crashes on the load), refuses, or answers findings the store refuses: boot validates in this thread, `validation.where` is `main` with the reason, the log has the `validation_fallback`, the replica status is `down`.

**DerivedIndexes (decision Q1, option B).** Lazy first build; `store.subscribe` queues each ChangeSet WITH the state right after it (apply needs the state of its own revision, not the newest); a read applies the queue in order; ONE build instead (never applies) on a `replace` or `remote` cause, on a revision gap, on a queue past 100 ChangeSets or 2,000 touched records (the sync's numbers), and, as a safety net that must never be what makes an answer right, when a listener reads before the service's own listener ran (`mismatch`). Rejected commits are never announced: no build, no apply (counter test). `current()` returns the handle of the store's revision; callers never keep a handle: an older one throws "Stale index handle" (existing behaviour, stated in the module header and tested). `stats()`: builds, applies, last build reason, last build and apply durations (injected clock), queued.

**Writer, follower, promotion.** `attachWriteQueue` runs after a writer boot and after the `promoted` event, never for a follower (INV-28: a test tries and gets `{ ok: false, reason: 'read_only' }`). SaveStatus is mirrored into AppStatus. `saveEverythingNow()` calls `replaceAll` of the store's state when `failureReason` is `revision_gap` (the test provokes a real gap) and answers `NOT_NEEDED`, `NOT_WRITER`, `NO_STORAGE` or `FAILED` as values otherwise. The follower controller gets the same validation port as boot; its events become fields and log entries: row_rejected -> rejectedRows, reload_failed -> reloadFailure, validation_fallback -> validation, reloaded and loaded -> followerReloads, promoted -> writer with a write queue, promotion_failed -> mode `read_only`, promotion `refused`, reason and detail. A refused promotion does not ask for the lock again (E11; a test counts the lock manager's requests). `shutdown()` (writer only, nothing pending, save state saved) writes the marker with the injected clock and answers `{ written, at }` or `{ written: false, reason }`. `retryReplica()` stops the old sync (terminating its client), creates a new sync from the store's current state and answers when it settles; later validations (a follower's reload, a promotion) go through the new sync's client. `dispose()` is idempotent: it stops the write wiring, the indexes, the sync, the follower, releases the lock, closes the channel and the adapter, clears every subscription, and logs a note if writes were pending (it does not flush).

**Tests.** `src/app/services`: status.test.ts (9: identity, freezing, listener errors, the bounded ring), boot.test.ts (the decideMode table, emptyState, notices), derived-indexes.test.ts (11: lazy, queue and order, stale handle, rejected commit, replace, remote, gap, both bounds and the default numbers, the mismatch net, clock); `src/app/providers.test.ts` (4). `tests/integration`: app-services.test.ts (shape and Node, fresh and demo boots, the three fallbacks and a client that gives up, 10), -modes (read-only reasons x3 with nothing written, session-only x2), -writer (commit reaches IndexedDB and the replica, a rejected commit moves nothing, saveEverythingNow after a real gap, the marker: shutdown, no shutdown, pending refused, clock; 7), -follower (read-only boot, INV-28 refusal, following a commit, nothing stored yet, row_rejected and reload_failed as status, promotion, refused promotion; 7), -lifecycle (retryReplica x3, dispose x2, an env with nothing; 6), -status (the AppStatus transition table, 9) and app-services-threads.test.ts (real-threads project: the demo boots with the findings of a real compute thread, one thread, one commit reaches IndexedDB and the thread's replica, whose findings equal the store's). Property `tests/property/derived-indexes.test.ts` (P-07 style, 2): after random commands with undo, redo, rejected commits, reads at random points and a replaceState, and on a follower after applyRemote spans and reloads, every answer of `current()` equals `indexEngine.build` of the store's state (the existing index snapshot helper, over every id seen), and `lastBuildReason` is never `mismatch`; green at the default 25 runs. Hand-mutated: skipping every third apply (caught only after the `mismatch` assertion was added: the safety net had hidden it), applying with the newest state instead of the state after the ChangeSet, and ignoring a remote cause were each caught; the model produces rejected commits, queue applies and replaces (asserted, not vacuous).

**Coverage.** `src/app/services` joins the 80% lines threshold (`vitest.config.ts` and `test:coverage`): 90.25% lines (87.1% statements, 83.1% branches) in a focused run; the whole coverage run is green.

**Benches** (`tests/perf/app-services.bench.ts`, entries in `tests/perf/budgets-app.mjs`, run alone, informal, no baseline, design tier hub_explosion, main-thread code in Node, mean / p99 ms, the `main` project of the last run; `bench:baseline` not run): DerivedIndexes first build 200.7 / 298.9 (183.8 / 208.0 in the first run); apply after an ordinary edit (1 record) 0.092 / 0.177; apply after a rename of the hub record (1 record, 1,000 referrers) 0.099 / 0.199; apply after replaceReferences on the hub (about 1,020 records) 206.2 / 222.7; store built from boot's precomputed findings 180.2 / 193.3; the whole `createAppServices` over fake-indexeddb with the compute host in memory on this thread 1,303.7 / 1,377.9 (an upper bound: it holds the worker's own work and fake-indexeddb's read). The hub edit lands about 200 ms on main at the first read after it, off the commit path.

**Also.** The stale adapter comment now names `createBootReplica().validate`. `src/app/main.tsx` is unchanged (5.1d replaces the side-effect import).

**Decisions.** (1) `createAppServices` always returns a value; a store/precomputed disagreement is the one programmer error that throws, after releasing the lock and the database. (2) The services always attach a store, an EMPTY one for a tab with nothing stored, so the follower's `loaded` event (a full read with no store attached) cannot occur; the mapping stays for safety. (3) A refused promotion shows mode `read_only` (not `follower`) with promotion `refused`. (4) `dispose()` does not flush; 5.1d's `beforeunload` warns on `pendingWrites()` and `pagehide` calls `shutdown()`, which refuses to write the marker while writes are pending. (5) Missing IndexedDB is `unavailable` with reason `unknown` and a detail, because `OpenResult` has no "missing" reason.

**REVIEW REQUIRED.**
1. Q1 cost, to judge: the first build is about 180-200 ms and the apply after a hub-wide edit (replaceReferences, about 1,020 records) about 200 ms, both on the main thread at the first read after the commit (the plan review expected 300-450 ms); an ordinary edit costs 0.09 ms. Both are off the commit path and below the 16 ms rows only for the ordinary case.
2. A follower of a database that holds NOTHING, promoted before the writer saved anything, is refused by the follower controller (`state_missing`: "the database holds no data although this tab had some"), because the store exists (empty) from the start. It shows as a refused promotion with Reload. Smallest fix if wanted: let the controller accept an empty state when the store holds none (a persistence change, its amendment).
3. The save state flickers `read_only` -> `saved` -> `read_only` while a refused promotion boots as the writer (the adapter's own state; the final value is right). Harmless; noted because the status table test lists it.
4. `shutdown()` writes the marker once; a commit made after it does not clear it (only boot writes `null`). 5.1d calls it from `pagehide` only, so this cannot matter there.
5. The whole-boot bench is an upper bound; the real main-thread cost of a boot with a real thread is measured by the browser work in 5.1d (the store build, about 180 ms, is the main-thread part this step can name).

### Step 5.1a review follow-ups (2026-10-02)

(a) A follower whose store holds ZERO records, promoted while the database still holds nothing, becomes an empty writer (write queue attached) instead of being refused with `state_missing` (`src/persistence/follower.ts`, `promote`: the check counts the store's records with `recordCountsOf`; no contract type changed; INV-28 guards unchanged because the write queue is attached only after the `promoted` event). A store that holds records while the database holds nothing is still refused. One sentence in PERSISTENCE §10. Tests: a unit test (empty store, empty database: `promoted`, no reload, the lock kept; the existing `state_missing` test stays for the non-empty case) and two integration tests (two empty tabs, the writer closes, the follower is promoted and a rename reaches IndexedDB, a third tab reads it; a store with records and a wiped database is refused with `read_only` and `state_missing`).
(b) `AppStatus.save` does not change while a promotion attempt is running (the adapter mirror is skipped while `promotion` is `waiting`); the `promoted` and `promotion_failed` handlers take the adapter's value in the same update as the outcome (`WriterPart.currentSave()`). The transition table test changed: a refused promotion no longer lists `save` among the changed fields, and a new row proves a promotion shows `read_only` until the notification that says `promoted` (hand mutation: removing the skip fails both). 

`npm run check` as ONE command, green: before (recorded at the end of step 5.1a) 245 files, 3,824 tests, Vitest 145.3 s; after 245 files, 3,828 tests (+4), Vitest 161.6 s. A baseline run started in this session was stopped because the tree was edited while it ran.

### Phase 5, step 5.1b — App services, part 2: engines (headless) (2026-10-02)

Session start: AC power checked (BatteryStatus 2). Part A (the step 5.1a review and its follow-ups) is commit 4b15bbb. `npm run check` as ONE command, before (after Part A): 245 files, 3,828 tests, Vitest 161.6 s. After: 253 files, 3,895 tests (+8 files, +67 tests), Vitest 142.8 s. The amendment (d) below is the only contract change; no schema change, no dependency change.

**What was built (`src/app/services`, plain TypeScript, no React, no DOM, no global).**
- `lazy-client.ts`: a client of the `RpcClient` shape that creates its worker thread at its FIRST CALL (PERFORMANCE §7). `worker-health.ts`: the RPC client's events become `AppStatus.workers.*` and log lines; `restarting` until the restart hook has finished, then `ok`; `gave_up` is `down`. `engine-client.ts`: lazy client + warm-up gate (`onRestart: gate.onRestart`) + health + the engine's state in AppStatus + `retry()`. `chem-layout.ts`: the chem and layout services over it (a missing factory gives a service whose every call answers "unavailable" as a value; the layout wrapper reports a fallback layout and the layout notes in the log and as `layoutNotice`, once per computed result, never for a cached one). `l4-part.ts`: the L4 service over its ports, fed by `store.subscribe` (`replace` -> `checkAll`, anything else `onChange`). `search-part.ts`: client, service, sync, the warm-boot cache. `depiction.ts`: the depiction service. `timing.ts`: the quiet periods. Every file is under 250 lines.
- **When each worker thread is created.** Compute: at boot (5.1a, unchanged). Chem: at the first L4 run (the L4 debounce, 400 ms after boot, fired by `checkAll`), or the first `chem` / `depictions` call; its engine is loaded by `warmUp` before the first real call. Layout: at the first `layout.layout(...)` request, never otherwise. Search: when its boot decision first calls the worker (the cache's `loadSerialized`, or the first load after the 200 ms debounce); a session-only tab loads after the debounce. A test proves the counts at each moment (`app-services-engines`).
- **Warm-up gate.** `retry()` (unavailable -> cold; false in any other state), `message()`, `subscribe(listener)`. `services.retryEngine('chem' | 'layout')` calls `retry()` and warms once now, answers a value, logs it, and after a successful chem retry queues every structure for L4 again. Nothing retries by itself: a test waits ten minutes of fake time, and the app tests count RDKit loads. A worker that gave up (`down`) is not helped by a retry (the answer says: reload the page).
- **L4.** `checkAll` after boot, after a `replace` change (import, restore) and after a follower's `reloaded` / `loaded` / `promoted` event (`FollowerPartOptions.onReload`). A follower runs L4 (its findings are derived data) and writes nothing canonical (a test compares the IndexedDB rows and revision before and after). Timeout, unavailability, a crash and `gave_up` give "check unavailable" (`AppStatus.l4`: `unavailable` with a message when the engine or worker cannot answer; otherwise the count of records not checked with the same words), never CHEM-001.
- **Search.** Client, service and sync with the ports: the state, the FULL indexes from `services.indexes.current()`, the revision, the 200 ms debounce, the L4 service's derived formulas. **Warm boot:** after the index has been up to date for **5,000 ms** (a commit or a pass restarts the quiet period; at most 10 re-arms while busy) the WRITER tab serialises it and stores ONE row (`derivedCache`, kind and key `search_index`, value `{ revision, json }`, `byteSize` = the JSON length). At boot a row of the store's revision is loaded with `loadSerialized` and adopted (`SearchSync.adopt`: no document is built, `DerivedIndexes` is not built, no `load` call reaches the worker); another revision, a malformed row, `BAD_INDEX`, a worker failure or a store that moved on while the cache loaded is ONE full load (each logged with its reason). A refused cache write (quota) is `search.cache.state = 'refused'` with the message, never an error, and the save status is untouched.
- **Depiction service.** `depictions.depict(structure, options)` answers `{ ok: true, key, svg, width, height, cached }` or a failure value (`unavailable`, `timeout`, `invalid`, `refused`, `cancelled`, `queue_full`, `bad_options`); at most **4** calls in flight (the chem client allows 16, so BUSY cannot occur; a test with 40 requests sees none), the rest in a FIFO queue (bound 2,000); identical requests share one call; `cancel(key)` drops a queued request and ignores a running one's answer (nothing cached); memory LRU of **256** drawings (a sanitised drawing is a few KB: a few MB at most); failures are never cached. `depictionKeyOf(structure, options)` = `hashParts` of (format, value, width, height, showAtomMaps, sorted distinct highlights) with core's stable hash. No object URL (step 5.4a).

**New `AppServices` members.** `chem` (ChemService), `l4` (statusOf, subscribe, subscribeFormulas, derivedFormulaOf, summary, flush, stats), `layout` (LayoutService with the notices), `search` (search, superseded), `searchSync()`, `depictions` (depict, cancel, stats, dispose), `retryEngine(name)`.
**New `AppStatus` fields.** `workers` ({ chem, layout, search }: state `absent | ok | restarting | down`, `started`, `reason`), `engines` ({ chem, layout }: `cold | loading | ready | unavailable`, `message`), `l4` (state `idle | checking | unavailable`, `notChecked`, `queued`, `message`), `search` (state `absent | idle | pending | down`, `indexRevision`, `loadedFrom` `none | full_load | warm_cache`, `reason`, `cache` { state `none | saved | refused`, `revision`, `message` }), `layoutNotice`. The transition table test has six new rows (boot, the search load, the first L4 run, a restart, a toolkit that cannot load and its retry, a layout fallback).

**Amendment (d), additive (CLAUDE.md §7; Phase 5 contract decisions Q9).** (1) `SpeciesCheckResult.formula?` (the toolkit's derived formula); the L4 service keeps `derivedFormulaOf(ref)` in memory, drops it as soon as the structure changes or the toolkit does not answer, and tells `subscribeFormulas` listeners the changed ids once per run; plus `L4Service.summary()` and a `SearchSyncPorts.onStatus` hook for the status. (2) `DocumentPorts.derivedFormulaOf`, `SearchDocument.derivedFormula?` (species only), indexed with `formula` as one field (boost 3, exact, a `formula` hit with the text that matched); the search configuration id is 2, so an old serialised index is `BAD_INDEX`; `SearchSync.refresh(ids)` and `SearchSync.adopt(revision)` (true only while the store is still at that revision; afterwards every rebuilt document and every removal is sent). (3) The incremental-equals-rebuild property (P-07 for search) has a new case where derived formulas are learned late, change without any record changing (a "toolkit version" bump) and are announced with `refresh`; hand-mutating `refresh` into a no-op fails it. Docs: SEARCH §3/§6, ADR-034 dated line, PERFORMANCE §7.

**Tests.** Unit: warm-up gate (+3: retry, subscribe, nothing by itself), L4 service (+5: formulas, summary), search document/engine derived formula (6), sync adopt/refresh (5), depiction service (9). Integration (in-memory hosts): app-services-engines (13), -l4 (8, includes THE end-to-end test: one edit through store -> IndexedDB -> replica -> search index -> L4 findings), -search (8), -depictions (2), -status (+6); real threads: app-services-engines-threads (1: real compute, RDKit, ELK and search threads; derived formula C2H6O for the demo's ethanol; ELK layout; search; the cache written by one session adopted by the next with 0 loads). Property: search-incremental +1. Hand mutations, all caught: `gate.onRestart` not wired, depiction cap 5, `refresh` a no-op. New test support: `app-engines-kit.ts` (real hosts over in-memory ports with a fake RDKit and a fake ELK; records every thread and call), `ManualScheduler.fireAfter / liveDelays`.

**Coverage.** `src/app/services` (focused run of the app-services tests): 92.44% lines, 89.3% statements, 84.0% branches (threshold 80% lines).

**Benches** (`tests/perf/app-engines.bench.ts`, entries in `budgets-app.mjs`, run once alone, informal, design tier dense, 7,109 documents, main-thread code in Node, mean / p99 ms, the `main` project): search full load on main, documents built plus the FIRST DerivedIndexes build, worker stubbed: **331.9 / 353.1**; the same plus the `structuredClone` of the load message: 337.2 / 357.6; boot until the index answers, full load (main work plus the engine's `load`): 379.5 / 440.5; warm boot (the engine's `loadSerialized` of a cloned cache plus `adopt`): **45.4 / 65.6** (8.4 times faster). The serialised index is **2,980,930 bytes** (about 2.98 MB, 419 bytes a document). `bench:baseline` not run.

**Remedy proposal for the document build (not built; plan review observation 11).** The 330 ms is two main-thread costs: the first `DerivedIndexes` build (about 200 ms, shared with the navigator) and the pure document builder (about 130 ms). Build the documents in the compute worker, which already holds the replica and the full index engine: an additive `ComputeWorkerApi.buildSearchDocuments({ revision, ids | 'all', derivedFormulas })`, so the search sync becomes a relay (it asks for documents, passes them to the search worker) and `DerivedIndexes` is no longer needed for search. A returning user already skips all of it through the cache (46 ms); this serves the first run, an import and a follower. Decide at this review; the cost is one compute-worker method and the move of the sync's `build` behind a port.

**Decisions.** (1) Threads are created at first need (lazy clients), including search; chem's first need is the first L4 run. (2) The cache is written by the writer tab only, and a follower reads it. (3) A missing factory is `absent`, never an error; `retryEngine` answers why it cannot help. (4) `AppStatus.l4.queued` is approximate (queued plus in flight); only idle versus checking is decided from it. (5) The L4 service's `announce` tells status listeners when work is queued and when a run ends (before, only a status change did).

**REVIEW REQUIRED.**
1. A follower's search sync does a full load for EVERY remote change (the 4.5 rule: `remote` is not analysed), about 340 ms of main-thread work per writer commit received, and its cache is not used after boot. Remedy candidate: analyse the remote span from the ChangeSet the store builds for `applyRemote`. Measure in 5.1d with two tabs.
2. After `adopt` the sync cannot know the signatures of what the cache holds, so each rebuilt document is sent once more, and `refresh(ids)` after a warm boot resends every species L4 reads (bounded by the number of species, one pass). Remedy candidate: store the per-document signatures in the cache row.
3. `searchSync().flush()` before the boot decision forces a full load (the sync starts as "needs load"); the services never do it, a test caution only.
4. `retryEngine` cannot revive a worker that gave up; the answer says to reload the page (E11 style). A "restart the worker" action would need the gate to go cold with a new client: not built.
5. L4's first species-change after boot calls `DerivedIndexes.current()` (to find referrers), which is where the first 200 ms build lands if search has not already paid it.

### Phase 5, step 5.1c — App services, part 3: flows (headless) (2026-10-02)

Session start: AC power checked (BatteryStatus 2). Part A (the step 5.1b review) is commit 70756a9. `npm run check` as ONE command, before (after Part A): 253 files, 3,895 tests, Vitest 209.8 s. After: 259 files, 3950 tests (+6 files, +55 tests), Vitest 148.5 s. Additive amendments only (below); no schema change, no dependency change. `npm run test:coverage` run once: green, whole run 97.73% lines, 96.09% statements, 90.37% branches, 97.44% functions; per directory (lines / branches): src/core 99.04 / 91.98, src/persistence 98.63 / 89.91, src/io 97.08 / 90.51, src/workers 97.21 / 88.67, src/chem 98.70 / 91.44, src/layout 100.00 / 96.38, src/search 97.71 / 91.23, src/app/services 93.83 / 83.43 (threshold 80% lines; the weakest new file is import-service.ts, 93.79% lines, 75.25% branches). The per-directory figures come from a second coverage run with the json-summary reporter, because the threshold run prints only the whole-run summary.

**What was built (`src/app/services`, plain TypeScript, no React, no DOM, no global).** `AppServices.flows` (`Flows`):
- Import: `preview(source, fileName, request?, options?)`, `revise(batchId, request, options?)`, `cancel(batchId)`, `apply(batchId, plan, options?)`, `undo(options?)` (the `pre_import` snapshot of the newest batch through the restore flow; `UNKNOWN_BATCH` as a value when there is none). Also `restore(snapshotId, options?)` and `export(options, format, flow?)` answering bytes, counts, missing assets and missing references. Every call answers a value; `FlowOptions` = `{ signal?, inProcess? }`.
- Files: `flow-types.ts` (the answers), `flow-gate.ts` (one operation at a time, mirrored in `AppStatus.operation`), `flow-route.ts` (worker or in-process; READ_ONLY and CANCELLED values), `flow-ports.ts` (the asset stage and its `blobsFor`, the guarded store, `durabilityOf`, the store router), `memory-store.ts` (session-only snapshots and batches), `import-service.ts`, `restore-service.ts`, `export-service.ts`, `flows.ts` (assembly, the persistent-storage request), `flow-deps.ts`.
- **Ports.** The Phase 4 flows (`createComputeImport`, `createComputeRestore`, `createComputeExport`) are built over the real ports: the adapter's `storeSnapshotBytes` / `readSnapshotBytes` / `deleteSnapshot`, `saveImportBatch` / `addQuarantine` / `listImportBatches`, the asset stage connected to `attachWriteQueue`'s `blobsFor` (the writer part passes it, also after a promotion), `getAssetBlob` as the `AssetBytesProvider`, `env.clock` and `env.random` (a NEW seed for every call; the in-process session gets a fresh `idFactoryFrom({ nowIso, seed })` per call). A flow is rebuilt when the replica sync is a new one (`retryReplica`).
- **Done means saved.** `apply` and `restore` answer after `flush()`: `{ ok: true, revision, saved, sessionOnly, save, inProcess, notes }`. `ok` means the state changed; `saved` is true only when the queue is idle at a revision at least the applied one; otherwise `save` is the `SaveView`. A session-only tab answers `saved: false, sessionOnly: true` and a note says nothing is saved.
- **Modes.** Writer: everything. Follower and read-only tab: preview, revise, apply, restore and undo answer `READ_ONLY` as values (the store is the judge); export works. Session-only: import, restore and undo work in memory; the `pre_import` / `pre_restore` snapshot bytes, the import batch rows and their quarantine rows are kept in memory behind the same ports (at most `MEMORY_SNAPSHOT_LIMIT` = 3, the oldest dropped, logged as `memory_snapshots_dropped` and listed in the answer's `notes`); the bytes of asset files are not kept and the answer says how many.
- **In-process paths (note E7, decision Q8).** A worker that is down (no sync, or `down`) answers `WORKER_DOWN` with `inProcessAvailable: true`; nothing runs. The caller asks again with `{ inProcess: true }`: the SAME Phase 3 code on this thread (`parseImportSource` + `createImportSession`, `restoreSnapshot`, `buildOrnJsonExport` / `buildOrnPackageExport`). A batch lives on the path that made it. Proved equal: the same state and stored records after an import, a restore and an Undo import, and the same bytes for both export formats.
- **One operation at a time.** ONE slot for all four kinds (import preview or revise, apply, restore, export): a second is `BUSY` with `busyWith`, logged (`flow_busy`), the status untouched. `AppStatus.operation` = `{ kind, stage, progress }` (stages of an apply: preparing, snapshot, committing, saving). `cancel(batchId)` is not gated.
- **Cancel.** Every flow takes an `AbortSignal`. A signal already aborted answers `CANCELLED` at once. A preview or export passes it to the worker call. An apply or restore cannot be interrupted inside the worker, so the store the flow commits through is guarded: if the signal is aborted at the commit point the flow's own refusal path runs (the snapshot it made is deleted, the pending state dropped, the adoption cleared) and the answer is `CANCELLED`. A cancel that arrives after the commit is too late: the answer is the success.
- **Batch report.** The stored report (and the answer's) carries `sourceSha256` (SHA-256 of the file's bytes, hashed on main in parallel with the preview) and `clockNowIso` (the instant of the preview or revise that made the batch). A file that cannot be hashed is logged and the field is absent.
- **Persistent storage.** After the first successful import in a writer tab `env.storage.persist()` is asked once; `AppStatus.storage` = `{ persistence: 'unknown' | 'persisted' | 'refused', message }`. A refusal is a status and a note in the answer; no API or a throwing request leaves `unknown` with the reason.

**New `AppServices` member.** `flows`. **New `AppStatus` fields.** `operation` (`OperationView`: kind `import_preview | import_apply | restore | export`, stage, progress `{ done, total } | null`) and `storage` (`StorageView`). **New `AppEnv` field.** `storage?` (`{ persist() }`).

**Tests.** Integration (in-memory hosts on fake-indexeddb): app-services-flows (14: the demo into an empty store resolves after saved, applied-not-saved carries the SaveStatus, a reload sees the demo, a package with assets reaches the write queue in ONE hand-off, Undo import brings back the old state and the old asset bytes, the report's SHA-256 and clock, persistent storage once / refused / unavailable / not used by a refused import, search and L4 follow an import, P-02), app-services-flows-guards (18: BUSY for five other calls with the log, operation stages and progress, cancel while held, aborted signals, cancel(batchId), a replaced batch, STALE_REVISION, SNAPSHOT_FAILED built and stored, a worker that cannot prepare, a bad file, follower, read-only tab, session-only with Undo import, the bound of 3 with the drops reported, UNKNOWN_BATCH), app-services-flows-restore-export (12: restore by id with undo restore, NOT_FOUND and a damaged snapshot, cancel during the pre_restore snapshot, WORKER_DOWN then the in-process import / restore / Undo import / export equal to the worker paths, export with assets, missing assets and missing references, progress, cancel, session-only), app-services-status (+4: the table extended with `operation` and `storage`, refusals change nothing). Real thread (the one *-threads file): app-services-flows-threads (1: import, export and Undo import over a real compute thread). Unit: flow-gate (2), memory-store (4). INV-21 is checked at each failure point by comparing a footprint (state, revision, history, every stored record and meta row, snapshots, batches, quarantine count, blob hashes) before and after. Hand mutations, all caught: the flush skipped, the cancel check at the commit removed, the BUSY check removed, the report metadata dropped.

**Benches (`tests/perf/app-import.bench.ts`, informal, run once alone, design tier dense, main-thread parts in Node, mean / p99 ms, `main` project, `bench:baseline` not run).** receive the merged state and the worker findings (structuredClone): 295.4 / 331.2; store.replaceState(merged, 'import', the worker findings) into an empty store: 574.4 / 633.9; the first DerivedIndexes build over the imported state: 603.7 / 622.4; the search full load on main (documents built, first DerivedIndexes build included, worker stubbed): 986.7 / 1,107.7. The real-threads project run of the same file gave 315.0 / 350.6, 572.7 / 616.6, 593.6 / 617.6 and 899.9 / 1,070.3. The search figure is about three times the step 5.1b figure for the same load (331.9 / 353.1) and the first-build figure about three times the step 5.1a one (about 200 ms): the machine or the state (here the state came through replaceState) is slower than in those runs; not explained, not re-run.

**Amendments (additive; see "Amendments").** `ImportBatchReport.sourceSha256?` and `clockNowIso?`; `AppEnv.storage?`; `AppServices.flows`; `AppStatus.operation` and `storage`. `src/io/contracts.ts` was at exactly 400 lines: one existing comment (`documentUnmapped`) was shortened to one line, its words kept.

**Decisions.** (1) One slot for every kind of operation, not only apply and restore: a preview, a revise and an export are also refused while another runs (a superset of the brief; the UI aborts a running preview before starting another). (2) Cancel is honoured at the commit point, not inside a worker call (above). (3) An apply that was made on the worker path while the worker is now down answers `WORKER_DOWN` (its batch is gone); the caller previews again with `inProcess`. (4) The in-process IDs come from a per-call factory (`idFactoryFrom`), so both paths mint IDs the same way. (5) `ok: true` with `saved: false` is "applied, not saved"; the 5.1d status line must show it, never "done".

**REVIEW REQUIRED.**
1. The default import mode into an EMPTY store is `merge`, which keeps the empty store's own dataset meta; the file's meta is only reproduced by `replace_all` (P-02 through the services uses it, as Phase 3's does). Step 5.1d's "Load demo dataset" and first-run "Import a file" must choose the mode on purpose (note E7).
2. A cancel of an apply or restore wastes the worker's prepare work (about 1.4-2.3 s at the design tier) and the snapshot write that is then deleted; it is correct but not cheap. Interrupting inside the worker would need a cancel path in `createComputeImport` / `createComputeRestore` (Phase 4 flows); not built.
3. The source file is read into memory on main to hash it (`Blob.arrayBuffer`, then `crypto.subtle`), in parallel with the preview; for a 20 MB package that is one more copy on main. The worker could hash it instead (an additive field of the preview answer); decide at Gate E from traces.
4. Session-only: the bytes of imported asset files are not kept (nothing stores them); the records are, so AST-002 will report them. The answer says how many.
5. The main-thread parts of an import apply (benches above) add up to well over a second at the design tier; judged at Gate E with the other watch items.
6. The bench figures above for the first DerivedIndexes build and the search full load are about three times those of steps 5.1a and 5.1b for the same work. Unexplained (machine state, or reading the state a store produced). Not re-run (once, alone, as asked); re-measure at Gate E before any decision rests on them.

REVIEW REQUIRED 1 (import mode): an import into a store that holds ZERO records uses `replace_all`, on purpose, so the file's own dataset meta is kept ("Load demo dataset" and the first-run "Import a file").
