### Phase 5, step 5.1d — Browser skeleton and smoke (2026-10-02)

**Part 1 (the window and one browser) is the commit "phase-5.1d: part 1".** Part 2 follows in this entry.

Session start: AC power checked (BatteryStatus 2). Part A (the step 5.1c review) is commit 847b262. `npm run check` as ONE command: before this step (after Part A) 259 files, 3,950 tests, Vitest 135.8 s; after Part 1 266 files, 4,033 tests (+7 files, +83 tests), Vitest 146.9 s. `npm run build:smoke` green (it now also proves the demo dataset). No schema change, no migration.

**What was built.**
- `src/app/main.tsx`: the side-effect module `@/workers/zod-jitless` first (z.config jitless), `createAppServices(createBrowserEnv())`, the React root with ONE provider (`window/context.tsx`), `installLifecycle`. It replaces the side-effect import of the worker factories (the factories are now used by `env.ts`, so the build still bundles the four entries).
- `src/app/env.ts`: the production `AppEnv`; the only reader of browser globals; whatever the browser lacks is left out (a status in the services).
- `src/app/lifecycle.ts`: `pagehide` -> `shutdown()`; `pageshow` with `persisted = true` -> `services.resume()`; `beforeunload` warns while `pendingWrites()`.
- `AppServices.resume()` (additive, `ResumeResult`): writes the clean-shutdown marker `null` again. The writer part remembers that THIS session wrote a marker; `resume()` with none is `not_shut_down`. A ChangeSet accepted after `shutdown()` calls it by itself (a store subscription started before the write queue is attached, so the marker write is queued first). Marker operations run one after another. Tests: tests/integration/app-services-writer.test.ts (+3: the pageshow path, the commit path, a later boot suspects a crash), follower and session-only answers.
- `src/ui/hooks` (`useSubscribable`, `useStoreSelector`, `useCounts`): generic and structural over `useSyncExternalStore`. `src/ui/primitives/error-boundary.tsx` ("Reload panel"). **Layering note:** `src/ui` may not import `src/app` (dependency-cruiser rule `ui-render-below-features`), so the hooks take the status, the store and the finding store by their shapes, and the components that know `AppStatus` live in `src/app/window` (the composition layer). The finding counts use a SECOND subscription (`store.findings.subscribe`), proved by a test that changes only the finding store.
- `src/app/window`: `banners.ts` (pure functions of `AppStatus`: every banner, the boot-report lines, the save text, the operation text), `status-parts.tsx` (status line, banners, boot lines), `use-import-flow.ts` + `import-panel.tsx` (the import flow and what it shows), `window.tsx` (first run, record counts, error boundaries). Plain, unstyled, semantic HTML, text nodes only.
- First run (zero records): "Load demo dataset" (the bundled file through the normal import pipeline, `replace_all`, no confirm step unless the plan has blockers), "Import a file" (summary of counts per kind, findings, decisions at their defaults as text, blockers; Confirm or Cancel), "Start empty" (React state only: a reload of an empty store shows the first-run screen again). INVALID_META from `replace_all` is said in words with "Merge instead"; `WORKER_DOWN` offers "Run on this page" (the in-process path, never taken silently). A read-only tab shows words, not buttons. The import panel outlives the first-run screen, so the result ("Imported N records", "applied, not saved yet", notes) stays visible after the data arrived.
- The demo file: `import demoUrl from 'data/seed/demo.orn.json?url'`, so Vite emits `assets/demo.orn-<hash>.json` and there is no second copy to drift. `scripts/check-build.mjs` fails unless exactly one such file exists and equals the repo file byte for byte (tests/integration/check-build.test.ts, +1 test); the e2e fetches the served file and compares it too.
- e2e: `playwright.config.ts` (tests/e2e, `*.spec.ts`, a webServer that runs `vite build` and `vite preview`, projects chromium / firefox / webkit; Firefox and WebKit run `smoke.spec.ts` only), `npm run e2e`, not part of `npm run check`. Specs: smoke.spec.ts (2) and app.spec.ts (7), helpers in tests/e2e/kit.ts (a watcher for console errors, page errors, `securitypolicyviolation` events and foreign requests; files made by the generator in Node and chosen through the real file chooser, note E15).

**Tests.** Component (jsdom, colocated): banners.test.ts (26: every banner, the boot lines, the save and operation text), status-parts.test.tsx (24: the status line, every banner and its button, the boot lines, the second subscription), window.test.tsx (17: first run, demo, file import, refusals, offers, cancel, hostile file name, data view), error-boundary.test.tsx (3), use-subscribable.test.tsx (3), lifecycle.test.ts (4), env.test.ts (2). Roles and names are used throughout (note E13).

**Dev dependencies (exact pins, `npm audit` 0).** @testing-library/react 16.3.3, @testing-library/dom 10.4.2 (its peer dependency; not named in the brief), jsdom 30.1.1, @playwright/test 1.63.0 (docs/DEPENDENCIES.md). Browsers: Playwright's own `npx playwright install chromium firefox webkit` FAILED here (the Node downloader timed out on the CDN download of Chrome for Testing, twice, also with a 180 s connection timeout; curl from the same machine downloaded the same files at about 10 MB/s). The five archives (Chromium 153.0.8010.12, its headless shell, ffmpeg, Firefox 155.0, WebKit 26.6, winldd) were fetched with curl from the URLs `playwright install --dry-run` prints and unpacked into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\ms-playwright` with the `INSTALLATION_COMPLETE` marker. This changes how the browsers got there, nothing in the product or the tests.

**Part 2 (this commit): STOPPED at item i, as the brief says.** The two-spec smoke FAILS in Firefox and in WebKit, for two different reasons that are not test mistakes. No product behaviour was changed to hide them. Item j (measurements) and the remaining e2e work were not done after the stop (see "Left undone").

e2e results per browser (`npm run e2e`, production build, 13 specs in all): chromium 9 passed, 0 failed (smoke 2, app 7: reload keeps the data with zero violations, zero console errors and nothing off the origin; the served demo equals the repo file; inline script and eval blocked, with their `securitypolicyviolation` events; second tab read-only and says so; RDKit WASM blocked -> "Check unavailable" with Retry and no CHEM-001; "Import a file" with an initial-tier generator file; Cancel imports nothing). firefox 0 passed, 2 failed. webkit 0 passed, 2 failed. Both smoke specs fail at the same first step in both browsers: after "Load demo dataset" the text "Imported N records from demo.orn.json." never appears (30 s).

**Firefox 155.0 (Playwright 1.63.0), exact behaviour.** The import itself works: the page shows all 20 record kinds (species 66, reaction 27, ...), the status line says "Saved (revision 1)" and the findings "E 0 / W 2 / I 17 / Review 38" (the chem worker ran and added its CHEM-001 warning). But `flows.apply` never answers: the screen stays at "Applying demo.orn.json..." and the status line at "Applying an import: saving", for as long as it was watched (15 s). Cause, confirmed by a throw-away run that replaced `navigator.storage.persist` with a function answering `false` (not committed): the import then completes and the page says the browser did not grant persistent storage. `afterImport` in `src/app/services/flows.ts` (step 5.1c) AWAITS `env.storage.persist()` before the apply answers; Firefox answers `persist()` with a permission prompt, and the promise stays pending until a user answers it (nobody does in a headless run, and a real user who ignores the prompt gets the same hang). Consequence in the product: the ONE operation slot (`AppStatus.operation`, stage `saving`) stays held, so every later import, restore and export would answer `BUSY`. Chromium answers `persist()` without a prompt, which is why 5.1c's tests (fake storage) and Chromium never showed it. Smallest proposed amendment (not made): `afterImport` starts the request without awaiting it for the answer (the status and the note arrive when the browser answers), or races it against a short injected timer and says "waiting for the browser's permission". The choice changes a 5.1c decision (the note of a refused request in the answer), so it is the reviewer's.

**WebKit 26.6 (Playwright 1.63.0, Windows), exact behaviour.** The import is refused: the screen shows "The import was not applied (SNAPSHOT_FAILED): the pre_import snapshot could not be written, so nothing was imported (Error preparing Blob/File data to be stored in object store UnknownError: Error preparing Blob/File data to be stored in object store)". Nothing is imported (INV-21 held: the status stays "Saved (revision 0)", no records). The snapshot bytes go to IndexedDB as a Blob (PERSISTENCE §6); this WebKit build refuses to store a Blob. Not established: whether this is specific to Playwright's WebKit (its default context keeps no data on disk) or also true of Safari. Smallest next steps (not taken): run the same spec with a persistent context to separate the two; if it is real, the snapshot store would keep the bytes as an `ArrayBuffer` or `Uint8Array` instead of a Blob (a persistence amendment, CLAUDE.md §7, which the reviewer decides). Chromium and Firefox store the Blob fine.

Also seen: Firefox logs one console WARNING "The WebAssembly exception handling 'try' instruction is deprecated ... recompile to use 'try_table'" (RDKit's WASM; a warning, not an error, so the zero-console-errors check, which counts errors, is unaffected).

**Left undone (after the stop).** (j) the measurements: warm-up `loadMs` of chem and of layout, cold boot to first status and to ready (demo and a design-tier file, after a reload), long tasks over 50 ms, heap after boot by CDP `Performance.getMetrics`, `FindingStore.counts()` after a commit at the design tier. The e2e of the boot-report lines and a browser-level `pageshow` with `persisted = true` (the marker paths are proved in Node: tests/integration/app-services-writer.test.ts and src/app/lifecycle.test.ts). The e2e specs of Firefox and WebKit stay red until the two items above are decided; `npm run e2e` is not part of `npm run check`. Step 5.1d is therefore NOT marked complete in PROJECT_STATE.json.

`npm run check` as ONE command at the end of Part 2: 266 files, 4,034 tests (Part 1: 4,033; +1 banner test), Vitest 136.4 s, green.

One more small fix in this commit: the "storage refused" banner repeated its message twice ("... persistent storage the browser did not grant ..."); it now shows the message once (banners.ts, two tests).

**REVIEW REQUIRED.**
1. Firefox: `flows.apply` hangs on `navigator.storage.persist()` (above). A product defect of step 5.1c, found by the first real browser. Decide the amendment (do not await the request, or a bounded wait).
2. WebKit: the `pre_import` snapshot cannot store a Blob here (above). Decide whether to investigate in a persistent context first and whether the snapshot store should keep `ArrayBuffer`s.
3. Layering: `src/ui` cannot import `src/app` (the dependency rule), so `src/ui/hooks` are generic and structural, and the status line, banners and first-run screen live in `src/app/window`. If the intent was for those components to live in `src/ui`, the rule or the location of the `AppStatus` type must change; I did not change the rule.
4. `@testing-library/dom` 10.4.2 was added (the peer dependency of `@testing-library/react`), beyond the three packages named.
5. Browsers were installed by curl and unpack, not by `playwright install` (above). A fresh machine may hit the same download timeout.
6. "Start empty" is React state only: a reload of an empty store shows the first-run screen again (no localStorage; `uiState` is step 5.2).
7. Marker ordering: a ChangeSet accepted after `shutdown()` clears the marker asynchronously; the marker write is queued before the write queue's own write (the subscription is created first), but IndexedDB gives no stronger guarantee. A crash inside that gap leaves a "clean" marker next to newer data.
8. The e2e `loadDemo` helper waits for the "Imported" line, so it cannot pass while item 1 stands; the specs are left as they are on purpose.

Next: the reviewer decides items 1 and 2; then Firefox and WebKit smoke, the measurements (item j) and the remaining e2e work finish step 5.1d.

Cold boot at the design tier (1.6 s) is inside the 2.5 s row; heap (86 MB) inside 150 MB. Gate E watch items, to be traced when the import UI is built (5.9a): the 1,404 ms main-thread task during a design-tier import preview, the 846 ms one during the apply, and the six boot long tasks (903 ms in total) at the design tier.

Follow-up b removes the false line. New openItem for Phase 6 step 6.1: draft recovery must not depend on an unload-time write (candidate: offer drafts whenever editor sessions exist at boot; then decide whether the marker stays). The `test.fixme` stays, its comment naming that openItem.
5. REVIEW REQUIRED 1 (ELK): fixed now (follow-up a). Lesson recorded: Phase 4 scanned the bundle but never ran the layout worker in a browser.

**Follow-up a (commit "phase-5.1d: ELK loads in the browser worker").** Cause CONFIRMED by reading the pinned elkjs 0.12.0 source (`elk-worker.min.js`, line 6440 of `elk.bundled.js`): `if (typeof document === 'undefined' && typeof self !== 'undefined') { var i = new h(self); self.onmessage = i.saveDispatch } else if (typeof module !== 'undefined' && module.exports) { module.exports = { default: j, Worker: j } }`. In a web worker the first branch runs: ELK installs its own `self.onmessage` and exports nothing, so `new Elk()` found no in-thread `Worker` ("_Worker is not a constructor"). Our RPC host listens with `addEventListener` (`src/workers/rpc/browser-port.ts`), so its handler was NOT replaced; ELK's `onmessage` would only have been a second, foreign listener on our scope. A first attempt, stubbing `document` only around the `import()`, did not help: the bundle is browserify style and runs `elk-worker` on the first `new Elk()`, not at import. Fix in `src/layout/elk/load.ts` only: in a worker scope (no `document`, a `self`) an empty `document` exists for the first import AND the first `new Elk()`, and is removed in a `finally`. elkjs stays pinned and unpatched in node_modules; no eval, no `new Function` (`npm run build:smoke` green), no CSP change, no nested worker. Tests first: `src/layout/elk/load.test.ts` (2; a simulated worker scope with a sentinel `self.onmessage`; both fail without the fix with "_Worker is not a constructor"); e2e in `tests/e2e/smoke.spec.ts` (the smoke grows from 2 to 4 specs per browser): the BUILT layout worker answers `warmUp` ok, a small layout answers `engine: 'elk'` with three positions and a second one does too; and after the demo loads the status line shows "Workers: compute ok, chem ok, layout (not started|ok), search ok" (new `workersTextOf` in `src/app/window/banners.ts`, +2 tests; the layout worker starts at its first need, PERFORMANCE §7). e2e per browser: Chromium 4 passed, Firefox 4 passed, WebKit 4 passed. ADR-007 dated line.

**Follow-up b (commit "phase-5.1d: review follow-ups").** The "a crash is suspected" line is shown only when there is something to offer. `BootNotices` gains `editorSessions` (the count of editor-session IDs the boot offers, additive, `src/app/services/status.ts`); `bootLinesOf` shows the line only when `crashSuspected` and `editorSessions > 0`; with none, `createAppServices` writes a `crash_suspected` entry to the event log and the screen says nothing. Tests: banners.test.ts (the count in the notice), status-parts.test.tsx (+2: the line is absent with no session, the report region is absent when nothing else is reported), boot.test.ts (the count), tests/integration/app-services-writer.test.ts (+2: no session -> one log entry; a session -> counted, no log entry), tests/e2e/lifecycle.spec.ts (the crashed renderer now leaves the marker running AND the next boot says nothing; the `test.fixme` stays, its comment names the Phase 6 step 6.1 openItem). PERSISTENCE §4 has the dated line. No schema change, no contract change beyond the additive notice field.


### Phase 5, step 5.2 — Design system and shell frame (2026-10-07)

**Part 1 (the commit "phase-5.2: part 1"): spike, tokens, primitives.** Session start: AC power checked (BatteryStatus 2), `git status` clean; Part A (the 5.1d review) is commits ba6e4cb and 9806ad0 (above). `npm run check` as ONE command: before this step (after Part A) 267 files, 4,045 tests; after Part 1 274 files, 4,108 tests (+7 files, +63 tests), green.

**CSP spike (throw-away, not committed; `spike/` and `dist-spike/` deleted).** A page built by Vite with the production CSP meta tag (the same policy as `vite.config.ts`), served by `vite preview`, driven by Playwright 1.63.0 in Chromium 153.0.8010.12, Firefox 155.0 and WebKit 26.6, twice (identical results). Keyboard activation (Enter on the focused trigger), because WebKit does not focus a clicked button. Measured per candidate: `securitypolicyviolation` events plus console and page errors, focus in, Tab x10 (where focus goes), Esc, focus return, background inert (`bg.focus()` while open must not take focus).
- native `<dialog>` `showModal()`: violations 0 in all three; focus moves to the first input; Tab never reaches the background (Firefox: 10 of 10 inside; Chromium and WebKit: 7 inside, 3 on the browser's own UI); Esc closes; focus returns (click activation in WebKit: NOT, the browser had no focused button; keyboard activation: yes); background inert yes.
- Radix Dialog 1.2.0 `modal={false}` + FocusScope (trapped, loop) + our own `inert` on the page container: violations 0 in all three; Tab 10 of 10 inside; Esc closes; focus returns; inert yes (OUR attribute, not Radix's).
- Radix DropdownMenu 2.1.25 `modal={false}`: violations 0 in all three; first item focused, ArrowDown, Enter selects; Esc closes; focus back to the trigger.
- Radix Popover 1.2.0 (non-modal by default): violations 0; focus into the content; Esc closes; focus back to the trigger.
- Radix Tooltip 1.3.0: violations 0; shown on keyboard focus; Esc hides.
- Toast in a polite live region (`role="status"`, `aria-live="polite"`): violations 0; the text appears in the region.
- React `style` props (`width`, `color`, a custom property): violations 0; computed values correct (React writes through the CSSOM, which `style-src 'self'` allows).
Decision (rule of note E3: a candidate with any violation is out; both dialogs pass, so native): **the Dialog is the native `<dialog>`**; Menu and Tooltip Radix non-modal; Toast our own; Popover not built (nothing in 5.2 uses it). ADR-013 has the dated line. Side effects of the native modal that the primitive handles: everything outside it, even a body-mounted portal, is inert (menus and tooltips inside a dialog mount inside it); WebKit does not focus a clicked button (the click target is remembered for the focus return).

**Plan change.** A primitive is built in the step that first uses it: 5.2 builds Button, IconButton, Menu, Dialog, Tooltip, Banner, Toast, Skeleton. `VirtualList` and `@tanstack/react-virtual` move to step 5.5b (plan rows 5.2 and 5.5b updated).

### Phase 5, step 5.4c — Navigator scale and expansion (core) (2026-10-07)

Session start: AC power checked (BatteryStatus 2), `git status` clean. Part A is the commit "phase-5.2: review recorded" (the step 5.2 review and measurement a, above). `npm run check` as ONE command: before this step (after Part A) 277 files, 4,173 tests (Vitest 241.8 s); after 283 files, 4,220 tests (+6 files, +47 tests; Vitest 214.3 s), green. Core only: no UI, no worker, no schema change, no `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump, no dependency.

**What was built.** The semantics of the step prompt (GRAPH_SEMANTICS §3, §7, §8, §10, §11 carry dated lines; ADR-036; the amendment below).
- `src/core/graph/navigator/pairs.ts`: pair grouping, admission order `(total desc, from, to)`, and the two-pass edge budget (best edge per admitted pair; then the spare budget to pairs in `(total asc, from, to)` order, each up to `min(total, DEFAULT_DEGREE_CAP)`, in rank order: selectivity exclusive, major, mixture_component, unspecified, minor, trace, then reaction, outcome, product ID). `expansion.ts`: members, direction, the member cap, the overflow node, the node and edge budget. `nodes.ts`: node and edge constructors. `reads.ts`: `READS_KINDS`. `build.ts`: the orchestration (the file header states the rules; `focus` and `collapsedEdgeIds` are ignored for now, Phase 7).
- Contracts (additive): `EdgeBundle.total` (required; `kit.bundleEdges` sets `edgeIds.length`), `Truncation.hiddenPairs` and `hiddenEdges`, `GraphNode.hiddenMembers` (overflow nodes), `ProjectionDefinition.readsKinds`; `sameProjection` in `core/graph/same-projection.ts`.
- Roll-up index (the per-pair table the prompt allows after a missed bench): `core/indexes/pair-table.ts` (`PairTable`: every ordered hub pair with its candidates ranked for drawing, maintained in the same retract-then-add step; `NavigatorRollupIndex.pairs()`, built once per commit and shared). `NavigatorCandidate` gains `substrate` and `product` (the refs; hub expansion reads them without a lookup) and now lives in `pair-table.ts` (re-exported from `navigator-rollup.ts` and `contracts.ts`; a type cycle otherwise). `Table.keys()`. P-07 covers it: `navigatorRollupSnapshot` now includes `pairs`, so apply == build is checked.
- Two build paths, one rule: an UNFILTERED request reads `rollup.pairs()`; a FILTERED one (topic, reagent family, scope or text) scans the candidates of every hub, because the filters are per reaction and the table holds unfiltered counts. Expansion with the table regroups only the pairs whose arrows move.
- Bundle IDs of the navigator are `bnd_` + hash of (from, to): stable per pair, so a layout cache and previous positions survive a change of which edges are drawn (the other views keep the hash over their edge IDs).

**Decisions (REVIEW REQUIRED where marked).**
1. REVIEW REQUIRED. `GraphNode.hiddenMembers` (optional, overflow nodes only) was added: the prompt says the overflow node's `hidden` holds "how many members and arrows"; `hidden` is `{ outgoing, incoming }`, so it carries the ARROWS (outgoing: those of members hidden in the out direction; incoming likewise) and the new field the number of MEMBERS (distinct members hidden in at least one direction; a member shown for out and hidden for in is counted, its in-arrows stay on the hub).
2. REVIEW REQUIRED. The membership and overflow edges (one per member node and one per overflow node) count against `maxEdges`, so `Projection.edges` never exceeds `maxEdges` even with expansion (the harness check INV-22 stays as it was): admitted pairs = the first `maxEdges - structural` pairs. An expansion needs room for its nodes (`maxNodes`) AND its edges (`maxEdges`); the first expansion in hub-ID order that does not fit, and every one after it (a prefix rule, so the result does not depend on how small a later hub is), is not applied; truncation says `max_nodes` (or `max_edges` when only the edge budget was short) and `hiddenNodeEstimate` is the hidden hubs plus the members of the unapplied expansions.
3. REVIEW REQUIRED. An expansion entry that names no admitted hub (a stale ID, a species, an unknown ID) is ignored without a notice: the entry is UI state and a collapse is removing it. A hub that `maxNodes` hid is reported by the existing `max_nodes` truncation. Say if a stale entry should be reported.
4. A member is shown per direction: for `both`, a member shown in the out list but ranked out of the in list keeps its in-arrows on the hub pair; the per-direction cap of 12 is exact.
5. The membership edge is `inferred: true` for a real hub (a member reaches it through class membership, the hub resolution) and `false` for Unclassified (resolution says not inferred).
6. Unfiltered and filtered requests use two code paths; the differential test runs both against the reference.

**Tests (+47).** `navigator-pairs.test.ts` (12), `navigator-expansion.test.ts` (16), `same-projection.test.ts` (6), `tests/integration/navigator-scale.test.ts` (6: the step 5.0 fact-sheet scenarios; largest bundle total 752 at design dense; hub_explosion ONE pair of 1,000 / 5,000 with no truncation), `tests/property/navigator-nothing-dropped.test.ts` (1: drawn totals + hidden candidates = filtered candidates, within the limits, for random states, limits, filters and expansions; also green at ORN_PROPERTY_RUNS=200), `tests/property/navigator-reads-kinds.test.ts` (2: the PROOF of `readsKinds`, below), the differential test (20 -> 21: now 10 requests per state including expansion in each direction under roomy and tight budgets and with filters; the tier tests also run on a state with 60 anchored hubs; the random-command test anchors its state; one test shows the expansion requests are not vacuous), `src/test-support/navigator-reference.ts` REWRITTEN as an independent full scan (no roll-up, no code of `pairs.ts`, `expansion.ts`, `nodes.ts`, `build.ts`; it shares only `HubResolver`, `hubTargets`, `primarySubstrate`, `mainProducts`, `reactionFilter`, `flagsOf` and the ID and key helpers, as the old one did). The projection harness gained two checks (`bundle-total`: total >= drawn edges; `bundle-empty`); P-10 and the INV-26 harness stay green for every view.
- `readsKinds` proof: random intents (the model's ten plus edits and cascading deletes of records of kinds OUTSIDE the list), from generated states and from the maximal fixture (all 22 kinds); for every commit whose ChangeSet touches only kinds outside `READS_KINDS`, `sameProjection(before, after)` holds for the plain request and for every hub expanded. Shown not vacuous (more than 5 outside-only commits and more than 5 commits that change the view per run). Mutation check (throw-away): dropping `navigator_anchor` from the list makes it fail; dropping `caveat`, `entity_display`, `topic_mapping`, `species` or `relationship` does NOT, because the generated outside edits are text edits and deletes (an edit of a species name does not change a navigator view, and a class membership edit is not generated for a kind taken out of the list). The list is therefore generous by reading `build.ts`, `select.ts`, `scope.ts` and `flagsOf`; the proof is "no kind outside the list changed the view", not "every listed kind is needed".

**Changed existing tests (an approved semantic change, not a weakened test).**
1. `navigator.test.ts` "INV-22: maxEdges keeps a prefix, counts what it hid on the hubs and reports the truncation": it asserted the old cut (one edge of a pair of two, truncation `max_edges`, hidden 1 and 1). One pair under `maxEdges` 1 is now one drawn path: one edge, a bundle with `total` 2 and one edge ID, truncation null, nothing hidden. Replaced by that test and a new one where pairs beyond `maxEdges` are counted exactly on the hubs and in the truncation (`hiddenPairs`, `hiddenEdges`).
2. `navigator.test.ts` "INV-22: maxNodes hides whole hubs": the expected truncation object gains `hiddenPairs: 1, hiddenEdges: 2` (additive fields); the assertions on nodes and edges are unchanged.
3. `projection-harness.test.ts`: the hand-written bundle in the "unknown edge" self-test gets `total: 1` (the field is required).
4. `tests/property/navigator-differential.test.ts`: `REQUESTS` became `requestsFor(indexes)` (see above); `src/test-support/index-snapshot.ts` adds `pairs`; `src/test-support/navigator-reference.ts` is the new reference (its old self encoded the cut at `maxEdges`).
The eight rows ADV-001, 002, 014, 015, 046, 047, 048 and 053 are checked by name in `navigator.test.ts`; their tests are unchanged and green (ADV-002: two reactions between the same hubs are one bundle of 2, edges never merged).

**Scale (default limits, 400 nodes / 800 edges; the step 5.0 scenarios).** demo (27 reactions): 35 candidates, 26 pairs; 16 nodes, 35 edges, 6 bundles, largest 4, truncation null. Initial tier dense, 60 anchors asked (50 hubs): 1,502 candidates, 1,026 pairs; 50 nodes, 800 edges, 332 bundles, largest 6; truncation `max_edges`, hiddenPairs 226, hiddenEdges 226. Initial dense without anchors: ONE pair Unclassified to Unclassified of 1,502 (a loop), 12 edges, no truncation. Initial hub_explosion: ONE pair of 1,000, 12 edges, no truncation. Design dense, 60 anchored hubs: 7,492 candidates, 1,702 pairs; 60 nodes, 800 edges, 800 bundles, largest 752; `max_edges`, hiddenPairs 902, hiddenEdges 987. Design hub_explosion: ONE pair of 5,000, 12 edges, no truncation.

**Benches (`tests/perf/navigator-overview.bench.ts`, run once alone with `node scripts/run-bench-cli.mjs`, mean / p99 ms, vitest bench project `main`; the file also runs in the `real-threads` project with similar or lower numbers; budgets in `tests/perf/budgets-navigator.mjs`; bench:baseline not run).**
- First, the scan only (before the per-pair table): design dense overview 11.73 / 23.28 (MISSED the 16 ms p99 row; the old build measured 6.80 / 12.12 in baseline.json), one hub expanded 18.76 / 27.46, every hub expanded 33.49 / 45.60; hub_explosion overview 2.28 / 4.47. Profile (plain Node, `--cpu-prof`): no single hot spot; the cost is the pass over 7,492 candidates (reaction lookup, grouping), 800 edge IDs, 800 bundle hashes, the sort of 1,702 pairs, and, with expansion, two reaction lookups per candidate.
- After the per-pair table: design dense overview 7.37 / 12.31; one hub expanded 11.26 / 15.29; every hub expanded 18.91 / 25.01. Design hub_explosion overview 0.10 / 0.21; one hub expanded 5.81 / 9.46; every hub expanded 6.11 / 8.90. Stress tier (400 anchored hubs) overview 11.49 / 15.79 (informal 32). `sameProjection` at the stress overview (400 nodes, 800 edges; after a small rewrite that builds no arrays per object, 2.36 / 3.68 before): equal projections 0.67 / 1.18 (informal 2), differing in the last edge 0.0104 / 0.0123.
- Plain Node (tsx, no bench harness, for the profile only): design dense overview before the table 9.16 / 14.34; every hub expanded after it 9.44 / 12.05. The bench harness runs about 2x slower than plain Node; the recorded verdicts of this repository are the harness numbers.
- The 16 ms p99 target is met for the overview (dense 12.31, hub_explosion 0.21) and for one hub expanded (15.29). NOT met: every hub expanded at design dense (25.01), and a FILTERED overview is not accelerated (it still scans, about 12 / 23 like the pre-table build). Per the prompt the per-pair table was added and measured again; the rest was not optimised further.

**REVIEW REQUIRED (continued).**
7. Every hub expanded at design dense misses its informal 16 ms (25.01 p99 in the harness, 12.05 in plain Node). It is an extreme: the node budget of 400 stops after about 13 hubs, but the members of all 61 hubs are still counted for the truncation (`hiddenNodeEstimate`). Cheap remedies if it matters: tally lazily per hub in ID order and, once the prefix stopped, count only the distinct members of the rest (a Set of IDs, not ranked tallies). Not done.
8. The filtered overview is not accelerated (two paths). If the exam-scope filter becomes the default in 5.5a, a per-scope pair table or a per-reaction pass cache is the next step; decide at 5.5a with a trace.

### Step 5.4c review follow-ups (2026-10-07)

Follow-up a (tests only; `tests/property/navigator-reads-witness.test.ts`, +14 tests): one WITNESS per kind of `READS_KINDS`, a concrete edit of one record of that kind that makes `sameProjection` false (a table test; it also checks the table names exactly the kinds of the list and that each witness edits exactly one record). Over the demo dataset with the real `createScopeOf`:

### Phase 5, step 5.3 — Scene and layout-request builders (pure; `src/render/scene`) (2026-10-07)

Session start: AC power checked (BatteryStatus 2), `git status` clean. Part A is the commit "phase-5.4c: review follow-ups" (8929610; the 5.4c review and follow-up a, above). `npm run check` as ONE command: before this step (after Part A) 284 files, 4,234 tests; after 288 files, 4,346 tests (+4 files, +112 tests; Vitest 213.7 s), green. No schema change, no `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump, no new dependency, no React, no DOM; `src/render/scene` imports `@/core` at run time and `@/layout/contracts` as types plus its constants.

**What was built (`src/render/scene`; every file under 150 lines).**
- `formatted-text.ts`: `richToRuns` (mini-markup AST to runs; the innermost emphasis wins, a link shows its label, blocks are separated by a line break, depth capped at 16 with the text kept), `plainRuns` (a name is ONE run, verbatim), `formulaRuns` (digits after an element symbol, that is an upper-case letter and at most one lower-case letter, or after a closing bracket become subscripts; a trailing charge, a lone sign after a letter, digit or bracket, or a 2 or 3 and a sign, becomes a superscript; nothing else changes). Total; the runs joined equal the input (property test over arbitrary strings).
- `sizes.ts`: `nodeSizes(projection, ports, measurer)`; boxes hub, species, reagent, member, overflow, Unclassified (`BOXES`: font, minimum and maximum width, padding); widths and heights rounded up to the 4 px grid (note E6); a measurer answer that is not a finite positive number counts as zero. `node-content.ts`: glyph, box kind, label, sublabel, depiction key of a node (a record that the port cannot name is shown by its ID, never blank).
- `paths.ts`: `drawnPaths(projection)`: a bundle (id = bundle id) or a single edge no bundle holds; a loop is a path with `from === to`. Shared by the request and the scene.
- `layout-request.ts`: `buildLayoutRequest(projection, sizes, options)`: one layout node per node, one layout edge per path that is not a loop, sorted by id, seed 1, direction of the view (precursor explorer LEFT, the others RIGHT) unless given, `previous` passed through for the nodes still present, caps as values (`TOO_LARGE`; also `MISSING_SIZE`, `DUPLICATE_ID`). Passes `checkRequest` (a test calls it).
- `build-scene.ts` (+ `edge-style.ts`, `aria.ts`, `loops.ts`): `buildScene(projection, layout, { sizes }, ports)`; the styling of GRAPH_SEMANTICS §4 as tables (a dated line there), bundles as one edge with their TOTAL, the loop glyph beside its node (right side, `LOOP_REACH` 28 px, bounds grown), arrow text above and below for a single edge, membership (solid light) and overflow (dashed light) edges, badges from the flags and the hidden neighbours, `dimmed` from `outside_scope`, `minLod` (hub and Unclassified 0, the rest 1; structural edges 1), `ariaLabel` on every node and edge with the counts in words, `labelAt` at the route midpoint, truncation copied. A layout result that misses a node, a size or a route is refused as `LAYOUT_MISMATCH` / `MISSING_SIZE`, never patched.

**Scene contract changes (amendment below; `src/render/scene/contracts.ts`, UI_ARCHITECTURE §6 dated line).**
1. `SceneNode.state`: `selected` and `focused` removed (`dimmed`, `onPath` stay). Decision Q5 a.
2. `Scene.truncation: Truncation | null` added, copied from the projection. Q5 f.
3. `Scene.revision` is defined as the store revision of the projection it was built from (informational).
4. A self-loop is a `SceneEdge` with `from === to` (documented on `SceneEdge.bundleSize`). Q4.
5. `SceneEdge.labelAt: Point` added (the prompt asks for label positions at the route midpoint; there was no field for them).
6. `SceneEdge.bundleSize` documented as the bundle's TOTAL (not the number of edges drawn).
7. New types: `TextStyle`, `TextMeasurer`, `NodeSize`, `NodeSizes`, `ScenePorts` (`describe`, which also answers the record's `charge`; `depictionKeyOf`; `arrowTextOf`, which answers the structured `ArrowText` of `core/graph`, not markup; changed in the 5.3 review follow-ups, 2026-10-07, with the new core function `arrowTextOf` and the types `ArrowText`, `ArrowTextPart`, `ArrowTextContext`). The step prompt's ports were names and formulas of a record and `depictionKeyOf`; `arrowTextOf` is the third, see REVIEW REQUIRED 1.

**Decisions (REVIEW REQUIRED where marked).**
1. REVIEW REQUIRED. The prompt says a single edge carries its arrow text "as core already computes it". Core does not: PROGRESS step 2.4 records that no arrow-text field exists and that Phase 5 reads participants for captions, and no function in `src/core` produces it. The smallest change that does not put chemistry in the render layer: the port `ScenePorts.arrowTextOf(edge) -> { above?, below? }` in the mini-markup, which the builder parses to runs and places (single edges only; a bundle carries its count). The tests use a fake from the participants (every participant but the carried substrate, so water appears). A pure producer is NOT built (roles and `reaction_scheme` placements per DOMAIN_MODEL §6.2, a never-a-node species included, the carried substrate and the products left out): decide where it lives (proposal: `core/graph`, like `flagsOf`) and in which step (before 5.5a); until it exists the navigator draws no arrow text. ADV-027 is proved at the scene level: water is no node and is on the arrow text.
2. REVIEW REQUIRED. `buildScene` and `buildLayoutRequest` return values (`{ ok: true, ... } | { ok: false, code, ... }`) where the prompt names the Scene and the request: errors across module boundaries are values (CLAUDE.md §6), and a stale layout result must not be drawn silently. `buildScene`'s options carry the `NodeSizes` the request was built from, because the layout result holds corners only.
3. A layout request's `previous` is filtered to the nodes still in the request (it is a hint; stale entries would change the cache key and bloat the request). A caller may pass any map; only the ids present are sent.
4. Node boxes are capped at a maximum width per kind (a hostile 5,000-character name must not make a 1,000,000 px box, which the request check refuses). The Scene label keeps the full text; the renderer (5.4a) must clip it and keep the full text reachable.
5. Formula typography is not chemistry: `CnH2n+2` gets only the subscript after H; a digit before a trailing sign is a charge only for 2 and 3 (so `NH4+` is a subscript 4 and a charge +; `Ti4+` is not recognised); `C2H3-` is read as a 3- charge. The table test lists the odd cases.
6. A bundle is dotted only when every DRAWN edge is inferred (a pair of 752 arrows with 12 drawn is judged by the 12); the stroke of a not-all-inferred bundle is the stroke of its best-ranked edge, as the prompt says.
7. A loop is drawn on the right side of its node; two loops on one node would overlap. The navigator has one pair per ordered node pair, so it cannot happen there; the other views have no loops today.
8. No `layerHint` or `orderHint` is sent: the layout worker's own ordering and the previous positions do the stabilising; whether hubs should get layer 0 is for 5.5a to decide with a real look.

Test support: `src/test-support/scene-kit.ts` (fake measurer, ports over a state, tiny projections, the pipeline with `layoutFallback`).

### Step 5.3 review follow-ups (2026-10-07)

**Follow-up a: the arrow-text producer (`src/core/graph/arrow-text.ts`, pure; +18 tests in `arrow-text.test.ts`).** `arrowTextOf(reaction, context)` answers `{ above: Part[], below: Part[] }`, a Part being `{ text, kind }` with kind `name | formula | condition | separator | more`; never markup. The context carries the schemes of the reaction, the condition variants of the arrow, the carried participant, and two injected functions (`describe`, `shownAsNode`), so core stays pure. Placement: the `reaction_scheme` placement for the participant (the scheme for the arrow's variant, else the general one, ties by ID), else the role default of DOMAIN_MODEL §6.2; `hidden` and spectators are left out; the carried substrate is left out; products are not participants; a participant listed in a condition variant applies under that variant only (§6.3). A reactant that is not drawn as a node (water, `entity_display`) is on the arrow (ADV-027). Order: stage order, then the order of the record. At most 3 parts a side (`MAX_ARROW_PARTS`), the rest is ONE `more` part `+N more`, never silently cut; a stage number never stands alone. `ScenePorts.arrowTextOf` returns this structure; `buildScene` maps `formula` parts through the formula typography and the others to plain runs (`partsToRuns`: parts joined by ", ", a stage number by a space).
Demo examples (carried substrate left out): PCC oxidation of ethanol: above `PCC`, below `CH2Cl2` (a formula part). Ethene hydration carrying ethene: above `H3PO4 catalyst` (the wording of the record), below `H2O` although water is never a node. Methylmagnesium bromide and propanone, with its scheme (`showStageNumbers`): above `1.` `C4H10O` (the ether, placed above by the scheme), below `2.` `Acid (H+)`; the same reaction without a scheme: work-up above, solvent below, no numbers.
OPEN CHOICES, decided provisionally so the producer could be built and tested, for the reviewer (the docs do not decide them; nothing draws arrow text before step 5.5a, so nothing is exposed): (1) a reactant that is not drawn as a node goes BELOW the arrow (`UNDRAWN_REACTANT_SIDE`); (2) a species is written by its formula, anything else by its name, and the participant's `originalText` ("alc. KOH") wins over both; (3) the stage number is a `separator` part `1.`, `2.`, only when the scheme says `showStageNumbers` and the reaction has more than one stage; (4) CONDITIONS (temperature, time, medium) are NOT produced: their placement and format are not in the docs, the `condition` kind exists in the type and is unused. Until (1) to (4) are decided, 5.5a should wire the port as it is or pass none.

**Follow-up b: formula typography and charge (`formulaRuns(text, charge?)`; the port `describe` answers the record's `charge`).** With a charge: a tail that matches it (`-`, `2-`, `-2`, `3+`, either minus sign; the longest first) is the superscript, every other digit follows the subscript rule; a tail that does not match is no charge. Without a charge: a lone trailing sign after a letter or a closing bracket is a superscript; digits then a sign (`NH4+`, `C2H3-`, `SO42-`, `Ca2+`, `[Fe(CN)6]3-`) are ambiguous and stay plain, not guessed. Table tests: SO4 with -2, NH4 with +1, C2H3 with -1 (subscripts, a 1- charge) and with -3, Ti with +4, the same strings with no charge, a charge that matches nothing, charge 0, and the earlier odd cases; the property "the runs joined equal the input" now also runs with a charge. The earlier table rows for `NH4+`, `Ca2+`, `Fe3+`, `SO42-`, `SO4 2-`, `H2-` and the complex ions changed (they were guesses); the changes are in the same commit.
`npm run check` as ONE command: before 288 files, 4,346 tests; after 289 files, 4,385 tests (+1 file, +39 tests: 18 in `arrow-text.test.ts`, 21 in `formatted-text.test.ts`; Vitest 152.4 s), green. ADV-027 now runs on the real producer (`tests/property/scene-builders.test.ts`, via `portsOf`). Commit: "phase-5.3: review follow-ups".

### Phase 5, step 5.4a — SVG renderer, static (`src/render/svg`) (2026-10-07)

Session start: AC power checked (BatteryStatus 2), `git status` clean. Part A is the commit "phase-5.3: review follow-ups" (d4083cd; the 5.3 review and its two follow-ups, above). `npm run check` as ONE command: before this step (after Part A) 289 files, 4,385 tests; after 293 files, 4,454 tests (+4 files, +69 tests; Vitest 159.2 s), green. `npm run build:smoke` once, green; the initial JS chunk is 312.99 kB gzip (312.7 at the 5.2 review: no screen imports the renderer, so the change is the core exports of the follow-ups). No schema change, no `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump, no new dependency, no e2e (no screen uses the renderer; 5.5a does).

**What was built (`src/render/svg`; every file under 360 lines).**
- `scene-svg.tsx`: `SceneSvg({ scene, lod, selectedIds, focusedId, depictions?, ariaLabel, worldRef?, onLayerCommit? })`: ONE root `<svg role="group">` with a name, ONE `<g data-world>` (nothing here transforms it; 5.4b does) and five `memo` layers in drawing order: `edges-layer`, `nodes-layer`, `labels-layer`, `badges-layer`, `overlay-layer`. Shared `<defs>` (`defs.tsx`): two arrowhead markers and ONE clip rectangle per distinct node size (boxes are on a 4 px grid, so the demo has 4 clip paths for 16 nodes), ids prefixed from `useId`.
- Every node group carries `data-node-id`, every edge group `data-edge-id`, each with `role="graphics-symbol"`, the Scene's `ariaLabel` as its accessible name and a `<title>`; the labels and badges layers are `aria-hidden` (their content is in those names).
- Glyphs (UI_ARCHITECTURE §3, `nodes-layer.tsx`): class hub = surface block with a 4 px left rule, species = square-ish outline, member = light outline, reagent = chip (round-ended), overflow = dashed outline (`6 4`) saying "+N more", Unclassified = dashed outline (`2 3`); other glyphs (reaction, concept, ...) use the species outline, `data-glyph` says which. Edges: the dash pattern and the width are ATTRIBUTES from `geometry.ts` (`STROKE_DASH` solid / `8 5` / `1.5 4.5`; `WEIGHT_WIDTH` heavy 3, normal 1.5, light 1), the colour is a class; arrowheads are SVG markers (filled, open, none); a bundle's count badge (the TOTAL) and the loop (the builder's points) are drawn. Badges (`badges-layer.tsx`): caveat circle, mechanism diamond, review square, warning triangle, error square with a cross, outside scope circle with a dash, hidden neighbours pill with the count, each with a `<title>`; shape and mark carry the meaning, not colour.
- Text (`text.tsx`): `<text>` with one `<tspan>` per run, sub and superscripts by class and a numeric `dy` (cumulative shifts that return to the baseline; works in every browser); text nodes only. A label longer than its box is clipped to the box (a `clipPath` of the box size) and its full text stays in the node's `<title>` and `aria-label`.
- LOD is an input: an element below its `minLod` is not rendered; labels from LOD 1, badges, bundle counts and arrow text from LOD 2, structures at LOD 3.
- Selection and focus are props drawn by `overlay-layer.tsx` from the Scene's boxes and routes only: selected = solid outline (a thick translucent stroke along an edge), focus = dashed ring; an id not in the Scene or not drawn at this LOD draws nothing; the layer takes no pointer events. Render counters (`onLayerCommit`, called from an effect of each layer, so a layer React skips never calls it) prove it.
- Depictions (`depictions.ts`, `use-depiction.ts`): `createDepictionManager({ fetchSvg, createUrl, revokeUrl, setTimer, clearTimer, capacity?, timeoutMs? })` with `request` / `snapshot` / `subscribe` / `size` / `dispose`; statuses idle, loading, ready (URL) and unavailable (reason); an LRU of `DEPICTION_LRU_CAPACITY` = 500 keys (PERFORMANCE_ARCHITECTURE §8) over loading, ready and unavailable entries, the least recently REQUESTED dropped, its URL revoked, its timer cleared, a late answer for it ignored; timeout 8,000 ms (`DEPICTION_TIMEOUT_MS`) is `unavailable` / "timeout"; a failure value, a rejection, a throw and a `createUrl` that throws are `unavailable`; an unavailable key is not asked again while cached. The nodes layer draws a ready depiction at LOD 3 as one `<image href>` (never inlined markup); in every other state the labels layer shows the name and formula, never an error. The wiring to the chem service is 5.5a.

**Scene contract amendment (additive; Amendments below; UI_ARCHITECTURE §6 dated line).** `SceneNode.box: BoxKind` is new (`BoxKind` moved from `node-content.ts` to `contracts.ts`): the Scene had the glyph only, so a member of a hub could not be told from a species outside one, and the renderer needs the box for fonts and shape.

**Decisions (REVIEW REQUIRED where marked).**
1. REVIEW REQUIRED. A node box does not grow for a structure: sizes (5.3) come from the name and formula, so a ready depiction at LOD 3 is drawn inside the same box (preserving its aspect ratio, 4 px inset) and the name and formula give way to it. A species box is about 96 to 220 px wide and 40 px high, so a structure is small. Whether LOD 3 species get a taller box (and the layout a second pass) is for 5.5a, with a real look.
2. The dash pattern and the width of an edge are attributes (numbers in `geometry.ts`), not CSS: they carry meaning and a test reads them from the DOM; colours, fonts and fills are CSS Modules and tokens only (`check:colours` green). No `style` attribute anywhere (CSP).
3. `role="graphics-symbol"` for nodes and edges (ARIA graphics module) with an `aria-label`; the root is `role="group"`. The keyboard model (roving tabindex, focus order) is 5.7; no element is focusable yet; axe is not run (no screen yet; 5.5a).
4. The text baselines use the box specs of `BOXES` (font sizes, paddings) and a 1.3 line height, as the fake measurer does; real metrics are 5.5a (note E6). No wrapping: one line per label, clipped.
5. A bug the tests caught in the depiction manager: `createUrl` ran before the check that the entry was still current, so a LATE answer for a dropped key leaked a URL nothing would revoke. `settle` now takes a thunk and calls `createUrl` only for a live entry (test "a dropped loading key ... its late answer makes no URL").
6. The demo navigator Scene has no badges (no flags, no hidden neighbours), so the badge tests use a hand-made Scene; the demo supplies bundles, arrow text and members.
7. The arrow text the demo Scene shows comes from the follow-up a producer with its provisional choices (see the 5.3 review follow-ups); nothing wires it to a screen yet.

**Tests (+69).** `src/render/svg/depictions.test.ts` (14: statuses, timeout, late answers, failures, the LRU bound and revokes over a 60-step run, dispose), `scene-svg.test.tsx` (37: layers and counts, ids and names, no colour literal and no inline style, glyphs, all nine edge styles read from the DOM and pairwise distinct by dash and width, arrowheads, loop, bundle count, tspans and baseline shifts, hostile strings ADV-033, clipping with the full text reachable, LOD 0 to 3, badges), `scene-svg-state.test.tsx` (15: overlay; render counters (a selection or focus change commits the overlay only; the same props commit nothing; a LOD or Scene change commits all); depictions: `<image href>` only, requests only at LOD 3, the name while loading / idle / unavailable / timed out, the real manager with a hostile SVG text that never reaches the DOM), `scene-svg-snapshot.test.tsx` (2: committed DOM snapshots `src/render/svg/__snapshots__/demo-navigator-lod2.svg.txt` (477 lines) and `demo-navigator-expanded-lod1.svg.txt` (1,129 lines), built with the fake measurer and the fallback layout; the `useId` prefix is normalised), and one assertion in `build-scene.test.ts` for `box`. Test support: `src/test-support/svg-kit.tsx`. A test showed that a new `ids` object per render broke the memo of every layer; the ids are memoised.

### Step 5.4a review follow-ups (2026-10-07)

**Follow-up a (code; `src/core/graph/arrow-text.ts`).** An undrawn reactant (water, `entity_display` never) is now on the `above` side, ahead of the other parts of its own stage (a rank in the stable sort: stage, then undrawn first, then record order, so a stage-2 reactant never jumps before the stage-1 number). Tests: the ethene-hydration table row is now above `H2O`, `H3PO4 catalyst`, below nothing; one new test (ordering, below untouched, within a stage only, numbers kept). The demo snapshot `demo-navigator-lod2.svg.txt` changed in one arrow (water first above, nothing below); ADV-027's scene part still finds `H2O` on the arrow text. `npm run check` as ONE command: before 293 files, 4,454 tests; after 293 files, 4,455 tests (+1 test; Vitest 251.0 s), green. Commit: "phase-5.4a: review follow-ups".

**Follow-up b (docs only; NOTHING BUILT): the proposal for condition text on the arrow.** For the reviewer to decide before step 5.5a wires the port. "Docs" means DOMAIN_MODEL §6.3 and `src/core/schema/conditions.ts`; everything else is a suggestion. The docs decide only these: the fields exist (below); structured fields are authoritative and the original wording is kept separately (`text`, stage `originalText`); a variant overrides fields of ONE stage (default: the first stage of kind `reaction`); a field filled by a parser and not confirmed is in `parsedFields` and raises REVIEW_REQUIRED (RXN-015); diazotisation is a range, not the string "0-5 C". Placement, wording and order on the arrow are NOT in the docs.

Proposed side, order, form (suggested): conditions go BELOW the arrow, on their own line after the participants (the textbook habit: reagents above, conditions below), as parts of kind `condition` joined by ", ", at most 3 (`MAX_CONDITION_PARTS`) and then one `+N more` part, never silently cut. Order: temperature, pressure, time, light, activation, atmosphere, anhydrous, other (record order), text. The values are the EFFECTIVE parameters: the stage's fields overridden field by field by the arrow's condition variant (a bundle or an arrow with several variants or none shows the stage's own fields only). With `showStageNumbers` and several stages the numbers of follow-up 5.3 apply to the conditions of each stage as to its participants.

Lines, field: text on the arrow: demo example: decided by the docs or suggested.
- temperature value: "413 K", "25 °C" (value, a space, the unit; a degree sign for C): 413 K and 443 K variants of ethanol + conc. H2SO4 give "413 K" and "443 K": field and units decided by the docs, wording suggested.
- temperature range: "0–5 °C" (en dash): diazotisation of aniline gives "0–5 °C": range decided by the docs, wording suggested.
- temperature qualitative: the enum word with spaces ("reflux", "room temperature", "low temperature", "cold", "warm", "heat", "strong heat"): ethanol to ethanoic acid (excess K2Cr2O7/H+) gives "reflux": suggested.
- pressure value or range: "60–70 atm", "1 bar"; qualitative "reduced pressure", "atmospheric pressure", "high pressure": ethene hydration gives "573 K, 60–70 atm": suggested.
- time: "2 h", "30 min", "1 d"; qualitative "brief" / "prolonged": the demo has none: suggested.
- light: "hν" for `hv`, "UV", "visible light", "sunlight", "diffuse light", "dark": propene + HBr with peroxide gives "hν": suggested.
- activation (list): each word as is ("electrolysis", "ultrasound", "microwave", "radiation"): the demo has none: suggested.
- atmosphere: "inert atmosphere" for `inert`; formula parts "N2", "Ar", "O2", "H2" for the gases; `air` left out (the default): the demo has none: suggested.
- anhydrous (true): "anhydrous"; false or absent: nothing: the demo has none (the "dry ether" of the Grignard reactions is a solvent participant): suggested.
- other (list): each qualifier as written ("sealed tube", "peroxide present"): propene + HBr gives "hν, peroxide present": suggested.
- text (free restatement): shown only when no structured field gave any text (it restates them); never beside them: suggested; the schema comment says "shown beside structured values", so the reviewer may prefer that.
- medium: LEFT OUT of the arrow (the acid or base is a participant already written above; the inspector shows it): diazotisation does not show "acidic": suggested.
- solventCharacter: LEFT OUT (a solvent participant is written by name; the inspector shows the character): suggested.
- parsedFields: nothing on the arrow; the existing review badge says a field is unconfirmed (RXN-015): suggested.
- stage originalText: NOT used on the arrow (it restates participants and conditions, so it would duplicate the parts); the inspector shows it: suggested; the participant `originalText` rule (decision 2) stays.
- stage label and kind, variant label and note: not on the arrow (the label "413 K, excess ethanol" gives its "413 K" through the field; "excess ethanol" is in the inspector): suggested.
What the arrow does not say is derived display only: no record loses anything, and the inspector (5.8) lists every field.

### Phase 5, step 5.4b — Viewport (`src/render/viewport`) (2026-10-07)

Session start: AC power checked (PowerOnline True). Part A (the 5.4a review follow-ups) is commit f4f1748. d3-zoom 3.0.0 and d3-selection 3.0.0 (direct, exact pins) and their `@types` (dev) are added; `npm audit` 0; no `eval` / `new Function` in the d3 sources; rows in docs/DEPENDENCIES.md.

**What was built (`src/render/viewport`; every file under 130 lines).**
- `contracts.ts`: the constants (scale 0.05 to 4, zoom step 1.25, fit padding 24, focus cap 1.5, overscan 0.5, hysteresis 5%, still time 250 ms, 4 depiction requests in flight) and `ViewportState` / `ViewportStore`.
- `transform.ts` (pure): clamp, zoom about a point, fit, focus, the world rectangle of the screen, the transform attribute. `lod.ts` (pure): the threshold table of the Scene contract, 5% hysteresis in both directions, and the budget cap (over 400 visible nodes or 800 visible edges the level is 1 and `capped` says so).
- `grid-index.ts`: a uniform grid (256 world units) over node boxes and edge boxes (route plus the label point); a query answers ascending indices; an item spanning more than 64 cells goes to a short "large" list, so building is bounded for any input. `cull.ts`: the culling rectangle (screen plus half a screen of overscan) and the depiction candidates (species strictly on the screen, nearest the centre first).
- `store.ts`: a plain external store (not React state). `controller.ts`: DOM-free; gestures and commands set a pending viewport, one frame is scheduled (injected `schedule`), and in it ONE transform is written (injected `write`), culling and LOD run, the store is updated and the depiction candidates go to the queue. `zoomIn`, `zoomOut`, `fit`, `focus(ids)` (false when no id is in the Scene: nothing moves, nothing silent).
- `depiction-queue.ts`: asks only at LOD 3, only for visible species, after the view has been still for 250 ms; at most 4 in flight (PROGRESS Q7: the chem worker takes 16 calls, the rest is for checks); cancels what left the screen and everything below LOD 3; a busy service is retried every 500 ms up to 5 times and never reaches the user.
- `zoom-binding.ts`: d3-zoom on the root `<svg>`; d3 never writes the DOM; double-click zoom off; the inline `style` d3 adds is removed (CSP; the stylesheet gives `touch-action: none`); `extent` is the element's laid-out box. `viewport-scene.tsx`: `ViewportScene` = `SceneSvg` plus the above; React reads the LOD only; the layers get a depiction source whose `request` does nothing (the queue decides what is asked for).
- In `src/render/svg` (additive; Amendments): `SceneSvgProps.svgRef` and `interactive` (the class with `touch-action: none`); `DepictionSource.cancel?`; the manager's `cancel(key)` and the `BUSY` answer (`DEPICTION_BUSY`): not cached, the key is idle again.

**Decisions.**
1. The queue holds 4 requests in flight, not 16 (docs/PROGRESS Q7 and PERFORMANCE_ARCHITECTURE: a burst must queue on main with a small cap).
2. Culling is for the LOD budget and the depiction candidates: the renderer still draws the whole Scene (the Scene is bounded by the projection; 5.4a draws every element). Removing off-screen elements from the DOM would re-render on pan, which this step forbids. The budget cap is what keeps a large Scene cheap.
3. A bug the tests caught: a busy answer arrives after the request, so the queue first saw the key as loading, then idle, and re-asked without counting a retry (unbounded). It now counts the retry when a key it asked for turns idle.
4. The e2e part of the pan/zoom row moves to 5.5a (there is no screen yet); the plan row says so.

**Tests (+33: 30 in 6 viewport files, 3 in the manager).** Grid index equals brute force (property, 150 runs, nodes and edges), a box beyond the cell budget, empty scene; LOD table, hysteresis, no flicker, budget cap; transform arithmetic; controller (one write per frame for 200 gestures, nothing written when unchanged, limits, fit and focus, LOD in the store, the cap, dispose); queue (stillness, bound, cancel, busy retry and its bound, ready and unavailable not re-asked, dispose); component (fit on mount, zero React commits and zero layer commits during pan and zoom with a Profiler and the layer counters, commands move the world group, one render at a level change, the layers start no fetch); manager (`cancel`, `BUSY`). Bench `tests/perf/viewport.bench.ts`: a culling query is 0.05 ms mean at 400 / 800 and 0.16 ms at 2,000 / 4,000 (informal budget 4 ms); the index build 0.5 ms and 1.7 ms.

`npm run check` as ONE command: before 293 files, 4,455 tests; after 299 files, 4,488 tests (+6 files, +33 tests), green.

**Not done.** Wiring into the navigator, the real measurer, hit areas and the e2e of pan and zoom (5.5a); 60 fps measured in a browser (5.10b).

Next: step 5.5a (navigator end to end).

The exporters in `src/io` are unchanged. No schema change, no `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump, no invariant change. Docs: PROGRESS.md step 4.2e.
- **2026-09-30, step 4.2d - `ComputeWorkerApi.snapshotDecode({ bytes, id, schemaVersion, baseRevision })` (`SnapshotDecodeParams`, `SnapshotDecodeAnswer`, `SnapshotDecodeRefused`); `ImportPrepareRefused` gains `BUSY`; `WorkerEvent` gains `validation_fallback`; `PersistenceAdapter.readSnapshotBytes` (`SnapshotBytesReadResult`); `BootPorts.validate` (optional, returns `PrecomputedFindings`); `BootReport.precomputed` and `BootReport.validation`; `StoreOptions.precomputed`; `FollowerOptions.validate`, `FollowerEvent.validation_fallback` and `precomputed` on `loaded`; `ReplicaSyncOptions.adopt`, `ReplicaSync.noteLoaded`; core exports `recordCountsOf`, `precomputedFor`, `checkPrecomputed` (contract additions, CLAUDE.md §7; Phase 4 contract decisions Q1, Q3, Q4, observation 3, master prompt of step 4.2d).** Problem: a snapshot had to be decoded, migrated, validated and prepared without the main thread doing the whole-state work; boot and a follower's reload had to take their findings from the worker and build indexes once. Smallest change: the calls and options above, all additive with defaults that keep every existing caller and test unchanged (`validate` absent means in process, `precomputed` absent means the store validates); `BootPorts.validate` answers `PrecomputedFindings` rather than bare findings so a worker of another build is refused by the store's own checks. No schema change, no `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump, no invariant change. Docs: ADR-034 bullet, PERSISTENCE.md §4, §9, §10.
- **2026-09-30, step 4.2c — `ComputeWorkerApi`: `importPreview({ source: Blob, fileName, request?, ids })`, `importRevise({ batchId, request, ids })`, `importCancel`, `importPrepareApply`, `snapshotBuild({ revision, reason, exportedAt })`, `adoptPending`, `dropPending` (new types `IdData`, `WorkerImportOutcome`, `ImportPrepared`, `ImportPrepareRefused`, `SnapshotBuildAnswer`, `AdoptAnswer`); `PersistenceAdapter.storeSnapshotBytes` (`SnapshotBytesInput`, `StoreSnapshotBytesResult`) and `WriteQueue.currentRevision`; `ImportSession` gains `prepareApply`, `consume`, `pendingBaseRevision` and `ImportSessionDeps` write ports become optional (contract additions, CLAUDE.md §7; Phase 4 contract decisions Q1 and item 2, master prompt of step 4.2c).** Problem: the import session had to run over the replica in the worker, apply needed the merged state and its findings from the worker, and the pre-import snapshot had to be built there without an injected builder. - **2026-09-30, step 4.2b — `ComputeWorkerApi`: `loadReplica` answers `PrecomputedFindings`; `applyChanges`, `validateAll`, `findRoutes` answer `ReplicaAnswer<...>` (ok or `ReplicaStale` with `have`); `findRoutes` takes `{ revision, query }`; `WorkerEvent` gains `replica_status` (contract additions and one shape change, CLAUDE.md §7; Phase 4 plan row 4.2b).** Problem: the replica must be revision-checked and its stale answers must be values the client can act on; `loadReplica` must return what the store accepts as precomputed findings; a bare `RouteQuery` cannot name a revision. Smallest change: the result types above (a stale answer is `{ ok: false, code: 'REPLICA_STALE', have }`, the same value for the three calls), `findRoutes` params `{ revision, query }`, one new `WorkerEvent` variant. No other method changed. ADR-034 gets a bullet; `tests/types/rpc-contracts.test.ts` updated for the new `validateAll` type. Reason: docs/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md step 4.2b and its specifics (loadReplica returns the findings; REPLICA_STALE as a value).
- **2026-09-30, step 4.2a — `CanonicalStore.replaceState` and `reload` gain an optional last parameter `PrecomputedFindings`; `CommandErrorCode` gains `FINDINGS_MISMATCH`; `StoreOptions.verifyPrecomputed`; `RULE_SET_ID` (contract additions, CLAUDE.md §7; Phase 4 contract decisions Q3).** Problem: `replaceState` and `reload` validated the whole state on the main thread (about 2-2.8 s of 2.9 s at the design tier), although the compute worker will already have the findings. - **2026-09-30, step 4.1 — RPC contracts (`src/workers/rpc/contracts.ts`) and worker rules (Phase 4 contract decisions Q5 (a)-(g), Q7 `WorkerEvent`, Q8).** Problem: `RpcClient<ComputeWorkerApi>` did not typecheck (TS2344, interfaces have no index signature), and the contract had no restart hook, method specs, port and factory types, transfer, in-flight cap error or event type. Change: for `reaction_type`, `mechanism_class`, `reaction_family` the key is `<kind>\u0000<folded text>` (one text under two kinds is two concepts); generic roles unchanged. Change, smallest: `int_v6-<reaction part>-<n>` (`n` = the participant's 1-based position in the legacy list, like `prt_` and `pdt_`), `LegacyIdSpace.intermediateId(reactionId, n)`, tested against `IntermediateIdSchema`; §4.2 states how `n` is counted. - **2026-09-29, step 3.7 review — content-addressed `assetBlobs`: DB version 2, `AssetBlobRow`, `assetBlobsUnverified`, `listAssetBlobHashes`/`listUnverifiedAssetBlobs`, `BootReport.unverifiedAssetBlobs`, `ImportAssetSummary.overwritten` removed (contract changes, reviewer-required in the step 3.7 review, item b).** Problem: `assetBlobs` was keyed by asset ID, so an import that replaced an asset with different bytes overwrote the old bytes and "Undo import" or a snapshot restore could not bring them back (irreversible loss, CLAUDE.md non-negotiable 5). Change: `DB_VERSION` 1 → 2, `DB_STORES.assetBlobs` = `'sha256'` (row `{ sha256, blob }`), new store `assetBlobsUnverified` (`'assetId'`; `UnverifiedAssetBlobRow`); writes verify the bytes' hash, then add a row only if none has that hash, and nothing is ever overwritten or deleted; `getAssetBlob` reads through the asset record's `sha256`; `PersistenceAdapter.listAssetBlobIds` → `listAssetBlobHashes` (AST-002 compares hashes) plus `listUnverifiedAssetBlobs`; `BootReport.unverifiedAssetBlobs`; `UnverifiedAssetBlob`; `ImportAssetSummary.overwritten` removed; `DB_STORES_V1` frozen in `db.ts` and the upgrade steps 1 → 1.5 → 2 in `DB_VERSION_STEPS` (`asset-rekey.ts`). No canonical schema change, no `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump (this is the IndexedDB layout, migrated by a Dexie upgrade with a test), no fixture change. Docs updated in the same commit: PERSISTENCE.md §2, §4, §5, §6.

No schema change, no `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump, no migration, no fixture change. Docs updated in the same commit: PERSISTENCE.md §5, §6, PROGRESS.md step 3.7.
